# Inside Story — Autoimmune Food & Health App — Project Brief

**Positioning note, 2026-08-08**: this project began as, and was titled, a Hashimoto's-only app. It is now explicitly positioned as an autoimmune food/health app more broadly — Hashimoto's remains the only condition built out to full depth today, but the app's own framing, naming, and in-app copy no longer present it as Hashimoto's-exclusive. See the [Beyond Hashimoto's research Artifact](https://claude.ai/code/artifact/c2c994c0-0890-45b0-8be9-404287d4afe0) for the full research behind this (top 10 autoimmune diseases, food allergies, multiple concurrent conditions, 9 non-autoimmune candidates, and a combined build-priority ranking), and this document's own "Monetization — tiers" section for the household/caregiver tier structure decided alongside it (full reasoning in a companion [Household & Monetization Tiers Artifact](https://claude.ai/code/artifact/e153d351-52c7-4df5-a4c1-e134ff86c085)). This document's own extensive build history below still reads as Hashimoto's-specific throughout, because it genuinely was until this date — that history is accurate and untouched, not retroactively rewritten.

Originally captured from a brainstorming session, before any build work started — that's still the "why and what" behind most of this document. It is now a living brief for an app under active development; the **Status** section below is kept current, while the rest of the document records architecture and product decisions (most still aspirational/unbuilt unless the Status section says otherwise). Read this before picking up app-development work in a new chat.

For a fuller build history, day-by-day decisions, and an explicit reconciliation of what's built vs. only planned, see the [Inside Story Notion hub](https://app.notion.com/p/3a853652f2728144bde2d78ead5227ea) — it's more detailed than this file and is where new findings get written up first.

## Working across sessions

This file is exactly what makes starting a fresh chat cheap: a new session reads it automatically and picks up the project's real status without needing a re-explanation of setup, architecture, or history. For genuinely separate pieces of work — a new feature area, a different day's session — starting fresh is a reasonable default specifically because this document carries the context over. There's no fixed message count where a session "should" end; a short conversational thread and a long one full of pasted code/tool output use up a weekly allowance very differently, so if usage is a concern, it's worth watching what a session actually contains rather than how many messages it's had.

## Status (last refreshed 2026-08-08)

App name finalized: **Inside Story**. Dev environment set up and verified (Node.js, VS Code, Expo Go on Android, project scaffolded at `C:\Dev\hashimotos-app` via `npx create-expo-app@latest --template default@sdk-54`, reset to a blank Expo Router starter). The project is now a real git repository (an auto-checkpoint mechanism commits work in progress) — the "no git repository" note elsewhere in this doc is stale, left over from before that existed.

**EAS build pipeline set up, 2026-07-26**, moving past Expo Go for anything that needs native config testing. Logged into `eas-cli`, linked the app to a new EAS project under the dedicated `inside-story` Expo account (`@inside-story/inside-story`, not the personal `antoniobanderock` account), installed `expo-dev-client`, and added `eas.json` (development/preview/production build profiles). This required deciding the app's permanent identity: package/bundle ID **`com.insidestoryapp.app`** on both Android and iOS, matching the reserved `insidestoryapp.com` domain. Day-to-day JS changes still hot-reload live via `npx expo start --dev-client`; only native/config changes need a fresh EAS build + reinstall on the dev-client app already on the test Android phone.

That first real dev-client build immediately surfaced a genuine bug invisible on every previous dev device: a fresh install crashed on Home with "no such table: user_profile," because `lib/db.ts`'s table-creation logic was only ever guaranteed to run as a side effect of a code path Home doesn't call. Fixed — the database now initializes once at app startup in `app/_layout.tsx`.

**Android's dark nav-bar-behind-TabHub bug is now understood and resolved for practical purposes.** Three build cycles of investigation (see the Notion App Development Log for the full blow-by-blow) landed on: Android **forces** a semi-opaque contrast scrim behind system bars for 3-button navigation specifically, as a hard accessibility guarantee — no app-level theme setting can override it (confirmed via pixel-level screenshot comparison; gesture navigation has zero scrim and needed no fix). Since 3-button nav is the user's actual preference, the real fix was layout, not theming: `constants/floatingButton.ts`'s `FLOATING_BUTTON_BOTTOM_OFFSET` raised by 20px so TabHub's floating button and the butterfly artwork's own overhang both clear the scrim strip entirely.

**Local Android builds now work, 2026-07-26**, as a faster alternative to EAS's cloud queue (10-15 min/build) for native/config changes — installed Android Studio (`ANDROID_HOME`/`JAVA_HOME` now point at its bundled SDK/JDK), and `npx expo run:android` builds via Gradle directly and installs over USB (`adb`) in as little as 12-20 seconds once Gradle's cache is warm. `eas build --local` itself doesn't work on Windows for Android (macOS/Linux only, a hard EAS CLI limitation) — this uses Expo's own `run:android`/Gradle path instead, which has no such restriction.

**Home tab: real initial-route fix, app icon, and a first design-system pass, 2026-07-26.** Home now genuinely opens first on a fresh launch — not just an initial-route *setting* (two attempts at that, matching Expo Router's own documented API, both failed to survive a real cold launch), but a literal file swap: Home's file is now `app/(tabs)/index.tsx` (Expo Router always treats a route named "index" as a group's real bare-path route, no override needed), and Food moved to `app/(tabs)/food.tsx`. The app icon is now the butterfly artwork (composited onto the app's own navy — true icon transparency isn't possible on either platform) rather than the Expo default, with a matching Android adaptive-icon/monochrome-themed-icon set. Home's own visual design also got its first real pass: `colors.surface` (the card background used almost everywhere in the app) is now translucent (85% opacity) so each tab's background photo shows through slightly, and Home's animated sky (`components/AnimatedSky.tsx`) now shows a real cropped sun photo (matching the moon's existing treatment) plus a starfield that fades in/out with the existing day/night tint curve.

**A substantial, mostly-working multi-screen app now exists** — this supersedes any earlier "no real feature code written yet" note. Built and functional: **Insights** (nutrient-vs-RDA and D1-D6 breakdowns with drill-down and citations), **Schedules** (Meals/Hydration/Supplements/Prescriptions/Doctor Appointments/Exercise sub-schedules), **Home** (personalized dashboard), **Bio-Compass** (flare-up/symptom/new-food tracker), **Profile**, and a symptom/healing-journey **Assessment**. Backed by a 153KB SQLite data-access layer (`lib/db.ts`) and a bundled 22,016-food reference database imported from the separate nutrition-database project. **Trends and Reports are still stub screens**, not built out yet. None of the cloud-backup/sync/multi-device/household-sharing architecture described later in this document has been implemented — all work so far is local-only, single-device.

**The Food tab (formerly "Meals") was deliberately deleted and is being rebuilt from scratch, 2026-07-25.** The old single-purpose meal builder (dish/side creation, ingredient search, favorites/templates, edit/delete, rotating ingredients, retroactive-logging date/time picker) is gone by explicit request — at the time this happened the project had no git repository yet (see Status above — it does now), so the full old implementation is preserved only as a manual backup at `ClaudeWork/backups/index.tsx.<timestamp>.bak`, not in any commit history. In its place, the Food tab is now seven focused builders reached from the same LensHub picker pattern already used by Insights/Schedule/Bio-Compass: **Meal Builder, Side Builder, Salad Builder, Smoothie Builder, Fermentation Builder, Beverage Builder, Snack Builder** — all seven currently plain "not built yet" stubs, to be built out one at a time. This is a more granular split than the "Meal assembler/Salad/Smoothie/Side-dish/Fermented" version of this plan described later in this document (that version predates Beverage Builder and Snack Builder as their own separate builders, and predates the decision to delete-and-restart rather than incrementally split the existing builder). **Known regression from this deletion, not yet fixed**: Schedule's "Log now" action and Home's Day Arc both navigate to the Food tab with params (`scheduleItemId`/`mealType`/`title`/`favoriteId`/`templateMealId`) expecting the old builder to read them and open pre-filled — nothing reads those params anymore, so right now both actions just land on a blank Food tab without actually logging anything. Needs reconnecting once whichever builder a scheduled meal maps to (almost certainly Meal Builder) is rebuilt.

**A dark navy/jewel-tone visual redesign, sourced from a commissioned thyroid-butterfly artwork, replaced the app's original light "Sage & Cream" theme in late July 2026** (`constants/colors.ts`), along with a wildflower-field background image behind every tab's content (`components/ScreenBackground.tsx`) — the Food tab is the one deliberate exception, using a fruits/vegetables scene instead, shown uncropped (`contentFit: 'contain'`) rather than cropped-to-fill, specifically so as much of the produce as possible stays visible; that tradeoff does mean visible dark-navy letterboxing above/below the image where its aspect ratio doesn't match the screen's, a known, accepted side effect rather than a bug. This redesign, plus the popup-menu (`TabHub`) navigation described below, is not otherwise written up in this document yet beyond what's captured here and in the Notion hub.

**Navigation has changed from the original design** (see the App Navigation Structure section below for the current shipped structure vs. the original four-tab plan).

**Side Dish Builder's wheel pickers (originally `components/WheelPicker.tsx`), replaced entirely, 2026-08-01.** A same-day performance bug (wraparound spinning implemented as several duplicated copies of each option list, reported as multiple seconds to open) got progressively fixed down to a single, non-duplicated option list per wheel — but on-device testing then surfaced two real, structural usability problems with the drag-to-scroll wheel metaphor itself, not fixable by further tuning: it always needed a first touch just to "wake up" before it could be dragged, and a dragging finger physically covers the one row (the live, centre one) a person is trying to watch settle. Both are inherent to a small, finger-dragged dial. **`WheelPicker.tsx` was deleted outright and replaced by `components/PopoverSelect.tsx`** — a plain field (current value + chevron) that opens a small list of directly tappable options beside itself (right side if the field sits in the left half of the screen, left side if it sits in the right half), reusing `Dropdown.tsx`'s proven `OverlayContext` portal approach (not React Native's own `Modal`) rather than inventing a third mechanism. No drag gesture anywhere now, so neither original problem can recur. Also added the same day: `NAVIGATION_HAND`-aware reordering (`useReorderedLabeledFields` in `SideBuilder.tsx`) — as each field in a row is filled in, it slides (animated via Reanimated's `LinearTransition` layout transition) to the end of the row away from the configured navigation hand, keeping still-blank fields clustered near the thumb; the whole row snaps back to original order once every field has a value. Confirmed working and "fast" on-device.

**`AppTextInput` never cleared itself from the shared "what's focused" state on unmount, only on a real blur event — fixed 2026-08-01.** A field that unmounts while still focused (e.g. Side Builder's autoFocused Dish Name field, left via LensHub/TabHub without tapping away first) left `AppKeyboard` — a single global overlay mounted once at the app root — rendering against a dead field's stale closures indefinitely: stuck visible across every other Food builder regardless of which one was actually open. Fixed in `AppTextInput.tsx`'s own unmount cleanup. Confirmed working on-device.

**Android's native text-editing toolbar (Cut/Copy/Paste/Autofill), not the separate Autofill *framework* popup, was the actual thing showing a black "Autofill" chip over text fields — root-caused and fixed 2026-08-01.** Two Autofill-framework-specific fixes (`importantForAutofill` at both the per-field and the app-manifest level, the latter via a new `plugins/withAutofillDisabled.js` Expo config plugin) were tried first and both failed to stop the chip appearing at all (they only stopped it from actually filling anything). The real clue: it only appeared when tapping a field that *already* had a cursor in it, not on first focus — the signature of Android's native text-selection contextual toolbar, which includes "Autofill" as one of its default menu items independent of any per-field opt-out. Fixed with `contextMenuHidden` on the shared `AppTextInput.tsx` (real tradeoff, accepted deliberately: this also removes the only touch-based way to Cut/Copy/Paste in these fields, since `AppKeyboard` provides no menu of its own to replace it). Confirmed fixed on-device. The two Autofill-framework-specific fixes were left in place as harmless defense-in-depth, not reverted.

**Keyboard visibly passing in front of the footer/TabHub button while closing — a real, explainable bug, fixed 2026-08-01.** `AppKeyboard`'s close animation used to translate its own box down through the footer's own screen space on its way off-screen (not just fade out in place), and since `AppKeyboard` is mounted last at the app root, it painted over the footer for that portion of the animation. Fixed by splitting the box into a fixed-position, fixed-size, `overflow: hidden` outer wrapper and an inner animated content view that slides *within* that fixed window — the box can no longer geometrically reach into the footer's space at all, regardless of paint order. Confirmed fixed on-device.

**Known cosmetic issue, unresolved as of 2026-08-01: TabHub's AND LensHub's popup menu cards both visibly drop in from above** (worse than the original 2026-07-28 "a few pixels" description), confirmed gated on a `WheelPicker` being mounted elsewhere on screen at the time — nine reasoned fix attempts across two sessions all failed, including on-device-captured timing data showing the card's own layout committing correctly on its first and only pass, with Android's Dialog window confirmation (`onShow`) arriving ~34ms later; gating the card's own visibility on that same `onShow` signal (a tenth, evidence-backed attempt) still didn't change the behavior. Deliberately shelved — harmless, and needs real on-device profiling to make further progress, not another guess. **Important update, same day, later:** the confirmed trigger (`WheelPicker`'s live Reanimated content) no longer exists anywhere in the app — `WheelPicker.tsx` was deleted and replaced by `PopoverSelect.tsx` (see above), which has zero Reanimated content. This bug has **not been retested** since that replacement; it may already be resolved as a side effect, or it may reproduce through some other mechanism. Worth a fresh on-device check before spending any more effort on it either way.

**Side Dish Builder can now actually save a side, 2026-08-01** — the first real persistence for the rebuilt Food tab's builders. New `sides`/`side_ingredients` tables in `lib/db.ts` (see `saveSide`) and a new `finishSide` function in `SideBuilder.tsx`, wired to both "Save & Finish Side" buttons (including one on a previously-dead-end "ready" screen that had no working finish button at all before this). Deliberately a third kind of record, not a `meals` row (which assumes something eaten at a specific moment) and not a `favorites` row (saving as a reusable template is explicitly a separate, not-yet-built action) — a real, standalone saved side, meant to be assembled into an actual meal later once Meal Builder exists to do that assembling. On save, the builder resets to a blank side (a meal is built from *multiple* saved sides, so returning to "add another side" is the expected flow) — only after a successful save, so a save failure never silently loses the in-progress dish. The other nine Food builders are expected to get their own similarly-shaped save paths as each is built out, per the same pattern, not one forced-generic table.

**Bell peppers didn't appear anywhere recognizable in the Vegetables list, root-caused and fixed 2026-08-02.** All 18 real USDA sweet-pepper rows had been collapsed into one generic `Sweet Pepper` base_name during the reference-DB build; a `bell pepper` search alias already existed and correctly pointed at it, but browsing the list with no typed query never goes through the alias table at all (it just lists real base_name strings alphabetically), so nothing recognizable as a bell pepper — and no color distinction — ever showed up that way. Fixed with a new `rename_sweet_pepper_by_color()` step in `scripts/build_food_reference_db.py`, splitting those 18 rows (every one already states its own color in the full food name) into `Red Bell Pepper` / `Green Bell Pepper` / `Yellow Bell Pepper`; updated the D6 oxalate backfill dict and `scripts/food_alias_data.py`'s aliases to match. Also found and fixed two unrelated dangling aliases (parsley, all-purpose flour) that had drifted stale from an earlier rebuild earlier in the same session — `scripts/add_food_aliases.py` fails loudly on a dangling alias by design, which is what surfaced them. Rebuilt `assets/data/foods_reference.db`, re-ran `add_food_aliases.py`, bumped `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION`.

**Real, confirmed race condition in `FoodLookup.tsx` fixed the same day**, found while testing the bell pepper fix: switching to a new food reset `prepMethod` to null synchronously, but the resolve effect's guard used the *previous* food's `prepMethods.length` (stale, since the new food's own prep-method list loads asynchronously) to decide whether it was safe to resolve yet. When the previous food had no prep choices to disambiguate, the resolve effect fired immediately for the new food with `prepMethod` still null, before the async "default to Raw" lookup (added 2026-08-01) had landed — `resolveFoodChoice()` then fell back to whichever row has no prep_method tag at all, which for foods like Sweet Pepper is a canned variant (confirmed: sweet peppers and carrots both did this). In `showNutrients=false` mode (Side Builder etc.) that wrong first resolution fires `onFoodResolved` immediately and the caller swaps the component away, so the later correct Raw re-resolve never got a chance to run. Fixed with an explicit `prepMethodsLoading` flag the resolve effect now waits on, instead of inferring readiness from list length.

**A second, genuinely different resolution bug found and fixed the same day, reported directly after the nine-builder session: garlic, a specific bacon variant, and chia seeds would silently fail to resolve at all (no pending-ingredient card, no error) in Side Builder and Salad Builder, while foods like asparagus and Snap Beans (Green Beans) worked fine.** Root cause, confirmed by reading `getPreparationMethods()`: it deliberately collapses a food's prep-method list down to `[]` whenever there's only one real option on file, so the picker UI doesn't ask someone to choose between one thing — but it did this *unconditionally*, even when that one option was a real, specific tag (Garlic's only USDA row is tagged `'Raw'`; Chia Seeds' only USDA row is tagged `'Dried'`; that specific bacon variant's only row is tagged `'Raw'`). `FoodLookup.tsx` then defaulted `prepMethod` to `null` (since an empty list obviously has no `'Raw'` to find), and `resolveFoodChoice()` translates `null` into a query for its own internal `'Standard'` sentinel — which only matches a row where `prep_method IS NULL`. None of these three foods have such a row, so the query matched nothing and resolution failed silently. Multi-prep foods (asparagus: Raw/Boiled/Canned; snap beans: 21 rows across Raw/Boiled/Canned) never hit this collapsed path at all, which is exactly the split reported. **Verified directly against the real reference database** (not just reasoned about) that the fix resolves all three foods correctly and leaves the already-working multi-prep foods unaffected. Fixed in two places: `getPreparationMethods()` (`lib/db.ts`) now only collapses to `[]` when the single value is genuinely the untagged sentinel, not any real tag; `FoodLookup.tsx`'s prep-loading effect now auto-selects a single real option the same way it already auto-selected `'Raw'` among multiple, regardless of what that one option is actually called. Side effect, accepted as correct rather than a regression: a food with exactly one real (non-Raw) prep tag, like Chia Seeds, now shows a small pre-resolved "Dried · Change" summary row where it used to show nothing at all — an honest reflection of a real resolved state, not new UI clutter.

**A related process note, since this surfaced right after a long multi-file session:** the first hypothesis was a stale Metro dev-server bundle, not a real code bug — a reasonable read given `FoodLookup.tsx` and `SideBuilder.tsx` were both confirmed byte-for-byte unchanged all session (`git diff` against the pre-session commit showed zero changes to either), and the new `lib/db.ts` schema additions were confirmed syntactically valid and non-interfering. That hypothesis was tested by restarting the dev server for real, which surfaced a **separate, genuine issue**: the running server had been started via a bare `expo start --dev-client --clear` (no `--port` flag) rather than through `npm start` (`scripts/start-with-qr.js`), which meant the 2026-08-02 auto-kill-leftover-server fix never applied — Expo fell back to its own stock "port 8081 busy, use 8082?" behavior. Killed the stray process tree, confirmed port 8081 free, restarted via the real `npm start` path, confirmed clean. Only after a genuine restart still reproduced the bug (with a sharper repro: "asparagus and snap beans work, garlic/bacon/chia seeds don't") did the actual root cause above get found. Worth remembering: **always launch this project's dev server via `npm start` / `npm run android` specifically** — any other launch path (a raw `expo start` command, an editor's own "run" button, etc.) skips both of `start-with-qr.js`'s protections (auto-kill leftovers, force port 8081) entirely.

**AppKeyboard input lag fixed the same day, reported directly after the food-picker fix: typing on the app's own custom keyboard (`components/AppKeyboard.tsx`) got noticeably slow and dropped keystrokes on the Food builders specifically.** Root cause, traced through the actual per-keystroke code path rather than guessed: `AppTextInput.tsx` re-registers the focused field with `AppKeyboard` via `focusField()` (a real `setActiveField()` context update) every time its own registration effect re-fires — and that effect's dependency array included the field's raw `value`/`selection`, both of which change on *every character typed*. Each keystroke therefore forced a full re-render of `AppKeyboard` *and* whatever screen owns the field — on the Food builders, each ~1,900 lines of JSX (PopoverSelects, DimensionFlags, the ingredient list) reconciled in full just to update one field's displayed text. Confirmed via `grep` that only `AppKeyboard.tsx` itself ever reads `activeField.value`/`.selection` directly (every other consumer across the app, including all nine builders and `FoodLookup.tsx`, only checks truthiness) — a fully-contained, three-file fix. `ActiveField`'s `value`/`selection` fields became `getValue()`/`getSelection()` — closures reading a ref (`AppTextInput.tsx`'s own `valueRef`/new `selectionRef`, kept live every render) instead of plain values captured at registration time. The registration effect now only fires on a real focus change or a genuinely new callback identity (already stable at every real call site — raw `useState` setters or `useCallback`-wrapped handlers, the same pattern `titleCaseDishName`'s own 2026-07-30 fix already established for exactly this reason), not on every keystroke. `insertText`/`backspace` in `AppKeyboard.tsx` now call `activeField.getValue()`/`.getSelection()` at press-time instead of destructuring stale-by-design plain fields. No behavior change for typing itself, no risk to the two previously-fixed infinite-render-loop bugs (both were about `searchRequest`/combined-context churn, a different mechanism this fix doesn't touch at all) — purely a reduction in how often the registration (and therefore the cascade) fires. Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint` on all three touched files (both clean).

**The OS's own soft keyboard could appear over AppKeyboard's own overlay after minimizing the app (with a text field still focused) and reopening it, fixed the same day, reported after a few real occurrences.** Root cause: `showSoftInputOnFocus={false}` (`AppTextInput.tsx`, the one prop this entire custom-keyboard feature rests on) only suppresses the OS keyboard at the moment JS-driven focus happens -- it doesn't survive the native window itself losing and regaining focus, a separate path Android's own InputMethodManager can trigger on its own when a backgrounded app with an already-focused EditText returns to the foreground, bypassing that prop entirely since no new `.focus()` call is what's causing it to show. Fixed with an `AppState` listener in `AppKeyboard.tsx` that calls `Keyboard.dismiss()` on every return to `'active'`, unconditionally (not gated on a field currently being focused) -- a harmless no-op when nothing's showing. **Confirmed on-device behavior, refined after the initial fix**: this doesn't leave the field sitting focused with just the OS keyboard suppressed -- Android appears to already blur the field itself as a normal part of the window losing focus while backgrounded, independent of this fix, so by the time `'active'` fires here, `activeField` has usually already cleared and AppKeyboard has already hidden on its own; this effect's real job is mopping up the OS keyboard the system tries to show anyway for that now-blurred view a beat later. Net effect, and the accepted outcome: both keyboards close and the field shows as deselected after resuming, needing one more tap to resume typing -- a clean, fully-closed state rather than a stale field silently still focused. Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint` (both clean).

**Salad Builder built out, 2026-08-02 -- the second of the Food tab's ten builders to go from stub to real, following the explicit build-order decision made the same day** (see Next steps below: Meal Builder has to come last since it assembles the other nine builders' own saved output). Built as a direct, deliberate adaptation of Side Builder's already-proven, on-device-tested pattern (`components/SaladBuilder.tsx`, ~1,950 lines) rather than a fresh design — same Name/Servings/Serving Size → per-ingredient Category/Type/Food/Prep/Quantity/Unit/Cut Prep/Cook Prep flow, same summary card, same PopoverSelect fields, same NAVIGATION_HAND-aware reordering. New `salads`/`salad_ingredients` tables in `lib/db.ts`, mirroring `sides`/`side_ingredients` exactly (`saveSalad`/`updateSalad`/`deleteSalad`/`listSalads`/`getSalad`/`getSaladIngredients`/`getSaladNutrientBreakdown`/`getSaladSixDimensionsBreakdown`) rather than reusing Side's own tables — Salad is expected to diverge from Side later (a dressing-specific field, say), and the per-builder-table decision was already made when Side's own tables were added. Wired into `app/(tabs)/food.tsx` (lens routing, `editSaladId` deep-link param, "Saved Salads"/"Favorite Salads" My Foods categories), `app/food-items.tsx` (list/edit/delete), and `app/food-item-detail.tsx` (Nutrients/6 Dimensions/Cooking & Prep drill-down, reusing the exact same Insights machinery Side's own detail view already reuses). The one genuinely new, salad-specific feature: finishing a salad now checks for two or more raw goitrogenic/cruciferous ingredients mixed together (reading each ingredient's own already-fetched "Goitrogenic Load" score, no re-query needed) and warns before saving — not a hard block, a real "are you sure," reviving the same concrete concern (and the same Cancel/Continue-anyway shape) the old, deleted meal builder had for its Salad/Smoothie meal types before the 2026-07-25 rebuild. Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit` (clean) and `npx eslint` on every touched/new file (clean) — **not yet exercised on-device**, since this session had no way to test on the phone directly; worth a real save-a-salad walkthrough (including triggering the goitrogenic warning on purpose, e.g. two raw servings of broccoli and cauliflower) before trusting it fully.

**Smoothie Builder built out, 2026-08-02 -- the third of the Food tab's ten builders, built immediately after Salad in the same session, at explicit user request ("full auto," scoped to Smoothie only).** Built as a direct adaptation of Salad Builder itself (`components/SmoothieBuilder.tsx`, ~1,970 lines) rather than Side Builder directly, since Salad already carried the raw-goitrogenic-load warning logic Smoothie also needs. New `smoothies`/`smoothie_ingredients` tables in `lib/db.ts`, mirroring `salads`/`salad_ingredients` exactly (`saveSmoothie`/`updateSmoothie`/`deleteSmoothie`/`listSmoothies`/`getSmoothie`/`getSmoothieIngredients`/`getSmoothieNutrientBreakdown`/`getSmoothieSixDimensionsBreakdown`); `listFavorites`'s itemType union widened to include `'smoothie'`. Wired into `app/(tabs)/food.tsx` (lens routing, `editSmoothieId` deep-link param, "Saved Smoothies"/"Favorite Smoothies" My Foods categories), `app/food-items.tsx`, and `app/food-item-detail.tsx`, same shape as Salad's own wiring. The raw-goitrogenic-load warning carried over unchanged in substance, only reworded: "Blending" rather than "Mixing them raw," matching the verb the old, deleted meal builder itself used to distinguish these two mealTypes for this exact check. Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint` on every touched/new file (both clean) — **not yet exercised on-device**, same caveat as Salad.

**Fermentation Builder built out, 2026-08-02 -- the fourth of the Food tab's ten builders, at explicit user request ("full auto," scoped to Fermentation only).** Built as a direct adaptation of Side Builder specifically, not Salad/Smoothie — neither of those two builders' raw-goitrogenic-load warning applies here (Fermentation's own placeholder copy never described that concern). New `fermentations`/`fermentation_ingredients` tables in `lib/db.ts` (`components/FermentationBuilder.tsx`, ~1,930 lines), mirroring `sides`/`side_ingredients` exactly, plus `listFavorites`'s itemType union widened to include `'fermentation'`. Wired into `app/(tabs)/food.tsx`, `app/food-items.tsx`, and `app/food-item-detail.tsx` the same shape as the other three (the latter's `mealNoun` prop factored into a small `mealNounFor()` helper once it reached its fourth branch, rather than another nested ternary). One real, deliberate scope boundary: **no bacterial-strain tracking** — that's still the separate, not-yet-started research workstream this document already flagged (specific cultures like Lactobacillus acidophilus/plantarum, Bifidobacterium species, etc., each needing real cited effects, comparable in rigor to the existing D1-D6 nutrition-database work) — this builder only covers logging the fermented food itself, structurally identical to Side/Salad/Smoothie. The one concrete, buildable addition: `'Fermented'` added to this builder's own local `COOKING_METHODS` list (each builder file keeps its own copy, not a shared constant), since the base list inherited from Side Builder had no way to select the actual prep step that makes something a fermentation. Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint` on every touched/new file (both clean) — **not yet exercised on-device**, same caveat as Salad/Smoothie.

**Beverage Builder built out, 2026-08-02 -- the fifth of the Food tab's ten builders, at explicit user request ("full auto," scoped to Beverage only).** Built as a direct adaptation of Side Builder (`components/BeverageBuilder.tsx`, ~1,935 lines), same base Fermentation used and for the same reason (neither Salad's/Smoothie's raw-goitrogenic-load warning applies). New `beverages`/`beverage_ingredients` tables in `lib/db.ts`, mirroring `sides`/`side_ingredients` exactly, plus `listFavorites`'s itemType union widened to include `'beverage'`. Wired into `app/(tabs)/food.tsx`, `app/food-items.tsx`, and `app/food-item-detail.tsx` the same shape as the other four (`mealNounFor()` grew its fifth branch). One real, deliberate difference from every builder before it: the "no cooking oil/fat or seasoning logged yet" soft nudge (`EXTRAS_TO_CHECK`) that Side/Salad/Smoothie/Fermentation all inherited unchanged from Side Builder's own original design doesn't apply to a beverage — emptied out here (`EXTRAS_TO_CHECK = []`) rather than left as a nonsensical nudge on a glass of water, with the now-dead nudge branch in the render kept in place (not deleted) so a real, beverage-appropriate nudge could slot back in later with no structural changes. Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint` on every touched/new file (both clean) — **not yet exercised on-device**, same caveat as the other three.

**Snack Builder built out, 2026-08-02 -- the sixth of the Food tab's ten builders, at explicit user request ("full auto," scoped to Snack only).** Built as a direct adaptation of Side Builder (`components/SnackBuilder.tsx`, ~1,935 lines), same base as Fermentation/Beverage. Unlike Beverage, kept the "no cooking oil/fat or seasoning logged yet" soft nudge (`EXTRAS_TO_CHECK`) unchanged from Side Builder's own original list — a real snack (spiced nuts, oil-popped popcorn) genuinely can involve either, unlike a beverage. New `snacks`/`snack_ingredients` tables in `lib/db.ts`, mirroring `sides`/`side_ingredients` exactly, plus `listFavorites`'s itemType union widened to include `'snack'`. Wired into `app/(tabs)/food.tsx`, `app/food-items.tsx`, and `app/food-item-detail.tsx` the same shape as the other five (`mealNounFor()` grew its sixth branch). Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint` on every touched/new file (both clean) — **not yet exercised on-device**, same caveat as the other four.

**Baked Goods Builder built out, 2026-08-02 -- the seventh of the Food tab's ten builders, at explicit user request ("full auto," scoped to Baked Goods only).** Built as a direct adaptation of Side Builder (`components/BakedGoodsBuilder.tsx`, ~1,955 lines), same base as Fermentation/Beverage/Snack. Kept the oil/seasoning nudge unchanged (same reasoning as Snack). No new Cook Prep option needed unlike Fermentation — `'Baked'` was already a real option in the inherited `COOKING_METHODS` list. New `baked_goods`/`baked_goods_ingredients` tables in `lib/db.ts` (the ingredient table's FK column is the singular `baked_good_id`, since it references one saved record, even though the table/component/function names use the plural "BakedGoods" to match the lens key `bakedGoodsBuilder`); `listFavorites`'s itemType union widened with `'bakedGoods'` (camelCase, not snake_case, matching the `itemType` route-param convention). Wired into `app/(tabs)/food.tsx`, `app/food-items.tsx`, and `app/food-item-detail.tsx` the same shape as the other six. Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint` on every touched/new file (both clean) — **not yet exercised on-device**, same caveat as the other five.

**Two real bugs surfaced by the Baked Goods pass specifically, both fixed retroactively across all six earlier builder files too (Side→Salad→Smoothie→Fermentation→Beverage→Snack)**:
1. **A genuine syntax error, not just a wording nit**: Baked Goods is the first builder whose singular noun is two words ("baked good," not one word like "salad"/"smoothie"/etc.). The mechanical `\bside\b`→`baked good` rename correctly rewrote prose, but it also hit a bare local variable (`const side = await getSide(...)`), producing `const baked good = ...` — invalid JS syntax, caught immediately by `npx tsc --noEmit`. Fixed by using `bakedGood` (no space) for that one identifier. Every future two-or-more-word builder name needs this same distinction: a real single-word camelCase identifier for code, and the natural (possibly multi-word) phrase only in comments/strings.
2. **A missed idiom, silent because it's comment-only**: `side-by-side` (hyphenated) was never added to the protected-idiom list, so every pass through Fermentation/Beverage/Snack had already been silently corrupting it into `fermentation-by-fermentation`/`beverage-by-beverage`/`snack-by-snack` etc. in one comment (`renderLabeledPicker`'s own history note) — harmless to compilation, so `tsc`/`eslint` never caught it, only manual review did. Fixed in all six files at once. The idiom-protection list should have included the hyphenated form from the start alongside the space-separated `side by side`.

**Soup Builder built out, 2026-08-02 -- the eighth of the Food tab's ten builders, at explicit user request ("full auto," scoped to Soup only).** Built as a direct adaptation of Side Builder (`components/SoupBuilder.tsx`, ~1,940 lines), same base as Fermentation/Beverage/Snack/Baked Goods. Kept the oil/seasoning nudge unchanged (sautéed aromatics, herbs/salt are genuinely common in soup). One concrete addition, same "add the one real missing prep option" move Fermentation made for `'Fermented'`: `'Simmered'` added to this builder's own local `COOKING_METHODS` list, right after `'Boiled'` — a real, distinct technique (lower heat, longer time, less mechanical breakdown), not just a wording variant. Deliberately does NOT attempt broth-base tracking or simmered-down ingredient-concentration math, both named in this builder's own placeholder copy as "room for" future handling — either would need real research (how does a given ingredient's nutrient contribution change as broth reduces?) before being more than a guess, the same reasoning that kept Fermentation Builder from attempting bacterial-strain scoring. New `soups`/`soup_ingredients` tables in `lib/db.ts`, mirroring `sides`/`side_ingredients` exactly; `listFavorites`'s itemType union widened with `'soup'`. Wired into `app/(tabs)/food.tsx`, `app/food-items.tsx`, and `app/food-item-detail.tsx` the same shape as the other seven. This pass applied both lessons from the Baked Goods bug fixes proactively (hyphenated `side-by-side` added to the idiom-protection list from the start, and "soup" being a single word meant no space-in-identifier risk to begin with) — clean on the first verification sweep, no retroactive fixes needed. Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint` on every touched/new file (both clean) — **not yet exercised on-device**, same caveat as the other seven.

**Sauces Builder built out, 2026-08-02 -- the ninth and last of the Food tab's nine sub-builders, at explicit user request ("full auto," scoped to Sauces only).** All nine of Meal Builder's own dependencies now exist. Built as a direct adaptation of Side Builder (`components/SaucesBuilder.tsx`, ~1,945 lines), same base as Fermentation/Beverage/Snack/Baked Goods/Soup. **Naming quirk specific to this builder**: its lens key (`saucesBuilder` in `app/(tabs)/food.tsx`'s `FOOD_LENSES`, written in an earlier session before this build pass) is plural, unlike most single-word builders' own lens keys — so the exported component is named `SaucesBuilder` to match it exactly, but every internal identifier for a single saved record (`editSauceId`, `getSauce`/`saveSauce`/`updateSauce`/`deleteSauce`, `sauceName`, the `sauces`/`sauce_ingredients` tables, `listFavorites`'s new `'sauce'` itemType) stays singular "Sauce" — the same pattern Salad/Smoothie/Fermentation/Beverage/Snack/Soup already use. Only Baked Goods went fully plural throughout (`BakedGoods`-prefixed functions), justified there because "baked good" itself is two words with no natural single-word form; Sauces has no such excuse, so it stays singular apart from the one component name. Kept the oil/seasoning nudge (vinaigrettes, mayo-based sauces, herb/salt seasoning are all common). One concrete addition: `'Reduced'` added to this builder's own local `COOKING_METHODS` list, right after `'Boiled'` — arguably THE defining sauce-making technique (pan sauces, gravies, glazes). Wired into `app/(tabs)/food.tsx`, `app/food-items.tsx`, and `app/food-item-detail.tsx` the same shape as the other eight. Clean on the first verification sweep (both idiom-protection and single-word-vs-multi-word lessons already baked into the process by this point). Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint` on every touched/new file (both clean) — **not yet exercised on-device**, same caveat as the other eight.

**Meal Builder built out, 2026-08-02 -- all ten Food tab builders now exist.** Unlike the other nine, this one isn't a copy-adapt of Side Builder — it assembles a real meal FROM the other nine builders' own saved output rather than adding raw ingredients itself, so it needed genuinely new design (published as an Artifact design doc, linked from this Status section's own build history, before any code was written). New `meal_components` table in `lib/db.ts` (bookkeeping alongside, not replacing, the existing flattened `meal_items` copy every Insights/Trends/Home lens already reads — see that table's own comment for why a real FK into "one of nine tables depending on a sibling column" isn't something SQLite can express) plus a generic `MealComponentType`-dispatch layer (`resolveMealComponent`, `createMealFromComponents`, `updateMealFromComponents`, `listMealComponentOptions`, `getMealComponentsGoitrogenicFlags`, etc.) that treats all nine sub-builders' tables identically without a separate code path per type. `components/MealBuilder.tsx`: name the meal (optional) and choose a meal type, then "Add from..." opens any of the nine categories' own saved list (reusing each sub-builder's existing `listX()`/ingredient-summary query, not a new one), pick how much of that saved item was actually eaten (a fraction/whole-number picker over the record's own stated servings, the same `yourSharePercent` concept `meal_items` already carries), repeat for as many items as the real meal has. Before logging, runs the same raw-goitrogenic-load check Salad/Smoothie/etc. already run on their own ingredient lists, but POOLED across every selected component — two individually-fine sides (one raw goitrogenic vegetable apiece) can still combine into the same real over-eating risk those builders already warn about alone. "Log This Now" creates a real `meals` row for right now via the existing, untouched `createMeal`. Saving a built meal to schedule for a *future* date/time isn't wired up yet — deliberately deferred, since Schedule already has its own independent scheduling flow that doesn't require Meal Builder at all; this is a real gap, just not the one that mattered most this session.

**The known Schedule's-"Log now"/Home's-Day-Arc regression (broken since the 2026-07-25 Food tab rebuild, described earlier in this Status section) is fixed the same day, as part of the same Meal Builder work.** `app/(tabs)/food.tsx` now reads the `scheduleItemId`/`mealType`/`title`/`templateMealId` params both callers already pushed (unread by anything until now) and routes straight into Meal Builder with them: `mealType`/`title` prefill its identity step and skip the "choose a meal type" gate (already answered by the scheduled occurrence itself); `templateMealId`, when it names a meal Meal Builder itself built (has real `meal_components` rows), resumes with that meal's own saved-item selections already loaded via the new `getMealComponentDisplayInfo`. A scheduled occurrence whose template has no `meal_components` (built by the old, deleted all-in-one builder, or never built via Meal Builder at all) simply starts with an empty component list rather than attempting a literal replay of that older, structurally-different meal — a deliberate scope boundary, not an oversight: still lets the person log something real today instead of hitting the same dead end as before. On "Log This Now," the new meal is linked back to the scheduled occurrence via the existing `markScheduledMealLogged`, and the screen returns to wherever Log Now was tapped from (`router.back()`) instead of resetting to a blank meal. `favoriteId` (also pushed by both callers) is deliberately left unread — none of the nine sub-builders' own favoriting is wired up to write one yet, so there's nothing for it to resolve against.

The now-dead `ComingSoonBuilder` placeholder component (and its final catch-all branch in `food.tsx`'s own lens switch) was deleted outright rather than left unreferenced, now that all ten lenses render a real builder — along with the `ScrollView`/`Text`/`useFloatingButtonScrollPadding`/`typography` imports and `styles.body`/`styles.emptyText` that only it had used.

Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint` on every touched/new file (`lib/db.ts`, `components/MealBuilder.tsx`, `app/(tabs)/food.tsx`) — all clean. **Not yet exercised on-device**, same caveat as the other nine builders this session.

**Water and choline added as real tracked nutrients, 2026-08-02, prompted by a Meal Builder brainstorming conversation about hydration.** The person asked whether hydration should include water content from food itself (watermelon, soup, etc.), not just literal drinks -- checked directly against the source workbook and confirmed every one of the 7 national sheets already carries a `Water (G)` column (and `Choline, total (MG)`/`CHOLINE, TOTAL (mg)`) that had simply never been imported into the app's "Phase 1 standard panel" of tracked nutrients. Added both to `NUTRIENT_DEFINITIONS`/`NUTRIENT_COLUMN_ALIASES` in `scripts/build_food_reference_db.py` and did a full rebuild (all 22,016 foods, not a hand-picked subset) -- spot-checked against known values (watermelon 91.45g water/100g, whisky ~65g/100g at 40% ABV, egg yolk 820mg choline/100g). Real DRI (Adequate Intake) rows added for both: water 2.7L/day (women) / 3.7L/day (men), NASEM 2005, from ALL sources including food, not a "drink this many glasses" target on top of it; choline 425mg/550mg, UL 3500mg, NASEM 1998 (the same B-vitamins report already cited for thiamin/riboflavin/etc.). Hydration now works as a true sum across everything logged in a day, food and drink alike, via the existing generic `getFoodNutrients`/DRI-percentage pipeline -- no per-builder or per-category hydration logic needed. A dedicated "Daily Hydration" display (e.g. a Home ring) is a real, separate follow-up, not built this session -- for now Water/Choline just show up like any other nutrient in Insights' own nutrient table.

**Found two real, fully-researched scripts sitting dormant in the repo, apparently written and never run: `scripts/add_water_tracking_data.py` and `scripts/add_supplement_powder_category.py`.** Both predate this session (part of the squashed "Initial commit," so real origin/timing is unknown) and both explicitly say they exist only because the master spreadsheet-driven rebuild "wasn't possible in this environment" -- untrue for this session, which successfully rebuilt from the real xlsx directly. Folded the genuinely reusable parts into the real pipeline instead of running either as a one-off patch: the water DRI values (verbatim, already well-cited) went into `build_food_reference_db.py`'s own `DIETARY_REFERENCE_INTAKES` list, and the Psyllium husk recategorization (`NutSeed` → `SupplementPowder`) went into `CATEGORY_OVERRIDES` -- confirmed `food_aliases`/`interaction_rules` tables survive a full rebuild untouched (the master script only `DROP TABLE`s the tables it owns), so nothing needed re-running there. Deliberately did NOT add the synthetic "Choline Bitartrate (Supplement Powder)" custom food row those scripts also proposed -- a smaller, separate call, still available if wanted later. Also fixed a stale top-of-file docstring that claimed the build "does NOT import" per-food nutrient columns at all, no longer true since the Phase 1 standard panel was added.

**Per-builder food-category scoping, 2026-08-02.** Every one of the ten Food-tab builders' connected `FoodLookup` used to show the exact same unfiltered 19-category list regardless of what it builds -- Beverage Builder could browse raw Meat, Side Builder could browse Alcohol. `FoodLookup.tsx` gained an `allowedCategories?: string[]` prop (undefined = unrestricted, so Insights' own general-purpose Food Lookup lens is untouched); a new `constants/foodBuilderCategories.ts` defines one curated allowlist per builder, deliberately generous rather than minimal (getting an edge case wrong is a one-line fix). Real data-quality quirks surfaced while drafting the lists, now documented in that file: there's no separate "Water" category (plain water only exists under `Bev > Water`, which is why Beverage/Fermentation Builder both need `Bev`); `Alcohol` exists as both its own category and a `Bev > Alcoholic` subcategory; plant milks are inconsistently filed under `NutSeed` rather than `Bev`/`Dairy` (Beverage Builder includes `NutSeed` specifically so "almond milk" is findable). Alcohol was deliberately included in four builders, not just Beverage -- Fermentation (kombucha/kvass-style mildly-alcoholic ferments), and Soup/Sauces (deglazing, wine reductions, a real common technique), reflecting the person's own framing that alcohol should be tracked wherever it realistically shows up, not just when someone's literally pouring a drink.

**A real, cited Hashimoto's-specific alcohol advisory, 2026-08-02, researched via WebSearch/WebFetch rather than written from assumption.** The literature turned out more nuanced than "alcohol is worse for Hashimoto's": two real peer-reviewed studies (Carle et al. 2013, *European Journal of Endocrinology*, a Danish population-based case-control study; Effraimidis et al., *European Thyroid Journal*, a prospective Amsterdam AITD cohort) found moderate alcohol consumption is NOT linked to new thyroid-antibody development and tracks with a LOWER risk of progressing to overt autoimmune hypothyroidism -- mirroring alcohol's documented protective association with other autoimmune diseases (rheumatoid arthritis, lupus). The real, dose-dependent concerns in the research concentrate specifically at heavier/more frequent drinking: the liver's ~80% share of T4-to-T3 conversion competing with alcohol's own hepatic processing load; a small (n=44) preliminary study linking chronic heavy drinking to gut permeability/inflammatory thyroid-axis effects (Sagaram et al. 2022, *Cells*); chronic alcohol's well-documented HPA-axis/cortisol dysregulation (Stephens & Wand, 2012, NIAAA *Alcohol Research: Current Reviews*). Several specific numbers repeated across patient-education sites (a percent figure for reduced levothyroxine absorption, a percent drop in free T3) could not be traced to a verifiable primary study and were deliberately left out rather than presented as cited fact -- the same standard already applied to the thiamine case-report correction earlier in this document. Presented as an honest, two-sided case per explicit user direction, not a one-sided warning. Implemented as a new `lib/alcoholAdvisory.ts` (title/message/`isAlcoholicFood()`), surfaced as a small tappable, non-blocking info row (not a red DimensionFlags-style warning, not a blocking Cancel/Continue confirm like the raw-goitrogenic-load check) on the pending-ingredient card in the four builders whose own category allowlist includes Alcohol -- Beverage, Fermentation, Soup, Sauces.

Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint` on every touched/new file (both clean) across all of the above. **Not yet exercised on-device.**

**Liters added to Beverage Builder's own Units picker, 2026-08-02, reported missing -- then generalized the same day into a real design change across every liquid-heavy builder, per explicit direction.** The first, narrower fix just added `'l'` to Beverage's own metric-only unit list (`lib/unitConversion.ts`'s `VolumeUnit`/`VOLUME_TO_ML` already fully supported it). The follow-up request reframed the underlying assumption: gating volume units behind the person's own profile metric/imperial preference makes sense for something like a body-weight scale (genuinely set to one system at a time), but not for which measuring *utensil* happens to be on hand for a liquid -- a liter jug, a gallon jug, a cup marked in mL vs. fl oz. Decided scope (explicitly confirmed): Beverage, Fermentation, Smoothie, and Soup Builder -- "every builder with real liquid content" -- now always offer the full merged liquid-unit family (`ml, l, tsp, tbsp, fl oz, cup, pint, quart, gallon`) regardless of profile system; only the mass unit (grams vs. ounces/pounds) still follows it. `lib/unitConversion.ts` gained `pint`/`quart`/`gallon` as real `VolumeUnit`s (US customary throughout, matching the existing `fl_oz` figure: 473.176/946.353/3785.41 mL). Also fixed a latent duplication risk found along the way: `lib/db.ts`'s own `normalizeUnitForConversion` kept a second, hand-maintained copy of the recognized-unit list instead of reading `MASS_UNITS`/`VOLUME_UNITS` directly -- now imports both, so a future unit addition can't update one list and silently miss the other. The other five sub-builders (Side, Salad, Snack, Baked Goods, Sauces) keep the original profile-gated units unchanged.

**A new 'Brewing' food category split out of Bev, 2026-08-02, reported the same day.** The person's point: dry, not-yet-brewed tea/coffee-type products (instant coffee granules, instant tea powder, ground matcha, chicory powder) don't belong under "Beverages" the same way an already-brewed cup does -- you brew a drink FROM them, they aren't the drink itself. Checked directly against the database rather than guessed at: confirmed no raw dried tea leaves or whole coffee beans exist anywhere in it (nor "capomo," the person's own third example -- not in any of the 7 national datasets, a genuinely new food to add later, not a recategorization), but `Bev > Tea`/`Bev > Coffee` did mix real brewed/ready-to-drink rows ("Tea, brewed, black," any "(infusion)" row, bottled NESTEA/SNAPPLE/etc.) together with dry instant-powder rows ("Coffee, instant, dry powder or granules," "Tea, instant, unsweetened, powder") -- confirmed by hand, row by row, since several base_names use "powder" for the PREPARED drink too (e.g. "...powder, water added" already has water added and correctly stayed put). Moved exactly 23 confirmed rows (7 from Tea, 16 from Coffee, one row each, no source duplicates) into a new `Brewing` category via `CATEGORY_OVERRIDES` in `scripts/build_food_reference_db.py`, full rebuild. Added to `FoodLookup.tsx`'s `CATEGORY_DISPLAY_LABELS` ("Brewing & Infusions") and to Beverage Builder's and Fermentation Builder's own category allowlists (`constants/foodBuilderCategories.ts`) -- Fermentation needs it too since kombucha is literally brewed from tea. No subcategory breakdown yet for this new category (23 rows is small enough that "Other" would be the whole list) -- same default as most smaller categories already have. Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx eslint` (clean) and directly against the rebuilt database (Bev's own Tea/Coffee counts dropped by exactly the expected 7/16; a spot-checked "already has water added" row confirmed it correctly stayed in Bev). Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx eslint` on every touched file (clean).

**A real, much bigger bug found the same day, reported as "no items listed in Brewing and Infusions": every food search/resolve function in the app (`searchReferenceFoodNames`, `getPreparationMethods`, `resolveFoodChoice`) has always silently defaulted to USDA-only, with no toggle anywhere to widen it.** Three entire categories have zero USDA rows at all -- `Alcohol` (159 foods), `Algae` (53 foods), and the brand-new `Brewing` (23 foods at the time) -- so browsing any of them returned nothing, not because they're empty but because USDA-only quietly excluded the only sources that cover them. This meant the alcohol advisory feature built earlier the same day was effectively unreachable, since no alcoholic food could ever be selected to trigger it. Fixed in `lib/db.ts` with a new `hasUsdaCoverage()`/`resolveEffectiveUsdaOnly()` pair (cached per category): before applying the USDA-only restriction, each function now checks whether the requested category has *any* USDA coverage, and searches every source instead when it doesn't. Categories with real USDA coverage (the common case -- Veg, Meat, Fruit, etc.) are untouched, keeping the single-source experience `searchReferenceFoodNames`'s own docstring explains the restriction exists for (avoiding near-duplicate "Spinach" x7 entries, one per national source). Verified directly against the database (`EXISTS` checks confirming 0 USDA rows for all three categories) and via `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx eslint` (clean).

**Four more rows moved into `Brewing` the same day, reported as "no green tea listed."** The original by-hand pass above only caught names containing "instant"/"powder"/"granules" -- it missed Japan_MEXT's own separate naming convention, where a bare "tea" qualifier (not "infusion") means the dry LEAF itself: "Green tea, Sencha, infusion" is the brewed cup, "Green tea, Sencha, tea" is the dry leaves you'd brew it from. This time confirmed decisively using the new `water` nutrient rather than eyeballing names alone: every "infusion" row measures 97-99% water; the missed "tea" rows measure under 7%. Also caught "Thé, feuille" (French_Ciqual; "Tea, leaf") -- missed the first time because it was never filed under the `Tea` subcategory at all, sitting in Bev's own "Other" bucket instead, outside where the original review looked. Sencha and Gyokuro are both real green teas -- exactly what was reported missing. `Brewing` is now 27 rows. Verified against the rebuilt database and via `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx eslint` (clean) -- worth treating this category's contents as still not fully audited, given two passes have each found real gaps the other missed.

**A full database re-audit for `Brewing`, 2026-08-02, requested directly ("check the rest of the database... I also do not see powdered cacau, or powdered cocoa") to get a real count before deciding whether barcode scanning is needed to fill this category out.** Systematic this time, not by-hand name scanning: every remaining `Bev` row whose own water content (the nutrient added earlier the same day) reads under ~13g/100g -- a reliable, data-driven "this is dry, not a prepared drink" signal -- plus a full cross-category keyword sweep (tea/coffee/cocoa/chicory/malt) across all 22,016 foods, not just Bev. Real gaps found and fixed:
- **Cocoa/cacao/chocolate/malted-milk drink mixes** -- the directly-reported gap. ~35 rows: Cocoa mix (plain, low-calorie, no-sugar), plain cocoa/cacao powder (multiple sources), Milo, Nesquik, drinking chocolate base, carob-flavor mix (carob is a real cocoa substitute, same relationship as chicory:coffee), malted milk (chocolate and natural, several strengths), OVALTINE, hot chocolate mixes (several variants), and several French `Poudre cacaotée/maltée...` (cocoa/malt powder "for beverage") rows.
- **Pure baking/drinking cocoa powder filed under `Sweets` instead of `Bev` entirely** -- same real product as the Bev-sourced cocoa powder above, just a different national source's own top-level bucket (USDA's "Cocoa, dry powder", Canada's "Sweets, cocoa, powder, unsweetened", Germany's deoiled variants, and one German row that already said "Beverage powder containing cocoa" in its own name yet was still sitting in Sweets).
- **More coffee/chicory powder**, French_Ciqual, missed the first time since that pass only reviewed the Tea/Coffee subcategories -- these had their own fully descriptive base_names the original review never saw, including plain "Café, moulu" (ground coffee).
- **A real, unrelated miscategorization bug stumbled onto along the way**: two herbal teas ("Tea, tundra...", "Tea, herbal, brewed, Hohoysi (Hopi)") were filed under category `Meat`. Both already say "brewed" in their own name, so the fix was `Meat` -> `Bev` (not `Brewing` -- they're a prepared drink, not a dry good).
- **A real structural risk found and handled correctly**: several USDA rows (the "Beverages, coffee"/"Beverages, tea"/"Beverages, Cocoa mix"/"Beverages, Malted drink mix" families) share a generic base_name with a real PREPARED sibling ("...instant, decaffeinated, powder" vs. "...instant, decaffeinated, prepared with water", both collapsed to plain "Beverages, coffee") -- USDA's own prep state was never split into a separate `prep_method` value for these the way most of the database's rows are. A plain `(category, base_name)` override would have swept the prepared sibling into `Brewing` right along with the dry one, so these ~24 rows were targeted individually via `NAME_CATEGORY_OVERRIDES` instead (full-name-keyed, each also given a real distinct base_name rather than the shared generic one), confirmed row-by-row against the database before writing the list, not guessed.

**`Brewing` grew from 27 to 90 rows** -- Canada_CNF (28) and USDA (26) are now the two largest contributors, with real representation from all 7 national sources. Deliberately excluded from this pass, worth naming since the person may want them included on a future ask: protein/meal-replacement shake powders, fruit-flavor drink powders (lemonade, orange drink, fruit punch), plain juice powders, milkshake mix, and cocktail mixers (whiskey sour mix) -- all genuinely dry/not-yet-a-drink the same way, but a different real category of product (supplement, juice, or cocktail mixer) than the tea/coffee/cocoa/malt "brewing" family this category is named for and was scoped to. Verified against the rebuilt database at every step (prepared siblings spot-checked to confirm none were swept in by accident; the two Meat rows confirmed landed in Bev with a real Tea subcategory).

**Protein/meal-replacement/supplement-style powders explicitly confirmed to stay OUT of `Brewing`, 2026-08-02** -- the person's own direction, matching what the audit above had already excluded on its own reasoning. They stay where they already were (17 rows under `Bev > Protein & Meal Replacement`, a few under `Mixed`) until a real decision is made on where they actually belong -- tracked as its own Next steps item (see below) rather than guessed at now.

**Dark/light (milk) chocolate specifically checked, 2026-08-02, one real miss found.** Every "Dark chocolate"/"Milk chocolate"/"Whole milk chocolate..." row (filled with caramel, hazelnuts, nougat, etc.) is correctly a candy bar under `Sweets` -- confirmed none were ever mis-swept into `Brewing`, and correctly left untouched. Chocolate SYRUP (both rows, 31g and 79g water per 100g) is also correctly untouched -- already a pourable liquid flavoring, not a dry good. One genuine miss from the earlier cocoa/chocolate sweep: "Beverages, chocolate powder, no sugar added" (7.4g water/100g, a real dry chocolate drink powder, fully unique base_name, no prepared-sibling risk) -- moved to `Brewing`, now 91 rows.

**Caffeine added as a real tracked nutrient, and a cited coffee-brewing-method advisory built, 2026-08-02 -- prompted by the person sharing outside research on coffee chemistry and asking that it inform how coffee is organized.** Checked against the actual source data first, same discipline as everything else this session: none of the 7 national sheets distinguish roast level or bean variety for any coffee row (zero name matches for "light/dark roast" or "Arabica/Robusta" anywhere in the whole database), and none of them measured chlorogenic acids or the diterpenes cafestol/kahweol at all -- so none of that can be scored per-food the way D1-D6 sub-criteria are; re-organizing the ~90 existing Brewing/coffee rows by roast or variety isn't possible without inventing data. Caffeine, though, IS a real measured column in all 7 sheets ("Caffeine (MG)," plus a second multi-line "Caffeine (mg) (mg)" variant found the same way choline's alt-casing alias was) -- added to `NUTRIENT_DEFINITIONS`/`NUTRIENT_COLUMN_ALIASES`, rebuilt, values spot-checked against real-world expectation (decaf ~1mg/100g, concentrated instant powders in the thousands per 100g of dry powder, espresso ~290mg/100g -- all correct). Deliberately did NOT alias the sheets' third "Caffeine (g/100 g)" column -- a real column too, but in grams; this table's aliasing has no per-alias unit conversion, so including it would have silently stored gram values as if they were milligrams, a real 1000x error, not just broader coverage. Deliberately no DRI row either -- NASEM has never published a formal RDA/AI/UL for caffeine, and `DIETARY_REFERENCE_INTAKES`'s own `DRI_AGENCY` is a single shared "NASEM..." attribution applied to every row, so forcing in the FDA's separate general 400mg/day guidance there would misattribute its real source.

Every claim from the person's own summary was independently re-verified via WebSearch/WebFetch rather than trusted, the same standard already applied to the alcohol advisory -- and one claim didn't hold up as stated: "milk cuts antioxidant absorption by 50% or more" turned out to be genuinely mixed evidence (some studies show ~28-40% less free chlorogenic acid bound to milk casein; others show the resulting protein-polyphenol complex measuring HIGHER antioxidant activity), corrected to present both sides rather than the one-sided claim. The filtered/unfiltered-cholesterol claim and the Arabica/Robusta caffeine claim both held up cleanly against real sources (Harvard T.H. Chan, AHA-cited trials; consistent food-science figures). The most directly Hashimoto's-relevant piece, found during verification rather than in the original prompt: a real pharmacokinetic study showing coffee taken with a levothyroxine dose delays and reduces its absorption (peak plasma down 19-36%, AUC down 27-36%), resolved by spacing doses roughly an hour apart -- more clinically actionable than the roast-level chemistry, and led the advisory's own content ahead of it. New `lib/coffeeAdvisory.ts` (title/message/`isCoffeeFood()`, detecting either `Bev > Coffee` or a coffee/chicory-keyword match within `Brewing`, since that category also holds tea/cocoa/malt), surfaced the same non-blocking, tap-to-read way as the alcohol advisory, in Beverage and Fermentation Builder (the two builders with `Brewing` in their own allowlist). Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx eslint` (clean).

**A new "group foods under a shared header while browsing" feature, 2026-08-02, explicitly requested after the person noticed "Cheese, Mexican blend"/"Cheese, port de salut" sorting nowhere near "Colby Cheese"/"Cottage Cheese" in Dairy & Eggs's plain alphabetical list.** Root cause, confirmed rather than assumed: `scripts/natural_name_reorder.py`, a real, separately hand-researched 635-line module already wired into the build pipeline (`reorder_base_name()`, called from `build_food_reference_db.py` -- its own top comment claiming "NOT INTEGRATED" turned out to be stale documentation, not current fact), deliberately only reorders SIMPLE "Head, single-word-modifier" names ("Cheese, cheddar" -> "Cheddar Cheese") and leaves multi-clause ones ("Cheese, cheddar, natural, regular fat") in original comma form rather than guess wrong -- so the exact same kind of cheese legitimately exists in both word orders across different source rows, with no single fix to the data itself that would resolve it.

Built as a new `lib/foodNameGrouping.ts` (`buildFoodNameGroups()`) instead -- a client-side grouping pass over whichever name list is currently on screen, run once per category rather than baked into the database, so it stays correct automatically as the reference data changes. Groups by whichever "kind of thing" word a name's own comma-prefix, last word, or first word produces, when at least one OTHER food in the same list produces the same word -- deliberately conservative (a food never joins a group on a hunch) and deliberately generous about candidates (checks all three positions, since "Colby Cheese" needs its LAST word ("Cheese") while "Chicken breast fillet" needs its FIRST word ("Chicken"), matching natural_name_reorder.py's own documented split between "flip to front" words and "anatomical/cut-of-meat noun keeps original order" words). `InlineSelectList.tsx` gained an optional `isHeader` flag on its own existing option type (additive, every one of its other 12 call sites across the app unaffected) to render a non-tappable section heading; wired into `FoodLookup.tsx`'s Food step specifically, and only while plain browsing -- an active search query still shows a flat filtered list, since headers add no value once the list is already down to a handful of real matches.

Not guessed at -- iteratively tested against real category data (a throwaway Node script mirroring the real TS logic, run against the actual Dairy/Meat/Veg name lists exported from the live database) and refined through three real rounds of false positives found this way, not assumed away: a 115-item false "Matter" group from French "min. 20% fat in dry matter" phrasing repeating across unrelated cheeses; cooking-method/storage-state groups in Meat ("Broiled" -- 37 different beef cuts that all happened to be broiled; "Deep-frozen"; French "Cru"/"Crue" for raw) sharing nothing but how the food was prepared, not what it was; a "Drained" group in Veg mixing artichokes/asparagus/aubergine/beansprouts for the same reason. Each fix (expanding `GROUP_STOPWORDS`, excluding `%`-containing names from edge-word extraction since their real head noun tends to sit buried mid-string not at either edge, demoting a group back to ungrouped if its real assigned membership ends up under 2 after higher-priority matches claim its would-be siblings) was verified by re-running against the same real data until the false positives were gone and the real ones (Cheese/Milk/Egg/Cream in Dairy; Beef/Pork/Chicken/Lamb/Veal/Turkey/Breast/Shoulder/Leg in Meat; Potato/Squash/Cabbage/Peas/Corn/Beans/Pepper/Onion/Carrot/Spinach in Veg) still worked. Not a claim of perfection across all 19 categories and 22,016 foods -- a reasonable, conservative v1 that may surface its own new edge case in a category not yet spot-checked, worth a quick look before assuming it's flawless everywhere. Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx eslint` on every touched file (clean). **Not yet exercised on-device.**

**"Chicken Egg" labeling fixed, 2026-08-02, same day, prompted directly by the new grouping feature above making the gap visible: the person asked why no "Chicken Egg" appeared anywhere despite chicken being by far the most common egg.** Confirmed via direct query this was never missing data -- 50+ real chicken-egg rows already existed across USDA, Germany_BLS, Canada_CNF, Australia_AFCD and Japan_MEXT -- but inconsistent, sometimes entirely silent, labeling: USDA's own unqualified "Egg, whole"/"Egg, white"/"Egg, yolk" rows are chicken by USDA's own unstated convention (every other species in the source data names itself explicitly) but said nothing about species once reordered to "Whole Egg"/"White Egg"/"Egg, yolk"; Canada_CNF/Australia_AFCD's "Egg, chicken, ..." rows and Japan_MEXT's "Eggs, hen, ..." rows (hen = female chicken) do say chicken already, but every one of them has 2+ commas, outside `reorder_base_name()`'s deliberately conservative single-comma-only scope (see the grouping-feature paragraph above), so none got the same "Duck Egg"/"Goose Egg"/"Turkey Egg" treatment their non-chicken counterparts already had; Germany_BLS's own rows already said "Chicken egg ..." but in sentence case, one letter off this app's Title Case convention, close enough to read as a near-duplicate rather than the same entry. Fixed with a new `rename_chicken_egg()` in `scripts/build_food_reference_db.py`, run right after the existing bell-pepper color-split rename: an exact-match dict (`CHICKEN_EGG_EXACT_RENAMES`) merges every bare, no-further-qualifier form ("whole"/"white"/"yolk"/"poached" alone) from all four sources onto one shared base_name per state -- the same single-family-per-species shape Duck/Goose/Turkey Egg already have -- plus two generic prefix substitutions ("Egg, chicken," / "Eggs, hen," -> "Chicken Egg,") for every row with a genuine extra qualifier (a specific omelet, a frozen/dried processing detail), minimally re-prefixed rather than fully reordered, consistent with this whole module's standing rule against guessing a fuller reorder than what's actually safe. "Chicken Egg Roll" (a USDA appetizer dish, not literally an egg) was confirmed to fall through both the exact-match dict and the generic prefixes untouched, exactly as intended. Rebuilt `assets/data/foods_reference.db` and verified directly against the rebuilt data: all ~50 rows now read "Chicken Egg..." at a glance, Duck/Goose/Turkey/Quail Egg entries are unaffected, and the merged bare families (e.g. "Chicken Egg" itself now spanning USDA/Germany_BLS/Australia_AFCD/Japan_MEXT with 15 source/prep_method combinations) show the same normal multi-source-per-prep-state spread every other common food in the database already has. Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit` (clean; pure Python/data change, no TS files touched).

**A batch of real Dairy & Eggs data/UI fixes, 2026-08-02, same day, reported directly after the person scrolled the new grouped list and found several things that didn't belong under "Butter" or "Cheese," plus two standing feature requests about how the list itself behaves.** Investigated each named item against the built database rather than assumed:
- **A genuine base_name-leak bug, the actual root cause of the reported "cheddar cheese (includes foods for USDA...)" entry**: `split_prep_method()`'s fallback strategy only strips a trailing clause when it matches a real cooking/raw/storage term -- for a handful of rows where the source spreadsheet's own "Short Display Name" was never separately curated (left equal to the full name, USDA's own "(Includes foods for USDA's Food Distribution Program)" commodity-program disclaimer included), that whole disclaimer had nowhere else to go and leaked straight into base_name, creating a confusing near-duplicate of the already-correct plain entry. Confirmed via query this hit exactly 4 rows (Cheddar Cheese, Whole-Grain Wheat Flour, Oats, Smooth Peanut Butter). Fixed with a new `strip_food_distribution_program_suffix()` in `scripts/build_food_reference_db.py`, applied right after `split_prep_method()` -- a literal, unconditional strip of that one known boilerplate string, safe because it's never itself a real food descriptor.
- **Three real Dairy miscategorizations**, added to `CATEGORY_OVERRIDES`: USDA's "Beverage, instant breakfast powder" rows (Dairy -> Bev, matching Canada_CNF's own already-correct placement for the identical real product -- the name itself starts with "Beverage"); "Cheese sauce, prepared from recipe" (USDA) and "Homemade Cheese Sauce" (Canada_CNF) (Dairy -> Mixed, matching every OTHER cheese-sauce composite dish already correctly filed there); "Macaroni cheese" (UK_CoFID's own term for mac and cheese) (Dairy -> Mixed), the last one found while spot-checking the grouping feature's own "Cheese" header against the rebuilt database, not from the original report.
- **Two more instances of the SAME split_prep_method info-loss mechanism already fixed for chicken eggs**, this time losing a real variety name rather than a species: "Cheese, dry white, queso seco" (queso seco is a real, recognizable Latin American dry cheese) lost "queso seco" entirely, landing as unhelpfully vague "Cheese, dry white"; "Cheese, low-sodium, cheddar or colby" lost "cheddar or colby" the same way. Answers the person's own direct question ("what kind of cheese is cheese, dry white?"). Fixed with a small hand-verified `CHEESE_VARIETY_RESTORE` dict + `restore_dropped_cheese_variety()`, run post-reorder alongside the chicken-egg fix -- narrow and targeted (these two specific rows), not a rewrite of `split_prep_method` itself, which likely has the same latent risk elsewhere in the database not yet audited (see Next steps). "Cheese, brick" and "Cheese, Mexican blend" were checked and confirmed to need no fix -- Brick is a real, if lesser-known, Wisconsin cheese style, and Mexican blend is a real retail shredded-blend product, genuinely non-specific by nature. "Anhydrous Butter Oil" was also checked and confirmed correctly classified as Dairy despite its name and oil-like physical form -- it's ~99.9% milkfat (concentrated butter with the water removed), a real dairy product by composition, not a plant/seed oil.
- **Sticky group headers -- attempted, crashed on-device, reverted the same day.** Reported as "the header for that grouping should stop at the top and the items within it scroll but it doesn't." First attempt used FlatList's own built-in `stickyHeaderIndices` prop (computed via `useMemo` from which options carry `isHeader`) in `InlineSelectList.tsx`. Real, immediate on-device crash reported directly after: opening ANY food category list produced an Expo/RN error screen ("too many things to list") followed by an Android ANR ("Inside Story isn't responding," Reload not working, forcing the person to close and reopen the app) -- happening specifically on category selection, i.e. exactly where the sticky FlatList first renders. The error mentioning `com.facebook.react.fabric.mounting` (React Native's own current UI-rendering engine, "Fabric" -- unrelated to any actual Facebook/Meta service, just the open-source framework's internal package namespace, inherited from RN's origin at Meta) confirmed this as a native rendering-layer crash, not a JS logic bug -- consistent with `stickyHeaderIndices` + `nestedScrollEnabled` (already required on this FlatList for gesture priority inside its own outer ScrollView) being a documented bad combination on Android, made worse here by Dairy/Meat/Veg each carrying 70-300 group headers, far more than `stickyHeaderIndices` was ever designed to track. Reverted immediately (removed the prop, the `useMemo`, and the now-unnecessary `useMemo`/`react` import) to restore basic list functionality -- `groupHeader`'s opaque background/border were left in place as harmless visual polish, no longer load-bearing for anything.

**Second attempt the same day, a hand-rolled sticky header instead of any native mechanism -- not yet exercised on-device.** The person pointed out a real, working precedent already existed in this exact file: the nutrient results table's own `SectionList` with `stickySectionHeadersEnabled` (its own comment block, ~line 841) has never had a reported problem.
