A genuinely different, third attempt at a feature this app had tried and reverted twice before (see `LensHub.tsx`'s own standing history comment) -- both earlier attempts auto-opened on EVERY arrival at a tab, including a horizontal swipe between tabs, which meant every swipe landed on an already-open popup that had to be dismissed before the next swipe could happen. This pass is scoped narrowly enough to avoid that exact problem: TabHub's own `go()` (and Home's Digest shortcut) now attach a fresh, ever-changing `openLensHub` query param (`` `${path}?openLensHub=${Date.now()}` `` , cast `as Href`) whenever they navigate -- `SwipeableTabScreen.tsx`'s own swipe-driven `goToOffset` navigates via a bare `router.navigate(path)` with no params at all, so a swipe can never carry this signal, only a genuine, deliberate tap from the hub can. A new shared `hooks/useAutoOpenLensHubSignal.ts` reads this param back out (`food.tsx` reads it directly out of its own existing, larger `useLocalSearchParams` call instead, to avoid a second redundant call in that one file); `LensHub.tsx` gained a new `autoOpenSignal?: string` prop and a `useEffect`/`useRef` pair that opens the popup the instant this value changes to something genuinely new (deduped against the last value it already reacted to, so it can't re-fire for a stale/unchanged value even if React Navigation's own param-persistence across a swipe-back ever left it lingering). Wired into all 7 of the tab screens that have a real LensHub (Food, Insights, Schedules, Trends, Signals, Reports, Purple Digest -- every one of `TAB_ROUTES` except Home, which has nothing else inside it to switch between).

`PageIdentityLabel.tsx`'s own resting-state prompt ("Tap the [icon] button to select a function," carried here since 2026-07-28) is removed entirely -- the component now returns `null` until a real lens is actually selected, rather than showing that message while nothing's picked yet. The "which lens am I in" box (`activeLensLabel`) is untouched and still shows for every lens on every tab, exactly as before -- confirmed and, in one case, actually fixed to match: `app/(tabs)/reports.tsx` had never actually passed `activeLensLabel` to `PageIdentityLabel` at all (the one real gap found while auditing all 7 screens for this pass), so its own "which lens" box never showed even after picking its one real placeholder option -- fixed alongside this same pass.

Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx eslint` across every touched/new file (`hooks/useAutoOpenLensHubSignal.ts`, `components/LensHub.tsx`, `components/TabHub.tsx`, `components/PageIdentityLabel.tsx`, `app/(tabs)/index.tsx`, and all 7 tab screens) -- clean; every eslint error surfaced in this sweep was independently confirmed pre-existing and unrelated (`git diff --stat` showed only a handful of small, additive lines changed per file, nowhere near the flagged line numbers). **Not yet exercised on-device** -- worth a real check that a TabHub tap genuinely opens the destination tab's own LensHub, that swiping between tabs never triggers it, and that re-tapping the same already-active tab from TabHub still reopens it.

**Insights grew from 4 lenses to 11 in one session, 2026-08-08 -- "trying to complete the Insights tab lenses," reconciling the previously-published Lens Coverage Audit Artifact (found via `Artifact` action:"list" rather than Notion -- it was published as a standalone page, not a tracker entry) with three new, explicitly-requested lenses.** Every real base_name/sub-criterion referenced below was independently verified via the `sqlite3.exe` CLI (bundled with the Android SDK's platform-tools, already on this machine) directly against `assets/data/foods_reference.db` before being written into code -- the same discipline the rest of this document already holds the reference-data work to, now extended to a same-session runtime feature for the first time.

**Nutrient Ranking** -- explicit request: "a lens that is used to show food items based on how much of any specific thing is within them, such as to provide the list of foods in order of most protein to least, but separated by whether they are animal protein versus plant protein." New `rankFoodsByNutrient(nutrientCode, limit)` (dedupes cross-source by category+base_name, keeping whichever source reports the largest amount, rather than a blanket usdaOnly filter that would have thrown away real cross-source variety) and `classifyProteinSource(category)` (Meat/Dairy -> animal; Legume/NutSeed/Grain/PastaNoodles/Veg/Fruit/Mushroom/Algae/PantryStaples -> plant, framed around what a vegetarian can actually eat, not strict biology -- mushrooms and algae count as plant here). Picker reuses the nutrient list via `listTrackedNutrients()` -- see the real, caught-in-the-same-pass duplication note below.

**Safe Foods** -- explicit request: foods "which means the foods listed in this section have zero relevance to the 6-DFF and will not cause a problem for them if they eat it." New `getSafeFoodIds()` (a real food qualifies when none of its own `food_scores` rows are a genuine yellow/red concern, reusing `isFlaggedTier`/`tierSeverity` directly from `lib/sixDimensionsReference.ts` rather than a second, drifting reimplementation of that severity logic) -- computed once per session and cached in memory (~180K rows, one bulk fetch) rather than a new DB column, since `food_scores` is static bundled data that never changes at runtime. Confirmed safe to import a real value from `sixDimensionsReference.ts` into `lib/db.ts` despite that file's own `import type { ... } from './db'` -- a type-only import is erased entirely at compile time, so there's no real runtime circular dependency (the same precedent `analyzeNutrientIntake`/`lib/nutrientAnalysis.ts` already established in this exact file).

**Healing Stage Food Finder** -- explicit request: "a lens that identifies foods based on the Healing Stages." Deliberately NOT the audit's own "Healing Stage" recommendation (a personalized advisory-reordering system blocked on a Profile self-declaration field that doesn't exist yet) -- a real food-*finder* instead, scoped to Stage 1 ("Getting Started") and Stage 2 ("Rebuilding"), matching this document's own already-decided practical-scoping note that only those two meaningfully drive food decisions. Built directly from the real, already-published Healing Stages guide Artifact. Stage 1 groups (Proteins/Vegetables/Starches/Fruits/Fats) are keyword+category queries verified row-by-row against the live database; Stage 2's four reintroduction rounds mix real D1-D6 sub-criterion tags (Goitrogenic Load, Gluten -- confirmed via direct query which tier values are real, e.g. `tier = 'High Risk'` for Gluten) with a hand-verified nightshade name list, since a genuine gap was found along the way: the "Nightshades" sub-criterion referenced in this app's own existing citation text (`SUB_CRITERION_SOURCES` in `sixDimensionsReference.ts`) has no matching row in the live `sub_criteria` table at all -- dead/unpopulated reference text, not a real per-food tag, so nightshades are matched by name instead of guessed at via a tag that doesn't exist. Gluten's own real query deliberately excludes Alcohol/Bev/Brewing/CommercialPremade (real wheat beer matches the tag but isn't a food reintroduction in any useful sense), scoping to Grain/PastaNoodles/PantryStaples instead.

**A real, consequential data finding surfaced while verifying Stage 1's poultry entry, reported rather than silently worked around**: nearly every plain chicken cut (breast, thigh, drumstick, whole bird -- 244 of 256 chicken base_names) is currently hidden from browsing database-wide, while beef/pork/turkey all kept their own common cuts visible through the same 2026-08-04 bulk-hide pass. This reads as an unintended casualty of that pass, not a deliberate choice -- nobody would deliberately hide "Chicken Breast" while leaving "Chicken Gizzard" visible. Not fixed here -- that bulk-hide pass was the person's own 10,165-decision curation call via the Reference Database Audit tool, not mine to silently reverse. `chicken breast`/`chicken, breast` stay in Stage 1's own keyword list as a harmless no-op today (Turkey Breast, confirmed visible, covers the real poultry recommendation meanwhile) that starts working the moment those rows are unhidden. **Fixed the next day -- see below.**

**Chicken unhidden, 2026-08-08, direct request following the finding above: "Unhide the 244 chicken entries so they can be browsed and add them to the Reference Database Audit webpage."** No Python interpreter was available this session (`python`/`python3`/`py` all resolve to Windows Store stub aliases) -- worked around entirely via direct SQL through `sqlite3.exe` (bundled with the Android SDK's platform-tools, already on this machine) rather than the usual patch-script pattern, a genuinely new tooling approach for this project worth remembering for any future session where Python turns out unavailable again. Ran `UPDATE foods SET hidden = 0 WHERE category='Meat' AND hidden=1 AND base_name LIKE '%chicken%' AND base_name NOT LIKE '%egg%'` directly against the live `assets/data/foods_reference.db`, verified via a before/after `GROUP BY hidden` count (all 255 distinct non-egg chicken base_names now `hidden=0`, "Chicken breast" spot-checked directly). `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` bumped so the app actually re-imports the patched database on next launch -- and confirmed this bump was NECESSARY, not just habitual: the dev server had been running since before this fix existed, so the version-check gate that would trigger a real reimport had nothing to trip until the constant changed (a real, on-device "no such table" style trap this project has hit before with schema changes; here it would have been a silent stale-data trap instead, same root cause).

Also fixed the same root cause at its source so a FUTURE full database rebuild (whenever Python is available again) won't silently re-hide these rows: `scripts/data/audit_decisions.json` (the ~5MB file `apply_audit_decisions()` replays on every rebuild) had all 244 "hide" decisions for these exact rows baked in from the original 10,165-decision bulk import. Removed them directly via a Node.js script (safer than Read/Edit on a 5MB file) -- first attempt used the CURRENT key format (`Meat|Meat & Poultry|<baseName>`) and matched zero of 244, since these decisions were recorded back when Meat had no subcategory scheme at all (added 2026-08-05, well after the original bulk import); the real key format turned out to be `Meat||<baseName>` (empty subcategory). Redone with the correct key, all 244 matched and removed (10215 -> 9971 total decisions), verified zero unmatched afterward.

**The Reference Database Audit Artifact itself regenerated from the now-patched live database, same day, closing the second half of the request.** Found the original generator, `build_audit_data.py`, sitting in an earlier session's own scratchpad (not in this repo) -- rather than trust a hand-guessed reimplementation, read it directly and ported its exact grouping/hide-reason logic to a new Node.js script (`build_audit_data.js`, since Python still wasn't available) run against a fresh `sqlite3.exe -json` export of the same join the Python script uses (`foods` LEFT JOIN a per-`(food_id, source)` aggregate over `food_scores`, counting real vs. placeholder tiers for the D1-D6-assessed-% column). One real transcription bug caught before trusting the output: the "not really assessed" oxalate placeholder tier uses literal double quotes around `"Low"` inside its own text, not escaped single quotes as first written into the SQL -- caught by directly querying `food_scores` for the real string before rebuilding, not assumed from memory of the earlier Python source. Confirmed the regenerated `DATA` blob shows all 257 chicken (non-egg) groups in the Meat category as fully unhidden, spliced it back into the existing Artifact's HTML in place of the old `DATA` line only (verified line-for-line identical everywhere else via `diff`, and the spliced file's own embedded script re-verified via `node --check`), and republished to the same URL rather than minting a new one. The Artifact's other three legacy hide mechanisms (Alcohol's `ALCOHOL_HIDDEN_BASE_NAMES`/pending-scan subcategories, Bev/Juice's curated allowlist, `CATEGORIES_HIDDEN_FROM_BROWSING`) don't touch Meat at all, so nothing else about this update needed touching. **Not yet exercised on-device or in-browser** -- worth a real check that the app itself shows chicken cuts browsable, and that the Artifact's own Meat category renders correctly, before treating either as fully confirmed.

This document captures decisions made during brainstorming and naming, plus ongoing architecture/product decisions, so nothing is lost across sessions.

## App name: Inside Story

Chosen after a long naming process that ruled out, in order: Aurelia, Chrysalis, and Monarch (each had a direct existing product/trademark collision), Second Nature (an established NHS-recommended weight-loss app of the same name), Food Sense (multiple existing food/symptom-tracking apps, one an almost exact concept match), FoodFlex (multiple existing nutrition-tracking apps), and Backstory (an existing storytelling-magazine app, different category but same exact name). Understory and Body of Work also cleared the check but lost out to Inside Story on fit.

Inside Story is a real English idiom (the hidden truth behind what's visibly happening) rather than an invented word — chosen deliberately for warmth and natural phonetics over a mechanical acronym/blend construction, after early invented candidates (Nectiva, Discerna, etc.) tested as too clinical-sounding. Web search turned up no colliding app in the health/nutrition/food-tracking space under this name.

**ASO / discoverability plan**: the app name doesn't need to carry the search keywords itself — Play Store/App Store listings have a separate short-description/subtitle field for that. Precedent: "Paloma: Thyroid Hormone Health" ranks for thyroid-related searches on a distinctive brand name plus a keyword-rich subtitle. Plan: pair "Inside Story" with a subtitle along the lines of "Hashimoto's & Autoimmune Food Tracker" to cover real search terms (hashimoto's diet app, autoimmune food symptom tracker, thyroid food tracker, AIP diet app, food trigger tracker) without sacrificing the name itself.

**Name-protection checklist status:**
- Domain `insidestoryapp.com` — confirmed available by user via registrar check (2026-07-18). Not yet registered — should be registered promptly before someone else takes it. Recommended registrar: Namecheap or Porkbun (not GoDaddy — real multi-year breach history 2019-2022 plus a 2026 FTC settlement over security failings; user's own antivirus flagged the GoDaddy site directly).
- Domain variants checked: `insidestory.app` is taken (an unrelated book-discovery/ARC platform), `insidestory.com` is taken (an unrelated home goods/bedding company) — neither conflicts with the app itself. `insidestoryapp.net` appears likely available and is a reasonable cheap defensive registration alongside the .com. Skipping `.io` deliberately — unrelated to the brand thematically, and carries real (if currently unresolved) long-term uncertainty tied to the 2025 UK-to-Mauritius Chagos Islands sovereignty transfer that the `.io` ccTLD is legally tied to.
- Social handles (@insidestoryapp on Instagram/Twitter/TikTok) — no existing account found via search, not yet confirmed via each platform's own signup check.
- USPTO trademark search — done by user directly via uspto.gov/trademarks/search. Two Live "INSIDE STORY" wordmark registrations exist (Class 035, real estate marketing / FloorPlanOnline LLC; Class 009, bedding-industry marketing training tapes / BIA IP Holdco LLC) — both in unrelated goods/services categories, no likelihood-of-confusion conflict with a health/nutrition app. No Live registration found in Class 042 (software/SaaS) or Class 044 (health/medical/nutrition services), the two categories that would actually matter. Name is clear to proceed with informally; a real trademark attorney clearance search is still the recommended step before ever filing to register the mark itself.

## Relationship to the existing nutrition database project

This app is the consumer of the `hashimotos_foods_combined_scored_and_nutrients_LIVE.xlsx` / `whole_foods_database_v5.sqlite` work (currently at `C:\Users\TonyR\OneDrive\Desktop\AppProject`; that project's own handoff doc, `SESSION_HANDOFF.md`, no longer exists on disk but was recovered — see the Status section above). The nutrition database — 22,016 foods across 7 national sources, with the 6-dimension Hashimoto's scoring rubric (D1-D6) — **has already shipped inside the app as bundled reference data**: `scripts/build_food_reference_db.py` converts the live xlsx into a compact `assets/data/foods_reference.db` SQLite asset bundled with the app (verified zero data loss against the 22,016-row source). That data-layer work and this app-layer work remain two different tracks with two different update cadences — the reference database gets re-imported whenever the source workbook changes, rather than hand-edited in this repo.

## Relationship to the companion book project

A separate book, written alongside this app, teaches the same Six Hashimoto's Food-Friendliness Dimensions framework in prose rather than software, centered on the author's wife's health journey. It shares this app's core scoring concept but is a distinct deliverable on its own timeline (a personal narrative chapter has been drafted; no other chapters started as of 2026-07-13). Full detail lives in the [Companion Book Manuscript page](https://app.notion.com/p/3a853652f27281358526f3ef3f7f92c0) of the Notion hub — not duplicated here since this document is scoped to the app-layer track.

**Confirmed 2026-08-07: the Purple Digest content and the standalone research Artifacts (food additives, fermented-strain guide, healing-stages guide, the food-industry/autoimmune-history document, etc.) do double duty as source material for this book, not just app content.** Explicit, direct statement from the user: "The information is just as important as all of the rest of the features and functions we are providing in the app... All of the documentation will also be used in my book that will be referencing the app." This research track should be treated with the same weight as feature work going forward, and written in a form (real citations, honest evidence tiering, plain language) that transfers directly into manuscript writing later.

## The core purpose (read this before making any scope trade-off)

This is not a generic nutrition tracker. The primary job: help someone with an autoimmune condition relearn how and what to eat, and understand — personally, for their own body — how food affects them. Hashimoto's thyroiditis was the first condition built out to full depth, and the one nearly every existing feature and citation below is written for as of this document's own earlier history — the direction, decided 2026-08-08 and restated even more explicitly by the person later the same day, is genuinely broader, not a lesser-effort broadening either: **"this is no longer just focused on Hashimoto's, it is focused on all 19 both individually and if a person has multiple of them combined. The scope of this app is not longer just to perfect it for Hashimoto's. The scope is to perfect it for all of the others, too."** Every one of the 18 non-Hashimoto's conditions is a real, standing target for the same ~176-entry depth Hashimoto's itself has, not a lesser tier that structural parity alone satisfies — see the Status section's own "volumetric depth push" entries for where this stands numerically at any given time. Real, combined-condition (polyautoimmunity) management is its own named, standing scope item too, not yet built as a first-class browsable destination — see the same Status entries for the honest state of that specific piece. Meal building, shopping lists, and scheduling are scaffolding that make daily use sustainable enough to generate that understanding; they are not the point on their own. When in doubt about priority, favor whatever helps someone discover and act on their own personal food-effect patterns over convenience features.

**Gut/microbiome healing is an explicit, named goal**, not just a side effect of good food choices — the app is also meant to actively help repair the person's microbiome and gut health, not just help them choose foods that avoid triggering symptoms.

**Refined framing (user's own words):** the real-world problem is that people are overwhelmed and confused about food, and most of them can't put the pieces together themselves the way a motivated, detail-oriented person might. The app's job is to remove that cognitive burden entirely — do the correlation/discovery work *for* the person, so day-to-day eating becomes a matter of following clear, pre-solved rules ("play by the rules of food, chemistry, etc.") rather than an ongoing research project. The complexity (matching foods to body chemistry, catching interactions, finding personal patterns) should live inside the app and its rules engine; what the person experiences should be ease — a grocery list that's already built for them, a plate that's already been figured out, so they can become the healthiest version of themselves without having to become their own nutritionist first. This reframes discovery/trend-finding as something the app does on the user's behalf, not a task handed to them.

**Home cooking over commercial/branded products, decided 2026-08-04.** Explicit product direction, given while discussing whether soups belong in `Mixed`: the app should promote actually cooking from real ingredients, not leaning on store-bought branded/commercial products as the default path. The person should be able to save their own real builds for things they make themselves — which the Food tab's ten builders (Meal, Side, Salad, Smoothie, Fermentation, Beverage, Snack, Baked Goods, Soup, Sauces) already do, each saving a genuine standalone record assembled from raw reference-database ingredients, not a generic branded pick. If someone wants to log a specific store-bought/branded product, the intended path is scanning it in (the still-unbuilt barcode/product-scan feature already named in Next Steps), not picking a generic "commercial"/"préemballée" reference-database entry standing in for it. Practical implication for the reference database work itself: don't expand or lean further into "commercial"/branded reference entries going forward, and treat the ones already in the database (heavily present across sources, e.g. France_Ciqual's many "..., préemballée" rows, Australia_AFCD's "..., commercial" rows) as a stand-in for the not-yet-built scan feature rather than the intended long-term path -- worth remembering when a future pass touches any category still mixing homemade-style entries with commercial ones.

## Architecture decisions made

*None of the cloud-backup, sync, encryption, or household-sharing architecture described in this section has been implemented yet — all app code so far is local-only, single-device (see Status above). This section remains the decided plan for when that work starts.*

**Local-first, no company server holding health data.** The live/authoritative database lives on the primary account holder's device. There is no backend server storing anyone's health data — full stop. This is a deliberate trust/privacy stance, not just a technical choice.

**Encrypted backup to the user's own cloud drive.** The primary device periodically writes an encrypted backup/snapshot to a folder in the user's own OneDrive/Google Drive/etc. (their account, their storage). This backup serves two purposes: disaster recovery, and (see below) transport for household sharing. The backup is not a readable file without importing it back into the app.

**Recovery model, chosen deliberately.** Encryption is protected by a master password or a locally-generated recovery key shown to the user once during setup (the same pattern password managers use). The app never holds the user's key — meaning there is intentionally no "forgot password" flow. This is the accepted cost of a genuine zero-knowledge design, not an oversight.

**Platform scope (revised): Android, iPhone, Windows, and Mac.** The app is no longer mobile-only — it needs to be usable from all four.

**Desktop technology — decided.** Expo already supports a "web" build target (via React Native Web), built on the same codebase as the mobile app — no separate toolchain needed. This web build is configured as an installable PWA (Progressive Web App): the UI and most business logic are shared with mobile; the local storage layer needs a platform-specific implementation (browser IndexedDB instead of on-device SQLite), and any native-only modules need web equivalents.

- **Windows**: full traditional "install from the Microsoft Store" experience, via PWABuilder packaging the PWA into an MSIX for Store submission. Zero platform fee — Microsoft eliminated individual developer registration fees in September 2025, and extended free registration to company accounts as well as of a Windows Developer Blog post in May 2026.
- **Mac**: installable PWA, not listed in the Mac App Store. This deliberately avoids Apple's Developer Program fee ($99/year, recurring, required for Mac App Store distribution regardless of framework) and its notarization/review process — a PWA installed via the browser's own "Add to Dock"/install mechanism never enters Apple's app-signing pipeline at all, unlike a traditionally distributed native binary (e.g., an Electron-wrapped app), which would still need notarization even outside the App Store to avoid Gatekeeper warnings. Revisit only if a future need for full Mac App Store presence outweighs the ongoing fee.

**Multi-device sync & control model (the person's own devices).** Refined from an earlier "one primary device is authoritative" framing into a cleaner, symmetric model: the encrypted cloud-backup folder (same location already described below) is the actual current state, not just a disaster-recovery copy. A device pulls the latest encrypted snapshot, decrypts it locally with the master password/key, and works with that as its own live local database from that point — preserving full offline capability, since the cloud only needs to be reachable at the pull/push transition points, not during everyday use (logging a meal with no signal still works). When done, the device pushes an updated encrypted snapshot back. This one mechanism now does three jobs: disaster-recovery backup, syncing between the same person's own phone/Windows/Mac, and the transport for household companion sharing described below (with the companion's access scope restricted, versus full access for the primary user's own devices).

Three layered safety mechanisms prevent two devices from editing the same data at once, fastest/most common to slowest/most rare:

1. *Locking ("control")*: whichever device pulls the database first holds exclusive control, recorded in the cloud metadata. A commit does not release control by itself — control persists until the app is closed, the screen times out/locks, or (if the person doesn't use a screen lock) an inactivity timer detects the app sitting open and idle in the foreground.
2. *Active takeover*: from a second device, the person taps a "take over/connect" button. This sends a *visible*, tap-required push notification to the controlling device — deliberately not a silent background push, since silent pushes are documented as best-effort and can be delayed or dropped by iOS/Android background throttling, while visible notifications are delivered far more reliably (that's the core job a notification system is built to do). The requesting device's screen names the controlling device by name (e.g., "Your iPhone currently has control") and instructs the person to check the notification there. Tapping that notification runs a background action that commits current local state and releases control; on iOS/Android the app then drops into a locked, read-only "this session moved to your other device" screen (apps can't force-quit themselves on mobile OSes the way a desktop program can; on Windows/Mac the app can cleanly exit itself, which is normal there). If the controlling device doesn't respond within roughly 1-2 minutes (a human needs to notice and tap, not an instant background process), the requesting device offers "take control anyway" — showing exactly how stale the other device's last save was (e.g., "iPhone last saved 2 hours ago") so the person makes an informed, explicit choice about what might be lost, rather than a silent automatic override.
3. *Stale-lock expiration*: a hard maximum lease of 30 minutes on control, independent of the graceful signals above — if no renewal happens in that window (crashed app, dead battery, lost/stolen device, permanent connectivity loss), control auto-expires regardless, so a single unreachable device can never permanently lock the person out of their own data.

Underneath all three, last-write-wins plus a visible activity log remains as the final safety net for whatever narrow edge cases slip past the above (e.g., cloud-sync propagation delay letting two devices briefly both believe they hold control) — the same conflict policy already used for household companion sync below, reused rather than inventing a second system. This design deliberately reuses two pieces already decided elsewhere in this document: the minimal content-blind push relay (APNs/FCM) originally built for reminders' cross-device nudges is the same signal used for takeover notifications, and the "show your own freshness/confidence" principle originally built for reminders is reused for the "last saved X ago" risk disclosure during a forced takeover.

**Household sharing, two access tiers, both reading from a shared cloud folder:**
- *Free viewer companion*: the primary shares their cloud backup folder (via the cloud provider's own native folder-sharing, not a custom mechanism) to a second person. That person's app reads periodic snapshots from the shared folder — modeled on how Anki's "shared decks" feature works (one-way, periodic reimport that merges in new content without wiping the viewer's own local state; no live subscription/auto-update exists in Anki either, and that absence is fine here too). The viewer can see the meal schedule, shopping list, and trend reports, and query details — but cannot edit most things.
- *One narrow exception*: the viewer CAN check off shopping list items. This is treated as a distinct, safe category of "write-back" — idempotent, single-field, low-conflict-risk actions (checking off a list item, marking a scheduled task complete) — sent back to the primary as a small delta/answer file. This is different in kind from full write access, and deliberately so: no general merge logic is needed for this category.
- *Paid interactive companion*: real read-write access to a shared "household domain" (meal plan, shopping list, schedule) — but NOT to the primary's "personal health domain" (symptom logs, labs, supplements, their own scoring/trend history), which stays owned by the primary and unreachable by any companion account, even the paid one. This split exists for both security and product-trust reasons.
- *Conflict handling for the interactive tier*: last-write-wins per field, plus a visible activity log so nothing silently disappears ("Alex removed milk at 4:12pm, overriding your edit from 4:10pm"). This is intentionally not a heavyweight CRDT/merge system — validated by the fact that AnkiWeb's own mature, decades-refined sync still falls back to a full one-way overwrite when changes genuinely can't merge (e.g., structural changes). Revisit only if real usage shows last-write-wins isn't good enough.
- *Cross-provider question, resolved 2026-08-08*: household sharing does **not** require both people to use the same cloud provider — confirmed directly. The real sync mechanism that makes a Partner/Guardian/Caregiver relationship work when each side backs up to a different provider (OneDrive on one end, Google Drive on the other) is deliberately still deferred to a later, dedicated design pass, not solved here.

**Reminders/scheduling, contextual from day one (not clock-based).** A reminder like "take your levothyroxine" should account for what's actually been logged — e.g., warn that a calcium-rich food logged earlier pushes the safe window later — not just fire at a fixed preset time. This splits into two different mechanisms:
- Fully local, on-device scheduled notifications (no server) for anything self-contained and recomputed instantly whenever the user is actively using the app.
- A minimal, content-blind push relay (APNs/FCM) only for cross-device nudges ("the other person just changed something") — this is a real server component, but it never carries actual health content, only a wake-up signal.
- Real mobile OS constraint: background execution is throttled on both iOS and Android, so "recompute the moment something changes" is only guaranteed while the app is in the foreground; background recomputation is best-effort on the OS's own refresh schedule, not instant. Design around this rather than assuming perfect real-time background updates.
- Reminders should show their own confidence/freshness (e.g., "based on food logged as of 7:45am") so the user can tell when contextual guidance might be based on stale data — because a late-logged contextual reminder can be confidently wrong, which is arguably worse than a dumb static reminder being honestly uninformed.

**Interaction rules engine — two kinds, both available from day one, kept visually distinct:**
- *Built-in, cited rules*: prescription/supplement/food timing interactions (e.g., levothyroxine + calcium/iron absorption interference, biotin interfering with certain thyroid lab assays) — authored with the same real-citation discipline as the nutrition database (FDA labeling, endocrinology guidance, pharmacology literature), not assumed/common-knowledge rules. Distinct research effort from the existing D1-D6 food scoring. Should probably ship as a maintainable, versioned data file/ruleset that can update independently of app releases, similar to how antivirus/spam-filter rule updates work.
- *Personal rule builder*: lets the individual user encode their own discovered pattern or their own doctor's specific instruction to them (which might differ from the general guideline). Must be labeled distinctly from the cited rules so personal hypothesis is never confused with verified medical fact, including in any doctor-facing report.
- *Synergy to build toward*: the trend engine should be able to surface candidate personal rules from observed patterns ("you've logged fatigue within a day of high-goitrogen meals four times this month — want to turn that into a reminder?"), closing the loop between passive tracking and actionable personal knowledge.

**Late/retroactive logging**: must be allowed and low-friction — guided quick-select questions rather than a blank form (what did you eat/take → quick-select from favorites/recents → when did it actually happen → smart time picker). Every late entry should immediately show its consequence (recalculated schedule impact), turning logging into a small teaching moment rather than a chore. Scheduled reminders should double as catch-up checkpoints ("anything since we last talked?") rather than relying on the user to self-initiate a late entry.

## Monetization — tiers

**Expanded to five real priced tiers plus one bundled role, 2026-08-08**, superseding the original two-tier sketch below it (written back when the app had a single meal builder and one condition; both are stale on their own terms now, not just narrow). Refined further the same day once real pricing and four open design questions got resolved. Full reasoning, a feature-access matrix, and the pricing framework behind each number is in the [Household & Monetization Tiers Artifact](https://claude.ai/code/artifact/e153d351-52c7-4df5-a4c1-e134ff86c085); this is the short version:

- **Free** (0x, $0): all eleven food builders; Insights limited to the Food Lookup lens only (single-food nutrition facts, no condition scoring); Schedule limited to Meals/Hydration/Exercise (no Supplements/Prescriptions/Doctor Appointments); Trends limited to exercise only; no Signals, no Reports, no interaction-rules engine, no household sharing. Purple Digest itself is not available — Free sees **"Food Basics"** instead, a real, differently-iconed (not the purple ribbon) tab covering only the Digest's genuinely general-knowledge categories (proposed starting split: Food Additives, Food Industry & History, Nutrient Interactions, Mitochondria & Metabolism, Complementary Therapies, Glossary — roughly a third of the current 18, final entry-by-entry tagging deferred to implementation). The same tab re-brands to "Purple Digest" in place on upgrade, not a second feature.
- **Individual** ($9.99/mo, $89.99/yr, the 1.0x base unit): everything Free lacks — full condition-specific scoring for every condition selected in Profile, the full interaction-rules engine, Trends, Reports, the full Schedule, the complete Purple Digest, sync across every device the person themselves owns. One person, their own data only.
- **Partner** ($14.99/mo, $134.99/yr for both — ~1.5x, not 2.0x): two people, each with full Individual-level access to their own data, plus granular, opt-in, per-category visibility into each other's (meals/shopping default shared; symptoms/labs/notes default private; medications a named explicit choice at setup) — a genuinely different, symmetric relationship from the household model below, not an extension of it.
- **Household / Family** (free for the first 2-3 seats, then $1.99/mo, $17.99/yr per seat, ~0.2x): the original "free viewer companion" concept, now with real per-seat economics attached (anchored to Calm's/Headspace's own real family-plan math) — read access to the shared meal plan/shopping list/Trends summary, one narrow write exception (shopping-list checkoffs, marking a scheduled task done), no visibility into the subscriber's personal health domain, no scoring of their own.
- **Guardian** ($0, bundled with Individual/Partner, per child): new, distinct from Caregiver below — a parent's own minor children only. Real, full condition tracking (medications, symptoms, doctor appointments, labs), turned on per category by the parent, who always retains full read/write access; the child's own direct access is separately and granularly configured (from zero self-interaction up to near-full self-service), entirely at the parent's own pace. Legally simpler than Caregiver's own consent problem, since a parent/guardian already has ordinary authority over a minor's health decisions.
- **Caregiver** ($4.99/mo, $49.99/yr per cared-for person, ~0.55x): real write access to another person's meals, medications, symptoms, and schedule on their behalf — not a viewer role, and explicitly **not** for a parent's own minor children (that's Guardian above). For aging parents, other relatives, or professional/independent caregiving. Requires the cared-for person's own explicit consent when they have the capacity to give it; when they don't, the app provides the technical capability and a plain attestation step, deliberately deferring the real legal/guardianship question rather than arbitrating it in-product. Built to work even when the cared-for person has no device or account of their own at all. Stackable on top of any other tier a caregiver already holds for their own use.
- **Addon tiers, later**: sleep tracking, exercise tracking, lab result tracking/trending, and other future health-tracking features layer on as paid addons, unchanged from the original plan.

**Cross-provider sharing, resolved 2026-08-08**: Partner, Guardian, and Caregiver relationships do **not** require both sides to use the same cloud backup provider. The real sync mechanism that makes this work across providers is deliberately still deferred to a later, dedicated design pass.

**Original two-tier sketch, kept for its own historical record**: Free was originally scoped as "single meal builder, general nutritional info only, no Hashimoto's 6-dimension scoring, no reporting, no trends" — accurate before the ten-builder Food tab existed. The original paid tier bundled "meal builder + shopping list, trends, the full 6-dimension scoring, the daily schedule with contextual reminders, doctor reporting, household sharing (both companion tiers), and cloud backup" as one flat tier, with the household economics decision that a companion seat should be a separate, cheaper seat rather than a second full-price subscription — a decision the Household/Family tier above keeps, just with a real number attached.

## Reporting

Doctor/nutritionist/trainer/self reports are different views over the same underlying time-stamped, taggable log data — designed so every log entry is timestamped and taggable from the start, since reporting becomes a query rather than a rebuild if the schema supports it from day one. Reports generate entirely on-device (PDF export, no server rendering) — consistent with the stated privacy model that the only data ever leaving the device is something the user explicitly exports/prints/emails themselves.

## Technology stack — decided

- **Framework: React Native, built on Expo.** Chosen over Flutter specifically because this project needs heavy third-party integration (OneDrive, Google Drive, push notifications, app store billing, encrypted local storage), and React Native's package ecosystem has broader off-the-shelf coverage for that kind of work — meaningful for a solo builder.
- **Why Expo specifically**: the user's dev hardware is a Windows PC + Android phone, no Mac (daughter has an iPhone, available for occasional testing only). Expo Go lets the user run the app live on their own Android phone during development with no Mac and no native build step. When a real installable build is needed later (including iOS), Expo's cloud build service (EAS Build) compiles it on Expo's servers — a Mac is never required, even for shipping to the App Store.
- **Builder's background**: 30 years as an IT Manager, "some scripting/basics" hands-on coding experience, no prior mobile app development, but a strong track record of learning new technical domains as needed. Building this themselves with ongoing step-by-step direction and code-level help from Claude Code in VS Code, not handing it to a separate developer.
- **First milestone: complete.** The "Hello World" toolchain validation (Node.js, VS Code, Expo tooling, Expo Go on the Android phone) succeeded and was superseded by real feature development starting 2026-07-19 — see Status above.

## Technology directions discussed (not yet finalized beyond the above)

- Cross-platform mobile framework — decided, see above.
- On-device storage: SQLite (matches the existing nutrition database format already), likely encrypted at rest via something like SQLCipher; envelope encryption pattern for the master-password/recovery-key model.
- Cloud backup integration: Microsoft Graph API (OneDrive) and Google Drive API, using each provider's app-specific-folder support.
- Push notifications: APNs (iOS) + FCM (Android) for the cross-device nudge signal only.
- Subscriptions/entitlements: Apple StoreKit + Google Play Billing for payment processing (required by platform policy), plus a small entitlement-check backend — the one honest exception to "no server," since it holds billing/tier metadata only, never health data.
- On-device PDF generation libraries for reporting/export.

**A second, deeper content pass across the 8 most-recently-built conditions, 2026-08-08, same day, direct instruction: "Make sure to keep all 3 up to date at all times. Continue the full parity work for the remaining 8 conditions."** The "remaining 8" are the last 8 of the 18 non-Hashimoto's conditions built out this session (Chronic Kidney Disease, Fatty Liver Disease/MASLD, Type 2 Diabetes, IBS, Migraine, Cardiovascular Disease, Gout, Prostate Health) — each already had a real structural-parity pass (staging/organ-systems/history/pregnancy-or-equivalent entries), so this pass added one genuinely new, well-researched finding per condition rather than repeating the earlier structural work, continuing to build real volumetric depth toward the same standard Hashimoto's itself carries.

Real, independently-verified findings added: CKD's real dialysis-diet reversal (the pre-dialysis protein-restriction and potassium-limiting rules often flip once dialysis starts, since dialysis itself removes protein and fluid/potassium restrictions often intensify instead — NIDDK); MASLD's PNPLA3 I148M gene variant (a real 2.76x fatty-liver-odds / 4.44x MASH-odds increase per copy, a genuine gene-environment interaction with alcohol/obesity/T2D — MedlinePlus Genetics); Type 2 Diabetes's dawn phenomenon vs. Somogyi effect (two real, distinct causes of high morning blood sugar requiring a real 2-3AM testing protocol to tell apart, with CGM-era research finding the Somogyi rebound less common than once assumed — PMID 21717414); IBS's real SIBO-overlap caveat (72% positive on the lactulose breath test vs. 36-46% on the glucose test, with a real, direct methodological flaw explaining the gap, and a real, honest finding that test positivity didn't reliably correlate with symptom severity — PMC10134763); Migraine's real, underrecognized vestibular subtype (roughly 1% of the general population, up to 73% of migraine-with-aura patients meeting real criteria, with real underrecognition data showing only 20% of patients volunteer the symptom unprompted vs. 75% when asked directly — PMID 38619053); Cardiovascular Disease's real CANTOS trial (a pure anti-inflammatory drug, canakinumab, reduced real cardiovascular events with zero cholesterol change at all, the first phase 3 proof that inflammation is a real, independently treatable cardiovascular driver — NEJM, PMID 28845751); Gout's real diuretic-driven prescribing cascade (a real, large case-control study found diuretics carry a roughly 2.5-fold gout risk overall, loop+thiazide combined use nearly doubling that again to 4.65-fold, a real, concrete example of this app's own already-built prescribing-cascade concept — PMID 24449584); and Prostate Health's real Harvard ejaculation-frequency finding (a real, 18-year, 31,925-man study found 21+ monthly ejaculations tracking with a real 31% lower prostate cancer risk, holding up after adjusting for PSA-screening frequency specifically — Harvard Health Publishing).

Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit` (clean across the whole project) and `npx eslint` on every touched file — clean. **Not yet exercised on-device.**

**A real, standing "keep all three current" discipline established the same day, per the same direct instruction** — git pushed to GitHub, the Notion hub (App Development Log + Project Tracker) updated, and the App Guide artifact redeployed, all treated as part of finishing any real batch of work going forward, not as separately-requested follow-ups. This followed a real, concrete gap found the same day: 161 local commits had never been pushed to GitHub, and the Notion App Development Log/Project Brief/Open Questions pages were all several days to two weeks stale despite an existing (insufficient on its own) standing auto-sync instruction — both fixed, and the practice change saved to memory (`feedback_keep_git_notion_appguide_current`) so it carries forward automatically.

**The second depth pass extended to the first 10 non-Hashimoto's conditions the same day, direct instruction: "Continue the full parity work for the other conditions."** Same shape as the CKD-through-Prostate-Health batch: one genuinely new, well-researched, independently-verified finding per condition, checked against each file's own existing ids first to avoid duplicating already-covered ground (e.g., Graves' already had a smoking entry, so the new finding there is genetic/family risk instead; MS already had an EBV-mimicry entry, so the new finding is gut dysbiosis).

Real findings added: RA's smoking-to-citrullination mechanism (a real gene-environment interaction with HLA-DRB1/PADI4 variants, amplifying ACPA-positive RA risk — PMC7072747); Psoriasis's real, dose-dependent smoking relationship (nearly 2x risk at light smoking, 2x severe-disease risk at heavy smoking, plus measurably blunted biologic-treatment response — PMC9133951/PMC11007762); Graves' real ~80%-heritability twin-study finding (HLA-DR3/CTLA-4/PTPN22, cross-linked to Hashimoto's own heritability entry — PMC3503798); Type 1 Diabetes's real closed-loop/automated-insulin-delivery evidence (Time in Range up from 61% to 69% in one trial, ~10% average gain across a meta-analysis — NEJM, DiaTribe); Celiac's dermatitis herpetiformis (15-25% of celiac patients, a real distinct skin-targeted antibody response, sometimes the only visible sign — PMID 26059085); IBD's fecal microbiota transplantation for ulcerative colitis (57.1% remission at 12 weeks in one real trial, honestly caveated as still a developing evidence base — PMC12006273); MS's real gut-dysbiosis/SCFA findings, including Bacteroides fragilis directly improving disease severity in a mouse model — PMC10001679; Lupus's drug-induced lupus (a real, usually fully reversible condition tied to procainamide/hydralazine, slow-acetylator genetics — StatPearls NBK441889); Sjögren's real, often-unmentioned vaginal/skin dryness as genuine exocrine-gland involvement beyond the mouth and eyes — Rheumatology/Oxford Academic; and PCOS's real gut-microbiome-diversity-to-hyperandrogenism link, including a real FMT-transplant mouse experiment showing causality, not just correlation — PMID 29370410.

Whole-directory verification: 765 unique ids across the whole Digest (up from 755), zero duplicates, zero dangling `relatedIds`. `PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION` bumped (double-checked as numerically later than the prior value). **A first real push toward genuine volumetric depth (not just structural parity) began the same day, direct instruction after confirming the numbered/second GitHub credential had been deleted: "continue the full parity work toward volumetric depth."** Ranked all 18 non-Hashimoto's conditions by current entry count (14-20 each, all far below Hashimoto's own 176) and picked the 6 lowest-count conditions — Cardiovascular Disease (14), Migraine (14), Celiac (15), Chronic Kidney Disease (15), Fatty Liver Disease/MASLD (15), Graves' Disease (15) — for a real, substantial 3-entries-each build-out (18 new entries total), each checked against that condition's own existing ids first to avoid duplicating already-covered ground.

Real findings added: **CVD** — lipoprotein(a) as a real, largely genetic, ~20%-of-the-population risk factor most people are never tested for; cardiac rehabilitation's real 26-31% mortality reduction against under-30% real-world enrollment; "holiday heart syndrome," a real, named binge-drinking-triggered atrial fibrillation pattern. **Migraine** — the real migraine-with-aura/combined-hormonal-contraceptive stroke-risk contraindication, honestly caveated as resting on genuinely lower-quality, decades-old evidence; the real bidirectional migraine-insomnia relationship, with CBT-I itself shown to reduce headache days; obesity's real, quantified dose-response risk for episodic-to-chronic migraine transformation (3x at overweight, 5x at obese). **Celiac** — non-celiac gluten sensitivity as a real, genuinely contested diagnosis (only 16-30% of self-reporters react to gluten specifically under blind challenge); dental enamel defects as a real, striking (48-94%) pediatric celiac sign; the real legal gap where wheat flour must be fortified by law and gluten-free substitutes almost never are. **CKD** — aristolochic acid as a real, well-documented herbal nephrotoxin that can cause irreversible kidney failure; NSAIDs' real, quantified AKI risk (73% general population, 63% in existing CKD); real gut-bacteria-manufactured uremic toxins (indoxyl sulfate, p-cresyl sulfate) tying CKD directly into this app's own gut-microbiome research. **MASLD** — real trial evidence that exercise alone, with zero weight change, measurably reduces liver fat; the real, landmark PIVENS trial's own quantified vitamin E benefit for non-diabetic NASH; the real statin-safety myth directly costing 40-50% of eligible MASLD patients real cardiovascular protection. **Graves'** — a real, current 13-study meta-analysis confirming stressful life events as a genuine onset trigger in genetically susceptible people; teprotumumab's real, structural reversal of thyroid eye disease (69% response vs. 20% placebo); the real block-and-replace-vs-titration dosing debate, roughly equally effective with a real practical convenience tradeoff.

**A real, explicit scope statement from the person the same day, worth recording verbatim rather than paraphrased, since it changes how "done" should be read for the rest of this whole workstream**: "with that much of a gap, I still say each of the other 18 deserve and require just as much in depth and fully encompassing information as Hashimoto's did because this is no longer just focused on Hashimoto's, it is focused on all 19 both individually and if a person has multiple of them combined. The scope of this app is not longer just to perfect it for Hashimoto's. The scope is to perfect it for all of the others, too." Two real, distinct implications: (1) every one of the 18 non-Hashimoto's conditions is a genuine, standing target for Hashimoto's own 176-entry depth, not a lesser-tier condition that structural parity alone satisfies; (2) real, multi-condition/polyautoimmunity combined-management content (already touched piecemeal via the many real comorbidity entries scattered across condition files — RA-Hashimoto's, Celiac-Hashimoto's, Type1-Celiac, PCOS-Hashimoto's, Sjögren's-RA/Lupus, etc.) is now named directly as its own real, standing scope item, not yet built as a first-class, browsable destination of its own — flagged here honestly rather than rushed into a new architecture blind; worth real, deliberate design thought (a dedicated category? a cross-condition query surface reading `user_conditions`?) the next time this is picked up specifically.

**The second real batch of the volumetric-depth push landed the same day, continuing from the first 6-condition batch**: ranked all 18 conditions by entry count again and added 3 substantial, independently-verified entries each to the next 7 lowest — IBS, Type 2 Diabetes, IBD, Lupus, PCOS, Sjögren's, and Type 1 Diabetes.

Real findings added: **IBS** — bile acid diarrhea as a real, genuinely underdiagnosed cause hiding inside up to a third of IBS-D diagnoses; CBT as a real, distinct, guideline-recommended brain-gut therapy separate from hypnotherapy; the real finding that ~90% of the body's serotonin is made in the gut, directly implicated in IBS's own mechanism. **Type 2 Diabetes** — bariatric surgery's real, substantially stronger remission data than lifestyle intervention alone (40% vs. 0% at 3 years); metformin's real, well-documented B12-depletion risk; real CGM benefit even in non-insulin-treated T2D (the CONNECT trial). **IBD** — vitamin D deficiency's real, direct correlation with disease severity and biologic response; the hygiene hypothesis's real, concrete supporting evidence (older siblings, cesarean delivery); anti-TNF biologics' real, named secondary-loss-of-response phenomenon and its real, actionable fixes. **Lupus** — antiphospholipid syndrome as a real, distinct, clotting-driven comorbid condition; complement C3/C4 as real, useful early-warning flare markers; discoid lupus's real, quantified progression risk to systemic disease. **PCOS** — the real, striking MASLD comorbidity (51.56% vs. 29.64% prevalence); combined oral contraceptives as PCOS's real, formal first-line treatment; real, genuinely favorable IVF live-birth-rate data. **Sjögren's** — the real labial salivary gland biopsy diagnostic procedure and its own real, imperfect sensitivity/specificity; Raynaud's phenomenon as a real, common, sometimes-first Sjögren's symptom; fatigue as the disease's real, most disabling symptom, more than dryness itself. **Type 1 Diabetes** — hypoglycemia unawareness as a real, dangerous, named phenomenon affecting 25-50% of patients; islet cell transplantation as a real, working path to insulin independence; a real, striking sevenfold hip-fracture-risk finding independent of bone density alone.

Real findings added: **CVD** — triglycerides as a real, distinct risk marker with the REDUCE-IT trial's own real 25% event reduction from purified EPA; a real, honest look at medication-nonadherence hiding underneath "resistant" hypertension; the real, documented gender gap in how women's heart disease gets diagnosed and treated. **Gout** — pseudogout (CPPD) as a real, commonly-confused, chemically distinct look-alike; real, quantified weight-loss benefit across diet/medication/bariatric-surgery paths; colchicine's own real, genuinely narrow therapeutic index. **Migraine** — the real PREEMPT trial evidence behind Botox for chronic migraine, including its own honest PREEMPT-1/PREEMPT-2 inconsistency; the trigeminovascular/CGRP mechanism itself, distinct from the CGRP-inhibitor drug-class entry; real, still-unsettled ketogenic/low-carb-diet evidence. **RA** — interstitial lung disease as a real, common, underrecognized complication; the real ORAL Surveillance trial behind JAK inhibitors' own FDA boxed warning; the real, quantified depression/anxiety comorbidity tracking directly with disease activity. **Prostate Health** — active surveillance's real, strong 10-year survival data for low-risk cancer; prostatitis as a real, distinct, commonly-confused-with-BPH condition; real, quantified exercise-and-mortality data specific to prostate cancer. **Celiac** — the real, quantified depression/anxiety burden and its honestly incomplete resolution on a gluten-free diet; the real "celiac iceberg" underdiagnosis concept; the real, specific gluten-challenge protocol needed for an accurate test.
