**A real Digest layout/UX pass began the same day, following a Google-search-sourced knowledge-base-design brief the person shared and asked to discuss.** Rather than adopt every recommendation uncritically, each suggestion was checked against what's actually built (`app/(tabs)/purple-digest.tsx`, `components/LensHub.tsx`) before deciding what to build: several were already effectively satisfied (related-entry chips already exist via `relatedIds`; the whole Digest is a bundled TS array, so "offline access" was already structurally true; full-text search already matches symptom words in prose). Four real, low-risk wins were picked to build first, explicitly deferring the two genuinely large asks (tracked-data "Pattern Reminders," a Direct-to-Tracker action button, both requiring cross-tab integration into Bio-Compass/Schedule) as their own separate, deliberate future initiative rather than folding them in blind.

**Win 1 -- a real, persistent search bar.** Previously "Search All" was one of ~21 tiles you had to open LensHub and tap into first. A new search input now sits permanently at the top of the screen (a real sibling of `SwipeableTabScreen`, not nested inside `GatedTabContent`'s own children, which render nothing at all until `revealed` -- the bar has to live outside that gate to be usable before any lens has ever been picked). Typing switches straight into the existing `'search'` lens and reveals the screen if it hasn't been already, writing into the same `searchQuery` state the lens's own content already reads -- a second way to populate one state, not a second search mechanism. The old in-content search input inside the `'search'` lens branch was removed as the now-redundant duplicate it would have become.

**Win 2 -- the person's own tracked conditions are pinned to the top of the LensHub picker, using data that already existed and was already unused for this purpose.** `getUserConditions()` (built earlier the same day's own multi-condition work, previously only read by Profile) is now fetched on every focus and used to reorder `LENSES`: Basic Health always leads, then every condition the person has actually selected (in their own already-established build order), then everything else -- real, fewer taps to reach a condition someone actually tracks, without hiding or removing anything else. A leading "★ " marks a matched tile's own grid label so the reorder has a visible, explained reason, not an unexplained shuffle. Required a real, hand-verified mapping (`CONDITION_CODE_TO_DIGEST_KEY`) between the `conditions` reference table's own snake_case codes and this screen's camelCase `DigestCategoryKey` -- confirmed directly against the live database via `sqlite3.exe` rather than guessed, since a wrong mapping would have silently failed to match anything.

**Win 3 -- every condition category (not just Basic Health) now uses the same real shelf-row-plus-detail-panel UI, organized into 4 real pillars, instead of one long flat accordion list.** This is the largest and riskiest of the four wins, a genuine behavioral change to how all 19 conditions (Hashimoto's included) are browsed. Rather than store a new field on 840 existing entries (a real content-authoring undertaking, not a UI change), pillar assignment is computed at render time from a heuristic reading each entry's own `id` and title, the same "reasonable v1, computed not stored" precedent already established by `BASIC_HEALTH_GROUPS`' own id-prefix matching and the food-name-grouping feature elsewhere in this app. Four pillars: Core Science, Self-Advocacy & Testing, Whole-Body Effects, History & Life Stages -- each condition's own real closing "tying-together" synthesis entry is pulled out of the shelves entirely and shown as its own standalone "Putting It Together" card below them, since it summarizes across the whole category rather than fitting one pillar.

**The classifier was validated against all 840 real entries before shipping, not assumed correct** -- a real Node script (mirroring the actual regex logic) run against every condition file surfaced two genuine misclassifications on the first pass: an "-overview" entry landing in "Whole-Body Effects" because its own title named body systems directly (MS's own overview literally says "brain and spinal cord"), and a real priority-order bug where a pregnancy entry's own title containing the word "diagnosed" caused it to match the (weaker, title-text-driven) self-advocacy check before ever reaching the (stronger, deliberately-named-by-this-app's-own-id-convention) pregnancy/stages check. Both fixed: `-overview` entries are now forced to Core Science outright before any other check runs, and the id-based stages check now runs before the title-text-driven advocacy check. Re-validated against all 840 entries afterward with zero remaining known misclassifications.

A real, latent bug in the underlying scroll mechanism was also caught and fixed while building this: `groupRefs` (keyed by plain group-label strings like "Core Science") is shared across every condition, and since every condition now has its own "Core Science" shelf, a stale ref left over from whichever category was open before a lens switch could theoretically be scrolled to for one frame before the new category's real shelf mounted and overwrote it. Fixed by resetting `groupRefs.current = {}` on every lens switch (both `LensHub`'s own `onSelect` and `jumpToRelated`), eliminating the collision risk structurally rather than trying to make every label globally unique.

**Win 4 -- a reading-time estimate and cross-condition tag pills, both computed from data this app already has, no new content authored.** `estimateReadingMinutes()` is a plain word-count-over-200wpm calculation on the same body text already shown when a card expands. `crossConditionCategories()` reuses each entry's already-existing `relatedIds` to surface, as small, deliberately non-tappable pills, which OTHER conditions a given entry's own Related list reaches into -- deliberately not a second tap target, since `RelatedChips` (already rendered further down the same card) already is the real, existing tap-to-jump mechanism, labeled by the specific related entry rather than by category.

Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit` (clean) and `npx eslint` on the one touched file (`app/(tabs)/purple-digest.tsx`, clean) -- no `lib/digest/*.ts` content files were touched this pass, so `PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION` was deliberately left unbumped, matching how prior pure-UI Digest changes this session were handled. **Not yet exercised on-device** -- this is a real, substantial interaction change to the primary way every condition is read, genuinely more so than most "not yet exercised" items already flagged throughout this document, worth a real, careful on-device walkthrough (the persistent search bar's reveal behavior, the pillar shelves across a few different conditions, the tying-together card, cross-lens ref-collision fix) before trusting it fully.

**A real, direct follow-up correction the same day: "Search the whole digest should be on the outside of Basic Health, not inside of it. Basic Health needs its own search utility, just as all of the other areas of the Digest do," plus a real request for genuine tree-style, hierarchical navigation ("all of the deep dive into macro, micro, acid, and hormone related nutrients should be one of the topics to dive into from the hierarchical structure").**

**Global "Search All" removed as a pickable LensHub tile entirely, staying purely the persistent top-of-screen bar built earlier the same day.** Previously it sat as a co-equal grid tile right alongside Basic Health and every condition, reading as "one of the topics" rather than the structurally separate, always-available capability it was meant to be. `lens` can still resolve to `'search'` internally (typing in the persistent bar does that, reusing the same results-rendering branch unchanged), it just never appears in `LENSES` anymore. Its own dedicated Info-sheet content was folded into the screen's regular help sections instead, since there's no longer a per-lens Info tile for it to hang off of.

**Every real category, not just Basic Health, now has its own separate, scoped "search within this section" box.** `basicHealthSearchQuery` was generalized into one shared `categorySearchQuery` state, rendered for every non-search lens (Basic Health and all 18 conditions alike), independent of the global bar -- reading directly from what the request named as a real gap ("just as all of the other areas of the Digest do").

**Basic Health rebuilt from a flat, 31-group, all-shown-at-once shelf list into a real, 2-level tree** -- a direct, considered response to the person's own UX principle ("moving strictly from broad categories down to highly specific, bite-sized pieces of information") applied to the one place in the Digest that genuinely needed it (conditions, with only 4 pillars each, already read fine as a flat shelf list; Basic Health's 31 groups did not). `BASIC_HEALTH_TOPICS` replaces the old flat `BASIC_HEALTH_GROUPS`: 9 standalone topics (Glossary, Problem Foods & Swaps, Food Additives, Nutrient Interactions, Fermented Foods, Lifestyle & Environment, Mitochondria & Metabolism, Self Advocacy, Food Industry & History) plus one real parent topic, "Essential Nutrients," itself containing the 22 individual nutrient deep-dives (Magnesium through Hormones) as real subtopics -- exactly the example given directly ("all of the deep dive into macro, micro, acid, and hormone related nutrients should be one of the topics"). Top level shows a real, scannable grid of 10 topic cards; drilling into Essential Nutrients shows a second grid of 22 subtopic cards; any real leaf (standalone topic, or one specific nutrient) shows a plain, scannable list of that leaf's own entries, reusing `DigestCard` exactly as every other category's own accordion list already does. A real, dynamic "More" safety net (not a hardcoded 32nd topic) only appears if some future entry's own id genuinely matches no known prefix.

**A real, pre-existing bug was caught and fixed while validating this new tree against all 296 real Basic Health entries before shipping** (the same throwaway-script discipline already established throughout this whole Digest build) -- the OLD flat list's own "Hormones" prefix (`'hormone'`) never actually matched 11 of that topic's own 12 real entries, since `lib/digest/hormones.ts` names most of its ids after the specific hormone itself (`insulin-`, `cortisol-`, `estrogen-`, `leptin-`, `testosterone-`, `thyroid-hormones-`), not a shared `hormone-` prefix. This wasn't introduced by the restructure -- it was already silently dropping those 11 entries into the old flat list's own unlabeled "More" bucket, just harder to notice there. Fixed with the real, complete prefix list; re-validated afterward with zero unmatched entries across all 296.

Real, always-searchable independent of any tree depth confirmed to remain true: `searchDigestEntries`/`searchEntries` operate over the flat `ALL_DIGEST_ENTRIES`/category `entries` arrays regardless of the browsing UI on top of them, so every nutrient deep-dive stays fully reachable via both the persistent global bar and Basic Health's own local search exactly as before, per the request's own explicit requirement ("can also be found through either the whole digest search or through the search within each section").

Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit` (clean) and `npx eslint` (clean) on the one touched file. No `lib/digest/*.ts` content files touched (840 unique ids confirmed unchanged), so `PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION` was left unbumped. **Not yet exercised on-device** -- this is a real, further extension of the same-day interaction change to how Basic Health specifically is browsed, on top of the condition-pillar change already flagged as unverified; worth checking together in one real walkthrough once on-device access is available (search-bar placement/behavior, the topic grid, drilling into Essential Nutrients, a leaf list, and the back-navigation at each level).

## Named, unresolved risks (product risk, not engineering risk)

1. **Logging discipline / retention**: the single biggest risk to the whole vision is whether a real person will log consistently enough, over weeks/months, for contextual reminders and trend-correlation to mean anything. Most health-tracking apps fail on this, not on missing features. Worth testing this specific assumption cheaply before building the full data model around it.
2. **Signal quality**: whether symptom/food correlation-finding on noisy, confounded, small-sample (n=1) personal data actually surfaces real patterns versus things that merely look like patterns. Needs to be handled honestly in the product (showing confidence, not asserting causation) rather than assumed to just work.

## App navigation structure

**Superseded by the actual shipped structure as of 2026-07-22.** The original plan (four top-level tabs — Meals, Trends, Schedule, Reports) was replaced mid-build with a **hub-and-spoke model**: a central popup-menu button (`TabHub`) replaces a persistent bottom tab bar, plus left/right swipe between tabs (`SwipeableTabScreen`); pages with their own internal sub-tabs (Insights, Schedules) get an equivalent secondary hub (`LensHub`). `constants/tabs.ts` is the single source of truth for the current tab list.

The shipped tabs are: **Home** (personalized dashboard, added — wasn't in the original plan), **Food** (renamed from **Meals**), **Insights** (renamed from the original nutrient-tracking concept, split out of Trends), **Schedules**, **Trends**, **Bio-Compass** (a new flare-up/symptom/new-food tracker, sitting between Trends and Reports — wasn't in the original plan), **Reports** (still a stub, not built out), and **Purple Digest** (the autoimmune-education/aggregator area, promoted from a Stack-push placeholder to a real 8th tab 2026-08-05 — see Status below).

**The Food tab's history**: a regular meal builder (ingredient search, favorites/templates, rotating ingredients, a raw-goitrogenic-quantity check for blended Salad/Smoothie meal types) was fully built and functional as of 2026-07-24. A fuller split into dedicated builder pages (Meal assembler, Salad, Smoothie, Side-dish, and a new Fermented-food builder, sharing one common `useSideBuilder` core) was planned in detail that same day but not implemented before, on 2026-07-25, the plan changed again: rather than incrementally split the existing builder, it was deleted outright and the Food tab is being rebuilt from scratch as seven builders (Meal, Side, Salad, Smoothie, Fermentation, Beverage, Snack), reached via the same LensHub picker pattern as Insights/Schedule/Bio-Compass rather than dedicated routes. See the Status section above for what that deletion broke (Schedule/Home's "Log now" deep link) and where the old implementation is backed up.

Schedule expanded beyond the original single-tab plan into explicit sub-domains: Meals, Hydration (including supplements/electrolytes dissolved in water), Supplements, **Prescriptions** (renamed from "Medications" per explicit product decision), Doctor Appointments (with device calendar integration), and Exercise.

**Fermented-food bacterial strain tracking — flagged here as "not yet started," first real research done 2026-08-05 (see Status section's own entry for that date).** The person should eventually be able to log homemade fermented foods (yogurt, sauerkraut, etc.) with the *specific* bacterial strains/cultures involved (e.g., Lactobacillus acidophilus, Lactobacillus plantarum, Bifidobacterium species, Streptococcus thermophilus, Leuconostoc mesenteroides), and have the app reflect back real, cited information about what's actually documented for each strain. This requires a new reference data layer that doesn't exist yet — comparable in scope and citation rigor to the existing nutrition database's D5 fermentability/microbiome work, just going one level deeper into specific strains rather than general fermentation effects. The 2026-08-05 pass verified 8 strains already in the person's own real kitchen use (see Status) plus researched several more (Akkermansia muciniphila, direct-Hashimoto's-trial strains) against real PubMed literature, published as a standalone Artifact — a real head start on this workstream's own research half, though the actual in-app data layer/logging UI described in this paragraph still doesn't exist.

## Healing-journey stages — decided 2026-07-31, not yet built

The app will model where someone is in their Hashimoto's recovery and use that to drive guidance in the Food builders. Decisions made, implementation not started:

- **Framework**: the 5-stage *healing/regression* model (distinct from disease-progression staging), sourced from functional-medicine/root-cause thyroid protocols, associated with Dr. Izabella Wentz: **1. Triage** (symptom relief, hormone stabilization) → **2. Digging** (removing triggers, notably gluten/dairy) → **3. Gut Repair** (intestinal permeability, 4R protocol) → **4. Rebalancing** (immune modulation, HPA/adrenal) → **5. Maintenance** (clinical remission, antibodies in range).
- **Stage source**: self-declared during app setup (a new user has no assessment history), then adjusted over time by the existing Assessment. The app *suggests* a stage change rather than silently reclassifying.
- **What stage does**: **advisory + reordering only, never gating.** Stage-appropriate foods surface first in the pickers; anything questionable for the current stage gets a color-coded flag linked to an explanation. Nothing is ever hidden or blocked.
- **Framing**: presented as a *named, attributed framework*, with individual guidance items tagged by evidence strength — reusing the same discipline already in `lib/sixDimensionsReference.ts` (where AIP's nightshade exclusion is scored "Mild Risk" precisely because its evidence is mechanistic/elimination-based rather than RCT). This matters because evidence strength genuinely varies across the five stages: selenium lowering TPO antibodies has real randomized-trial support, while "adrenal fatigue"/HPA-reset framing and "leaky gut" as a clinical entity are contested in mainstream endocrinology. Functional-medicine root-cause staging is **not** mainstream endocrinology consensus, and the app must not imply otherwise.

**Sources supplied 2026-07-31** (the earlier blocker — bare [1]–[21] markers with no source list — is resolved). Listed here with an honest evidence tier per source, since they differ enormously and the app has committed to per-claim tiering:

| Claim | Source | Evidence tier |
|---|---|---|
| Selenium reduces TPO antibodies over 3–6 months | Systematic meta-analysis of 21 RCTs, "Clinical Efficacy of Selenium Supplementation in Patients with Hashimoto Thyroiditis" (PubMed) | **Strong.** Meta-analysis of randomized trials, the highest tier in this set. |
| High-dose thiamine (B1) resolves autoimmune-thyroid fatigue | "Thiamine and Hashimoto's Thyroiditis: A Report of Three Cases", PubMed 24351023 | **Weak.** This is a 3-patient *case report*, not a trial — no control group, no randomization. The supplied text described it as "a landmark peer-reviewed clinical pilot trial", which overstates it. Must not be presented as trial evidence. |
| Gluten/dairy/soy as top inflammatory triggers | Patient-survey metrics, thyroidpharmacist.com | **Weak/indicative.** Self-reported survey of one practitioner's own audience: real signal, but selection-biased and uncontrolled. (Note the separate, stronger celiac/thyroid autoimmunity literature exists independently and is the better citation for the gluten claim specifically.) |
| Disease progression Stages 0–5; gland regeneration | Dr. Izabella Wentz, thyroidpharmacist.com articles | **Practitioner framework.** The framework's own author explaining it. Legitimate to attribute, not independent peer review. |
| Healing/regression staging; 90-day sequencing | Functional Medicine University clinical protocol guide; *Hashimoto's Protocol* (book) | **Practitioner framework.** Training-organization material and a trade book, not peer-reviewed literature. |
| Definition of clinical remission | Paloma Health patient-education page | **Secondary/patient-education.** A telehealth company's explainer, useful for plain-language framing, not a primary source. |

Net: exactly one claim in this set (selenium) carries strong trial-level evidence. Everything else is practitioner framework, case-report, or survey data. That is fine to build on **provided the app labels it honestly**, which is the whole point of the per-claim tiering decision above. Individual source URLs/claims still need verifying against the actual documents at build time rather than trusted from the summary.

**Already exists and should be reused, not rebuilt**: `app/assessment.tsx` (3 cited domains, 23 items, built for periodic retaking — this is the "self-assess at intervals" machinery) and `app/purple-digest.tsx` (placeholder shell; the intended home for this educational content, sources already chosen: MedlinePlus, ATA, Autoimmune Association). Nothing resembling a journey stage exists in the codebase yet — confirmed by search.

**Practical scoping note**: only stages 2 and 3 meaningfully drive *food* decisions. Stages 1, 4, and 5 are largely about hormone dosing, nutrient correction, adaptogens, sleep, and lab markers. The five stages are right for education and orientation, but builder rules will key off fewer distinctions than five.

**The real food-by-stage substance this section always lacked was finally researched 2026-08-05** (see Status section's own entry for that date) — a new standalone Artifact, "The Healing Stages: A Grounded, Staged Food Guide," cross-maps this five-stage clinical scaffold onto a simpler, practically-useful three-tier structure (Getting Started / Rebuilding / Well-Healed, matching the practical-scoping note above almost exactly) and fills in what this section never had: a real, reasoned "first foods" list for day one, a real reintroduction order and timeline, real milestones per stage, and every specific week/month figure traced back to an actual cited study rather than invented. The five-stage scaffold itself remains exactly what this section already says — a practitioner framework, not peer-reviewed consensus — but the guide is explicit about which parts are real evidence (elimination-diet/SIBO/gut-barrier/Hashimoto's-specific trial data) versus framework language, holding to the same per-claim tiering discipline this section already committed to. Meant as the direct source content for whenever this section's own still-unbuilt advisory/reordering feature and Purple Digest actually get built — a real head start on the content half of this task, not the implementation.

14. ~~`split_prep_method()`'s trailing-clause info loss~~ -- **fixed at the root, 2026-08-02, same day, see Status above.** What this item used to flag as a risk turned out real and much larger than the 2 cheese rows that first surfaced it: reported directly as "the alcohol brands seem to have been removed," which led to finding 36 USDA wine varietals and 8 distilled-spirit rows had ALSO silently collapsed into one indistinguishable entry each. Fixed the actual mechanism (keep the full name instead of discarding an unrecognized leftover clause) rather than patching a fourth one-off case -- collapsed-group count across the whole database dropped from 295 to 92 as a result. The remaining 92 haven't been individually audited (some may be legitimate, e.g. two rows that are genuinely the same food from the same source) -- worth a look if another confusing "everything collapsed into one entry" report comes in, but not chased further proactively right now.

Distinct from the barcode-scan idea (item covers *packaged* alcohol) -- this is closer to how Fermentation Builder already lets someone build a food from real ingredients rather than pick a pre-made item. No design work done yet; flagged here so it isn't lost given canned/packaged beer and cider were deliberately hidden from browsing the same day (see Status above) specifically to make room for one of these two real paths (scan or home-brew) rather than a generic placeholder entry.

No design work done; would need real research into feasible data sources (a barcode API, OCR of a nutrition label, or manual entry against a photo) before this is buildable.

**Two final `Brewing` misses found on one last broad "any powder still in Bev" sweep, 2026-08-02, requested directly ("check for any other beverage powders we might have missed").** "Horlicks LowFat Instant powder" (UK_CoFID) is a real malted-milk drink powder, the same family as OVALTINE (already moved) -- a genuine miss, now fixed. "Beverages, Dairy drink mix, chocolate, reduced calorie, with low-calorie sweeteners, powder" (USDA) shared a case-collapsed base_name ("Beverages, Dairy drink mix" / "...dairy drink mix...") with a real prepared sibling ("...prepared with water and ice") -- the same structural risk already handled for the other USDA "Beverages, X" families, so this one went through `NAME_CATEGORY_OVERRIDES` too rather than a plain override that would have swept the prepared row in with it. Also checked and confirmed correct, no action needed: Powerade (both rows measure 99%+ water -- already a ready-to-drink liquid, not a powder despite matching earlier keyword searches), "Beverages, Eggnog-flavor mix" (only one row exists, and it already says "prepared with whole milk" -- no dry sibling to move), and the Strawberry/orange/generic "fruit flavour" drink-mix family (same excluded category as lemonade, item 12 above, not a new gap). `Brewing` is now 93 rows.

**The same "dry, not-yet-prepared base" audit applied to Soup/Sauces Builder, 2026-08-02, requested directly ("check the sauces and soups for anything similar").** Different shape of bug than Brewing's -- Germany_BLS/Japan_MEXT already file their own bouillon cubes, instant gravy powder, and instant curry/hash roux under `Herbs`, which is already in both Soup Builder's and Sauces Builder's own category allowlists, so those were never actually hidden (just an odd-sounding but harmless label -- a bouillon cube isn't literally an herb, but it's reachable, and 9 more rows didn't justify inventing a whole new category the way Brewing's 90+ did). The real, Brewing-equivalent bug: 9 genuinely dry items -- 6 French dehydrated bouillon/court-bouillon rows ("Bouillon de boeuf, déshydraté," etc. -- confirmed by name: "déshydraté" alone, not "...reconstitué"/"prêt à consommer," which are the real prepared versions and correctly stay put), 2 Australian "instant dry mix" soup rows (4.9-5.2g water/100g, confirmed), and "Gravy powder, dry mix" -- were sitting in `Mixed`, which is deliberately excluded from every builder's own allowlist (composite prepared dishes, not a raw ingredient anyone adds). These 9 were genuinely unreachable in Soup/Sauces Builder before this fix, not just oddly labeled. Moved into `Herbs`, joining the existing bouillon/gravy/roux precedent already there rather than inventing a separate bucket. Every real prepared broth/consommé/gravy/ready-to-serve soup sharing this same keyword search was checked by hand and deliberately left in `Mixed` -- confirmed via direct water-content/name check ("Fish broth"/"Soup, broth, fish" both measure 96% water; every consommé variant, every "ready-to-serve" row, "Gravy, prepared from dry powder with water" all correctly stayed).

**Baked Goods and Snacks checked, 2026-08-02 -- genuinely clean, no fixes needed.** Baking mixes (cake, muffin, biscuit, brownie, cornbread -- both dry and prepared variants) are already correctly split as separate rows under `Baked`, already in Baked Goods Builder's allowlist. Popcorn (including raw "unpopped kernels") and trail mix are correctly under `Grain`, already in Snack Builder's allowlist. Granola/energy/protein bars split between `Baked` and `Sweets`, both reachable. First clean audit pass of this whole run -- worth noting as a real negative result, not an incomplete check.

**The remaining builders (Side, Salad, Smoothie) checked the same day.** Salad/Smoothie: genuinely nothing found -- no dry dressing-mix packet (checked ranch/Italian/vinaigrette by name specifically, not just "dressing mix") or smoothie-mix powder exists anywhere in the database; every real salad dressing on file is already a prepared liquid under `Fats`, already reachable. Side Builder: found real, unreachable dry rice-side-dish mixes sitting in `Mixed` -- RICE-A-RONI and its unbranded equivalents, Spanish rice mix, seasoned/wild rice mixes (9 rows) -- moved to `Grain` (a flavored rice mix is fundamentally a rice/grain product, not a seasoning concentrate the way bouillon/gravy powder are, so `Grain` rather than joining the `Herbs` precedent). Deliberately left in `Mixed`, sharing this same search: Hamburger-Helper-style boxed pasta-mix "dinner kits" with meat/cheese flavoring built in ("Pasta mix, classic beef," boxed mac-and-cheese) -- a genuinely different, composite whole-meal product, not a side ingredient, same reasoning already applied to the mac-and-cheese kit excluded earlier -- and frozen prepared dishes (lasagna, Salisbury steak, pizza rolls, potstickers), which aren't a mix at all, just a whole meal not yet heated. This broader sweep also caught 3 more instant soup mixes the original Soup/Sauces pass missed (that search targeted "bouillon/broth/stock/consomme" specifically) -- "Soup, cream variety, instant dry mix," "Soup, vegetable & noodle, instant dry mix," "Soup, vegetable, instant dry mix" -- now joining the "Soup, broth style..." pair already in `Herbs`.

**The grouped-food-list "sticky header" feature had two real bugs, both in the SHARED `components/InlineSelectList.tsx`/`lib/foodNameGrouping.ts` machinery every category's food list already uses (Dairy, Meat, Veg, Fish, Bev, etc., not just one list) -- found and fixed 2026-08-02 after an extended, repeated back-and-forth on Bev's own Juice subcategory specifically, where they were easiest to reproduce (a small, mostly-ungrouped list with only 2-3 real groups).** Worth reading in full since both are now fixed everywhere at once, not per-category -- a future category hitting either failure mode should NOT need its own investigation.
1. **A real dev-time trap, not a logic bug**: `onViewableItemsChanged` (the callback driving the pinned group-header overlay) was wrapped in `useRef(fn).current`, which only ever evaluates `fn` on a component's FIRST render -- every edit after that was silently ignored by an ordinary Fast Refresh (ties directly into [[feedback_metro_staleness]]'s own standing "check Metro before re-guessing the code" advice, except this time the server genuinely was fresh and the STALENESS was inside React itself, not Metro). Multiple fixes to the pinned-header logic across this session likely never actually ran on-device because of this, independent of whether the logic itself was right. Fixed by moving the real implementation into a plain ref reassigned on every render (which Fast Refresh does pick up), behind a stable outer wrapper FlatList still gets one consistent identity from.
2. **A real logic bug once the fix above could finally be tested**: the pinned overlay only checked the TOPMOST visible row's own group -- but a small group (2-3 members) can be shorter than however many rows the box shows on screen at once, so the box could display a group's last member AND the next, genuinely unrelated rows simultaneously, with the pin staying up the whole time because the top row was still technically grouped. Fixed by checking every currently visible row, not just the top one -- the pin drops the instant any visible row shows the real boundary, since that boundary is already on screen. Also added a real end-of-group visual cap (bold colored bottom border + spacing gap on a group's own last member) so the transition reads clearly even without scrolling.

**A third, separate and more consequential fix, same day, driven directly by the person's own explicit instruction after Grape/Honeydew-melon confusion kept recurring even past both fixes above**: `buildFoodNameGroups()` used to sort a FORCED (source-based) group -- e.g. Juice's own "Japanese" cultivar group -- interleaved alphabetically by its header label, same as a real name-pattern group ("Cheese," "Beef"). For "Japanese" that meant its whole 18-entry block landed between "Honeydew melon juice" (H) and "Kiwi fruit juice" (K), close enough that Kiwi kept reading as part of it no matter how strongly the boundary itself was styled -- the adjacency was the actual bug, not the styling. **Forced groups now always sort to the very end of the whole list, never interleaved** -- a general rule, not a Juice-specific fix, so any future forced/source-based group (grouping by origin, brand, or anything else that isn't literally "a kind of food") gets this same end-of-list placement automatically, with no extra decision needed next time. Ordinary name-pattern groups (Cheese, Beef, Coconut, Grape) keep sorting alphabetically in place -- that distinction (kind-of-food groups interleave; everything-else groups go last) is now the standing design rule for this whole feature.

Also fixed the same day: the Bev/Juice allowlist (`BEV_JUICE_ALLOWED_NAMES`, `lib/db.ts`) had two leftover near-duplicates from an earlier trim pass -- a mature/young-immature Coconut Water pair (now just young/immature, the one virtually everyone means by "coconut water" as a drink) and a stray plain "Grapefruit juice" sitting alongside the intended Pink/White pair (removed, since the design intent was always exactly those two named varieties, not three).

**A real Legume miscategorization found and fixed 2026-08-02, prompted by a direct request to check whether any Veg "beans" belonged in Legume instead.** Checked every real "bean"-named row in `Veg` by hand (not a keyword scan): the existing convention there -- fresh/immature pods (green beans, snap beans, immature broad/lima/soy beans) and sprouts (mung/kidney/navy/pinto "mature seeds, sprouted," confirmed via each one's own real nutrient data: 76-93g water/100g, 20-78 kcal/100g, genuinely vegetable-like) -- is correct and stayed untouched. Exactly one row broke it: Germany_BLS's "Lima bean/butter bean, mature" -- its own name says "mature," and its raw nutrient profile (325 kcal, 11.5g water, 20.6g protein per 100g) is unambiguously a dried legume. Legume already carries this same real product from USDA/Japan_MEXT/Canada_CNF/Australia_AFCD -- Germany_BLS's own version had just never been moved over. Fixed via `CATEGORY_OVERRIDES` in `scripts/build_food_reference_db.py`.

**A second, related Veg subcategory bug found the same day, reported directly: "Cabbage isn't a processed or preserved food... Eggplant... potato... radish... sweet potato."** Investigated per base_name rather than assumed: 5 base_names (`Cabbage`, `Eggplant`, `Potato`, `Radish, hawaiian style`, `Sweet potato`) each had some rows correctly under `Whole / Fresh Vegetables` and OTHER rows -- a pickled/sauced/canned-in-syrup variant of the exact same food -- separately flagged `Processed & Preserved`, with no visible qualifier distinguishing them in the picker (this app's own established design already handles raw-vs-pickled through the `prep_method` picker, not a second subcategory). Two real root causes, both fixed:
1. Cabbage/Eggplant/Radish were triggered by the `"pickle"/"pickled"/"pickles"` keyword in `SUBCATEGORY_RULES["Veg"]` -- inconsistent with that exact rule's own stated philosophy two paragraphs up ("curing/canning for preservation alone doesn't make a food a manufactured product"), since pickling is the same class of brine preservation. Removed the keyword -- no data lost, pickled Cabbage/Eggplant/Radish/kimchi rows are still fully selectable, just correctly alongside their own raw/boiled siblings with "Pickled" as one more prep_method choice.
2. Potato ("hash brown... with butter sauce") and Sweet potato ("canned, syrup pack") were narrower, single-row "sauce"/"syrup" keyword matches -- those keywords are still correctly needed elsewhere (real applesauce/jam/syrup products with no whole-food identity of their own), so a new targeted override (`veg_processed_false_positive_subcategory()`, keyed on base_name, not a keyword removal) forces just these two specific base_names back to `Whole / Fresh Vegetables`.

Verified directly against the rebuilt database both times (all 5 named items now show exactly one subcategory; zero rows lost; `Tomato products` -- a genuinely different case, canned puree vs. flavored sauce-with-mushrooms/onions/cheese, no bare-vegetable identity collision -- deliberately left split, matching what the person actually asked about). Rebuilt `assets/data/foods_reference.db` and re-ran `add_food_aliases.py` for both fixes (`REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` now `20260802231342`). Pure Python/data changes, no TS files touched. **Not yet exercised on-device.**

**Explicit direction from the person, end of this session:** there's more of this same kind of category/subcategory audit work still to do across the reference database -- picking back up next session, not treated as finished.

**A real, interactive audit tool was built the next session, 2026-08-03, directly in response to "I need to see the whole lists at once."** Published as a Claude Artifact (an HTML page, not part of the app itself) covering all 16,000+ distinct `(category, subcategory, base_name)` combinations in the reference database -- searchable, with per-row Hide/Restore/Move actions, a real D1-D6 assessed-coverage percentage per row (most real foods land ~64-71% assessed out of ~24-25 sub-criteria; many are legitimately not-applicable rather than missing), a bulk "Hide all N shown here" action with a scoped single-level Undo, and an exportable decisions file/text summary meant to come back into a real session as instructions. Deliberately marks the three mechanisms that ALREADY hide something in the real app (Alcohol's two hidden subcategories, `ALCOHOL_HIDDEN_BASE_NAMES`, Bev/Juice's curated allowlist) as "already decided" rather than mixing them in with unreviewed rows -- explicitly does NOT fold in the separate, much bigger "USDA-only by default" behavior that silently limits sources for most other categories, since conflating the two would drown out the real signal. This tool is the primary vehicle for the category/subcategory audit work flagged above -- expect decisions to keep arriving as an exported file/pasted summary rather than one-off chat reports.

**Two real fixes landed from that tool's first real session, 2026-08-03:**
1. **Konjac fine powder was sitting in Vegetables despite being a cooking/thickening ingredient, not a food eaten on its own** -- reported directly ("kind of a cooking ingredient in powder form... nowhere to move things like Konjac to"). First moved to `SupplementPowder` (joining Psyllium/Baobab) -- corrected minutes later, same session, by direct pushback: "Konjac is not a supplement, it is a cooking ingredient like baking powder would be." Right call -- see the new `PantryStaples` category below, which is where it actually landed.
2. **All genuinely alcoholic cocktail entries were already fully hidden** -- checked directly rather than assumed, after being asked to hide "all cocktails" too: every real one (Margarita, Mojito, Bloody Mary, daiquiri, piña colada, whiskey sour, Kir royale, etc.) already falls under one of the three existing Alcohol-hide mechanisms. The remaining database matches for the word "cocktail" are a different sense entirely (canned fruit cocktail, shrimp cocktail, cocktail sausages, cocktail dressing) or non-alcoholic juice blends already excluded by the Juice allowlist -- nothing new needed there.

**The audit tool's category names were showing raw internal codes (`Bev`, `Veg`, etc.) instead of the real app's own friendly labels, reported directly.** Turned out to already be solved on the app side -- `components/FoodLookup.tsx`'s `CATEGORY_DISPLAY_LABELS` already maps `Bev` -> "Beverages", `Veg` -> "Vegetables", and everything else the app itself needed relabeled; the audit tool just wasn't using that mapping yet. Ported the same object into the tool (kept in sync by hand, since the tool is a static artifact, not live app code) rather than inventing a second set of names -- every category in the tool now reads exactly as it does in the real app.

**A real design decision worth remembering for the alcohol-tracking discussion that prompted a lot of this:** the person pushed back on the earlier "don't chase branded alcohol data" framing specifically for wine, backed by real ABV/residual-sugar chemistry (`calories = volume × ABV% × 0.789 × 7` for alcohol, `+ volume × RS(g/L)/1000 × 4` for sugar) -- correct, and it changes the answer, because wine's ABV/RS vary continuously across a real range in a way fixed-proof spirits don't. Agreed direction, refined by scope: build an ABV/RS calculator as an add-on (not a replacement for the real lab-measured varietal rows already in the database) for **wine, cider, and liqueurs/cordials** (all have genuinely variable added/residual sugar) and a simpler **ABV-only variant for plain spirits** (to cover an off-catalog proof, e.g. cask strength or overproof rum -- unflavored spirits are ~0 carb regardless of proof, already confirmed). Explicitly **not** for **beer** -- real brewing chemistry (dextrins/starches survive fermentation as real, unfermented carbs) means an RS-style formula would systematically undercount it, which is exactly why Beer & Cider is already deliberately hidden pending a real barcode-scan feature rather than approximated; extending the calculator there would quietly reintroduce the same wrong-number risk that decision was made to avoid. Cocktails stay out of scope too -- already correctly handled by building them from real ingredients in Beverage/Fermentation Builder rather than a lookup entry. **Not yet implemented** -- scope agreed, no code written yet.

**A new `PantryStaples` category added the same day, 2026-08-03, correcting the Konjac move above.** The person's own distinction was right and led to a real, larger fix: Konjac's real-world role is functional (thickening/gelling), not nutritional-supplement, so it doesn't belong with Psyllium/Baobab (both primarily consumed AS a supplement). Investigating just the one named comparison (baking powder) surfaced a genuine, pre-existing inconsistency worth fixing at the same time, not guessed at: baking powder, cream of tartar, and baker's/leavening yeast were each independently split across BOTH `Baked` (USDA/Canada_CNF's own "Leavening agent(s), ..." naming) and `Herbs` (Japan_MEXT/Germany_BLS/Australia_AFCD's own plainer naming) -- the same real ingredient, filed inconsistently depending on which of the 7 national sources measured it. Gelatine/apple pectin/agar-agar showed the identical pattern, scattered across `Herbs`, `Meat`, `Algae`, and `Mixed`. 33 rows total now live in `PantryStaples` ("Pantry Staples" in the app), every one checked against its own full `name` before moving -- an EATEN preparation that merely mentions one of these ingredients (agar JELLY, a gelatine-set fruit CREAM, lingonberry sugared WITH pectin) stayed exactly where it was; only the raw, add-a-small-amount ingredient form moved. Nutritional yeast and yeast extract spread deliberately stayed put too -- real foods eaten directly, not a leavening aid, unlike baker's yeast itself. A smaller, separate, already-confirmed fix rode along: "Arrowroot flour" (same real product as cornstarch) was split three ways (Grain/Baked/Mushroom) -- unified into `Grain`, consistent with cornstarch, rather than folded into `PantryStaples`.

`PantryStaples` added to `components/FoodLookup.tsx`'s `CATEGORY_DISPLAY_LABELS` and to all 9 Food-tab builders' own category allowlists in `constants/foodBuilderCategories.ts` -- specifically every builder that already included `Herbs` (all 9), since that's effectively where these ingredients were already reachable from; adding the new category everywhere `Herbs` already was preserves that same reachability rather than silently making baking powder/gelatine/Konjac unfindable in any builder that used them before. Rebuilt `assets/data/foods_reference.db`, re-ran `add_food_aliases.py` (`REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` now `20260803095309`). Verified directly against the rebuilt database: all 33 intended rows moved, the 3 spot-checked eaten preparations stayed put, arrowroot flour now reads consistently across all 3 sources. `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx eslint` clean on both touched TS files.

**A real, immediate regression from the new category, caught the same day by direct on-device report: "I have the new category, but only 7 rows of data in the app."** This is the SAME "silently defaults to USDA-only" bug already fixed twice before in this exact file (Alcohol/Algae/Brewing's zero-USDA-coverage case; Bev/Juice's "I 19 juices listed" case) -- `resolveEffectiveUsdaOnly()` only bypasses its own USDA-only default for categories explicitly special-cased, and `PantryStaples` has SOME real USDA coverage (7 of its 29 real ingredients, the "Leavening agent(s), ..." naming), so the function's default silently crushed the whole category down to just those 7, hiding the other 22 -- agar-agar, gelatine, apple pectin, Konjac, most baking-powder/baker's-yeast variants -- from every other of the 7 national sources. Confirmed via direct query before fixing (exactly 7 `PantryStaples` base_names have a USDA/Derived row, matching the reported count precisely). Fixed with one more bypass line in `resolveEffectiveUsdaOnly()` (`lib/db.ts`), same reasoning as the Bev/Juice fix: `searchReferenceFoodNames` already collapses same-base_name rows from different sources into one distinct selectable item regardless of this flag, so there's no "Spinach x7" duplication risk to guard against for a category explicitly built to consolidate cross-source ingredient variants. No database rebuild needed -- pure query-logic fix. `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx eslint` clean. **Confirmed on-device same day** -- Metro restarted cleanly (killed the whole process tree, relaunched via the real `npm start` path since this exact server had been running for hours across the whole session), person force-closed and reopened the app, confirmed all 29 Pantry Staples entries now show.

**Worth flagging for the next time a new top-level category gets added**: `resolveEffectiveUsdaOnly()`'s default (USDA-only unless a category has zero USDA coverage or is explicitly bypassed) is a real, recurring trap specifically for any NEW category that happens to have partial USDA coverage -- it silently passes for a category with zero USDA rows (already handled generically via `hasUsdaCoverage()`) and for one with full/near-full USDA coverage (nothing hidden), but a PARTIAL-coverage category needs its own explicit bypass line added by hand every time, or it silently ships broken like this one did. Worth checking `hasUsdaCoverage`-style partial coverage as a standing step whenever a new category is introduced, not just after a report comes in.

**That "worth checking" was acted on immediately, same day: a full audit across every category found one more real, serious case of the exact same bug -- Alcohol, not hypothetical, a genuine already-existing regression.** Asked directly "have you unhidden things in other categories as well?" after confirming Pantry Staples worked -- rather than just answering "no, only Pantry Staples," ran the actual check across all 20 categories (`COUNT(DISTINCT base_name)` total vs. USDA/Derived-only, per category/subcategory) to see whether anything else was silently affected. Alcohol's non-hidden subcategories (Beer & Cider and Cocktails & Mixed are unaffected either way, already hidden entirely) were showing a small fraction of their real content: Wine & Champagne 36 of 111, Spirits & Liqueurs 21 of 82, Other 1 of 24. Root cause, confirmed by re-deriving it rather than guessed: Alcohol genuinely had zero USDA rows when the original Alcohol/Algae/Brewing bypass was written (documented at the time), but a LATER fix that same day (`reclassify_bev_alcoholic_to_alcohol()`, moving 264 rows -- including every real USDA wine/beer/spirit row -- out of Bev's own "Alcoholic" subcategory into the proper Alcohol category) silently flipped `hasUsdaCoverage('Alcohol')` from false to true, re-triggering the USDA-only default for every Alcohol subcategory not already fully hidden -- invisible in any code diff, since it's a runtime recomputation triggered by unrelated data movement, not a code change anyone would have thought to re-check. Means the real, carefully-curated German/French/UK/Australian/Canadian wine and spirit diversity from that whole day's alcohol cleanup work has been silently ~80% hidden ever since, with nobody having noticed. Fixed the same way, safe for the same reason: Alcohol already has its own purpose-built duplicate-avoidance mechanism (`ALCOHOL_HIDDEN_BASE_NAMES`), so USDA-only on top of that was hiding real, non-redundant variety, not doing a job nothing else covers.

Confirmed by checking: only three categories have their own purpose-built curation mechanism justifying a bypass (`ALCOHOL_HIDDEN_BASE_NAMES`/`ALCOHOL_SUBCATEGORIES_PENDING_SCAN_FEATURE` for Alcohol, `BEV_JUICE_ALLOWED_NAMES` for Bev/Juice, plus the new PantryStaples case) -- Alcohol, Bev/Juice, and PantryStaples, now all three bypassed. Every other category (Meat, Veg, Fruit, Dairy, Fish, Grain, NutSeed, Legume, Fats, Sweets, Herbs, Baked, Mushroom, Sprouts) genuinely relies on USDA-only as its ONLY defense against near-duplicate "Spinach x7"-style noise for common everyday foods -- leaving those exactly as they are is the deliberate, documented design, not an oversight. `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx eslint` clean. Metro restarted cleanly a second time the same way. **Not yet confirmed on-device** -- worth checking Wine & Champagne/Spirits & Liqueurs specifically next time the app's open, since this one was never actually seen working correctly by anyone until now.

**The Reference Database Audit tool (the Claude Artifact, not the app itself) grew substantially across 2026-08-03/04, entirely in response to real, specific requests while going through the database together:**
- **D1-D6 assessed-coverage percentage** per row (most real foods land ~64-71%; many sub-criteria are legitimately not-applicable, not missing, so this is shown as plain data, not color-coded good/bad).
- **Bulk "Hide all N shown here"** per section, with a single-level, batch-tagged Undo that restores each row's PRIOR state (not just deletes) and skips any row changed individually since, so a later, more specific choice never gets silently clobbered.
- **Bulk rename / "AKA"**: check any set of rows (persists across searches/categories), a Find/Replace bar with a live before/after preview, applies as a `renameTo` field independent of any hide/move decision on the same row (a row can have both at once). Real underlying identity never touches this -- display only.
- **Drag-to-resize columns** and **click-to-sort any column** (Base name = food name, Source(s) = "the list they come from"), both page-wide settings (every table shares one set, not per-table) and both persisted the same best-effort-localStorage way decisions already were.
- **Actions column reordered** to sit right after Source(s) (was last) -- reported directly from a laptop without a wide screen, so the two most-needed-at-a-glance columns now sit together near the left edge.
- **Pending "Move" decisions get a distinct pale-green row background**, separate from the violet used for hide/rename/restore and the grey for already-decided-hidden rows -- explicitly requested so a move-in-progress reads differently at a glance from everything else.
All of this lives in the tool's own template/build pipeline (scratchpad, not the app repo) -- same URL each time, redeployed in place.

**A new `PastaNoodles` category added 2026-08-04, explicitly requested ("Please add a Pasta & Noodles Food category").** Investigated before building anything, the same discipline as PantryStaples: 410 rows matched a pasta/noodle keyword search across 9 categories. The bulk (156 distinct names, Grain) was handled with a keyword rule in `reclassify_category()` (scoped to `category_code == "Grain"`, word-boundary regex so it can't match "Grenouille" -- French for frog, contains "nouille" as a false mid-word substring, the exact class of bug this project has been burned by before) plus a small, hand-checked exclusion set (`PASTA_NOODLE_GRAIN_EXCLUSIONS`) for real composite dishes (spaghetti/ravioli canned IN sauce) and rice-forward flavored mixes (same "a flavored rice mix is fundamentally a rice product" call already made for RICE-A-RONI-style products). Everything outside Grain got individual, hand-verified `CATEGORY_OVERRIDES` entries after checking each one's own full name and `raw_category`: Konjac shirataki noodles (Veg -- the ready-to-eat noodle form specifically, distinct from Konjac's OTHER forms which stay Veg/PantryStaples per yesterday's own work), soy/mung-bean glass noodles under three different names across three sources (Legume, NutSeed), and homemade fresh pasta plus Japanese starch noodles that had landed in Mixed/Veg via two unrelated EXISTING rules (Germany_BLS's own "Soups, Stocks & Consommes" source label mapping straight to Mixed; Japan_MEXT's "Potatoes & Starches" mapping to Veg and then getting swept into Mixed by the existing "starch products -> Mixed" rule) -- neither rule was wrong for what it was built for, these specific rows just got carried along with it.

**A real self-caught mistake during this same pass, worth remembering**: the first attempt at moving 4 "boiled" sibling rows (the cooked-state counterpart to the Japanese starch-noodle rows above) used the WRONG starting category_code (`"Mixed"`, matching what they showed as in the pre-fix database) instead of their TRUE incoming code (`"Veg"`, the same as their own "uncooked" sibling) -- `CATEGORY_OVERRIDES` is checked against a row's real incoming category_code, not whatever an unrelated existing rule later turns it into, so the first version of this override silently did nothing. Caught by re-querying the rebuilt database rather than trusting the fix had worked (the same discipline this file has documented catching itself on before) -- total PastaNoodles count hadn't moved from before the "fix," which is what gave it away. Corrected by keying to "Veg" instead; re-verified the count moved as expected afterward.

Final: 255 rows / 204 distinct base_names in `PastaNoodles` ("Pasta & Noodles" in the app), cross-source from 6 of 7 national databases. Added to `CATEGORY_DISPLAY_LABELS` and to the 6 Food-tab builders that already had `Grain` in their own allowlist (Side, Salad, Fermentation, Snack, Baked Goods, Soup) -- Smoothie/Beverage/Sauces never had Grain either, so they correctly stay without pasta too. **Proactively checked for the by-now-familiar partial-USDA-coverage trap before shipping, not after a report**: confirmed 204 distinct products with only 31 USDA-covered, added the same `resolveEffectiveUsdaOnly()` bypass line as PantryStaples/Alcohol before calling this done, for the identical reason (searchReferenceFoodNames already collapses cross-source duplicates into one distinct entry regardless of this flag). Rebuilt `assets/data/foods_reference.db`, re-ran `add_food_aliases.py` twice (once before catching the boiled-sibling mistake, once after; `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` now `20260804102629`). `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx eslint` clean on all touched TS files. Metro restarted cleanly the same way as every other fix this session. **Not yet exercised on-device.**

**Flour and malt items moved out of `Grain` into the existing `PantryStaples` category, 2026-08-04, explicitly requested ("run the same level of filtering to move things from the grains group that are flours or ground version of the grain, such as malt... to Pantry Staples").** Same investigation discipline as PastaNoodles: 177 rows / 174 distinct base_names in `Grain` matched a flour/malt/farine keyword search, checked by hand rather than swept wholesale. The bulk (barley/buckwheat/corn/oat/rice/rye/sorghum/spelt/triticale/wheat flour, barley malt extract/flour) went through a new keyword rule in `reclassify_category()` (scoped to `category_code == "Grain"`, `\b(flours?|malts?|farine)\b`), with two guards: a `FLOUR_MALT_GRAIN_EXCLUSIONS` set for real finished foods that merely mention flour as one ingredient (4 pretzel snacks, 5 French "Pain..." breads, Incaparina drink-mix, a prepared rice-flour snack cake), and a `"malt-o-meal" not in lowered` substring guard for the 25 MALT-O-MEAL branded cereal rows -- a plain `\bmalt\b` word-boundary regex would still have matched the brand name, since a hyphen counts as a word boundary in Python regex, the same nuance already documented for "Grenouille" in the PastaNoodles work above but inverted (there a boundary was needed to AVOID a false match; here a boundary alone wasn't enough to avoid one).

**Two real gaps found only by re-querying the rebuilt database for leftover flour/malt matches, not assumed clean after writing the rule -- the same "count didn't move, go find out why" discipline PastaNoodles' own boiled-sibling mistake was caught with:**
1. **A non-breaking space defeated one exclusion-list entry.** "Pain, baguette ou boule, bis (à la farine T80 ou T110)" still leaked into PantryStaples after the first rebuild -- the source spreadsheet's own name uses U+00A0 (non-breaking space) between "T80" and "ou," not a plain space, so the hand-typed exclusion string with a regular space silently failed to match. Its regular-space-using sibling ("...bio...") matched fine, which is what made the mismatch visible on inspection. Fixed by re-typing the exclusion with the real character, confirmed via direct codepoint check before rebuilding again.
2. **A real, larger structural gap: root-vegetable flours (Arrowroot, Cassava, Potato, Sweet Potato, Yam) never reached the new rule at all.** An existing, older `if category_code == "Veg": ... return "Grain"` rule (built for a different, earlier "flour/starch -> Grain" pass) uses an early `return`, which exits `reclassify_category()` immediately -- so these rows arrive at their FINAL category as "Grain" without ever being re-examined by anything added later in the function, my new rule included. Fixed at the actual branch point rather than patched around: that Veg rule now splits "flour" matches to `PantryStaples` directly and leaves "starch" matches (cornstarch, potato starch, cassava starch, etc. -- confirmed via query these are a real, distinct 15-row set with zero overlap with the flour list) going to `Grain` as before. This also surfaced a third, smaller inconsistency: two pre-existing `CATEGORY_OVERRIDES` entries from an earlier session (`("Baked", "Flour, arrowroot")` and `("Mushroom", "Arrowroot flour")`) had deliberately consolidated those two source rows onto `Grain` to match cornstarch, explicitly flagged at the time as "out of scope" for moving further -- now genuinely in scope, since the whole point of this pass is separating flour from starch. Repointed both to `PantryStaples`, matching the USDA row of the identical product that was already landing there unprompted.

Verified directly against the twice-more-rebuilt database: all 25 MALT-O-MEAL rows and all 11 real exclusion-list rows (pretzels/Pain breads/Incaparina/rice flour cakes) confirmed still in `Grain`; all 6 root-vegetable flour rows (Arrowroot both sources, Cassava, Potato, Sweet Potato, Yam) confirmed now in `PantryStaples`; the 15-row plain-starch set confirmed untouched in `Grain`; `PastaNoodles` confirmed unaffected (still 255/204). `PantryStaples` grew from ~12 pre-existing rows to 167 distinct base_names. Two dangling `food_alias_data.py` entries ("all purpose flour"/"plain flour," both aliasing the same 3 wheat-flour base_names, now relocated) caught immediately by `add_food_aliases.py`'s own fail-loud-on-dangling-alias design -- repointed their category tag from `Grain` to `PantryStaples` to follow the rows. Confirmed `PantryStaples`'s existing `resolveEffectiveUsdaOnly()` bypass in `lib/db.ts` is unconditional for the whole category, so no new code was needed there -- spot-checked anyway (53 of 167 distinct products USDA-covered, confirming the bypass is still genuinely load-bearing). No `CATEGORY_DISPLAY_LABELS`/builder-allowlist changes needed since items moved into an already-integrated category rather than a new one. Pure Python/data change -- no TS files touched, nothing for `tsc`/`eslint` to check. Rebuilt `assets/data/foods_reference.db` three times total (the third-and-final rebuild folding in both the non-breaking-space and Veg-flour fixes together), re-ran `add_food_aliases.py` each time (`REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` now `20260804105002`), Metro restarted cleanly the same way as every other fix this session. **Not yet exercised on-device.**

**A new `SaucesCondiments` category added 2026-08-04, prompted by a "Mixed Dishes has things that aren't ready-made meals" report naming tomato sauce, Worcestershire sauce, capers, and dark chocolate directly.** An earlier same-day exchange had explored a Google-search-sourced "4 clean grocery-aisle categories" proposal (Sauces/Condiments/Dressings, Dips/Spreads/Pastes, Oils/Vinegars/Cooking Wines) for items scattered outside `Mixed` -- that framing undersold the real problem once actually investigated: `Mixed` is deliberately excluded from every one of the 9 Food-tab builders' own category allowlists (composite dishes aren't a raw ingredient anyone adds), so anything standalone sitting in `Mixed` wasn't just cluttering browsing, it was **unreachable in every builder, including Sauces Builder itself** -- confirmed directly (692 "sauce"-named items, 540 in `Mixed`; 155 dressings, 45 in `Mixed`; 96 vinegars, 66 in `Mixed`; 173 ketchup/mustard/mayo items, 85 in `Mixed`).

Traced the entire 2,552-item `Mixed` category back to its real `raw_category` source labels rather than guessing from keywords -- found it's not one problem but several stacked together: two giant, mostly-legitimate "prepared dish" buckets (Germany_BLS "Soups, Stocks & Consommes," 1,050 items; "Prepared/Cooked Dishes," 714 items) with real standalone sauces and bare raw-ingredient leakage mixed in; France_Ciqual's "Starters & Prepared Dishes" (325, mostly legitimate); **France_Ciqual's "Culinary Aids & Miscellaneous Ingredients" (203 items) -- not dishes at all**, a raw grab-bag of herbs/spices/salt, dried seaweed, leavening agents, and standalone condiments dumped wholesale into Mixed; **Australia_AFCD's own "Sauces, Dressings & Condiments" (38 items) -- already correctly pre-sorted by Australia's own data**, just generically remapped to Mixed like everything else; plus several much smaller buckets.

Did the two lowest-risk, highest-confidence fixes first (explicitly agreed as "do 1 and 2 now," with the larger sweep through the two giant dish buckets deferred to a later pass):
1. **Australia's own "Sauces, Dressings & Condiments" raw_category redirected straight to the new category** -- a one-line change in `RAW_CATEGORY_TO_CODE`, no per-row judgment needed since Australia's own curators already did the sorting (Worcestershire sauce, mayonnaise, mustard, vinegar, curry paste, hummus dip, olives, gherkins).
2. **France's "Culinary Aids & Miscellaneous Ingredients" (203 items) hand-classified into its real constituent parts**, not swept wholesale: 15 dried seaweed items → `Algae` (matching existing Nori/Kombu/Wakame entries); 55 raw herbs/spices/salt/dehydrated-stock items → `Herbs` (matching existing Spice/Salt/bouillon entries, including reusing the exact dehydrated-bouillon precedent from the 2026-08-02 Soup/Sauces Builder audit); 3 raw/roasted garlic forms → `Veg` (matching "Garlic Bulb"; only the dried powder form went to Herbs, matching "Spice, garlic, powder"); 9 baking/leavening/gelling agents plus vanilla extract → `PantryStaples`; 5 bran items → `Grain` (matching "Oat bran"/"Wheat bran" already there -- missed on the first pass, caught by re-querying what was still left in Mixed afterward rather than assuming the pass was complete); wheat germ → `Grain`; nutritional yeast → `Mushroom` (matching the existing, if unusual, "Yeast flakes/nutritional yeast" entry); silken tofu → `Legume` (matching every other Tofu variant); and 98 genuinely standalone sauces/dressings/dips/pastes/vinegar/pickled-garnish items → the new `SaucesCondiments` category (Miso, Moutarde, Tapenade, Tzatziki, Guacamole, Vinaigre, and roughly 60 bare "Sauce ..." items like Béarnaise/Hollandaise/Béchamel/Bourguignonne that don't carry a "with X" composite-dish clause). Left 13 items deliberately in `Mixed` as genuinely not fitting any clean home: a real prepared composite salad (Céleri rémoulade), 4 canned-fruit canning-syrup/juice byproduct rows, 4 low-calorie meal-replacement products (the same already-deferred protein/meal-replacement-powder question this file's Next Steps already flags), a sports drink, and 3 bee products (royal jelly, pollen).

Confirmed zero base_name collisions between this bucket and any other Mixed-arriving raw_category before writing the 195 `CATEGORY_OVERRIDES` entries, so none of them risk pulling in an unrelated composite dish from a different source that happens to share a name. `SaucesCondiments` added to `CATEGORY_DISPLAY_LABELS` ("Sauces & Condiments") and to all 9 Food-tab builders' own allowlists (same reasoning as `PantryStaples`: every builder could plausibly reach for ketchup, mustard, soy sauce, gravy, dressing, or vinegar). `Mixed` dropped from 2,552 to 2,324 distinct base_names. Confirmed zero USDA rows in the new category, so the existing generic `hasUsdaCoverage()` bypass in `lib/db.ts` already covers it with no new code needed (checked proactively, not after a report, per this project's own now-standard practice for any new category). Verified directly: Worcestershire sauce and capers (the person's own cited examples) both resolved correctly; dark chocolate's real miscategorization (a separate raw_category, Australia's "Snack/Muesli Bars") and the huge "standalone sauce hiding inside a mostly-legit dish bucket" sweep are both confirmed still open, deliberately deferred to a later pass rather than rushed. `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx eslint` clean on both touched TS files (one pre-existing, unrelated warning in `FoodLookup.tsx`, already documented, not caused by this change). Metro restarted cleanly, audit tool regenerated and republished to the same URL. **Not yet exercised on-device.**

**The deferred "big sauce sweep" through Mixed's two giant dish buckets, done 2026-08-04, same day, at explicit request ("tackle the big sauce sweep through the two dish buckets").** Queried all 630 remaining Mixed rows matching a sauce/dressing/gravy/vinegar/mayonnaise/mustard/ketchup keyword (spanning Germany_BLS's "Soups, Stocks & Consommes" and "Prepared/Cooked Dishes," France_Ciqual's "Starters & Prepared Dishes," Canada_CNF's "Mixed Dishes" and "Soups, Sauces and Gravies," USDA's "Meals, Entrees, and Side Dishes" and "Soups, Sauces, and Gravies," and Japan_MEXT's "Prepared Foods"), pre-filtered with a dish-vs-standalone heuristic (dish markers: "salad," "sandwich," "pizza," "lasagna," "ravioli," a cooking-verb-then-parenthetical pattern like "Boiled (with...)," or a protein/vegetable/fish name as the sentence's own subject followed by "with") down to 267 real candidates, then hand-read every one -- not trusting the heuristic alone, since it's exactly the kind of blind sweep that miscategorized composite dishes as standalone sauces the first time this database was built. Cross-checked the heuristic's own "dish" bucket too (363 items) for any wrongly-excluded standalone dressing/sauce -- found none; every one was a genuine "X salad with Y dressing"/sandwich-with-mayo pattern.

The dividing line, consistent with the earlier France_Ciqual pass: a name is a **standalone product** if the sauce/dressing itself is the grammatical subject ("Bearnaise sauce," "X sauce from white basic sauce," "Y dressing") -- the "from white/brown basic sauce" phrasing just describes the sauce's own roux base, not that it's serving a larger dish. A name is a **dish** (left in Mixed) if a food is the subject and the sauce is something added to it ("Beef Boiled (with caper sauce...)," "X salad with Y dressing," any sandwich/pizza/lasagna/ravioli with a sauce, "Potatoes with herb sauce..."). 143 of the 267 candidates were genuinely standalone and moved to `SaucesCondiments`; the rest (composite dishes, sandwiches, dressed salads, branded frozen meals like BANQUET/HUNGRY MAN Salisbury Steak With Gravy) stayed in Mixed on purpose. Confirmed zero base_name collisions with any other Mixed raw_category bucket before writing the 143 overrides.

**A real, second instance of the "keyed to the wrong starting category_code" mistake, caught the same way as the PastaNoodles one earlier this session**: two items ("Cheese sauce, prepared from recipe," "Homemade Cheese Sauce") were re-verified as still sitting in Mixed after the rebuild despite having their own `("Mixed", ...)` override entries written. Root cause: both rows' TRUE incoming category_code is `"Dairy"` (from "Dairy and Egg Products"), not `"Mixed"` -- a *pre-existing* override from 2026-08-02 (`("Dairy", "Cheese sauce, prepared from recipe"): "Mixed"`) already claims them first, so the new `("Mixed", ...)` entries were dead code that never got reached. Fixed by updating the original 2026-08-02 entries directly (Dairy → SaucesCondiments, superseding their old Dairy → Mixed target now that a better home exists) and removing the two now-dead duplicate entries rather than leaving unreachable code behind.

Deliberately **not** touched in this same pass: the family of flavored compound butters (Basil/Herb/Horseradish/Lemon/Mustard butter) sitting in this same Mixed bucket -- moving just "Mustard butter" because it happened to match this sweep's own keyword search would have split an otherwise-identical small family inconsistently; flagged for a future pass that reviews all of them together instead.

Final: `SaucesCondiments` grew from 137 to 280 distinct products; `Mixed` dropped from 2,324 to 2,181. **Proactively checked the by-now-standard partial-USDA-coverage trap before calling this done**: the category crossed from zero USDA coverage (safe on its own) to 3-of-280 once the two re-homed Dairy items landed -- added the `resolveEffectiveUsdaOnly()` bypass in `lib/db.ts` immediately, the same standing practice established after PantryStaples/Alcohol/PastaNoodles each hit this independently. `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx eslint` clean on the touched file. Metro restarted cleanly, audit tool regenerated and republished to the same URL. Bare raw-ingredient leakage inside these same two giant buckets (plain "Carrot," "Beetroot," "Chanterelles," "Halibut fillet," "Pork chop" with no dish descriptor) is a separate, still-open miscategorization, not sauce-related -- confirmed still present, not chased in this pass. **Not yet exercised on-device.**

**The raw-ingredient leakage inside Mixed's two giant dish buckets cleaned up 2026-08-04, same day, at explicit request ("clean up the raw-ingredient leakage too").** Same discipline as the sauce sweep: queried Mixed for bare protein/fish/vegetable/mushroom headwords (Beef, Pork, Lamb, Chicken, Halibut, Carrot, Chanterelles, etc.) with no dish-composition signal, narrowing 165 keyword hits down to 85 genuinely bare items by hand. The dividing line: a name moves out if it's a plain cut/fillet/vegetable with at most one generic cooking verb (boiled, fried, floured, poached, braised) or an explicit "without sauce"/"tossed in butter" -- nothing that amounts to a real added recipe. Stayed in Mixed on purpose: named regional styles ("Berlin style," "Polish style," "Zurich style," "Baden style," "American style"), "marinated" (a real flavoring, not just doneness), any real sauce/cream addition, a crust/batter/dough coating, ragout/fricassee/skewer/roulade/cordon bleu (inherently composite formats), "Olive" (this dataset's own term for a rolled/stuffed meat preparation, not the fruit), aspic, gratinated dishes, and compotes (a separate, not-today question). Existing stock/consomme entries stayed put too, matching the already-established "real soups/stocks stay in Mixed" precedent. Moved 37 items to `Meat`, 28 to `Fish`, 14 to `Veg`, 2 to `Mushroom` (Chanterelles, Porcini mushrooms), 1 each to `Fruit` (Pear), `Legume` (Kidney beans, matching the existing "mature bean → Legume" precedent from an earlier session), and `Grain` (Rice). Confirmed zero base_name collisions with any other Mixed raw_category bucket before writing the 85 overrides. `Mixed` dropped from 2,199 to 2,096 distinct base_names.

**A real, pre-existing bug found as a direct side effect of checking whether the newly-moved items would even be visible -- not caused by today's work, but caught because of it.** `Mushroom` has exactly 1 real USDA row ("Fungi, Cloud ears") out of 109 genuinely distinct species already in the database (Chanterelle, Morel, Shiitake, Maitake, Nameko, Matsutake, oyster/beech/king oyster/black poplar mushrooms, black truffle...) -- meaning the default USDA-only browsing behavior has apparently been silently collapsing this whole category down to that one row for as long as the category has existed, unrelated to and predating every other fix in this recurring bug class this session (PantryStaples, Alcohol, PastaNoodles, SaucesCondiments). Fixed the same way: added a `resolveEffectiveUsdaOnly()` bypass for `category === 'Mushroom'` in `lib/db.ts`, safe for the same reason as every prior bypass (real cross-source species variety, not "Spinach x7"-style near-duplication). Worth remembering: this bug class isn't only a risk for brand-new categories -- it's worth spot-checking any EXISTING category with a surprisingly small or single-source USDA footprint too, not just ones created this session.

Also noticed, deliberately **not** fixed today (out of scope for a raw-ingredient-leakage pass, flagged for later): `Mushroom` itself contains several unrelated root-vegetable and pasta items that don't belong there at all -- "Batata/sweet potato," "Jerusalem artichoke/topinambur," "Manioc/cassava," and three "Gnocchi" rows (a potato dumpling, not remotely mushroom-related). A real, separate miscategorization worth a future look, not chased now to avoid scope creep on top of an already-large pass. A minor, harmless pre-existing near-duplicate was also noticed and left alone: "Beef stock" (the standalone product, correctly left in Mixed by this pass) and "Beef stock canned" (already correctly in Meat) are two genuinely different real products from the same source that happen to share a stripped base_name -- cosmetic, not a bug this pass caused or needs to fix.

`npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx eslint` clean on the touched `lib/db.ts`. Rebuilt `assets/data/foods_reference.db`, re-ran `add_food_aliases.py` (`REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` now `20260804121743`), Metro restarted cleanly, audit tool regenerated and republished to the same URL. **Not yet exercised on-device.**

**The `Mushroom` category's own leakage cleaned up 2026-08-04, same day, direct follow-up ("check the Mushroom category for the rest of that leakage too") to two items flagged but not fixed during the raw-ingredient-leakage pass.** Investigating turned up a much bigger root cause than those two items suggested: Germany_BLS's own `"Starches & Potato Products"` raw_category has *always* defaulted to `"Mushroom"` via `RAW_CATEGORY_TO_CODE` -- a known, disclosed imprecision this file's own header comment already named ("spans Mushroom/Veg/Grain/Mixed") but had never been individually resolved. Every potato/root-vegetable/starch product from that one raw source label had been landing in Mushroom purely by default, alongside the real mushroom species that happen to make up most of it -- confirmed by pulling the full 197-row bucket and checking every row's real `raw_category`, not just the base_name.

Hand-classified all 33 non-mushroom rows individually (destinations genuinely varied, so a keyword rule wasn't appropriate): plain root vegetables/potatoes with minimal prep (peeled/boiled/braised/steamed, no real recipe -- the identical "raw-ingredient leakage" test as the Mixed pass earlier the same day) → `Veg` (Batata/sweet potato, Jerusalem artichoke, Manioc/cassava, Yam, 6 "Potatoe peeled/unpeeled" variants); plain starches → `Grain` (Potato starch, Tapioca/cassava starch, matching "Potato Starch"/"Starch, cassava starch" already there from the earlier flour/malt work); dry instant mashed-potato powder (not eaten alone until reconstituted) → `PantryStaples`, the same test already established for flour; real prepared potato dishes (croquettes, 4 dumpling variants, 3 gnocchi variants, pancakes, stuffed cream-cheese pockets, 6 rösti variants, potato sticks) and both cream-of-mushroom soup products → `Mixed`, matching the identical dish/soup precedent already established there. Every genuine mushroom species (Chanterelle, Morel, Shiitake, Maitake, Nameko, Matsutake, Porcini, black truffle, boletus/bolete varieties, oyster/beech/king oyster/black poplar/phoenix/abalone mushrooms) stayed untouched, along with the three already-correct non-mushroom precedent items (Yeast flakes/nutritional yeast, Fungi Cloud ears, Jew's ear). Confirmed zero base_name collisions with any other Mushroom raw_category bucket before writing the 33 overrides.

`Mushroom` dropped from 109 to 76 distinct base_names -- a much cleaner, genuinely mushroom-only category now. Re-confirmed the `resolveEffectiveUsdaOnly()` bypass added for `Mushroom` earlier the same day is still correctly needed post-cleanup (1 of 76 USDA-covered). A cosmetic, unrelated data quirk noticed along the way and deliberately not fixed (a naming bug, not a categorization one, out of scope for this pass): 4 Japan_MEXT "winter mushroom" rows have their own footnote text (`［*Syn. Enokitake, Enoki］`) embedded as a literal newline inside the base_name itself, splitting the display across two lines -- worth a look someday, not chased now. No TS files touched this pass (pure Python/data change). Rebuilt `assets/data/foods_reference.db`, re-ran `add_food_aliases.py` (`REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` now `20260804122432`), Metro restarted cleanly, audit tool regenerated and republished to the same URL. **Not yet exercised on-device.**

**A new `CommercialPremade` category added 2026-08-04, same day, directly executing the "home cooking over commercial/branded products" decision just recorded in this file's core-purpose section.** Explicit request: "The Mixed Dishes should not have anything in it that is a pre made dish... move them to the area we're using for things that don't meet the criteria for this app" -- no such area existed yet, so this created it. Display label "Commercial / Pre-Made," deliberately excluded from every Food-tab builder's own allowlist, same as `Mixed` itself always was.

Scoped in two tiers, both requiring real hand review rather than a blind keyword sweep, per explicit direction to check the second tier against real examples before trusting it:
