1. **229 unambiguous items**: explicit prepackaged/canned/instant-mix/branded signals -- French "préemballé(e)" (prepackaged) and "appertisé" (canned/tinned), "déshydratée reconstituée" (just-add-water mix), ALLCAPS brand names (BANQUET, HUNGRY MAN, HOT POCKETS, LEAN POCKETS), box/dry mix wording.
2. **A second, broader tier surfaced 51 more candidates matching product-format words** (sandwich, burger, nugget, egg roll, taquito, pocket, dinner, entree, refrigerated, "not prepared") -- but only 11 of those 51 actually moved after reading every one by hand. The other 40 are genuine homemade-style dish descriptions and stayed in Mixed on purpose: several "Sandwich, ham/egg salad/salmon salad/tuna salad, homemade" rows literally say "homemade" (the opposite signal from what this category is for); Club sandwiches, "Sandwich baguette, camembert, beurre"-style entries, and plain "Chicken burger"/"Quinoa burger patty" carry no préemballé/commercial marker at all, unlike their siblings that do. The 11 that did move all had a real product-state signal: "restaurant prepared," "refrigerated, heated," "frozen, heated in oven," or "as purchased" (fresh-refrigerated tortellini/pasta).

Confirmed 4 apparent "collisions" during validation (the same base_name, e.g. "Macaroni and cheese, box mix with cheese sauce," appearing under both USDA and Canada_CNF) were not a bug -- both rows are genuinely the same real commercial product independently measured by two sources, so both correctly move. `Mixed` dropped from 2,134 to 1,876 distinct base_names; `CommercialPremade` now holds 238. Proactively added the now-standard `resolveEffectiveUsdaOnly()` bypass in `lib/db.ts` (13 of 238 USDA-covered). Ported the new category into the Reference Database Audit tool's own hand-copied `CATEGORY_DISPLAY_LABELS` (also caught and fixed a real gap there: `SaucesCondiments` had been missing from that same list since it was created, showing as a raw code instead of "Sauces & Condiments" in the tool this whole time).

**The much larger remaining question -- the ~1,850 Germany_BLS "Prepared/Cooked Dishes"/"Soups, Stocks & Consommes" entries with no explicit commercial OR homemade marker either way -- is deliberately NOT resolved by keyword guessing.** There isn't reliable signal to classify these automatically (BLS itself is a standardized food-composition survey database, not tagged by home-vs-commercial origin), so per direct request this is left for the person to review visually in the Reference Database Audit tool instead -- the tool's own Move-target dropdown is fully data-driven (`Object.keys(DATA)`), so `CommercialPremade` already appears there as a real destination for whatever gets flagged next. `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx eslint` clean on both touched TS files (`lib/db.ts`, `components/FoodLookup.tsx`; same pre-existing unrelated warning already documented). Rebuilt `assets/data/foods_reference.db`, re-ran `add_food_aliases.py` (`REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` now `20260804125837`), Metro restarted cleanly, audit tool regenerated and republished to the same URL. **Not yet exercised on-device.**

**Bulk move added to the Reference Database Audit tool, 2026-08-04, same day, explicitly requested ("there is a way to bulk rename, but there also needs to be a way to bulk move food items from one group to another group").** Reused the existing bulk-rename row-checkbox selection (`selectedForRename`, renamed to `selectedRows` since it now drives two actions) rather than building a second, separate selection mechanism -- checking a set of rows and then deciding what to do with them is the same underlying action whether the outcome is a rename or a move. The bulk-actions bar (formerly rename-only) now has two labeled sections sharing one selection: Rename (unchanged) and a new Move section -- a category `<select>` (populated from real live categories) and a subcategory text input with a datalist that refreshes to that category's own real subcategories on change, mirroring the single-row move form's own UX exactly. "Apply move" writes an `action: 'move'` decision to every selected row in one batch (preserving each row's own existing `renameTo` if it had one, same "independent of any hide/move decision" design the tool already had), tracked under the same `lastBulkAction`/undo mechanism bulk hide and bulk rename already use -- extended the undo button's label logic to say "Undo last bulk move" rather than falling back to the generic "hide" label. Confirmed the existing decision-display code (the pale-green pending-move row highlight, the "&rarr; MOVE to X" tag, the decisions panel, the exported summary text) needed zero changes to handle bulk-created move decisions -- all of it was already driven generically by `action === 'move'` regardless of how that decision got set. Also caught and fixed two stale references to the old function/variable names left over from the rename during this same edit (`renameFindInput`/`renameReplaceInput`'s own `input` listeners were still calling the pre-rename function name). Verified via `node --check` on the extracted script (both the template alone and the final merged output) and cross-checked every new element ID between the HTML and the JS by hand. Republished to the same URL. **Not yet exercised by hand in the browser** -- worth a real click-through (select a few rows, apply a bulk move, confirm it shows correctly in the decisions panel, undo it) before trusting it fully, same caveat as every other browser-only piece of work this session.

**Builder consolidation discussion and the eleventh builder, 2026-08-04, same day.** Prompted by a Google-search-sourced proposal to consolidate the Food tab's ten builders down to four ("Layers" for sandwiches/wraps/burgers/tacos, "Tossed/Bowl" absorbing Salad, "Liquid/Blend" merging Soup+Smoothie+Beverage, "Skillet/Mix" absorbing Side, plus removing Fermentation and Sauces entirely). Evaluated on the merits rather than accepted wholesale: agreed a genuine sandwich/wrap/burger/taco builder was a real, missing gap, and that expanding Salad's own scope/name to cover grain bowls/poke/burrito bowls was low-cost and well-justified (its own category allowlist already covered all of that ingredient-wise). Disagreed with merging Soup/Smoothie/Beverage -- each has real, deliberately-built divergent behavior (Smoothie's goitrogenic warning, Beverage's alcohol/coffee/juice advisories, Soup's own Cook Prep addition) and its own real database table, a past deliberate decision ("a third kind of record... not one forced-generic table") the proposal didn't account for; the actual code duplication across the nine sub-builders is real but the right fix is refactoring the shared engine, not merging the user-facing concepts. Disagreed with folding Side into a new concept -- it's the original template every other builder was copy-adapted from, not "a smaller bowl." Pushed back directly on removing Sauces and Fermentation: both run against this session's own "home cooking over commercial/branded products" decision (recorded in the core-purpose section above) -- Sauces Builder exists so someone builds their own hollandaise from real ingredients rather than picking a generic reference row, and Fermentation ties directly to the app's own named gut-health core purpose plus the still-flagged future bacterial-strain-tracking research track.

**Builder naming went plural, 2026-08-04, same day, explicitly decided ("I think we should choose if they should all be singular or all plural... make the rest plural that also should be").** Grounded in the app's own existing precedent -- `CATEGORY_DISPLAY_LABELS` in `components/FoodLookup.tsx` already used plural food-category names throughout (Vegetables, Fruits, Beverages, Mixed Dishes) -- rather than an outside convention. Renamed: Side -> Sides, Salad -> Salads & Bowls (also the real scope expansion agreed above), Smoothie -> Smoothies, Beverage -> Beverages, Snack -> Snacks, Soup -> Soups. Two deliberate exceptions with real reasons, not leftovers: Meal stays singular (it isn't a food category -- it's the assembly action that combines the other builders' saved output); Fermentation stays singular (unlike Sauces/Snacks/Soups/Sides/Beverages it has no natural plural food-category form -- "Fermentations" reads clinical, not like a menu label). Baked Goods and Sauces were already correctly plural. Scoped the actual rename carefully after checking: only category-LEVEL naming changed (`FOOD_LENSES` grid labels, `FOOD_LENS_COPY` prose and its cross-references between builders, `FOOD_LENS_FULL_NAMES` corner-box names, the "My Foods" Saved/Favorite section labels) -- every builder's own internal per-item UI text ("Save & Finish Side," "Name the dish") correctly stays singular throughout, since you're building ONE side/salad/soup at a time regardless of what the category is called; this matches the precedent Sauces Builder already set (plural display name, singular internal identifiers throughout) and was confirmed directly against `SaucesBuilder.tsx` before assuming it, not guessed.

**The new "Handhelds" builder, 2026-08-04, same day -- the eleventh, and the first genuinely new builder since Meal.** Named after real debate, not the proposal's own working name: "Layers" was rejected because every other builder is named after a recognizable food category (Soup, Snack, Sauces), not a structural description; "Handhelds" is a real, already-used food-industry/menu term that genuinely covers sandwich/wrap/burger/taco/burrito as one thing, matching that same naming convention. Built by directly adapting `SaucesBuilder.tsx` (copied, then a case-sensitive `Sauce`->`Handheld`/`sauce`->`handheld` substring rename applied programmatically rather than by hand, given the file's ~2,000-line size) -- picked as the base over `SideBuilder.tsx` specifically because it already demonstrates the "plural display name, singular internal identifiers" pattern Handhelds also needs (same plural-lens-key situation). The mechanical rename left real artifacts that needed hand fixing, not just accepted: a stray "mayo-based handhelds"/"pan handhelds, gravies, glazes" (nonsense after blind substitution), 'Reduced' (Sauces' own real addition, replaced with 'Toasted' -- the single most common thing done to a Handhelds outer casing, which the inherited Side Builder base list had no option for), and the alcohol-advisory feature (real for Sauces, since its own category allowlist includes Alcohol; removed entirely for Handhelds, whose allowlist doesn't -- import, render block, and both now-dead styles all removed cleanly rather than left as dead code).

Deliberately does NOT add a new "layer role" tag per ingredient (outer casing/protein/topping/condiment) -- every other builder's flat, unordered ingredient list already covers this fine in practice (a person naturally adds bread, then protein, then toppings, then condiment in that order), and inventing a new per-ingredient UI field would be new interaction design no other builder has, not proven pattern reuse, and wasn't asked for. `HANDHELDS_BUILDER_CATEGORIES` mirrors `SIDE_BUILDER_CATEGORIES` exactly (the broadest existing allowlist) rather than a narrower hand-picked list -- a sandwich/wrap/burger/taco can genuinely draw on almost any real food category as an ingredient, the same "deliberately generous" reasoning already established for every other builder.

New `handhelds`/`handheld_ingredients` tables in `lib/db.ts`, mirroring `sauces`/`sauce_ingredients` exactly (`saveHandheld`/`updateHandheld`/`deleteHandheld`/`listHandhelds`/`getHandheld`/`getHandheldIngredients`/`getHandheldNutrientBreakdown`/`getHandheldSixDimensionsBreakdown`), extracted and renamed the same programmatic way as the component. `MealComponentType` gained a `'handheld'` member with dispatch cases added to `getComponentDetail`/`getComponentIngredients`/`listMealComponentOptions` (the raw-goitrogenic-flags check needed no new case -- it already routes through the same shared `getComponentIngredients` dispatcher); `listFavorites`'s itemType union widened with `'handheld'`. Wired into `app/(tabs)/food.tsx` (lens entry, `editHandheldId` deep-link param and its `useFocusEffect` branch, My Foods counts/loading/Saved-Favorite entries, render switch), `app/food-items.tsx` (load/edit-route/supportsEdit/supportsDelete/deleteItem), and `app/food-item-detail.tsx` (`mealNounFor`, `loadSide`) -- the same shape as every other builder's own wiring.

`npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint` clean across every touched/new file (`app/(tabs)/food.tsx`, `app/food-items.tsx`, `app/food-item-detail.tsx`, `components/HandheldsBuilder.tsx`, `lib/db.ts`, `constants/foodBuilderCategories.ts`) -- zero errors, zero warnings, a fully clean pass this time (no pre-existing unrelated warnings touched by this change). **Not yet exercised on-device** -- same caveat as every other builder before it, and the first new one built without any prior on-device confirmation of the underlying pattern still working correctly today.

**The first real bulk import from the Reference Database Audit tool, 2026-08-04, same day.** After a long, thorough independent review session using the audit tool, the person exported 10,165 decisions (8,058 hide, 2,002 move, 105 rename-only, plus 31 rows combining a move with a rename) and asked to have them applied as-is -- explicitly not re-litigated one by one, which at this scale would also defeat the point of building a review tool in the first place.

**A real architecture decision, not just a big data patch: added a genuine `hidden` column to the `foods` table itself, rather than extending the existing `ALCOHOL_HIDDEN_BASE_NAMES`-style hand-typed TS Set pattern in `lib/db.ts` to 8,000+ entries.** That pattern was fine at the 10s-100s scale it was built for (Alcohol's own hidden beer/cocktail list, Bev/Juice's allowlist) -- it was never meant to carry a bulk curation pass two orders of magnitude larger. `buildScopeClause()` (the one shared query-building function `searchReferenceFoodNames`/`getPreparationMethods`/`resolveFoodChoice`/alias-resolution all already route through) now filters `hidden = 0` universally, one change covering every caller; `getReferenceSubcategories()` and `hasUsdaCoverage()` got the same filter directly, since neither goes through that shared function and both would otherwise have kept offering an already-fully-hidden subcategory as pickable or miscounting USDA coverage from a hidden row.

**Applied as a new `apply_audit_decisions()` pass in `scripts/build_food_reference_db.py`, run as the LAST step over the fully-resolved `foods_rows` list, right before the INSERT -- deliberately NOT folded into `CATEGORY_OVERRIDES`/`reclassify_category`.** Those key off a row's raw, pre-resolution incoming `category_code`, a repeated real source of bugs this same session whenever that distinction was missed (the PastaNoodles "boiled sibling" mistake, the Cheese Sauce dead-override mistake). The audit tool's own decision keys are built from the FINAL, fully-resolved `(category, subcategory, base_name)` a person actually saw on screen -- matching against that same final state, after every other rule has already run, is the only way to guarantee these land on the rows they were actually made against. "hide" decisions set the new `hidden` column; "move" decisions reassign category (and subcategory, cleared to NULL when the decision doesn't specify a new one, since an old category's own subcategory label usually stops meaning anything once the row has moved); "renameTo" replaces `base_name` only, matching the exact precedent already established for `SPIRIT_CLEAN_RENAMES`/`JUICE_CLEAN_RENAMES` -- `name` (the real source-provided name) is never touched.

**105 of 10,165 decisions didn't match on the first pass -- investigated rather than silently dropped, and every single one turned out to be the exact same benign cause: a food this same session had already independently reorganized earlier the same day (flour → PantryStaples, noodles/pasta → PastaNoodles) before the person's own audit review, so the decision's own recorded starting category was already stale by the time this import ran.** Confirmed directly, not assumed: every one of the 17 unmatched "move" decisions was already sitting exactly at its own requested target category (a genuine no-op). Added a base_name-only fallback match for "hide"/rename-only decisions specifically (excluding "move," whose whole point is reassigning FROM a known starting category -- guessing that defeats the purpose), gated on the base_name being unambiguous in the current data (maps to exactly one real category/subcategory pair) rather than guessed at when genuinely ambiguous. That fallback resolved 88 of the remaining 105; the final 17 (all "move," all already no-ops as confirmed above) needed no further action.

Result: 10,283 rows hidden, 2,092 moved, 198 renamed (counts exceed the 10,165 decision count since a single decision with `rowCount > 1` matches multiple real source rows). `Mixed` dropped from ~2,134 to 3 distinct base_names -- the person's own review went far beyond what this session's earlier, narrower Mixed-cleanup passes covered. Re-ran `add_food_aliases.py` -- zero dangling aliases despite the scale of renames/moves, a real relief given how much data moved. Spot-checked several renames/moves directly against the rebuilt database (old names gone, new names present with correct category/subcategory/hidden state) rather than trusting the aggregate counts alone. `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx eslint` clean on `lib/db.ts`. Regenerated the audit tool's own data pipeline to read the new `hidden` column directly (now the dominant hide mechanism, 10,283 rows vs. the ~300 the hand-ported Alcohol/Juice logic still covers) rather than relying solely on the old hand-ported JS logic; both mechanisms are checked and combined. Metro restarted cleanly, audit tool regenerated and republished to the same URL for round 2. **Not yet exercised on-device** -- and worth noting for whoever opens the freshly republished audit tool next: their browser's own `localStorage` still has all 10,165 decisions marked "pending" from the export -- since those are now baked into the real data, clicking "Clear all pending decisions" once round 2 loads avoids seeing stale pending tags stacked on top of the already-applied, now-correctly-reflected state.

**A real performance bug in the Reference Database Audit tool itself, found and fixed 2026-08-04, same day, reported directly ("There is no Clear all pending decisions to click").** Root cause, traced rather than guessed: `renderDecisionsPanel()` rebuilt one full HTML card per pending decision and ran on EVERY call to the tool's own main `render()` function -- every search keystroke, every checkbox toggle, every Hide/Move click -- not just while the Decisions side panel was actually open. That's harmless at the tens-to-low-hundreds scale the tool was built and tested against all along, but with the person's own browser still holding all 10,165 decisions from the bulk export in `localStorage` (plus more added since), every single interaction with the tool was silently rebuilding a 10,000+ node HTML blob -- almost certainly what made the page unresponsive before the button could ever be reached, not a genuinely missing button.

Two real fixes, not a workaround: (1) `renderDecisionsPanel()` now early-returns entirely while the panel isn't open -- nothing in it needs to be live off-screen; the toggle button's own click handler now explicitly triggers a real render the moment the panel opens, rather than waiting on whatever the next unrelated `render()` call happens to be. (2) Even while open, the rendered card list is now capped at 200 (`DECISIONS_PANEL_CARD_LIMIT`), with a plain note when more exist -- Export and Clear All still act on the complete set regardless of how many cards are actually shown. The plain-text summary (used for both the export file and the read-only textarea) is deliberately NOT capped -- it has to stay complete and correct for a real bulk export, and building a text string for 10,000 entries is cheap enough that the same DOM-node limit isn't needed there.

Caught and fixed two copy-paste typos from the earlier same-day bulk-move-feature edit while writing these same comments (`#` used instead of `//` for a line comment, in a page that has no Python involved at all) via `node --check` before publishing, not after. Republished to the same URL. **Not yet exercised on-device** (browser-only, same as always) -- worth a real click-through confirming the Decisions panel opens promptly and "Clear all pending decisions" works before trusting it fully at this scale.

**A redundant, always-visible "Clear all pending decisions" button added to the audit tool's own header, same day, after the panel-based fix above didn't resolve the report ("As I told you before, there is no 'Clear all pending decisions' button for me to press").** Rather than keep guessing at why the side panel's own copy might not be reachable (stale tab, a real layout issue specific to their browser/viewport, or something else not yet diagnosed), added a second, completely independent path to the same action directly in the header, next to the pending-decisions count itself -- it doesn't depend on the side panel opening, scrolling, or rendering correctly at all. Both buttons now share one `clearAllPendingDecisions()` function, which also gained a real `window.confirm()` safety check (naming the exact pending count) that the original panel button never had -- worth adding now that a second, more prominent/easier-to-reach copy of a genuinely destructive-sounding action exists. Directly answered a real, important question raised alongside this: clearing pending decisions in the tool has zero connection to the real database -- the 10,165 decisions applied earlier the same day were written straight to the SQLite file via the Python build script and are already permanent; "Clear all" only empties the tool's own local to-do list so old entries stop cluttering the count (and, before the earlier same-day performance fix, freezing the page). Republished to the same URL. **Not yet confirmed working** -- this is the second attempted fix for the same underlying complaint without a screenshot or more precise description of what's actually being seen, so if this doesn't resolve it either, the next step should be asking for exactly that rather than a third guess.

**A second, smaller decisions re-import, 2026-08-04, same day, prompted by a real risk raised directly: "if I press [Clear all]... I won't be selecting to clear those decisions meaning they will just all become unchecked, right?"** Correctly identified a real gap in the earlier "safe to clear" reassurance -- the tool's own `decisions` object doesn't distinguish already-applied entries from new ones made since, so a blind "Clear all" at that point would have destroyed real, not-yet-applied work alongside the already-safe-to-discard old entries. Resolved the right way: asked for a fresh export (captures old + new together) rather than trying to diff anything, since re-applying the same already-applied decisions is inherently a no-op (setting `hidden = 1` on an already-hidden row, or moving a row to the category it's already in, changes nothing). Diffed the new 10,215-decision export against the previously-applied 10,165 first, confirmed 50 added and zero removed/changed before touching anything -- mostly Algae renames/hides (real Japanese seaweed product names: "Iwa-nori," "Ego-nori," "Sea lettuce," etc.). Same `apply_audit_decisions()` pipeline, same result shape: 10,305 hidden, 2,092 moved, 229 renamed, the identical 17 already-confirmed-no-op "move" decisions still unmatched (same flour items, already at PantryStaples). Re-ran `add_food_aliases.py` (zero dangling), Metro restarted cleanly, audit tool regenerated and republished to the same URL for round 3. **Now genuinely safe to clear the tool's pending list** -- everything currently in it has been applied for real as of this pass.

**A second attempt at the "no Clear all button" report, 2026-08-04, same day.** The panel-only performance fix from earlier the same day didn't resolve it, so rather than guess a third time blind, added a completely independent, always-visible "Clear all pending decisions" button directly in the header next to the pending count -- doesn't depend on the side panel opening, scrolling, or rendering at all. Both the header button and the original panel button now share one `clearAllPendingDecisions()` function, which also gained a real `window.confirm()` safety step (naming the exact pending count) it never had before, given how much easier the new header button is to hit by accident. **Still not confirmed working** -- if this doesn't resolve it either, the right next step is asking for a screenshot or an exact description of what's on screen, not a fourth guess.

**The actual root cause of the whole "Clear all pending decisions" saga found and fixed, 2026-08-04, same day, reported directly: "when I click the clear all pending decisions button, nothing happened."** Not a missing button, not a rendering/layout problem -- the confirmation step itself, added earlier the same day as a safety measure once the button became easy to reach from the header. `window.confirm()` is a native browser dialog, and Artifacts render inside a sandboxed iframe that very likely blocks `confirm()`/`alert()`/`prompt()` entirely unless the sandbox explicitly grants `allow-modals`. A blocked `confirm()` call resolves falsy (or does nothing), which the code's own `if (!ok) return;` treats identically to someone clicking Cancel -- so the button silently did nothing, exactly matching the report, on every attempt including the ones after the earlier panel-performance and header-button fixes (neither of which was actually the problem by that point).

Replaced with a plain in-page "click again to confirm" pattern -- ordinary DOM/state (an `armed` flag, a 4-second auto-disarm timeout, a pulsing red visual state on both Clear buttons), no native browser API dependency at all, so it can't be silently blocked by a sandbox the same way. Both the header button and the Decisions panel's own copy share one `clearAllPendingDecisions()` function and the same armed state, so confirming via either button works regardless of which one was clicked first. Verified via `node --check` and a careful manual re-read of the state machine (arm -> timeout-or-second-click -> disarm-and-clear, with the button labels/classes reset correctly in every exit path) rather than trusted on the first pass, given how many attempts this same feature has already needed. Republished to the same URL. **A real lesson for this specific environment, worth remembering**: native blocking browser dialogs (`confirm`/`alert`/`prompt`) should be assumed unavailable inside a published Artifact page and avoided from the start, not reached for as the default "add a safety confirmation" pattern the way they might be in an ordinary web page.

**A bulk-restore feature added to the audit tool, 2026-08-04, same day, explicitly requested: "If I want to restore some things, I don't want it to close the section each time I click a restore button... Make it so I can select multiple using the check box... and when I click a complete button... they are flagged for being restored."** Root cause of the described annoyance, traced before building anything: the "Already hidden" group within each subcategory section renders as a plain `<details>` element, closed by default outside of cross-category search mode -- and every single-row Restore click runs the tool's own full `render()`, which rebuilds `main`'s entire `innerHTML` from scratch, discarding that `<details>` element's live open/closed state along with it. With 10,000+ rows now genuinely hidden via the bulk import earlier the same day, restoring even a handful one at a time meant re-finding and re-opening the same section after every single click.

Fixed by extending the bulk-actions bar (already shared by Rename/Move, both added earlier this session) with a third "Restore" section -- one button, no extra inputs needed. Reused the exact same `selectedRows` checkbox mechanism already present on every row including hidden ones (checking a box only triggers the cheap `renderBulkActionsBar()`, never a full `render()`, so any number of already-open sections stay open while building up a selection -- across searches and category switches too, same as the existing rename/move selection already supported). Clicking "Restore selected" writes `action: 'unhide'` to every selected row's decision in one pass (preserving any existing `renameTo`/`note`, matching the single-row handler's own shape exactly), tracks the batch under `lastBulkAction` with a new `'restore'` actionType (extending the existing rename/move/hide undo-label logic), then does exactly one `render()` for the whole batch -- by which point the person is done with that section anyway, so the one unavoidable collapse only happens once per batch instead of once per item. Verified via `node --check` on the extracted script block both before and after the full template merge. Republished to the same URL. **Not yet exercised on-device** (browser-only, same as every other audit-tool change) -- worth a real click-through confirming a multi-item restore selection survives across an open "Already hidden" section before trusting it fully, given how many rounds the Clear-All button needed before its real root cause was found.

**`CommercialPremade` ("Commercial / Pre-Made") hidden from the app's own category picker, 2026-08-05, explicitly requested.** The category itself (branded/packaged/box-mix products already present in the source USDA/national databases this app derives from, moved out of `Mixed` on 2026-08-04) was already excluded from every Food-tab builder's own category allowlist, but Insights' general-purpose Food Lookup lens (`components/FoodLookup.tsx`, called with no `allowedCategories` restriction) had no such filter and still listed it. Fixed at the shared source: `getReferenceCategories()` (`lib/db.ts`) gained a `CATEGORIES_HIDDEN_FROM_BROWSING` Set, filtering `CommercialPremade` out of the category list it returns -- the same query-time-filter pattern `getReferenceSubcategories()`'s own `ALCOHOL_SUBCATEGORIES_PENDING_SCAN_FEATURE` already uses, not a database change. The underlying rows and category stay fully intact in the reference database. **Explicit correction from the person, same day**: this category is NOT where a future barcode-scan feature's own scanned products will land -- `CommercialPremade` exists solely to hold the branded/packaged entries that were already present in the source national databases (USDA etc.) this app was built from. Where a real scanned product should actually appear in the food list is a separate, not-yet-decided design question for whenever that feature is actually built, not this category.

**`Baked` ("Baked Goods") hidden from the app's own category picker too, 2026-08-05, same day, immediate follow-up ("not needed for our purposes right now").** Same mechanism, generalized rather than re-implemented: `CATEGORIES_HIDDEN_FROM_BROWSING` (`lib/db.ts`) now holds both `CommercialPremade` and `Baked`, with a short comment on each entry explaining why it's there. As with `CommercialPremade`, this is a query-time filter only -- the real bread/cookie/cake/pastry reference rows stay fully intact in the database, and every Food-tab builder's own allowlist (`constants/foodBuilderCategories.ts`) that already includes `Baked` (Side, Salad, Fermentation, Snack, Baked Goods itself, Soup, Sauces, Handhelds) is unaffected code-wise -- the category simply won't appear as a pickable option anywhere, builder-restricted or not, since the shared `getReferenceCategories()` this all routes through no longer returns it at all. Both hide entries are easily reversible later (just remove the Set entry) if either category is wanted back. `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx eslint` clean on `lib/db.ts` (pre-existing, unrelated `app-example/` template errors aside; confirmed by running `tsc` against the whole project via its own `tsconfig.json`, not a single file directly, which produces unrelated target/lib noise). **Not yet exercised on-device.**

**`Legume` category display label added ("Legumes"), 2026-08-05, same day, explicitly requested.** Turned out `Legume` (like `Fish`, `Algae`, `Fats`, `Alcohol`) had no entry in `CATEGORY_DISPLAY_LABELS` at all, so `categoryLabel()`'s own fallback (`?? category`) had been silently showing the raw internal code "Legume" verbatim in the picker rather than a real display label. Fixed with a one-line addition to the dict in `components/FoodLookup.tsx`. Purely a label change -- no data/category-code change, no rebuild needed.

**`Fish` merged into `Meat` ("Animal Protein") as a real category-level data change, 2026-08-05, same day, explicitly requested ("The Fish category belongs in the Animal Protein category, not out on its own").** Unlike the two hides above, this is a genuine merge, not a picker-level filter -- `Fish` no longer exists anywhere in the rebuilt reference database; every former-Fish row (1,632 of them) now has `category = 'Meat'`. Implemented in `scripts/build_food_reference_db.py`: a `came_from_fish` flag is captured right before a normalization step (`if effective_category == "Fish": effective_category = "Meat"`) placed at the row-append call site rather than as one more early-return branch inside `reclassify_category()` -- that function has multiple return paths into "Fish" (the direct source-category mapping, plus ~28 hand-verified `CATEGORY_OVERRIDES` entries routing specific Mixed-derived fish/seafood dish rows straight to "Fish"), and a single post-hoc normalization catches all of them reliably rather than needing every path patched individually.

A real functional risk was caught and fixed before this could ship as a regression: `Meat` had never had any `SUBCATEGORY_RULES` entry, so merging Fish in with no subcategory split at all would have made the vast majority of the category (beef, pork, chicken, etc., subcategory `NULL`) silently unreachable the instant Fish's own subcategory value made `subcategories.length > 0` -- `FoodLookup.tsx`'s own subcategory-step logic blocks all food browsing until a subcategory is picked the moment a category has ANY real subcategory value, and there'd have been no subcategory value to select the NULL-subcategory rows with. Fixed with a new `meat_subcategory()` helper: every row landing in `Meat` now gets a real subcategory, either `"Fish & Seafood"` (former-Fish rows, both paths) or a plain `"Meat & Poultry"` umbrella (everything else) -- a real, functional two-way subcategory picker, not just Fish sitting alone.

**That same subcategory split broke something else, caught by comparing a full rebuild against a stashed pre-change baseline rather than trusting the first rebuild's clean-looking output.** The first rebuild reported 66 unmatched Reference Database Audit decisions (up from a baseline of 17 known, already-documented flour-related no-ops) -- investigated rather than dismissed. Root cause: every pre-existing "move" decision ever recorded against `Meat` was keyed with `subcategory: ""` (recorded back when the category had no subcategory scheme at all), and "move" decisions are deliberately excluded from `apply_audit_decisions()`'s own base-name-only ambiguity fallback (a move's whole point is reassigning FROM a known starting category, so guessing that starting point defeats the purpose) -- so the instant `Meat` rows started carrying a real subcategory value, EVERY one of those decisions' exact-key lookup broke, with no fallback path left to catch them. Fixed with a second, narrower lookup inside `apply_audit_decisions()`: when the exact 3-key match fails and the row's own current category still equals the decision's own recorded category, retry with the decision's original empty subcategory -- safe specifically because it doesn't touch the row's actual category, only recognizes that the category gained a subcategory scheme after the decision was recorded. Confirmed via a real before/after `git stash` comparison (not just re-reading the code) that this restored the unmatched count to exactly the 17-item baseline, zero regressions.

**One further, genuine pre-existing data-quality gap surfaced by that same investigation, also fixed**: one of four "Lingcod Fish" rows (USDA's "Fish, lingcod, meat, raw (Alaska Native)") had always landed in the generic `Meat` bucket rather than `Fish` -- not from this merge, but from a already-documented, older imprecision (`RAW_CATEGORY_TO_CODE`'s own comment: USDA's "American Indian/Alaska Native Foods" raw_category is a known heterogeneous catch-all defaulting to "Meat" for ~350 rows across the whole database, "wrong for the minority of rows that aren't actually meat"). Confirmed directly against the database that this specific row's 3 siblings (from "Finfish and Shellfish Products") were already correctly Fish-derived; this stray one had been silently un-hideable by a real audit "hide" decision the whole time (genuinely ambiguous base_name split across two different categories, correctly left unmatched rather than guessed at) -- an existing, already-hidden inconsistency this merge happened to surface, not one it created. Fixed with a single new `CATEGORY_OVERRIDES` entry, `("Meat", 'Lingcod Fish'): 'Fish'`, precisely scoped (keyed on the row's own pre-merge category_code, so it can't affect the other three already-correct siblings). All four now correctly share `Meat`/`Fish & Seafood` and are all correctly hidden together, matching the person's original intent.

Also updated for consistency, no functional effect: `constants/foodBuilderCategories.ts`'s five builder allowlists that listed both `'Meat'` and `'Fish'` had the now-redundant `'Fish'` entries removed (every one already had `'Meat'` alongside it, so nothing became unreachable); `components/FoodLookup.tsx`'s `CATEGORY_DISPLAY_LABELS` gained an explanatory comment (no entry needed -- `'Fish'` never had one, having shown as the raw code before); `lib/devSeed.ts`'s one dev-only seed row referencing `category: 'Fish'` updated to `'Meat'` to match. Final state, verified directly against the rebuilt database: `Meat` now holds 7,830 total rows (1,632 `Fish & Seafood` / 6,198 `Meat & Poultry`; 236 / 1,346 visible after existing hide decisions), `Fish` no longer exists as a category at all. Rebuilt `assets/data/foods_reference.db`, re-ran `add_food_aliases.py` (zero dangling aliases -- confirmed no alias anywhere was tagged `category: 'Fish'`; `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` now `20260805062521`). `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx eslint` clean on every touched TS file (one pre-existing, already-documented unrelated warning elsewhere in `FoodLookup.tsx`, not caused by this change). **Not yet exercised on-device.**

**A dedicated "All Hidden Items" view added to the Reference Database Audit tool, 2026-08-05, explicitly requested after the palm-oil/Fats-category discovery above: "I need to be able to see all of the entries that are hidden from the app in one place... so I can go through all of them 1 by 1 to add some of them back into the live app database that were mistakenly hidden for some reason."** Rather than a checkbox toggle, implemented as a pinned entry at the top of the tool's own sidebar (above the ordinary category list, visually set apart with its own border), showing a live count and -- when clicked -- every group with at least one hidden row across EVERY category and subcategory at once, reusing the exact same cross-category rendering path search mode already used (category labels shown, hidden `<details>` blocks auto-open) rather than inventing a second display mode. Combines with the existing search box and Pending-only toggle, so it's possible to search WITHIN the hidden set too. Every existing per-row action (Restore, Move, Rename, bulk-select) works unchanged inside this view -- no new mechanism needed there.

The tool's underlying data was also fully regenerated directly from the current, rebuilt database (a fresh Python export replicating the prior session's own JS grouping logic exactly, verified line-for-line against the live `ALCOHOL_HIDDEN_BASE_NAMES`/`BEV_JUICE_ALLOWED_NAMES` sets in `lib/db.ts` rather than trusted from an old copy) -- meaning it now reflects the Fish-into-Meat merge, the Legumes rename, and the CommercialPremade/Baked category hides, none of which the tool had seen before this pass. A new hide reason was added for that last mechanism specifically: `CATEGORIES_HIDDEN_FROM_BROWSING` (CommercialPremade, Baked) is a whole-category, query-time hide in `lib/db.ts`, not a per-row `hidden` column value, so without this the tool would have shown those ~2,700+ rows as normal/visible even though they're fully invisible in the real app. **A real limitation flagged directly in the tool's own reason text for these rows and the legacy Alcohol/Juice hides**: clicking Restore on one of them records the intent in the decisions panel/export same as any other row, but replaying that decision via the normal `apply_audit_decisions()` database-rebuild step won't actually bring it back, since none of those three mechanisms are backed by the `hidden` column at all -- restoring one of those specifically needs a direct code change (removing it from the relevant Set in `lib/db.ts`), which the person should ask for by name rather than expect to happen from a decisions-file reimport. Of the 10,579 groups the tool now shows as hidden (fully or partially) across the whole database, 7,011 are the row-level `hidden` column from the original bulk audit import (Restore works exactly as it always has); the other 3,568 split across the three other mechanisms (3,171 CommercialPremade/Baked's whole-category hide, 273 the Bev/Juice curated allowlist, 124 the Alcohol legacy hides) and need a direct code change to actually restore, per the reason text now shown on each of those rows.

**A real, much bigger data-lineage discovery the same day, prompted by a direct question while reviewing the palm-oil/Fats-category finding above: "The raw data that came from each of the agencies... also arrived with lots of rows of data that was never included in the app's visible database, right?"** Confirmed true, and traced to real, surviving files rather than guessed at. **Turned into the audit tool's second pinned sidebar group, "🚫 Never Made The Cut," 2026-08-05, same day, explicitly requested as a SEPARATE group alongside "All Hidden Items" (not merged into it) -- "I am talking about everything that didn't make the original cut to even be considered for the app to use," distinguished from the recent in-app hide/curation work.** A genuinely different data shape from everything else the tool shows: these 3,871 rows were excluded before this pipeline ever assigns a category, so there's no category/subcategory to group them by -- rendered as a flat, ungrouped, sortable table instead (`EXCLUDED_DATA`, a separate JS blob from the main category-grouped `DATA`), per direct request ("a flat searchable list is fine but I need to be able to sort the data at least, in ways that make sense") -- click-to-sort on Food name / Source / Exclusion reason / Local-language name, reusing the existing search box (filters this list too when active).
