Deliberately NOT touched: the `//` code comments throughout every one of these files, which are this codebase's own established, separate internal-development-history documentation convention (dense, "--"-heavy prose, consistent with how this very document is written) — a considered scope boundary stated up front, not an oversight.

Worked through all 19 files in sequence (bigPicture, pregnancyFamilyPlanning, complementaryTherapies, otherAutoimmune, nutrientInteractions, nutrients, history, mitochondriaMetabolism, organSystems, foodAdditives, healingStages, labsMedication, fermentedFoods, gutMicrobiome, lifestyleEnvironment, problemFoods, foodIndustryHistory, selfAdvocacy, glossary), each Read in full then rewritten in full via Write, replacing "--" with periods/commas/colons/parentheses as grammar called for, cutting "not X, it's Y" contrastive constructions, and sharply reducing "real"/"genuinely"/"honest(ly)"/"worth"/"not just" as filler — while preserving every `id`, `category`, `citations` (source text AND url unchanged), `overallTier`, `relatedIds`, `stageNote`, and `chart` exactly. Two special-case voices were deliberately preserved rather than flattened: `problemFoods.ts`'s own plainer, friendlier voice (established 2026-08-06) and `gutMicrobiome.ts`'s explicit preservation of the user's own personal framing (the firewall/network-checkpoint analogy, "gut as first line of defense," tied to his own IT-Manager background and his wife's own 15-20-year undiagnosed Hashimoto's journey — see `project_hashimotos_book` memory) — both noted directly in each file's own updated header comment. `foodIndustryHistory.ts`'s closing `foodhistory-opinion-synthesis` entry, the one entry in the whole Digest written in first person as a stated personal opinion, also kept its argumentative, first-person register intact through the rewrite.

One real syntax bug caught and fixed mid-pass, the same class already documented earlier in this file: a literal, unescaped `"` inside a double-quoted string in `labsMedication.ts`'s `labs-combination-t3-ndt` entry (`not "prove I'm not making this up"`) broke the string literal and cascaded into several `tsc` errors; reworded to avoid the nested quote and re-verified clean.

`npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint` run after every file (clean throughout, zero errors introduced), plus a final whole-directory verification script (the same grep-based Node.js pattern already established this session): **302 total unique ids across all 19 files, zero duplicates, zero dangling `relatedIds`** — an unchanged count from before the rewrite, confirming no entries were lost or renamed in the process. `PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION` bumped. **Not yet exercised on-device** — this is a full-content rewrite of already-shipped-but-unseen-on-device prose, worth a real read-through on the phone before treating the new voice as fully validated, same standing caveat as the rest of this whole research track.

**A real gap in that same rewrite pass, found the next day by the person actually reading it on-device: "I still see lots of double dashes."** The prose fields (`title`/`teaser`/`summary`) had genuinely been cleaned — confirmed directly, zero matches there — but `citations[].source` and `stageNote` were both nominally in scope per the rewrite's own stated plan, yet the actual line-by-line rewrite had missed converting the "--" inside nearly every citation's own `Author Year, Journal -- description` shape. A systematic re-scan (the same non-comment-line grep pattern used to size the original problem) found 176 real remaining instances across 19 files, every single one following the identical `" -- "` shape with exactly one occurrence per line — safe to fix as one mechanical, scripted pass (`" -- "` → `": "`) rather than 176 individual hand edits, since a colon reads correctly in every citation's own `Author/Journal/Year: description` grammar. Two edge cases (a parenthetical aside in `mitochondriaMetabolism.ts`, an evidence-clause in `healingStages.ts`) were spot-checked by hand afterward and read fine with the same colon substitution — no separate handling needed. Re-verified after the fix: zero `" -- "` remaining anywhere in `lib/digest/*.ts` outside `//` code comments (the two lines that still matched were a multi-line JSX `{/* */}` comment in `purple-digest.tsx`, correctly out of scope), `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx eslint` clean, and the same duplicate-id/dangling-`relatedIds` check still shows 302/0/0 — confirming the scripted replacement changed no structure, only the one punctuation pattern. `PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION` bumped again. **Worth remembering for any future prose-cleanup pass on this codebase**: a "the fields are in scope" statement isn't the same as confirming every field actually got touched — a real post-pass grep across the *specific* pattern being removed, not just a spot-read of a few entries, is what actually catches this kind of partial-completion gap.

**My Meds started, 2026-08-08 — a new, real Schedule lens, direct request following the earlier Lens Coverage Audit's own "where would this go" question about a unified prescription/OTC/supplement registry.** The request was large and explicit: exact ingredients for every supplement (including multivitamins and named combinations like "D3 & K2"), the specific chemical *form* used and how it affects absorption, real research-backed timing rules (empty stomach vs. with food, water- vs. fat-soluble), food/substance pairing and avoidance advice, powdered supplements (potassium citrate, inositol, glycine) tracked here, salt as a trackable electrolyte with real "healthy salt" guidance, and a researched database of common prescriptions so a person can pick from a list rather than typing everything by hand, falling back to online lookup for anything missing.

**Investigated before building anything, and found real, substantial infrastructure already existed with zero UI on top of it — the same "schema exists, no lens uses it" pattern the Lens Coverage Audit had already flagged elsewhere.** A `supplement_forms` reference table (real, cited, 21 rows across 9 nutrients — calcium, magnesium, iron, zinc, vitamin D, B12, folate, selenium, iodine, complete with absorption/GI-tolerance notes and evidence tiers) and a working `getSupplementForms()` function in `lib/db.ts` had existed since some earlier, undocumented pass — confirmed via direct query, not assumed — but no screen anywhere ever called it. `treatment_nutrients.supplement_form` (the column meant to record which form a person actually takes) existed in the schema but had no field in the Supplements lens's own ingredient-row UI to set it. Real, if modest, `nutrient_interactions` (5 rows) and `nutrient_system_effects` reference tables existed too, already covering sodium/potassium/magnesium physiology directly relevant to the electrolyte request.

**Scoped as a real, deliberately bounded Phase 1, not a claim of complete coverage — the same discipline Food Additives and every other Digest research category already established.** This session's own `WebSearch` budget was already exhausted (inherited from earlier work), so every new citation was found via the same `WebFetch`-against-PubMed fallback already used repeatedly this session, never fabricated. Real research landed for exactly the items named directly:
- **Potassium**: citrate vs. chloride, backed by a real 2023 RCT (Wouda et al., Clin J Am Soc Nephrol, PMID 37382933) finding citrate produces higher intracellular uptake and real acid-base/kidney-stone/bone benefits chloride doesn't have, plus a real, serious hyperkalemia-with-ACE-inhibitors/ARBs safety citation (Raebel 2012, PMID 21883995).
- **Inositol**: pure myo-inositol vs. the 40:1 myo:D-chiro-inositol blend (the real physiological serum ratio, Minozzi 2013 PMID 23467955), with an honest note that the blend's own trial evidence comes mostly from PCOS research, not this app's own already-cited Hashimoto's-specific myo-inositol-alone finding.
- **Glycine**: a real, cited sleep-quality finding (Bannai & Kawai 2012, PMID 22293292 — glycine before bedtime, via a measured drop in core body temperature), the actual mechanism behind the common "take glycine at night" advice.
- **Vitamin K2 (the real form question behind "D3 & K2")**: MK-7 vs. MK-4 vs. K1, with a real, classic head-to-head trial (Schurgers 2007, Blood, PMID 17158229) establishing MK-7's much longer half-life and higher accumulation, and — a genuinely important find along the way — real, dose-dependent interference with warfarin at 50mcg/day or more, which became a new interaction rule (see below), not just a form-comparison footnote.
- **Salt**: the actual evidence-backed "healthy salt" answer isn't sea salt vs. table salt, it's iodization — a real, directly Hashimoto's-relevant study (Chen et al. 2017, Thyroid, PMID 29092685) tying non-iodized salt use to both lower urinary iodine AND higher thyroid antibody positivity in a real urban Chinese cohort, plus a 2024 case-report review (Patel et al., PMID 38920131) naming "newer trends of non-iodized salts" as a real, re-emerging deficiency driver.
- **Medications**: a real, explicitly bounded starting set of 10 (6 prescription, 4 OTC) — levothyroxine, liothyronine, metformin, amiodarone, lithium, omeprazole/PPI class, ciprofloxacin, ibuprofen, acetaminophen, diphenhydramine — reusing this app's own already-verified `lib/digest/labsMedication.ts` citations directly for the six that already had them (amiodarone, lithium, PPI, ciprofloxacin, levothyroxine itself) rather than re-researching, and adding one genuinely new, real finding: metformin measurably lowers TSH in people already on levothyroxine (Isidro et al. 2007, Endocrine, PMID 17992605; Vigersky et al. 2006, PMID 16219720) — a real lab-interpretation pitfall, not a dosing-timing issue. Explicitly **not** a claim of covering "every commonly prescribed medication" — that's a much larger, ongoing research project, comparable in scope to the original 22,016-food nutrition database build, started here the same deliberate way Food Additives started with ~10 entries and grew over many sessions.

**New `scripts/add_my_meds_reference_data.py`, patching the compiled `assets/data/foods_reference.db` directly** — same precedent as `scripts/add_interaction_rules.py` (patching the compiled db, not a full spreadsheet rebuild, is the right move in this environment). Extends `supplement_forms` with 10 new rows (potassium ×2, inositol ×2, glycine ×1, vitamin K ×3, sodium ×2); adds two brand-new reference tables, `nutrient_timing` (14 rows — solubility, when to take it, what to avoid, what pairs well, for every nutrient now covered by supplement_forms) and `common_medications` (10 rows, the bounded starting set above); adds two new `nutrients` rows (glycine, inositol — supplement-tracking only, no food-content backfill and deliberately no DRI row, the same honest precedent already established for caffeine); and adds two new `interaction_rules` rows — a reference-only vitamin-K2/warfarin dose-consistency caution (the first real use of the `referenceOnly` code path in `lib/interactionRules.ts`, which had existed unused since that file was first built) and a genuinely new checkable rule type, `concurrent_use_caution` (metformin + levothyroxine), evaluated by simple co-occurrence rather than dose-timing math. Run successfully against the real bundled database; every row verified directly via query before moving to the app layer. `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` bumped.

**Real schema/engine changes in `lib/db.ts` and `lib/interactionRules.ts`**, not just new reference rows: a `generic_name` column added to `treatments` (the same `PRAGMA table_info` + conditional `ALTER TABLE` migration pattern already used for every other column added to this table) — a structured, canonical identity for a prescription/OTC item, separate from whatever free-text name the person actually typed, so interaction matching doesn't have to depend on a name substring the way it always has. A real third `treatment_type`, `'otc'`, with its own `createOtcTreatment`/`listOtcTreatments`/`updateOtcTreatment` functions mirroring the existing Prescription ones exactly (deliberately not a generic parameterized helper — matches this file's own established preference for a few duplicated, readable functions over one shared abstraction for genuinely different real-world categories). A new `listAllActiveTreatments()` unifying all three types into one sorted list. `lib/interactionRules.ts`'s own `activeTreatmentsForSubject` now prefers an exact `genericName` match when one is set, falling back to the original free-text substring match for treatments that predate it — a graceful-degradation change, not a breaking one. New `getNutrientTiming`, `listCommonMedications`, and `searchCommonMedications` (matches generic name OR brand name, so searching "Advil" finds ibuprofen) reference-data getters.

**The actual "My Meds" lens, a new `MyMedsLens()` function in `app/(tabs)/schedule.tsx`**, following this file's own established convention of every lens being an inline function in the same file rather than extracted (Meals, Hydration, Supplements, Prescriptions, and Appointments are all built this way already). A real, unified "everything currently active" view across all three treatment types; an "Add" flow that opens the right sub-form per category — for supplements, the existing per-ingredient row gained a real Form picker (populated from `supplement_forms` once a nutrient is chosen, showing the real absorption/GI-tolerance note inline) plus a live cross-reference against today's already-logged food (via the same `getDailyNutrientAnalysis` Insights itself uses) showing "you're already getting ~X% of today's [nutrient] from food" — directly answering the "first try to get it through food" half of the request; for prescriptions/OTC, a searchable dropdown over the real `common_medications` list that prefills name/generic name and shows the full researched card (drug class, thyroid-relevant notes, timing, key interactions, side effects), with a manual-entry fallback carrying an honest, explicit note that automatic online lookup for anything not in the list is a planned future capability, not built. Every tracked item's own "Details" view surfaces its real timing/interaction/food-status research inline. Reuses the existing "Things to check" interaction section verbatim, now also picking up the two new rules.

**A real, explicit scope boundary, stated in the lens's own Info content and here**: reminder times and repeat schedules for prescriptions and supplements still live entirely on those two existing lenses, unchanged — adding an item through My Meds does not also set up a dose reminder for it. Building a genuine third parallel reminder system for OTC (which has no existing lens to fall back on) in the same pass as everything else above would have meant rushing it; tracking on/off (the toggle that actually feeds interaction checking and nutrient totals) works identically across all three types today, and OTC reminder scheduling is a real, named fast-follow, not forgotten.

Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit` (clean across the whole project) and `npx eslint` on every touched file (`lib/db.ts`, `lib/interactionRules.ts`, `app/(tabs)/schedule.tsx` — a handful of unescaped-apostrophe errors in the new JSX text were caught and fixed; the rest of that file's own pre-existing unescaped-entity warnings, unrelated to this change, were left as-is per this project's own standing practice). **Not yet exercised on-device.**

**Purple Digest search, a real visual-preferences opt-out for the shared/per-tab backgrounds and animated sky, and the D1-D6 rename to 6-DFF, all 2026-08-08, three separate direct requests handled in one pass.**

**Search All, a new synthetic lens alongside every real Digest category.** "There needs to be a way to search for things the person wants to read about in the Digest... draw from the entire list of all the available information," the same shape as Insights' own Food Lookup searching across everything rather than one category at a time. New `searchDigestEntries()` (`lib/digest/index.ts`) — a plain, in-memory, every-term-must-match substring search over `ALL_DIGEST_ENTRIES` (title/food name, teaser, full summary/problem/mechanism/swaps text, and every citation's own source text), cheap enough at a few hundred entries that no debounce or indexing is needed. `app/(tabs)/purple-digest.tsx` gained a screen-local `PurpleDigestLens = DigestCategoryKey | 'search'` type (deliberately not folded into `DigestCategoryKey` itself — `'search'` isn't a real content category, so every other consumer of that type is unaffected) and a new `'search'` lens placed first in the picker, same "reached for constantly" reasoning Glossary's own front-placement already established. Typing a query shows live result cards (category label + title/food name + teaser); tapping one reuses the existing `jumpToRelated()` mechanism verbatim — switches to that entry's real category, expands it, and scrolls it into view, exactly like tapping a Related chip. Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx eslint` (clean). **Not yet exercised on-device.**

**A real, live-reactive visual-preferences system, `lib/visualPreferences.ts` + `hooks/useVisualPreferences.ts`, added for a genuine appearance opt-out — explicitly framed as being "for the few men who would have this app and have a problem with all the colors and flowers," plus a real, named battery/resource concern about the animated sky running continuously.** Explicitly scoped, per direct instruction: header/footer colors, box/font/line colors, and the iridescent shimmer are untouched by any of this — only the background layer itself. Stored the same way `getStoredMeasurementSystem` already does (one JSON blob under a single `app_meta` key), with a small module-level cache + subscriber list so a change saved on the Profile screen reaches the shared background (mounted once, permanently, in `app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx`) and every tab's own revealed background immediately, no app restart needed.

Three real, independent controls, matching the person's own three-part framing rather than one all-or-nothing switch: (1) **`skyAnimationsEnabled`** — the sun/moon/starfield/day-night tint (`components/AnimatedSky.tsx`), the actual continuously-running animation named as the real "power hog"; only has any effect while the shared background is set to Photo, since there's no open-sky band to animate over a generic gradient or a flat off state. (2) **`homeBackgroundStyle`** (`'photo' | 'generic' | 'off'`) — "the flowery shared background," the single persistent layer behind Home and every tab at rest. (3) **`tabBackgroundStyle`**, a `Partial<Record<route path, style>>` — "selectively turn off the tab backgrounds instead of an all-or-nothing rule," each of the 7 `GatedTabContent`-driven tabs' own revealed image (Food, Insights, Schedules, Trends, Signals, Reports, Purple Digest) settable independently, absent meaning "use the photo" so a future new tab needs no migration here. A shared `genericPalette` (`'lavender' | 'seafoam' | 'sand' | 'dusk'`) applies wherever any of the above is set to Generic — new `components/GenericBackground.tsx`, a static (zero-animation, zero-new-asset) gradient-plus-soft-blob look per palette, deliberately calm rather than trying to compete with the real photography, and using its own self-contained hex values, never the app's own iridescent/tab palette, so "the iridescence also stays" holds exactly as asked.

`ScreenBackground.tsx` gained an optional `routeKey` prop (undefined for the one shared/global call in `_layout.tsx`, which follows `homeBackgroundStyle` instead) and now branches on the effective style: `'photo'` renders the existing `Image` (unchanged), `'generic'` renders `GenericBackground`, `'off'` renders neither (a new `bodyFlat` style fills the space with `colors.background`, matching the header/footer). `GatedTabContent.tsx` resolves `routeKey` from its own already-required `pageTitle` via a `TAB_ROUTES` lookup (all 7 real call sites' `pageTitle` values already matched `TAB_ROUTES` titles exactly, confirmed before relying on it, so no call site needed touching). Profile gained two new cards — "Shared background" (style + sky toggle) and "Individual tab backgrounds" (one three-way pill row per tab, plus the shared palette picker) — using the exact same pill-row pattern every other Profile card already uses, wired straight to `setVisualPreferences()` via the same `useVisualPreferences()` hook everything else reads. Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx eslint` (clean; a few unescaped-entity errors in the new prose were caught and fixed, the many pre-existing ones elsewhere in `profile.tsx` left untouched per this project's own standing practice). **Not yet exercised on-device** — worth a real check that the live-reactive update (toggling a setting on Profile and seeing it take effect without restarting) actually works smoothly, since that's the one part of this that can't be verified by `tsc`/`eslint` alone.

**D1-D6 renamed to 6-DFF (6 Dimensions of Food Friendliness) in every genuinely user-facing spot, "6 Dimensions" kept as the already-established, still-fine plain-language alias.** Investigated before touching anything: the app's own actual UI never showed bare "D1"/"D2" labels to begin with (`group.dimension` in Insights' own `SixDsView` already renders real names like "Goitrogenic Load," not a code) — the only real user-facing use of the literal string "D1-D6" was as shorthand for the *system as a whole*, in a handful of HelpSection bodies (`trends.tsx` ×2, `log.tsx`) and three Purple Digest entries (`glossary.ts`'s own "D1-D6" glossary entry — now titled "6-DFF (The 6 Dimensions of Food Friendliness)," with its summary explicitly noting these are "specific to Hashimoto's" and that other food dimensions exist relevant to anyone, per direct request; `foodAdditives.ts`'s phosphate entry; `nutrients.ts`'s Nigella sativa entry). Deliberately left untouched: every one of the ~15 remaining "D1-D6" occurrences, all in `//` code comments across `lib/db.ts`, `lib/sixDimensionsReference.ts`, `lib/trendAnalysis.ts`, `lib/nutrientAnalysis.ts`, `lib/checkinTags.ts`, `lib/symptomAssessment.ts`, `lib/coffeeAdvisory.ts`, `lib/alcoholAdvisory.ts`, and several Food builders — internal developer documentation, not user-facing text, consistent with how this whole file treats comments elsewhere; and every real reference to the deiodinase enzymes D1/D2/D3 (`lifestyleEnvironment.ts`'s IL-6 entry, `glossary.ts`'s own separate "Deiodinase (D1, D2, D3)" entry) — a real, unrelated biological term this app's own glossary already flags as an easy naming collision with the scoring system, not something to rename. Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx eslint` (clean).

**Profile's own number-entry fields switched from free-text typing to `PopoverSelect`, 2026-08-08, same day, direct follow-up: "all Profile text boxes where the user has to type in something... I want the same kind of selectable list as we have for the Side Builder creation Dish Name page."** Scoped exactly as asked — every field where a person typed a number (birth year/month/day, height in either cm or ft/in, usual meal times' hour/minute) now opens the same tap-a-list popover Side Builder's own Servings/Serving Size fields already use, leaving First/Last name as the one real remaining text entry. Also applied, on the same reasoning, to the Fasting/eating-window start and end hour/minute fields — not named directly in the request, but the identical "type a number" pattern in the same screen; left inconsistent otherwise.

New module-level option-list constants (`app/profile.tsx`): birth year runs every real year 1900 through the current year, newest first; month/day stay plain unpadded numbers, matching how they were already stored; height offers a generous real range in either system; meal/window hour stays unpadded ("1".."12"), minute is zero-padded ("00".."59") to match `splitTime24`'s own output for an already-saved time. `commitBirthDate`/`commitHeight` both gained an `overrides` parameter, the same stale-closure fix `commitMealTime`/`commitEatingWindow` already used for their own AM/PM pill press — a `PopoverSelect` selection updates state AND needs an immediate commit in the same synchronous tap, so reading straight from closure state would still see the pre-update value. AM/PM stayed the existing pill row throughout, since that was never a text box to begin with. Uses `colors.tabProfile` (Profile's own identity pink) as `PopoverSelect`'s accent color. Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx eslint` (clean; the file's own pre-existing unescaped-entity warnings elsewhere, unrelated to this change, left as-is). **Not yet exercised on-device.**

**My Meds' own first real on-device error, 2026-08-08, same day: "Could not load My Meds: Call to function 'NativeDatabase.prepareAsync' has been rejected... no such table: common_medications" -- traced to a real, familiar root cause, not a code bug.** The Metro dev server had been running continuously since **August 4th**, several days before the My Meds work (which added `common_medications`, `supplement_forms`'s new rows, and `nutrient_timing`, plus the `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` bump that's supposed to trigger a real device to re-import the reference database) ever existed. Confirmed directly, not assumed: `assets/data/foods_reference.db` on disk genuinely already has the table; the phone was just still being served the pre-My-Meds bundle, whose own baked-in `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` constant already matched what was stored on-device from the last real import -- so `getReferenceDatabase()`'s own version-mismatch check (lib/db.ts) never tripped, and the reimport that would have created the table never ran. Fixed by killing the whole stale process tree (confirmed via `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process`, not guessed -- exact `CreationDate: 8/4/2026 5:05 PM` on every node in the tree) and restarting via `npm start -- --clear`, confirmed via the log's own "Bundler cache is empty, rebuilding" line as a genuinely clean rebuild, not a reused cache. **Confirmed fixed on-device** after a real force-close/reopen. Worth remembering directly alongside the existing Metro-staleness guidance already in this document: a long-idle dev server doesn't just serve stale JS, it can leave a whole bundled *data* migration (anything gated on `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION`) permanently unreached on a real device until a genuinely fresh bundle reaches it.

**Every dropdown in My Meds converted from `Dropdown.tsx` to `PopoverSelect`, and this made the app's own standing pattern going forward, 2026-08-08, same day, direct request: "all dropdown menus need to be selectable scroll lists just like those on the Side Dish Builder Dish Name page, and make that a standing order for all of them in the future. The app keyboard can pop up to search as the list could definitely be longer."** `Dropdown.tsx`, it turns out, was never actually restyled for this app's own dark navy/jewel-tone redesign -- its hardcoded colors (`#e8f5e9` green, `#eaf2ff` blue) are the OLD, pre-redesign "Sage & Cream" theme, plus it still carried two leftover `console.log` "TEMPORARY diagnostic logging" statements from an old menu-position bug investigation. `PopoverSelect.tsx` (built 2026-08-01 to replace `WheelPicker.tsx`) already used the current dark palette but, per its own original design, only ever supported a short, fixed, unsearched list of plain words (`options: string[]`) -- not a fit for My Meds' own nutrient/medication pickers, which needed both a real label distinct from its stored value AND, per this request, real search.

`PopoverSelect` gained, as real, general capability rather than a My-Meds-only patch: `options` now accepts `DropdownOption` (`{label, value}`) alongside a plain string, so a picker's displayed text can differ from what's actually stored; a `searchable` mode that reuses `Dropdown.tsx`'s own already-proven AppKeyboard search-row mechanism (`useActiveInputControls().setSearchRequest`) rather than inventing a second one -- opening a searchable picker raises the app's own keyboard with a live search box, and the popover's list filters as you type; and a `width` override (default 160px, sized for short single words) for callers whose real option labels run longer. The overlay's own content is now rebuilt from live render state and pushed via a dependency-free `useEffect` (mirroring `Dropdown.tsx`'s own identical pattern) rather than the previous one-time snapshot taken at open -- necessary now that a searchable list's own visible content genuinely changes while open (typing filters it), harmless for a non-searchable picker where nothing changes after opening. Every existing `PopoverSelect` caller (Side Builder and its eight siblings, Profile) is unaffected -- every new prop defaults to the exact old behavior.

All four of `MyMedsLens`'s own `Dropdown` usages (`app/(tabs)/schedule.tsx`) converted: the per-ingredient Nutrient picker (now searchable, `width={220}`) and Supplement-Form picker (`width={240}`, short list per nutrient, not searchable), the Unit picker (short, fixed list, not searchable), and the common-medication picker (searchable, `width={260}`). `nutrientOptions`/`commonMedOptionsForCategory` moved into `useMemo` (keyed on `nutrients`/`commonMedications`+`medForm.category`) rather than staying plain inline `.map()` calls -- `PopoverSelect` is memoized against referentially-stable `options`, unlike the old `Dropdown`, so a fresh array on every keystroke elsewhere in the same form would have defeated that. `Dropdown.tsx` itself is NOT deleted -- `SupplementsLens`/`PrescriptionsLens` and others still use it as of this writing; converting those is fair game whenever one of those lenses is touched again, not done proactively here, matching the request's own explicit scope ("the My Meds lenses"). The broader "standing order for the future" is saved to memory (`feedback_popover_select_standard.md`) so it carries into later sessions without needing to be repeated. Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx eslint` (clean; every eslint error in the touched file is pre-existing, well outside this change's own lines). **Not yet exercised on-device.**

**Picker colors iterated three more times the same day, each a direct, real-time correction.** First: "make the selectable list fields be the same color as those on the Side Dish Builder" -- all four My Meds pickers' `tabColor` switched from `TAB_COLOR` (`colors.tabSchedules`) to `colors.tabFood`, matching Side Dish Builder's own `tabColor` prop exactly (confirmed by tracing it back to `app/(tabs)/food.tsx`'s own `const TAB_COLOR = colors.tabFood`, not guessed). Second, immediately after: "Actually, I also meant for the ones on the Profile page" -- Profile's own 12 `PopoverSelect` instances (birth date, height, meal times, eating window) switched the same way, from `colors.tabProfile` to `colors.tabFood`.

Third, a real correction of that second change, not an extension of it: "Actually, the Profile icon is a dark grey. The lists should have that color as the line and lighter grey for the field and scrollable table backgrounds." Traced "the Profile icon" to its real, actual on-screen appearance rather than assumed from the pink `colors.tabProfile` identity token -- TabHub only ever paints Profile's icon in that pink when Profile is the literal active screen (rare, since it's a Stack push, not a tab); the rest of the time (i.e., what's actually seen while browsing) it renders in `colors.menuIconMuted` (`#6C7A93`), a deliberately muted, "recede into the background" dark grey-blue, confirmed directly in `TabHub.tsx`. Profile's 12 pickers' `tabColor` switched to `colors.menuIconMuted`. The "lighter grey for the field" half was already free: `PopoverSelect`'s existing `inputBackground(tabColor)` already computes a lightened, alpha-blended tint of whatever `tabColor` it's given, so passing a dark grey there automatically yields a lighter grey field with no extra code. The "lighter grey... scrollable table background" half wasn't -- the popover's own list background was a fixed, unrelated `colors.menuSurface` for every caller, not derived from `tabColor` at all. Rather than changing that shared default (which would have silently changed the popover color for Side Builder and every one of its eight sibling builders, never asked for), `PopoverSelect` gained a new opt-in `tintedSurface?: boolean` prop (default `false`, every existing caller unaffected) plus a new `popoverBackground(tabColor)` export in `constants/colors.ts` -- the same lighten fraction `inputBackground` already uses, just fully opaque rather than alpha-blended, since the popover floats via a portal over arbitrary content rather than sitting on a known card background the way a closed field does. Profile's 12 pickers pass `tintedSurface`; every other `PopoverSelect` caller in the app (Side Builder, My Meds, etc.) keeps the standard `colors.menuSurface` list background, untouched. Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx eslint` (clean).

**Profile's own screen background made to follow the shared "Generic" background choice, 2026-08-08, same day, direct request: "Make the profile screen background follow the color of the shared generic background if the user has that selected, and stay the same color as the header and footer otherwise."** Profile previously always used a flat `colors.background`, identical to the header/footer, regardless of what the new visual-preferences "Shared background" setting (see the earlier same-day Appearance-card entry) was set to -- meaning picking "Generic" changed every tab's own resting look but left Profile visibly the odd one out. Fixed by reading the same `useVisualPreferences()` hook Profile's own Appearance card already uses: a new `showGenericBackground = visualPrefs.homeBackgroundStyle === 'generic'` flag renders `GenericBackground` (the same component the shared/per-tab background system already uses, see the original Appearance-card entry) as an absolute-fill layer behind Profile's content, with `wrapper`/`screen`/`loadingContainer` all made conditionally transparent (a new `transparentBackground` style, `backgroundColor: 'transparent'`) so it actually shows through instead of being covered by their own normally-opaque background -- the same "make the real content layer transparent so a shared backdrop shows through" approach `ScreenBackground.tsx`/`app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx` already use for every tab screen's own scene. Deliberately narrow, matching the request exactly: Profile never shows the "Photo" option (it has no background image of its own and isn't one of the per-tab `GatedTabContent` screens) -- only "Generic" is followed; "Photo" and "Off" both leave Profile exactly as it's always looked. Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx eslint` (clean).

**Three more real, small Profile polish requests the same day: per-field labels above every picker, lighter typed-text color for First/Last name, and lighter card headers.** "Put labels above the list box scrollable selectors on the Profile page for each field... The same for the headers of the boxes in Profile, they need to be a lighter color of grey, maybe like the color of the icon." A new `PickerField` component (`app/profile.tsx`) wraps a small `typography.eyebrow` label (colored `colors.menuIconMuted`, matching each picker's own `tabColor`) above each of the 12 `PopoverSelect` fields (Year/Month/Day, Feet/Inches or Centimeters, Hour/Minute ×3 for meal times and both eating-window ends) -- the same shape as Side Builder's own `renderLabeledPicker`, just without that version's measured-minWidth stretching, which these fixed-width fields don't need. Card headers ("Birth date," "Height," etc., `styles.label`) had no color set at all before this -- defaulting to React Native's own plain black -- fixed to `colors.menuLabelMuted`, the same grey-blue family as `colors.menuIconMuted` (confirmed as literally "the color of the icon" in the prior correction above) but deliberately lighter, reusing the token `constants/colors.ts` already split off for exactly this "stay legible as real text" reason. First/Last name's own typed text had the identical no-color-set problem -- `styles.input` gained `color: colors.textPrimary`, this app's standard readable light body-text color; scoped safely since `input` is local to this file and, with every numeric field now converted to `PopoverSelect`, only these two `AppTextInput`s use it anymore. Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx eslint` (clean).

**A real, three-part symptom-check-in overhaul, 2026-08-08, same day: explicit time frames on every periodic assessment question, an automatic 30-day due-reminder, and a genuine new daily "how do you feel" quick-check on Home.** Direct request, quoted in full since all three parts trace back to it: "the questions need to be tracked, but they need to be all related to in the past however many days or stated to be when the user is asked to answer them because they don't get a frame of reference per question otherwise... They need to automatically pop up every 30 days or on the first of every month, and on the home page they need to have the ability to select how they feel today, just a one question thing... so the list to choose from... might need to be quite extensive."

**Part 1 -- every one of the periodic assessment's 23 questions now states a real, explicit time frame, not just its domain's own shared description.** Checked the live prompt text before touching anything, not assumed: of 23 items, only 1 (`ibs_pain_frequency`) already had one ("Over the last 10 days..."); the other 22 were bare ("Fatigue or low energy," "I have felt cheerful and in good spirits") with no day count anywhere in the item itself -- exactly the "no frame of reference per question" gap named directly. All 22 gained one, each period traced to something real rather than invented freely: hypothyroid-symptoms items -> "the past 30 days" (matches the new automatic re-prompt cadence below, so the period being asked about now matches how often the question is actually asked again); digestive/IBS items -> "the last 10 days" (the real IBS-SSS instrument's own standard recall window, already used by the one item that had a time frame); wellbeing items -> "the past two weeks" (WHO-5's own real, standard recall window, already stated in the domain's own description, just never per-item). No item needed the other allowed framing ("stated to be when the user is asked to answer them," i.e. "right now") -- all three domains are modeled on real recall-period instruments (Zulewski/ThyPRO-style, IBS-SSS, WHO-5), not momentary snapshots, so a real day count was the honest fit for every one of them.

Implemented as a new `scripts/patch_assessment_item_timeframes.py` (same "patch the compiled db directly, not a full rebuild" approach as `add_my_meds_reference_data.py` -- `assessment_items`/`assessment_domains` are small, self-contained, hand-authored tables with zero dependency on the source xlsx, so a full 22,016-food rebuild would have been real overkill for a 23-row text change), run successfully against the live `assets/data/foods_reference.db` and verified directly (every one of the 23 new prompts read back correctly). `scripts/build_food_reference_db.py`'s own `ASSESSMENT_ITEMS` source list was updated to match in the same pass -- unlike the My Meds patch (which added genuinely new tables a full rebuild simply lacks), this patch OVERWRITES existing rows, so leaving the old text in the main build script would have meant a future full rebuild silently reverting this fix; folding the same new prompts back in there closes that gap. `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` bumped.

**Part 2 -- the periodic assessment now automatically surfaces itself on Home, not just via the existing quick-action pill someone would have to already know to look for.** A rolling 30-day-since-last-completion cadence, not a calendar-anchored "1st of the month" one -- explicitly chosen over the user's own second suggested option, and explained rather than silently picked: anchoring to the 1st would mean a real gap anywhere from 1 to 31 days depending on when someone happens to finish one, where a rolling window keeps the actual gap consistent regardless of when someone started, and it's also what the new "past 30 days" hypothyroid-domain framing from Part 1 already assumes. New `ASSESSMENT_DUE_AFTER_DAYS = 30` constant and `assessmentDueBanner` (`app/(tabs)/index.tsx`) -- a real, hard-to-miss banner (solid-tinted `colors.primaryMuted` background, not just a bordered card like everything else on the page) shown at the very top of Home's own content, above the Day Arc, whenever `daysSinceAssessment` is null (never taken) or >= 30, computed from `listSymptomAssessments(1)`'s own `completedAt` in the existing `load()` function. Tapping it opens `/assessment` directly. The existing "Daily check-in" quick-action pill further down (added 2026-07-28) was relabeled "Symptom check-in," 2026-08-08, same day -- it always opened this same 23-item periodic assessment, never a real daily action, and calling it "Daily" became actively misleading the moment a genuine daily action (Part 3, below) exists on the same page; the pill itself is unchanged otherwise, still available any time regardless of whether the new due banner is currently showing.

**Part 3 -- a genuine new "Today's Check-In" daily quick-check on Home, reusing existing infrastructure rather than building parallel machinery.** Investigated before building anything and found the exact "extensive list" already existed: `lib/checkinTags.ts`'s own `CHECKIN_TAGS` (30 tags across 7 categories -- Digestive, Energy, Mood & Stress, Sleep, Skin, Pain & Physical, Cognitive -- deliberately including positive tags like "Slept well"/"Good, steady energy" alongside symptoms), and the existing `wellbeing_checkins` table already has a `'general'` `checkinType` built for exactly this "not tied to a specific meal/exercise/flare" case. No new table, no new tag vocabulary -- the whole feature is a new Home UI (`app/(tabs)/index.tsx`) plus one new targeted query, `getCheckinForDate(date, checkinType)` (`lib/db.ts`), so Home can know whether today's entry already exists without fetching/filtering a whole `listCheckins()` result client-side.

The card shows a categorized, multi-select pill grid (`getCheckinTagsByCategory()`) when no entry exists yet for today, or a plain summary ("Fatigue, Brain fog · Tap to update") once one does, reopening the same picker pre-seeded with what's already saved. Deliberately no separate valence question -- asking a second question would have broken "just a one question thing" -- `valence` is instead derived from the real `usualValence` of whatever got picked (all-positive -> positive, all-negative -> negative, a genuine mix or nothing selected -> neutral, the same "informational, not inherently good or bad" reading `wellbeing_checkins`' own schema comment already gives an ambiguous entry). Saved via the existing `recordCheckin({ checkinType: 'general', tags, valence })`, same function Bio-Compass/Signals' own flare logging already uses -- this is genuinely the same underlying record type as a flare/reaction check-in, just a different, Home-surfaced entry point and a different `checkinType`. Named "Today's Check-In" deliberately, not "How You're Feeling" -- that name was already taken by the existing `EnergyOrb` widget one card below it (a passive display of recent flare/reaction severity, tap-through to Signals, not an input), and reusing it here would have read as the same feature twice.

`HOME_HELP_SECTIONS` gained two new entries explaining both the new daily card and the automatic reminder. Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx eslint` (clean; every eslint error touched by this pass is pre-existing and unrelated, confirmed line-by-line). **None of this session's Profile-polish or symptom-check-in work has been exercised on-device yet** -- worth a real walkthrough (the due banner's own timing, the daily picker's save/reopen/update cycle, and confirming the new assessment prompt wording reads naturally) before treating any of it as fully settled.

**Home's own spacing normalized to a uniform 10px, 2026-08-08, same day, direct request: "Lets try to make the individual boxes and buttons per rows be the same 10 pixel distance away for each other on the Home page, both vertically and horizontally."** Before this, vertical spacing between Home's own top-level cards was handled by each card individually carrying its own margin -- `sectionHeadingSpaced` (`marginTop: 24`) on most of them, `statRow`/`quickActionsRow` each with their own separate hardcoded `marginTop: 16`, `greetingCard` with its own `marginBottom: 16` -- three different, inconsistent values across the same page, none of them 10. Replaced all of it with a single `gap: 10` on `content` (the ScrollView's own contentContainerStyle) -- RN's flexbox `gap` applies uniformly between every real direct child at that level, including correctly skipping a gap on either side of whatever doesn't currently render (the assessment-due banner, `trendCard`), and correctly reaches through the `<>...</>` fragment most of these cards are wrapped in, since a Fragment is transparent to layout. `sectionHeadingSpaced` itself (and all 6 of its remaining JSX usages) removed outright now that nothing needs it; `statRow`/`quickActionsRow`/`greetingCard`'s own individual margins removed the same way, so nothing stacks on top of the new shared gap.

The horizontal half of the same request -- spacing between boxes/buttons sitting *within* one row -- meant normalizing four different, inconsistent existing values down to 10 too: `statRow` (the two stat tiles, was 12), `ringRow` (the nutrient rings inside Fuel Gauges, was 16), `flipRow` (the Purple Digest flip cards, was 12), `feelingTagRow` (Today's Check-In's own tag chips, was 8). `quickActionsRow` and `feelingActionsRow` were already 10, left unchanged. Deliberately left alone: icon-to-text gaps *within* a single button/label (`cardLabelRow`'s gap 4, `quickActionSecondary`'s gap 6, the assessment-due banner's own icon/text/chevron gap 12) -- these aren't "boxes and buttons," they're sub-parts of one already-together control, a different kind of spacing than what was actually asked about; and the BP/exercise quick-log `Modal`'s own internal form spacing (`modalActions`/`quickInputRow`/`pillRow`), a separate secondary flow rather than one of the dashboard's own visible boxes. Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx eslint` (clean; the one remaining eslint error is the same pre-existing, unrelated line already noted above). **Not yet exercised on-device.**

**The multi-autoimmune expansion actually started building, 2026-08-08, same day, direct request: "I think we should build them in the order you stated, starting with Rheumatoid arthritis."** The first real code (not just research) toward the priority order from the Beyond Hashimoto's/Household-Tiers Artifacts. Deliberately staged rather than attempting RA's full depth in one pass -- the same pacing this whole document has already stated explicitly (comparable in scope to the original 22,016-food database build, not to be rushed). This pass covers the architecture prerequisite plus RA's own bounded, real first slice; RA's full per-food scoring (a 6-DFF-equivalent dimension set of its own) is explicitly **not** attempted here -- flagged as its own later, large research effort, the same way this document has flagged other big remaining pieces throughout.

**Real multi-condition schema, replacing the single Hashimoto's-only assumption.** `user_profile.has_hashimotos` (a single boolean) is left completely untouched -- a real column-drop migration wasn't worth the risk for a column that simply becomes unused -- but a new local `user_conditions` table (`condition_code TEXT PRIMARY KEY`) is now the real source of truth going forward, with `getUserConditions()` one-time-migrating an existing `has_hashimotos=1` into it the first time it's read, so nobody who already answered that question loses the answer. A new bundled reference table, `conditions` (code/name/category/status/sort_order), seeded via direct `sqlite3.exe` SQL (Python still unavailable this session) with the full 18-condition roster from the Beyond Hashimoto's research -- `hashimotos` marked `built`, `rheumatoid_arthritis` marked `in_progress`, the other 16 marked `planned`. Alzheimer's deliberately excluded from this table entirely, consistent with its own already-decided reference-only scope (no Profile condition, no scoring). `lib/db.ts` gained `ConditionReference`/`listAllConditions()`/`getUserConditions()`/`setUserConditionSelected()`.

**Profile's own single Hashimoto's yes/no pill row replaced with a real multi-select.** `app/profile.tsx`'s "Your conditions" card now lists every `built`/`in_progress` condition as a tappable pill (RA shown with an "(early access)" suffix), plus a plain "Coming soon: ..." line naming every `planned` condition below it -- transparent about the roadmap without letting anyone select something not yet functional. The one existing real gate this drove (`profile.hasHashimotos ? (...)` showing the "Where you're at" symptom check-in card) now reads `selectedConditions.includes('hashimotos')` instead -- same behavior, new source of truth.

**Rheumatoid Arthritis's own first real content: 5 medications, 2 interaction rules.** All added via direct `sqlite3.exe` SQL against the bundled `common_medications`/`interaction_rules` tables, matching their existing style/depth exactly (including the same honest "no thyroid-specific interaction documented" pattern already established for ibuprofen, rather than inventing one). New medications: methotrexate, leflunomide, sulfasalazine, adalimumab (the representative TNF-inhibitor biologic -- etanercept/infliximab named in its own notes as real, common alternatives in the same class, not separately added), and prednisone (representative corticosteroid). Ibuprofen wasn't duplicated -- the existing general NSAID row already covers it. New interaction rules, reusing the already-condition-agnostic engine with zero code changes: `methotrexate_folate_interaction` (a real antifolate relationship -- folic acid is often deliberately co-prescribed on methotrexate's own off-days, so this is a "stay consistent, doctor-guided" reference-only rule, `checkable=0`, matching the existing vitamin-K2/warfarin precedent exactly, not a timing-separation rule) and `methotrexate_adalimumab_infection_risk` (a real, checkable `concurrent_use_caution` rule -- fires when both are active, `severity='note'`, matching the metformin/levothyroxine precedent's shape). A real mistake caught before shipping, not after: the folate rule was first written with `checkable=1`, which would have silently done nothing since `dose_consistency_caution` isn't one of the engine's checkable branches -- caught by re-reading `lib/interactionRules.ts` directly rather than assuming, fixed to `checkable=0` to match how the one existing rule of this same type already behaves.

Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit` (clean) and `npx eslint` on every touched file (`lib/db.ts`, `app/profile.tsx` -- the 8 eslint errors present are all confirmed pre-existing via `git diff`, none introduced by this pass). `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` bumped; Metro restarted cleanly (killed the whole stale process tree, relaunched via the real `npm start` path, confirmed a genuine cache rebuild) since the reference database itself changed. **Not yet exercised on-device.**

**Deliberately not done that pass, named explicitly rather than left silent**: RA's own real food-scoring dimensions (the actual 6-DFF-equivalent work -- omega-3/Mediterranean/elimination-diet criteria, scored against real foods with real citations) is a genuinely large, separate research undertaking, the same one this document has already compared to the original nutrition-database build, and still not attempted. RA's own Purple Digest content was the other deferred item -- picked up immediately after, same day, see below.

**Rheumatoid Arthritis got its own real Purple Digest category, 2026-08-08, same day, direct request: "Let's continue with the RA Digest content next."** A genuinely new kind of category for this Digest: the other ten are all still Hashimoto's-specific in one way or another, even Other Autoimmune Diseases (corroborating evidence FOR Hashimoto's, every entry explicitly labeled as someone else's disease) -- this one is RA's own primary content, written for someone who has RA rather than Hashimoto's, or both. New `lib/digest/rheumatoidArthritis.ts`, 9 entries, wired into `types.ts`/`index.ts`/`purple-digest.tsx`'s own `DIGEST_LENS_HELP` the same way every prior category addition was (that Record type's own compile-time safety net confirmed nothing was missed, same as it caught a real gap once before on the Big Picture addition).

Every citation independently verified via `WebSearch` before being written in (this session's own budget was available, unlike some earlier passes that had to fall back to `WebFetch`) -- several came back with real, specific numbers sharper than what the earlier Beyond Hashimoto's scoping research had only estimated. Omega-3s: a 2024 meta-analysis (18 RCTs, 1,018 patients) plus an older one establishing a real dosing threshold (>2.7g/day for 3+ months measurably reduces NSAID use) -- upgraded from the scoping document's own vaguer "2,000-3,000mg" estimate. Mediterranean diet: two independent RCTs, including the MADEIRA trial's own real DAS28 reduction and vitamin D increase, plus a separate feeding trial finding a 76% DAS28 drop over 12 weeks. A landmark 1991 Lancet fasting-then-vegetarian trial, real and significant, but honestly tiered moderate rather than strong once the paper's own "responder vs. non-responder" split at one-year follow-up is read in full -- framed directly as a real, personal hypothesis to test rather than a rule that applies to everyone, matching this app's own core mission. Two medication-food findings, both genuinely more precise and more forgiving than the folk warnings patients often hear: methotrexate and folate need consistency, not elimination (folic acid is often deliberately co-prescribed on methotrexate's own off-days); and a real 11,839-patient study found alcohol under 14 units/week showed no measurable increase in liver-enzyme risk on methotrexate, with the real risk concentrated above 21 units/week, a considerably more permissive and more honest threshold than "avoid alcohol entirely." The category closes on the same RA-Hashimoto's polyautoimmunity connection already established in the Beyond Hashimoto's research, cross-linked both directions with the existing `other-rheumatoid-arthritis` entry so a reader lands on the right one regardless of which they open first.

A real syntax bug caught the same way this exact bug class has been caught before in this Digest: two `summary` fields used double-quote string delimiters while also containing literal double quotes inside their own prose (`"Responder"`, `"avoid alcohol entirely"`) -- `npx tsc --noEmit` caught it immediately, fixed by escaping the inner quotes. The whole-directory dangling-`relatedIds` verification script (the same grep-based Node.js pattern established earlier this session) also caught one real, self-introduced bug before it shipped: a guessed cross-link id (`gut-scfa-treg-induction`) that didn't match the real entry (`gut-scfa-treg`) -- fixed by checking the real file directly rather than trusting memory. Final state: 311 unique ids across the whole Digest, zero dangling references, zero duplicates. `PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION` bumped (and a real near-miss caught in the process: the freshly-generated timestamp came out numerically *earlier* than the existing version string, an artifact of this session's own system clock oscillating between the 7th and 8th all session -- caught before committing by diffing against the prior value, not assumed monotonic just because it was freshly generated).

Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint` on every touched file (`lib/digest/rheumatoidArthritis.ts`, `lib/digest/types.ts`, `lib/digest/index.ts`, `lib/digest/otherAutoimmune.ts`, `app/(tabs)/purple-digest.tsx`) -- all clean. **Not yet exercised on-device** -- this is a pure JS/TS content change, so it should reach a running dev server via ordinary Fast Refresh without needing the kind of forced restart a `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` change requires, but that assumption itself hasn't been confirmed on a real device yet either.

**RA's own real per-food scoring dimensions built the same day, direct follow-up request: "Let's start on the RA food-scoring dimensions next."** This is the "6-DFF-equivalent" work for RA -- making an individual food's own RA-relevance genuinely queryable, the same way `getFoodScores()` already does for Hashimoto's own D1-D6. Investigated feasibility before writing anything: confirmed the whole existing display/query stack (`getFoodScores()`, `groupDailyScoresByDimension()` in `lib/sixDimensionsReference.ts`, all 11 `get*SixDimensionsBreakdown` builder functions) is already fully dynamic and dimension-agnostic, keyed only on whatever `dimension` string a `sub_criteria` row happens to carry -- meaning new scoring data would already surface through that machinery with zero code changes, a real, load-bearing finding that shaped everything below.

**The real, scope-defining discovery: RA doesn't need a from-scratch re-score of all 22,022 foods.** Cross-checking RA's own already-verified Digest research (omega-3/fat quality, Mediterranean-pattern whole-food eating, gut-microbiome support, individual elimination-diet triggers) against Hashimoto's existing 25 `sub_criteria` found 7 that are chemically/scientifically identical to what RA's own literature calls for -- Omega-3 vs 6, Fat Processing, and Oxidation Risk (all real proxies for the same omega-3/anti-inflammatory-fat research); Additives and Processing (both real proxies for the same Mediterranean/whole-food-pattern research); Microbiome Effects (a direct match to the 1991 Lancet fasting-trial gut-flora finding); and Gluten (the exact food eliminated in that same trial's own protocol). All 7 are already 100%-scored across every food in the database -- reusing them means zero new food-level scoring work for the vast majority of RA's own real dimensions. "Irritants" was deliberately left out of this reuse list despite being a plausible-sounding fit -- no real, specific RA citation was found connecting it directly, and forcing a citation that doesn't really support the claim was judged worse than a smaller, more honest reuse set.

Designed a genuinely non-duplicative reuse mechanism rather than copying `food_scores` rows or bolting a single `condition_code` onto `sub_criteria` (which can't express one sub-criterion serving multiple conditions at once): a new `sub_criteria.home_condition_code` column (backfilled `'hashimotos'` on all 25 existing rows, marking which condition a sub-criterion was originally built for) plus a new `sub_criterion_condition_relevance` junction table (`sub_criterion_id`, `condition_code`, `dimension_label`, `relevance_note`, `citation`) carrying each reused sub-criterion's own RA-specific framing without touching a single existing `food_scores` row. Populated with 7 real rows grouped into 4 real RA dimensions (Anti-Inflammatory Fat Profile / Whole-Food, Mediterranean-Pattern Fit / Gut & Microbiome Support / Individual Elimination-Diet Triggers), each with its own real relevance note and the same citations already verified for the RA Digest content (the 2024 Clinical Rheumatology meta-analysis, the MADEIRA trial, the 1991 Lancet trial).

**One genuinely new `sub_criteria` row was still needed**: "Common Elimination-Diet Trigger Food" (id 26, dimension "Individual Elimination-Diet Triggers", `home_condition_code='rheumatoid_arthritis'`) -- covering the RA elimination-diet foods not already reachable through the reused Gluten sub-criterion: nightshades, dairy, corn, citrus, eggs, and coffee. Scored across the full `foods` table via direct SQL (no Python available this session, same `sqlite3.exe` fallback already established) using category- and name-based rules checked against real data before being written, not guessed blind: nightshades required explicitly excluding sweet potato (`NOT LIKE '%Sweet Potato%'`) from the potato match, since sweet potato is botanically unrelated (Convolvulaceae, not Solanaceae) despite the shared name -- confirmed zero sweet-potato rows leaked through afterward; dairy required excluding `%Egg%` since chicken eggs are oddly filed under the `Dairy` category in this database and eggs are their own separate trigger family; corn required excluding `%peppercorn%` and `%cornichon%`, two real false-positive substring collisions found by inspecting the actual matched rows first. Full coverage confirmed (22,022 of 22,022 foods scored, matching the table's own total row count exactly) with a sane tier distribution (19,803 Not a Common Trigger / 1,063 Dairy / 449 Nightshade / 293 Citrus / 236 Corn / 96 Egg / 82 Coffee/Caffeine) and spot-checked directly against real rows (Potato Russet -> Nightshade, Chicken Egg -> Egg, Goji berry -> Nightshade, Corn sweet yellow -> Corn, every Sweet Potato variant correctly excluded).

**New `getFoodScoresForCondition(foodId, source, conditionCode)` added to `lib/db.ts`**, a genuinely additive function -- `getFoodScores()` and all 11 `get*SixDimensionsBreakdown` builder functions are completely untouched. It joins `food_scores` to `sub_criteria` to the new `sub_criterion_condition_relevance` table, returning either a condition-owned sub-criterion (matched via `home_condition_code`) or a reused one (matched via the relevance table), with the reused ones carrying their RA-specific `dimension_label`/`relevance_note`/`citation` instead of the Hashimoto's-framed originals. Verified directly against a real food (Cheddar Cheese): correctly returns its own elimination-trigger tag (Dairy) alongside all 7 reused sub-criteria under their RA dimension labels and relevance notes, tier values flowing through unchanged from Hashimoto's original scoring.

**Real UI wiring -- making an actual Insights or builder screen condition-aware, and any per-dish/per-meal aggregation equivalent to `aggregateBySubCriterion` for a condition -- is a deliberate, separate next step, not done this pass.** This pass only made the data reachable and correct, matching the same "build the real mechanism, defer the screen" scope boundary already used elsewhere in this app's own history. `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` bumped (with the new value double-checked as numerically later than the prior one, after the earlier same-day near-miss on this exact bug). Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit` (clean across the whole project) and `npx eslint` on every touched file (`lib/db.ts`, `lib/referenceDbVersion.ts`) -- clean. Metro restarted cleanly (stale process tree from earlier the same day killed via PowerShell, relaunched through the real `npm start -- --clear` path, confirmed via the log's own "Bundler cache is empty, rebuilding" line as a genuine rebuild). **Not yet exercised on-device.**

**Psoriasis / Psoriatic Arthritis built out the same day, right after RA, direct request: "Let's move on to the next condition in the priority order."** The priority order itself lives in the `conditions` reference table's own `sort_order` column (added alongside RA's own schema work) -- confirmed psoriasis is next (sort_order 3) rather than guessed, flipped its `status` from `planned` to `in_progress`, and built it out following the exact same shape as RA: real research, a Purple Digest category, medications/interaction rules, and reuse-first food-scoring dimensions.

Research covered weight loss (a real RCT plus a 2025 meta-analysis, mean PASI reduction of 2.5 points more than control), the Mediterranean diet (the 2025 MEDIPSO randomized trial plus an independent case-control study), alcohol (a real, if sex-inconsistent, severity link, and a striking 60% higher alcohol-related mortality finding independent of that inconsistency), the gluten-antibody subgroup (a real, biopsy-confirmed tissue-transglutaminase mechanism behind the finding this app's own `otherAutoimmune.ts` already names), and two honestly weak/mixed findings kept deliberately unresolved rather than oversold: nightshade avoidance (mostly anecdotal, no real trial evidence, the proposed solanine mechanism doesn't hold up at real dietary amounts) and oral vitamin D supplementation (one positive trial at 3 months, a larger null trial at 12 months, a meta-analysis that stopped being significant after a stricter statistical adjustment) -- explicitly distinguished from topical vitamin D analogs like calcipotriene, a well-established first-line treatment through a completely different route. Two real, specific medication-food interactions round out the research: cyclosporine and grapefruit juice (a measured 60%+ increase in drug levels via intestinal CYP3A4 inhibition, a real overdose risk) and acitretin and alcohol (alcohol converts the drug into etretinate, a related retinoid with a roughly 120-day half-life versus acitretin's own 49 hours, extending real teratogenic risk for years past when the drug would otherwise have cleared).

**11 new Digest entries in `lib/digest/psoriasis.ts`**, wired into `types.ts`/`index.ts`/`purple-digest.tsx`'s `DIGEST_LENS_HELP` the same way every prior category was, with the existing `other-psoriasis` entry in `otherAutoimmune.ts` cross-linked back to the new category's own overview entry (the same reciprocal-link pattern RA's own addition established). One deliberate, instructive contrast built directly into the content: psoriasis's own omega-3 entry (`psoriasis-omega3-mixed`) explicitly cross-links to RA's own strong omega-3 finding, naming the same nutrient landing in two genuinely different evidence tiers for two different diseases as exactly the kind of disease-specific honesty this whole Digest is built to hold onto rather than assume transfers automatically. Whole-directory verification (the same grep-based duplicate-id/dangling-`relatedIds` script used throughout this Digest's build) confirmed 322 unique ids, zero duplicates, zero dangling references, after catching and fixing one real relatedIds typo (`lifestyle-alcohol-liver-thyroid`, corrected to the real id `lifestyle-alcohol-advisory`).

**5 new `common_medications` rows** (acitretin, cyclosporine, apremilast, secukinumab as the representative IL-17-inhibitor biologic, and calcipotriene, whose own `thyroid_relevant_notes` field is used to head off a real, likely confusion: it's a topical vitamin D analog, not a dietary vitamin D source, and doesn't interact with vitamin D intake tracked elsewhere in this app) and **2 new `interaction_rules` rows** (`acitretin_alcohol_avoidance`, `cyclosporine_grapefruit_avoidance`, both `checkable=0`/reference-only, since neither maps to the existing checkable rule-evaluation branches in `lib/interactionRules.ts` -- the engine already pushes any `checkable=0` row straight to reference-only display regardless of its own `rule_type` string, confirmed by reading the actual evaluation code before writing these rather than assumed). Methotrexate and adalimumab, both already added for RA, needed no duplication -- both drug classes are genuinely common to psoriatic arthritis too, a real, free instance of the same condition-agnostic medication design already in place.

**The food-scoring dimensions needed zero new sub-criteria at all -- a genuinely different, and more revealing, outcome than RA's own one new sub-criterion.** Every real psoriasis food finding maps onto a sub-criterion that already exists: Additives, Processing, Fat Processing, and Oxidation Risk (all reused under a "Whole-Food, Mediterranean-Pattern Fit" dimension label, matching the weight-loss/Mediterranean-diet research); Gluten (reused under "Individual Elimination-Diet Triggers," carrying the tissue-transglutaminase mechanism as its own psoriasis-specific relevance note); and, most tellingly, RA's own brand-new "Common Elimination-Diet Trigger Food" sub-criterion (id 26) reused a second time for its nightshade tag, with a relevance note honestly noting that this same shared tag also flags dairy/corn/citrus/egg/coffee, foods this app's own psoriasis research didn't specifically verify. Omega-3 vs 6 was deliberately reused too, but under its own separate dimension label ("Omega-3 Balance (Mixed Evidence for Psoriasis)") carrying an honestly weaker citation than RA's own strong claim on the exact same underlying sub-criterion and tier data -- the same real food, the same real database row, two different condition-specific framings sitting side by side without contradiction. 7 new `sub_criterion_condition_relevance` rows total. Verified directly against a real food (Cheddar Cheese, via `getFoodScoresForCondition`'s own live SQL query run manually) and a real nightshade (Potato, Russet, confirmed still tagged `Nightshade` when queried under `condition_code='psoriasis'`) -- both confirm the reuse-first architecture built for RA generalizes to a third condition with **zero changes to `getFoodScoresForCondition()` itself**, the strongest evidence yet that this design choice was the right one.

Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit` (clean across the whole project) and `npx eslint` on every touched file -- clean. `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` bumped a third time the same day (value double-checked as numerically later than the prior one before committing, the same discipline established after the earlier same-day near-miss). Metro restarted cleanly (the process tree from the RA restart earlier the same day killed via PowerShell, relaunched through the real `npm start -- --clear` path, confirmed via the log's own "Bundler cache is empty, rebuilding" line). **Not yet exercised on-device.**

**The whole Purple Digest reorganized into 4 real areas, same day, direct request: "all of the Hashimoto's specific Digest information should be within a Hashimoto's area of the Digest, just as the Rheumatoid arthritis has... basic health and knowledge everyone should be aware of... should be listed in a Basic Health category like the free version will have. Each specific autoimmune disease... need each their own areas as well. Self advocacy should also be specific to each disease."** This collapsed what had grown into 20 topic-named LensHub categories (Glossary, Food Additives, Problem Foods & Swaps, Gut & Microbiome, Fermented Foods, Nutrients & Micronutrients, Labs & Medication Timing, Lifestyle & Environment, Mitochondria & Metabolism, Other Autoimmune Diseases, Healing Stages, Organs & Body Systems, History & Milestones, Nutrient Interactions, Food Industry & History, The Big Picture, Self Advocacy, Pregnancy & Family Planning, Complementary & Manual Therapies, plus RA and Psoriasis) down to 4: **Basic Health**, **Hashimoto's**, **Rheumatoid Arthritis**, **Psoriasis** -- matching the pattern RA and Psoriasis already used from day one (one area per condition, containing everything about that condition together, not split further by sub-topic).

**Architecture, not a content rewrite**: every one of the 20 old topic files still exists on disk, still holds its own real entries -- only each entry's own `category` field was individually reassigned, from its old topic key to either `'hashimotos'` or `'basicHealth'` (RA and Psoriasis needed no reassignment, already correct). `ProblemFoodEntry.category` (previously hardcoded to the literal `'problemFoods'`) was widened to the same `DigestEntryCategory` union `DigestEntry` uses, since a problem-food entry (raw goitrogenic crucifers, say) is exactly as Hashimoto's-specific-or-not as a citation-review entry -- `isProblemFoodEntry()` switched from comparing `category` to a structural check (`'foodName' in entry`) since `category` could no longer serve as the type discriminant.

**Every entry across all 20 files was individually read and categorized, not bulk-reassigned blind.** Files that were 100% Hashimoto's-specific by their own original design (Nutrients, Labs & Medication Timing, Healing Stages, Organs & Body Systems, History & Milestones, Pregnancy & Family Planning, Other Autoimmune Diseases -- corroborating evidence curated FOR a Hashimoto's reader, Big Picture -- its own narrative device is a Hashimoto's dosing day) bulk-reassigned to `'hashimotos'`. Files that were 100% condition-agnostic (Food Additives, Food Industry & History, Glossary, Complementary Therapies, Nutrient Interactions, Fermented Foods -- every strain/ferment is real general microbiology, nothing Hashimoto's-exclusive) bulk-reassigned to `'basicHealth'`. Six genuinely mixed files needed real entry-by-entry review: Gut & Microbiome (3 of 17 Hashimoto's-specific -- the vitamin D/CLDN2 entry, the one study actually run on Hashimoto's patients, the molecular-mimicry-to-thyroid entry -- the rest is condition-agnostic gut-barrier biology), Lifestyle & Environment (11 of 21 route through a specific thyroid mechanism -- cortisol/HPA-axis-to-deiodinase, IL-6-to-deiodinase, TSH's own diurnal rhythm, the Hashimoto's-vs-Graves' smoking split, EBV found inside Hashimoto's thyroid tissue specifically, COVID-19 as a documented thyroid trigger, sleep apnea's thyroid association, perchlorate's NIS-inhibition mechanism), Mitochondria & Metabolism (10 of 13 Hashimoto's-specific by original design, 3 general enough -- visceral-fat-as-endotoxin-barrier, exercise intensity's null effect, exercise's autophagy mechanism -- to stay basicHealth), and Problem Foods & Swaps (7 of 16 route through a specific thyroid mechanism -- gluten's zonulin pathway framed for Hashimoto's, raw goitrogenic crucifers' iodine-uptake interference, sugar's cortisol-to-T4/T3 route, soy's TPO-enzyme interference, coffee's levothyroxine-absorption timing, kelp/iodine's Wolff-Chaikoff mechanism, the gluten-free-without-celiac antibody finding).

**Self Advocacy dissolved into per-condition content, the request's own explicit fourth point.** The old shared `selfAdvocacy.ts` category (25 entries) split the same way: 16 genuinely Hashimoto's-specific entries (TSH/FT4/FT3, TPO/Tg antibodies, reverse T3, ferritin, vitamin D, B12/folate, selenium, the CMP's liver/kidney-thyroid framing, the lipid-panel/thyroid link, A1c's Hashimoto's-T1D comorbidity framing, cortisol/APS-2, the elimination-protocol exception, the TSH-range debate, seronegative Hashimoto's, the fibromyalgia overlap, and the closing testing calendar) now carry `category: 'hashimotos'`; 9 genuinely condition-agnostic entries (why patient activation matters at all, magnesium's own testing limitation, zinc/copper, CBC, fasting insulin, hs-CRP, sex-hormone/perimenopause testing, how to actually ask a doctor, the prescribing-cascade concept) carry `category: 'basicHealth'`. The file itself still holds all 25 -- only `category` changed, matching this whole restructure's own standing practice of keeping one-topic-per-file for authoring while `category` does the real grouping.

**RA and Psoriasis each got real, new, disease-specific self-advocacy content added directly to their own files, researched fresh rather than left thin.** RA gained 4 entries (RF vs. anti-CCP antibody testing, with anti-CCP's real ~98% specificity advantage; methotrexate's own ACR-guideline monitoring interval, 8-12 weeks for liver enzymes; RA's roughly doubled cardiovascular risk and the real EULAR recommendation to assess it routinely; glucocorticoid-linked bone density, with the real 3-month risk threshold and DEXA screening). Psoriasis gained 4 entries (the PEST psoriatic-arthritis screening tool -- a real, validated 5-question checklist, 92% sensitivity/78% specificity, NICE-recommended annually; psoriasis's own documented status as an independent cardiovascular risk factor with a real 1.5x risk-score multiplier for severe disease; a genuinely useful, slightly uncomfortable finding that dermatology's own methotrexate-monitoring guidelines are looser than rheumatology's for the identical drug, tracking with worse real-world outcomes; a topical-vitamin-D-analog calcium caveat for extensive body-surface coverage). RA grew from 9 to 13 entries; Psoriasis from 11 to 15.

**A real, structurally confirming result from testing the reused sub-criteria architecture**: nothing about this restructure required any change to how food-level condition scoring works (`getFoodScoresForCondition()`, `sub_criterion_condition_relevance` -- see the RA/Psoriasis Status entries directly above) -- confirming that mechanism and the Digest's own `category` field were always two independent systems, correctly.

**Two real self-inflicted syntax bugs caught by `tsc`, not shipped**: two separate `Edit` calls (on `lifestyleEnvironment.ts` and `mitochondriaMetabolism.ts`) accidentally dropped the file's own `export const ..._ENTRIES: DigestEntry[] = [` line while rewriting the header comment immediately above it -- both caught instantly by a full `npx tsc --noEmit` pass (a cascade of `';' expected`/`Expression expected` errors makes this exact mistake unmistakable) and fixed by re-adding the missing line. A third near-miss, caught by `git diff`-style comparison before committing: a `PMC11827834` citation was mistyped as `PMC11827864` in a newly-written entry, caught because the `old_string` in the very next Edit call, copied verbatim from the file, didn't match what I'd actually typed -- the mismatch itself was the signal, not a separate check.

**Whole-directory verification**: 330 unique ids across the whole Digest (322 before this pass + 8 new self-advocacy entries), zero duplicates, zero dangling `relatedIds` -- confirmed via the same grep-based Node.js script this Digest's build has used throughout. `PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION` bumped a second time the same day (double-checked as numerically later than the prior value). `DIGEST_CATEGORY_META`, `DIGEST_LENS_HELP` (the `Record<DigestCategoryKey, HelpSection>` type's own compile-time completeness check caught every stale key automatically, the same safety net it's caught real gaps with before), and `DIGEST_GRID_LABEL_BREAKS` (now empty -- all 4 real category labels are short enough to need no forced line break) all rewritten in `index.ts`/`purple-digest.tsx`. Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit` (clean across the whole project) and `npx eslint` on every touched file (clean; a full-project `eslint` pass separately confirmed every remaining error/warning anywhere in the repo is pre-existing and unrelated to this change). Metro restarted cleanly (the process tree from the Psoriasis restart earlier the same day killed via PowerShell, relaunched through the real `npm start -- --clear` path, confirmed via the log's own "Bundler cache is empty, rebuilding" line). **Not yet exercised on-device** -- this is a large structural change to how the whole Digest is browsed (4 categories instead of 20, each now much larger), worth a real walkthrough before trusting it fully, especially confirming the Search All lens (unaffected by this restructure in principle, since it searches `ALL_DIGEST_ENTRIES` regardless of category) still surfaces everything correctly.

**Explicitly deferred, not done this pass**: visual sub-grouping within the now-much-larger Basic Health (177 entries) and Hashimoto's (133 entries) lenses -- e.g., a food-list-style grouped-header treatment by original topic -- was considered and deliberately not built, to keep this pass scoped to the actual category-level reorganization requested.
