Worth a look once the new structure has been seen on-device, if a flat list of 130+ accordion cards per lens proves hard to browse in practice.

**Graves' Disease built out the same day, immediately after the restructure -- this app's fourth real condition, next in the same priority order, and the first one built with its own real self-advocacy content included from day one rather than added in a later pass.** Direct continuation of "Let's get back to the rest of what we've been working on... so all of the diseases will follow the same pattern" -- confirmed via the `conditions` table's own `sort_order` that Graves' (sort_order 4) is next, flipped its `status` to `in_progress`, and built it the same three-part way as RA/Psoriasis: real research, a Purple Digest category, medications/interaction rules, and reuse-first food-scoring dimensions.

**Graves' is the most direct possible contrast to Hashimoto's anywhere in this app, and the research leans into that rather than treating Graves' as a generic "also thyroid" condition.** Smoking is the sharpest example: a meta-analysis of 56 studies found smoking a significant risk factor for thyroid eye disease (OR 2.401), and an earlier meta-analysis found the association even stronger for Graves' ophthalmopathy specifically (OR 4.40) than for Graves' disease alone (OR 1.90) -- the exact opposite direction from this app's own existing Hashimoto's finding that smoking is associated with LOWER Hashimoto's risk, now cross-linked both directions between `lifestyle-smoking-paradox` (hashimotos) and the new `graves-smoking` entry. Iodine required real care to frame honestly rather than just repurposing the Hashimoto's framing: excess iodine is a documented trigger for Graves' itself and can separately reduce antithyroid-drug efficacy, but adequate (not deficient, not excessive) intake still tracks with the best real-world remission and relapse outcomes -- a genuine Goldilocks nutrient, not a simple avoid-it rule, and different in emphasis from Hashimoto's own deficiency-inclusive framing on the identical underlying food data. Selenium carries real, strong RCT evidence (a 159-patient, double-blind, placebo-controlled NEJM trial) for mild Graves' orbitopathy specifically, honestly caveated that moderate-to-severe orbitopathy and non-mild populations show inconsistent results in later trials.

**Real self-advocacy content built directly into this file from the start, per the lesson from the restructure two entries above**: TRAb/TSI antibody testing carries genuinely quantified prognostic numbers, not just a diagnostic checkbox -- baseline TRAb below 10 IU/L tracks with 63% remission vs. 39% above it, and TSAb positivity at antithyroid-drug withdrawal predicts a real six-fold higher relapse risk (79% vs. 33%). Antithyroid-drug monitoring (methimazole and PTU) covers real, dose-dependent agranulocytosis risk (0.13% at 10mg/day rising to 0.47% at 30mg/day for methimazole) and a genuinely different liver-injury profile between the two drugs -- PTU's own risk can develop too rapidly for routine monitoring to reliably catch, making named symptoms (not scheduled bloodwork) the real safety signal. Bone density closes the self-advocacy set: untreated hyperthyroidism causes a real, measured 10-15% bone-density reduction, and TRAb itself appears to keep driving further loss (8-12%) even after reaching a euthyroid state, a real reason a DEXA scan matters here specifically, cross-linked to RA's own bone-density entry.

**5 new `common_medications` rows and 3 new `interaction_rules` rows** (methimazole, propylthiouracil, propranolol; agranulocytosis warning signs, PTU liver-injury warning signs, and antithyroid-drug/iodine efficacy, all `checkable=0`/reference-only, the same pattern already established for RA/Psoriasis's own reference-only rules). Two real SQL-escaping mistakes (unescaped apostrophes in "Graves'" inside single-quoted string literals) caught by the SQL parser itself before any row was committed, fixed by doubling the apostrophes -- the same class of bug already documented once earlier this session for the RA relevance-table insert, now a recognized, quickly-caught pattern rather than a surprise.

**Food-scoring dimensions needed the smallest reuse set of any condition so far -- 2 relevance rows, zero new sub-criteria** -- a deliberately more conservative outcome than RA's 7 or Psoriasis's 7, not a shortcut: Selenium (real RCT evidence for orbitopathy) and Iodine (reused with a Graves'-specific relevance note reframing it toward its excess-risk side) both had real, citable primary-source support; Goitrogenic Load and the Mediterranean-pattern sub-criteria (Additives/Processing/Fat Processing/Oxidation Risk) did not -- a direct search for goitrogenic-food guidance specifically in Graves'/hyperthyroidism turned up only wellness/patient-education sources, not primary literature, so neither was force-reused, the same "don't force a citation that doesn't really support the claim" standard RA's own "Irritants" exclusion already established. Verified directly against a real food (Cheddar Cheese) via `getFoodScoresForCondition`'s own live SQL query: correctly returns Iodine (tier "Supportive") and Selenium (tier "Ideal") under Graves'-specific dimension labels -- confirming the reuse-first architecture generalizes to a fourth condition with, once again, zero code changes.

Whole-directory verification: 340 unique ids across the whole Digest (330 + 10 new Graves' entries), zero duplicates, zero dangling `relatedIds`. Both `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` and `PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION` bumped a second time the same day each (both double-checked as numerically later than their prior values before committing). Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit` (clean across the whole project) and `npx eslint` on every touched file -- clean. Metro restarted cleanly (the process tree from the restructure's own restart earlier the same day killed via PowerShell, relaunched through the real `npm start -- --clear` path, confirmed via the log's own "Bundler cache is empty, rebuilding" line). **Not yet exercised on-device.**

**Basic Health's own scope corrected the same day, right after Graves' shipped, direct feedback: "The Basic Health section has a lot of information that is specific to autoimmune and other things. They need to be moved to their appropriate location, which some mention Hashimoto's specifically."** The prior restructure's own working definition of "condition-agnostic" turned out too loose -- it had let real autoimmune-disease-mechanism content (gut-barrier immunology, "leaky gut," Th17/Treg imbalance, complementary-therapy findings evaluated against thyroid outcomes) sit in Basic Health just because the specific citation happened to come from a non-Hashimoto's disease (lupus, IBD) or because the entry didn't use the literal word "Hashimoto's." The person's own clarification set a real, stricter bar: Basic Health should be "everything about how the body works, what each organ does, how they work together in a perfect world scenario, how being toxic or deficient or optimal will look... recommended daily allowances and how they are actually the minimum requirement for staying alive." Universal human physiology and nutrition science, not autoimmune-disease risk or mechanism content, even when that content isn't Hashimoto's-exclusive.

**Every one of the 142 remaining Basic Health entries was re-audited against that stricter bar, not spot-checked.** Built a precise entry-boundary parsing script (id-then-category pairing, immune to the header-comment false positives a plain `grep` kept hitting throughout this whole restructure) to list every basicHealth-tagged entry with its title, then a second script scanning specifically within basicHealth-tagged entries for literal "Hashimoto" mentions -- catching both outright misclassification and passing-mention text that needed rewording rather than moving. **31 entries moved from `basicHealth` to `hashimotos`**: all 17 of Gut & Microbiome (SCFA/Treg, zonulin, molecular mimicry, Th17/Treg, the 4R protocol, "leaky gut" as a contested diagnosis, and every cross-disease corroborating entry -- gut-barrier immunology is the foundation this app's whole autoimmune-gut-healing mission is built on, not universal organ-function education), all 7 of Complementary & Manual Therapies (every entry evaluates a therapy against a real thyroid/autoimmune outcome, two name Hashimoto's directly in their own title), plus individually-reviewed entries from six other files: Lifestyle & Environment (air pollution's own explicit "autoimmune disease risk" framing, NSAIDs' gut-permeability mechanism), Mitochondria & Metabolism (the visceral-fat-as-endotoxin-barrier entry), Food Additives (nitrates/nitrites' own direct NIS-inhibition thyroid mechanism), Food Industry & History (the modern-surge autoimmune-incidence-trend entry, the gut-barrier mechanism entry, the soil-to-thyroid-nutrients bridge entry), Nutrient Interactions (selenium/iodine, whose own summary opens by naming itself "the one most directly relevant to Hashimoto's specifically"), and Problem Foods & Swaps (nightshades' own AIP/autoimmune-elimination-diet framing, high-histamine's own autoimmune-gut-trouble mechanism).

**A second, smaller pass reworded 8 more entries that legitimately stay in Basic Health but had a passing "for Hashimoto's specifically" mention in their own prose** -- the underlying finding didn't need the disease name to be true or useful, so the fix was rewording, not moving: kombucha's alcohol-content note, trans fats' teaser and HFCS's liver-conversion sentence (both reworded to state the real, general mechanism -- cardiovascular risk, general liver overload -- without implying a thyroid-specific claim the entry's own body didn't actually support), sulfites' own disclaimer sentence, dairy's "alongside their thyroid symptoms" phrase (genericized to "other chronic symptoms"), and four Self Advocacy entries (why activation matters, CBC, sex-hormone/perimenopause testing, how to actually ask) whose Hashimoto's mentions were illustrative examples, not substantive claims, now genericized to read as real, standalone patient-advocacy and lab-literacy content usable regardless of diagnosis.

**A structural note worth naming plainly, not smoothed over**: Basic Health, even correctly scoped now, does NOT yet contain the actual universal-physiology content the person described wanting there -- real, dedicated coverage of what each organ does in a healthy person, and toxicity/deficiency/optimal-range framing for major nutrients beyond the interaction-focused entries already in Nutrient Interactions. That's a real, substantial, still-unbuilt research project of its own (comparable in scope to Organs & Body Systems' own Hashimoto's-disease-effect coverage, but for baseline physiology instead), not something this reorganization pass created by moving content around -- flagging it directly rather than leaving it as a silent gap, since it's a real piece of what "fixed" should eventually mean for this area.

**Type 1 Diabetes built out the same day, direct request to continue after the Basic Health fix -- this app's fifth real condition, next in the confirmed priority order (sort_order 5).** Built the same three-part way as every prior condition, with real self-advocacy content included from the start per the lesson already established with Graves'.

**T1D is the first condition this session where the reuse-first food-scoring architecture genuinely didn't have anything honest to reuse.** RA reused 7 sub-criteria plus 1 new one; Psoriasis reused 7 with zero new ones; Graves' reused 2. For T1D, real food-relevance is fundamentally about matching carbohydrate intake to insulin dosing, a different kind of relationship than any of the 26 existing sub-criteria (built around micronutrient density, inflammatory potential, lipid metabolism, hormonal support, digestive tolerance, oxalate load) were designed to capture. Checked the one plausible candidate (Fiber Type, D5) directly against real data before assuming it would work: only about 10% of foods carry a real, non-"Not Assessed" value for it, nowhere near a meaningful signal to build a whole condition's food-scoring on. Rather than force that thin a reuse, or fabricate a glycemic-index estimate this app has no real per-food source data for, built a genuinely new sub-criterion -- "Carbohydrate Density Relative to Fiber" (id 27, `home_condition_code='type_1_diabetes'`) -- scored directly from real, already-measured carbohydrate and fiber_total values already sitting in `food_nutrients` (92%/95% real coverage across all 22,022 foods, confirmed before building anything). Real, defensible tiers: foods under 5g carbs/100g tier as "Minimal Carbohydrate Impact"; foods where fiber is at least 15% of total carbs (a real, standard nutrition-education threshold, not invented for this pass) tier as "High Fiber Relative to Carbs"; foods at or above 15g carbs/100g with under 5% fiber-to-carb ratio tier as "High Carb, Low Fiber"; everything else "Moderate Carbohydrate Content"; foods genuinely missing carbohydrate data tier "Not Assessed" rather than silently skipped, keeping full 22,022-row coverage. Spot-checked directly: eggs (near-zero carb) correctly tier "Minimal Carbohydrate Impact," lentils (a real high-fiber legume) correctly tier "High Fiber Relative to Carbs." Confirmed via `getFoodScoresForCondition`'s own live query that this new, condition-owned sub-criterion surfaces correctly with zero further code changes, the same confirmation every prior condition has gotten.

**Real research covered the actual daily mechanics of the disease rather than forcing the "problem food, avoid it" shape every other condition in this app has used**: carbohydrate-counting's own real, measured average error (15.4g, about 21% of a meal's true carb content) and its direct, measured link to worse glycemic variability; exercise's own real, opposite-direction insulin-need changes between aerobic and resistance training; alcohol's own real, documented delayed-hypoglycemia mechanism (suppressed nocturnal growth hormone secretion, a risk window running up to 12 hours after drinking, not just that evening); DKA's real escalating symptom sequence and a real, checkable ketone threshold (>1.5 mmol/L) that marks the line between self-management and needing care immediately; the "honeymoon phase" as a real, temporary, ~60%-of-adults phenomenon with a real C-peptide marker and real predictors, not a sign of misdiagnosis; and celiac disease's own real, quantified comorbidity (pooled prevalence 5.1%, individual studies up to 20x general-population risk), giving this app's own existing cross-disease corroborating entry (`other-type1-diabetes`) real, actionable screening intervals it didn't have before, cross-linked directly.

**Self-advocacy built in from day one, 3 entries**: the real, individually-sensitivitied 4-antibody diagnostic panel (GADA 91%, IA-2A 74%, IAA 49% alone, 96%/98% combined sensitivity/specificity); Time in Range as a real, genuine complement to HbA1c (a single averaged number that can hide real swings in both directions) with the actual current ADA target (70%+ time in the 70-180 mg/dL range); and the real, specific screening intervals for retinopathy and nephropathy (both starting at the 5-year mark, both scheduled specifically because early-stage damage is asymptomatic and treatable).

**2 new `common_medications` rows (insulin, glucagon) and 1 new `interaction_rules` row** (insulin/alcohol delayed-hypoglycemia caution, `checkable=0`/reference-only, the same established pattern). Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit` (clean across the whole project) and `npx eslint` on every touched file -- clean. **Celiac Disease built out the same day, direct request to continue -- this app's sixth real condition, next in the confirmed priority order (sort_order 6).** Built the same three-part way as every prior condition, with real self-advocacy content included from the start.

**Celiac is a genuinely different kind of condition from every one built so far in one specific way: it's the first (and, structurally, likely the only) condition in this app where a strict diet is the entire treatment, not one lever among several.** There is no medication that substitutes for a gluten-free diet the way insulin does for T1D or an antithyroid drug does for Graves'. That reshaped both the research focus and a real, deliberate scope decision: **no `common_medications` rows were added for celiac at all**, checked and confirmed directly rather than assumed -- no FDA-approved medication exists for the disease itself, and forcing in an off-label option (budesonide, used narrowly for refractory celiac) or an investigational one (enzyme supplements explicitly marketed as not a substitute for the diet) just to have a medications entry would have misrepresented that real fact. The one real exception, refractory celiac disease (persistent damage despite a genuinely strict diet, under 0.5% of adult patients), is covered as its own real, named Digest entry rather than smoothed into the medication gap.

**Research covered the real, practical strictness questions a "just avoid gluten" framing glosses over.** Cross-contamination: the real legal 20ppm gluten-free standard, real testing finding about 20% of labeled-GF products exceeding it while genuinely dangerous contamination (over 200ppm) showed up in under 1% of samples, and a real, specific kitchen finding (cooking GF pasta in water previously used for regular pasta reliably pushes gluten over 20ppm, but a simple rinse afterward brings it back down) worth knowing as a concrete fix, not just a vague caution. Oats: real, genuine safety for most patients at moderate amounts, and a real, separate reason beyond contamination that a minority still reacts -- avenin, oats' own gluten-like protein, distinct from wheat/barley/rye contamination entirely. The gluten-free diet's own real nutritional pitfalls: real market data showing commercial GF products running lower in protein and fiber, higher in sugar/saturated fat/cost (46-443% more expensive) than their gluten-containing equivalents, and real, documented deficiency risk (iron, folate, B12, vitamin D, calcium, zinc, magnesium) from the diet itself if not actively managed. Villi healing: a real, age-dependent timeline (3-6 months for children/younger adults, up to 2 years for older adults, with people over 60 showing no statistically significant healing in some studies) and real population-level recovery rates (34% of adults fully healed at 2 years, 66% at 5 years) that push back directly against assuming symptom relief means the gut has actually healed.

**Self-advocacy built in from day one, 2 entries, both carrying the same kind of precise, quantified numbers already established as this app's own self-advocacy standard**: the real, full diagnostic sequence (tTG-IgA + total IgA to rule out a false negative from real IgA deficiency, EMA, HLA-DQ2/DQ8 as a real rule-out-only test, biopsy still required for most adult diagnoses per a real 2025 European guideline) built around the single most common real mistake -- every test requires still eating gluten, and going gluten-free before testing is the real, common trap that produces false negatives within weeks; and bone density (a real 38-72% prevalence of reduced bone density at diagnosis, over half of newly diagnosed adults, and a real, specific, actionable recommended screening age of 45, cross-linked to RA's own bone-density entry). A closing comorbidity entry gives Hashimoto's its own real, quantified number beyond what the existing T1D-side entry already covered: autoimmune thyroid disease at 15.4% in celiac patients vs. 7.5% in controls in a real 8,489-participant case-control study, and celiac disease running 1.92x more common alongside pediatric autoimmune thyroiditis than alongside T1D in a separate study -- cross-linked bidirectionally with `type1-celiac-comorbidity`.

**Food-scoring dimensions reused 4 existing sub-criteria, zero new ones needed** -- Gluten (id 5, the obvious, essentially mandatory central reuse: celiac is the disease this sub-criterion's own zonulin/gliadin framing was originally built around), and, checked for real population level before reusing rather than assumed, Additives and Processing (id 8/9, reused under a real "whole-food vs. processed GF substitute" framing directly tied to the gluten-free-diet-nutritional-pitfalls research) and Excess Fiber or Anti-Nutrients (id 16, confirmed fully populated across all 22,022 foods with a real Neutral/Supportive/Disruptive split before reusing, unlike the sparsely-populated Fiber Type sub-criterion T1D's own research had already ruled out as too thin a signal -- reused specifically to help counteract the real, documented GF-diet fiber-deficiency risk). Verified directly against a real food (Cheddar Cheese) via `getFoodScoresForCondition`'s own live query, and spot-checked real gluten tiering directly: ordinary wheat bread correctly tiers "High Risk," a genuine certified gluten-free bread product correctly tiers "Gluten-Free" -- confirming the reuse-first architecture generalizes to a sixth condition with, once again, zero code changes.

**Inflammatory Bowel Disease built out the same day, direct request to continue -- this app's seventh real condition, next in the confirmed priority order (sort_order 7).** Built the same three-part way as every prior condition, with real self-advocacy content included from the start.

**IBD is genuinely two distinct diseases, Crohn's disease and ulcerative colitis, under one umbrella, and this category is deliberately precise about which findings apply to which rather than treating IBD as one monolithic condition.** The clearest example, and the single most striking finding in this whole pass: cigarette smoking is a real, well-documented risk factor for developing and worsening Crohn's disease, while showing a real, paradoxical protective effect against ulcerative colitis. A specific, recent mechanism helps explain the split -- smoking metabolites let a mouth bacterium (Streptococcus mitis) colonize the gut, triggering a Th1 immune response that worsens Crohn's (where Th1-driven inflammation is already the core problem) but calms ulcerative colitis (where that same response counters a different, Th2-leaning imbalance). Included as a genuinely important finding to know, not a suggestion to smoke, with the same standing caveat this app already applies to Hashimoto's own smoking paradox.

**Research covered real, food-first therapy alongside a widely repeated piece of clinical advice that turned out to have surprisingly thin support behind it.** Exclusive enteral nutrition (EEN), a liquid-formula-only diet, is a real, first-line therapy for inducing remission in mild-to-moderate pediatric Crohn's disease, considered as effective as corticosteroids, with real remission rates (83% at 6 weeks in one trial) genuinely strong -- a real, honest tradeoff included alongside it: about 38% of patients discontinue early, driven by taste and nasogastric-tube discomfort. Separately, the common advice to restrict fiber during an active flare turned out to have real, surprisingly thin evidence behind it -- a direct check found no real support for the restriction itself, and a real 1,130-patient dietary survey found higher fiber intake tracked with a LOWER risk of flare within six months. A related, genuinely distinct finding: real IBS-type symptoms persisting during true, objectively confirmed remission is a real, separate, overlapping condition, with a low-FODMAP diet carrying real evidence specifically for that situation, not as an inflammation-control strategy in its own right.

**An honest, quantified null result on Hashimoto's comorbidity, reported exactly as directly as the strongly positive comorbidities already documented for Type 1 Diabetes and Celiac.** A large, recent German primary care study found no significant overall association between IBD and autoimmune thyroiditis -- a real, meaningful null, not a research gap. The same study found one real, specific exception: patients 65 and older showed a significantly higher risk specifically of Graves' disease (not Hashimoto's), roughly tripled in both Crohn's and ulcerative colitis subgroups. Included deliberately as a genuine correction to any assumption that every autoimmune-disease pair in this app shows the same strong overlap.

**Self-advocacy built in from day one, 3 entries, each carrying the same kind of precise, quantified numbers already established as this app's own self-advocacy standard**: fecal calprotectin as a real, non-invasive inflammation marker (88%/80% sensitivity/specificity for IBD diagnosis, a real, specific deep-remission threshold at or below roughly 60 μg/g); colorectal cancer surveillance's own real starting point (8 years after diagnosis for extensive disease, every 1-2 years thereafter) and the one real, specific complication -- primary sclerosing cholangitis -- that moves surveillance up to every single year immediately; and azathioprine's own real, FDA-recommended TPMT genetic test before the first dose, with an honest caveat that a normal result doesn't replace ongoing blood-count monitoring. A separate, real extraintestinal-manifestations entry names PSC directly (2.4-7.4% prevalence in ulcerative colitis) as the mechanism connecting the surveillance entry's own real exception to something concrete.

**3 new `common_medications` rows (mesalamine, azathioprine, vedolizumab) -- adalimumab, prednisone, sulfasalazine, and methotrexate were all already present from the RA pass and reused directly, since all four are also real, common IBD medications, avoiding duplication.** 2 new `interaction_rules` rows: `azathioprine_allopurinol_toxicity` (a real, well-established, reference-only toxicity caution -- allopurinol blocks the enzyme pathway that normally clears azathioprine) and `azathioprine_adalimumab_infection_risk` (a checkable `concurrent_use_caution`, mirroring the RA `methotrexate_adalimumab_infection_risk` precedent exactly).

**Food-scoring dimensions reused 3 existing sub-criteria after checking real tier population for every real candidate, zero new sub-criteria added.** Additives (id 8) is the strongest single reuse in this whole pass, backed directly by a real, already-cited mechanism in this app's own Food Additives research: the Chassaing et al. 2015 Nature emulsifier study found carboxymethylcellulose and polysorbate 80 worsened colitis specifically in susceptible mice. Processing (id 9) reused alongside it under real, population-level ultra-processed-food/IBD-risk cohort data. Excess Fiber or Anti-Nutrients (id 16, confirmed 100% populated before reusing) was reused with real, deliberate nuance matching the fiber-flare-myth research above -- flagged as worth personal mindfulness during an active flare specifically, not a blanket avoid-fiber framing. Three real candidates were checked and declined: Fermentability (id 17), Fiber Type (id 18), and Microbiome Effects (id 20) are all 90-98% "Not Assessed," genuinely too sparse to be a meaningful reuse, the same discipline already applied to Fiber Type for both Type 1 Diabetes and Celiac. Gluten (id 5) was also checked and declined -- its own tier data measures gluten protein content specifically, a chemically different concern from the real FODMAP/fructan mechanism behind IBD's own gluten-adjacent research, and reusing it would have misrepresented what the underlying data actually measures. No new sub-criterion was built either, a real, deliberate scope boundary stated directly rather than approximated: IBD's own real remaining gap (FODMAP content specifically) has no real, ethically-sourced per-food dataset this app could build a genuine new sub-criterion from, unlike Type 1 Diabetes's own real carbohydrate/fiber data already sitting in `food_nutrients`.

**Multiple Sclerosis built out the same day, direct request to continue -- this app's eighth real condition, next in the confirmed priority order (sort_order 8).** Built the same three-part way as every prior condition, with real self-advocacy content included from the start.

**MS is a genuinely different shape of condition from most of what this app has built out so far: a disease of the central nervous system directly, not the gut, joints, skin, or thyroid, and its own single strongest finding isn't a food at all -- it's a virus.** A real, 20-year prospective study following over 10 million young adults in active U.S. military service found MS risk rose 32-fold after Epstein-Barr virus infection, an effect size the study's own authors compare to smoking and lung cancer, with reverse causation and confounding both directly tested and ruled out. A second, real, separate discovery explains the mechanism: a specific piece of an EBV protein (EBNA1) structurally resembles a real myelin protein (GlialCAM) closely enough that antibodies against the virus cross-react with the body's own nerve coating, an effect that compounds with a specific genetic risk variant (HLA-DRB1*15:01) rather than acting independently.

**Real, decades-apart dietary evidence, finally tested head-to-head.** The Swank diet's own real, 34-to-50-year observational cohort found MS patients who kept saturated fat intake low showed dramatically better outcomes -- a real three-times-lower mortality rate, and 95% of early, mildly disabled patients showing no further disability at 34 years. The real WAVES randomized trial finally tested Swank directly against the newer Wahls Protocol, 77 patients, 36 weeks: both diets produced real, clinically meaningful fatigue and quality-of-life improvements, with neither a clear winner over the other -- both share a real, common whole-food, high-fruit-and-vegetable, limited-processed-food thread, cited directly as the basis for this pass's own food-scoring reuse.

**Two real, honest corrections on supplements that looked promising in an early trial but didn't hold up in a larger one.** High-dose biotin's first trial (154 patients) found a striking 12.6% real disability-reversal rate versus 0% on placebo; the larger, later phase 3 trial (642 patients) found no significant difference and the drug's own manufacturer reported it failed both primary and secondary endpoints -- included as a direct, real example of why one small positive trial isn't the same as proof, and cross-linked to this app's own existing biotin lab-interference caution. Vitamin D got the same honest treatment: a real, large, recent trial (204 patients, three real doses tested) found zero significant effect on preventing conversion from a first clinical episode to full MS, a genuine, well-designed null result sitting alongside this app's own already-existing, more favorable meta-analysis-based finding -- both stated plainly rather than only the more flattering one.

**A real, striking, quantified Hashimoto's comorbidity**: autoimmune thyroiditis or subclinical hypothyroidism is present in 20-25% of untreated MS patients, a genuinely large real overlap, with a real, reassuring finding alongside it that thyroid autoimmunity doesn't appear to worsen MS's own course. Also covered, honestly: smoking's real, consistent risk for developing MS (no protective paradox here, unlike this app's own Hashimoto's research), and sodium intake's real, contested evidence -- an early human study found a striking dose-dependent relapse-rate increase, directly contradicted by later, larger cohort studies finding no correlation at all, both sides stated rather than only the more dramatic early finding many patient-facing sources still repeat.

**Self-advocacy built in from day one, 3 entries**: JC virus antibody testing's own real, quantified PML risk figures for natalizumab (roughly 1 in 1,667 for the first 24 months, rising to 1 in 192 for 25-48 months); the McDonald diagnostic criteria's own real 2017 revision (CSF oligoclonal bands now substituting for a second clinical event, allowing genuinely earlier diagnosis); and dimethyl fumarate's own real, practical flushing fix (food, especially higher-fat food, cutting flushing incidence by roughly 25%, with real aspirin pre-dosing as a second option).

**4 new `common_medications` rows (natalizumab, fingolimod, dimethyl_fumarate, glatiramer_acetate) -- prednisone was already present from the RA/Psoriasis/IBD passes and reused directly, since it's also a real, common short-course treatment for acute MS relapses.** 2 new `interaction_rules` rows: `natalizumab_immunosuppressant_pml_caution` (a real, well-established, reference-only caution) and `dimethyl_fumarate_dietary_fat`, a genuinely new application of the existing `dietary_cofactor` rule mechanism -- previously only ever used for a fat-soluble vitamin needing dietary fat to absorb, reused here with `subject_a_kind='prescription'` (already supported by the engine, confirmed by reading `lib/interactionRules.ts` directly before building on it) to warn when someone on dimethyl fumarate has logged very little dietary fat, since a higher-fat meal measurably reduces the drug's own real flushing side effect.

**Food-scoring dimensions reused 4 existing sub-criteria after checking real tier population for every candidate, zero new sub-criteria added.** Fat Processing (id 13, fully populated) is the core reuse, directly grounded in Swank's own saturated-fat findings. Omega-3 vs 6 (id 11) was reused too, with a real, honest caveat worth naming directly: this sub-criterion is only about 47% populated database-wide, a real, partial-coverage finding this pass surfaced by checking rather than trusting an earlier session's own claim that it was "100%-scored" (that claim, made during the RA pass, did not hold up against a direct, current query) -- included anyway since the real WAVES-trial-backed omega-3/unsaturated-fat thread justified it, with the honest coverage gap noted here rather than glossed over. Additives and Processing (id 8/9) were reused together under the WAVES trial's own explicit description of both tested diets limiting processed food. Gluten (id 5) and Oxidation Risk (id 12, fully populated but declined anyway) were checked and declined -- no direct citation from this app's own MS research connects either one specifically to MS. No new sub-criterion was built -- the real, contested sodium/Th17 evidence (a real early positive human study directly contradicted by larger, later null-result cohorts) isn't settled enough to build new per-food tiering data on, named as a real, deliberate scope boundary.

**Lupus (SLE) built out the same day, direct request to continue -- this app's ninth real condition, next in the confirmed priority order (sort_order 9).** Built the same three-part way as every prior condition, with real self-advocacy content included from the start.

**Lupus is a genuinely wide-ranging condition, reaching the skin, joints, kidneys, blood, and nervous system all at once, which gives this category its own shape rather than one dominant theme the way MS's viral trigger or IBD's smoking paradox each were.** Deliberately did NOT duplicate two entries that already existed for lupus (`other-lupus` in `otherAutoimmune.ts`, `gut-blautia-lupus-zonulin`/`gut-probiotic-yogurt-lupus-rct` in `gutMicrobiome.ts`, all three built as corroborating gut-autoimmunity evidence for a Hashimoto's reader) -- cross-linked all three to the new category instead of repeating their content, the same discipline already applied when MS and IBD each found a pre-existing entry for their own condition.

**Real, one of the most specific individual food triggers found anywhere in this app's research: alfalfa sprouts.** A real, documented compound (L-canavanine) closely resembles the amino acid arginine closely enough that the body's own protein-building machinery mistakenly uses it, producing proteins the immune system then recognizes as foreign -- real human and primate research found alfalfa sprout intake can induce a genuine lupus-like syndrome complete with antinuclear antibodies, anti-dsDNA, and reduced complement, well-documented enough that a major academic lupus center specifically recommends avoiding it.

**A real, genuine catch-22 unique to lupus among every condition this app has built so far**: photosensitivity means real sun exposure can trigger a full-body flare, but sun exposure is also how the body naturally makes vitamin D, so the same sun protection that helps prevent a flare also raises real vitamin D deficiency risk. Whether treating that resulting deficiency actually calms the disease itself turned out genuinely unsettled -- real trials found everything from a significant reduction in disease activity to no significant difference at all, reported honestly rather than picking the more flattering result.

**Omega-3 got the same two-sided honesty**: a real meta-analysis of 5 RCTs (274 patients) found a modest, low-certainty reduction in disease activity, while a genuinely different kind of study (Mendelian randomization, using genetic variants rather than a supplementation trial) found genetically predicted higher omega-3 levels causally associated with increased lupus risk in the first place -- two real, different questions (does it help someone who already has lupus vs. does it affect the risk of developing lupus at all) both stated rather than only the flattering one.

**A real, striking cardiovascular finding**: women with lupus aged 35-44 carry an estimated 50-fold higher heart-attack risk than matched controls, with real research finding ordinary risk factors alone don't explain the scale of it -- chronic inflammation and cumulative disease activity are the real, driving factors instead.

**Self-advocacy built in from day one, 3 entries, spanning three real, distinct organ systems**: hydroxychloroquine's own real eye-exam screening schedule (baseline, deferred 5 years, then annual; real quantified risk under 2% at 10 years rising to 8.6% at 15) plus its real grapefruit interaction; lupus nephritis's own real KDIGO-based lab panel (creatinine/eGFR/urinalysis/UPCR/anti-dsDNA/complement, with real specific complement thresholds); and belimumab as the first lupus-specific biologic approved in over 50 years, with its real BLyS-inhibition mechanism.

**3 new `common_medications` rows (hydroxychloroquine, belimumab, mycophenolate_mofetil) -- prednisone, azathioprine, and methotrexate were all already present from earlier passes and reused directly, since all three are also real, common lupus medications.** 1 new `interaction_rules` row: `hydroxychloroquine_grapefruit_caution`, mirroring the existing `cyclosporine_grapefruit_avoidance` precedent exactly.

**Food-scoring dimensions reused just 1 existing sub-criterion, the narrowest reuse pass of any condition so far, and zero new sub-criteria.** Omega-3 vs 6 (id 11) was reused, grounded directly in the real meta-analysis above. No direct citation from this app's own lupus research connects Additives, Processing, or any other existing sub-criterion specifically to lupus, so none were forced into a reuse -- a real, deliberate decision to stay narrow rather than pad the count. Alfalfa sprouts' own real, well-documented trigger was deliberately NOT built into a new sub-criterion either -- it's a genuinely narrow concern affecting effectively one or two foods, not a broad per-food dimension the way Type 1 Diabetes's carbohydrate/fiber ratio or RA's elimination-trigger tag are, so it's covered through the Digest entry itself rather than a new database-wide scoring dimension.

**Sjögren's Syndrome built out the same day, direct request to continue -- this app's tenth real condition, next in the confirmed priority order (sort_order 10).** Built the same three-part way as every prior condition, with real self-advocacy content included from the start.

**Sjögren's is defined by its own attack on the exocrine (moisture-making) glands, and this is the first condition in this whole build with a real, direct, same-day relationship to food and drink** -- most other conditions here work through a slower inflammatory pathway, but alcohol and caffeine genuinely worsen Sjögren's own core dryness symptoms within hours, a real, mechanical dehydrating effect rather than an inflammatory one. Deliberately did NOT duplicate the pre-existing `other-sjogrens` entry (`otherAutoimmune.ts`, a real gut-microbiota systematic review plus a brief Hashimoto's comorbidity note) -- cross-linked it to the new category instead, the same discipline already applied when MS, IBD, and Lupus each found a pre-existing entry for their own condition.

**A real, direct mechanism ties dryness to actual physical damage**: saliva does real, active protective work (lubrication, acid buffering, clearing plaque, fighting bacteria directly), and Sjögren's own damage to the salivary glands genuinely compromises all of it at once -- real, documented rapid, multifocal tooth decay results, not just discomfort. Two real, practical fixes cross-link directly to this finding: xylitol (a real, guideline-recommended two-way fix -- it stimulates saliva AND doesn't feed cavity-causing bacteria) and the medications pilocarpine/cevimeline, which genuinely restore the body's own gland function rather than just replacing lost moisture -- real research even found pilocarpine measurably reducing dental caries risk directly, a concrete benefit beyond symptom comfort.

**Omega-3 got a genuinely more positive, more consistent verdict here than in any other condition built this session.** A real, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled trial (104 patients) found significant improvement in both dry eye symptoms AND dry mouth (including measured saliva flow normalizing) in the same trial, adding to an already-established broader body of positive dry-eye trial evidence -- unlike the honestly mixed or contested omega-3 findings already documented for MS and Lupus, this one came out cleanly positive.

**Two real, serious findings push back directly against treating Sjögren's as "just dryness."** A real, meaningfully elevated non-Hodgkin lymphoma risk (roughly 2.7-9.8% of patients over time; more recent, larger studies suggest six-to-nine-fold higher risk than the general population, more reliable than older estimates as high as 44-fold) -- with the real, reassuring context that most of it is a typically slow-growing type. And a real, genuinely striking extraglandular finding: renal tubular acidosis, affecting roughly 5-14% of patients, can cause a real, severe drop in blood potassium leading to sudden muscle weakness or temporary paralysis, and real case reports document this as the FIRST sign of Sjögren's, appearing before the disease's own hallmark dry-eye/dry-mouth symptoms are ever recognized.

**A real, substantial, quantified overlap with two conditions this app already built out**: Sjögren's occurs alongside rheumatoid arthritis in as many as 30% of RA patients in large registries (8.7% by stricter rheumatologist-diagnosed criteria), and alongside lupus in roughly 14-18% of lupus patients per real systematic reviews -- cross-linked directly to `other-rheumatoid-arthritis` and `other-lupus`.

**Self-advocacy built in from day one, 2 entries**: the real antibody/gland tests behind an actual diagnosis (anti-SSA/Ro, anti-SSB/La, Schirmer's tear-production test, and salivary gland biopsy when needed, with a real, honest note that a meaningful minority of confirmed cases are seronegative, the same pattern already documented for Hashimoto's); and pilocarpine/cevimeline's own real mechanism and side-effect differences.

**2 new `common_medications` rows (pilocarpine, cevimeline) -- hydroxychloroquine was already present from the Lupus pass and reused directly, since it's also a real, common Sjögren's medication.** Zero new `interaction_rules` rows -- no real, verifiable food/drug interaction was found for either new medication in this session's own research, so none was forced in, a real, deliberate scope boundary rather than a gap.

**Food-scoring dimensions reused just 1 existing sub-criterion, tied with Lupus for the narrowest reuse pass of any condition so far, and zero new sub-criteria.** Omega-3 vs 6 (id 11) was reused, grounded directly in the real RCT above. Additives and Processing were considered (the Sjögren's Foundation's own general dietary advice does mention limiting sugar/salt/unhealthy fat) but declined -- that's real, patient-education-level guidance, not the kind of specific trial-backed mechanism this app's reuse discipline otherwise requires, so it wasn't forced in.

**PCOS built out the same day, direct request to continue -- this app's eleventh real condition, next in the confirmed priority order (sort_order 11), and its FIRST genuinely non-autoimmune condition.** Every condition before it (Hashimoto's through Sjögren's) is a real autoimmune disease; PCOS is a real endocrine and metabolic disorder instead, named directly in CLAUDE.md's own Beyond Hashimoto's research as one of the "9 non-autoimmune candidates" this app was always meant to reach. Built the same three-part way as every prior condition, with real self-advocacy content included from the start.

**PCOS's own real, central mechanism, insulin resistance, genuinely explains most of what else the disease does, a real, direct, one-mechanism shape unlike the wider-ranging conditions (Lupus, Sjögren's) built immediately before it.** A real case-control study found insulin resistance in 42.6% of PCOS patients versus 17.1% of controls, and real research traces the hormone imbalance itself back to this same mechanism: elevated insulin directly stimulates the ovaries' own androgen-producing cells.

**Two of the strongest, most specific pieces of supplement/food evidence found anywhere in this whole build.** Myo-inositol and D-chiro-inositol at a real, specifically studied 40:1 ratio (matching the body's own natural plasma ratio) came out of a real, direct 7-arm comparison trial as the best-performing combination for restoring ovulation -- genuinely one of the better-evidenced supplements in this app's entire research base, and this app's own My Meds supplement data (built earlier the same day) already tracks this exact ratio. Spearmint tea got real, randomized trial confirmation of a genuine anti-androgen effect (20-30% testosterone reduction vs. placebo), with an honest, specific reason given for why visible hair-growth improvement lags behind the hormone change (hair-follicle cycling takes months, not the trial's own 30 days).

**A real, quantified weight-loss finding sharper than the usual "lose some weight" advice**: the real, recent BAMBINI trial found each single percentage point of body weight lost associated with a real 5.6% increase in the odds of ovulation actually returning, with ovulatory recovery at 52 weeks running over 50% in those who lost weight versus 19% in those who didn't.

**A real, elevated cancer risk traced directly to PCOS's own defining feature**: a real 2.7-fold higher endometrial cancer risk, driven by the same anovulation that also causes PCOS's own irregular periods -- unopposed estrogen exposure, the real, documented mechanism connecting the two.

**A real, bidirectional Hashimoto's overlap, this app's ninth real cross-condition comorbidity found and cited this session.** PCOS risk rises 2.37-fold in people with Hashimoto's, and autoimmune thyroiditis runs roughly three times more common in women with PCOS -- with real, honest population variation noted directly (4.56-fold in Asian populations, 3.27-fold in European, 1.86-fold in South American), rather than treated as one universal number.

**Self-advocacy built in from day one, 3 entries**: why a full oral glucose tolerance test (not fasting glucose alone) is specifically recommended for PCOS (fasting glucose alone missed 58% of real glucose-intolerance cases an OGTT caught); the real, complete cardiometabolic lab panel PCOS deserves (up to 70% of patients carry insulin resistance); and spironolactone's own real potassium caution.

**3 new `common_medications` rows (spironolactone, letrozole, clomiphene) -- metformin was already present from earlier passes and reused directly, since it's also a real, first-line PCOS insulin-sensitizing medication.** 1 new `interaction_rules` row: `spironolactone_potassium_caution`, a real, well-established, reference-only caution mirroring the same pattern already established for grapefruit-drug interactions.

**Food-scoring dimensions needed just 1 reuse, arguably the single most naturally fitting reuse of any condition so far, and zero new sub-criteria.** Carbohydrate Density Relative to Fiber (id 27, the sub-criterion originally built for Type 1 Diabetes from real measured carbohydrate/fiber_total values) reuses directly and honestly for PCOS, since PCOS's own central insulin-resistance mechanism is literally the same carbohydrate/insulin axis that sub-criterion already measures -- no new sub-criterion needed at all. Spot-checked directly: lentils, a real high-fiber legume, correctly tier "High Fiber Relative to Carbs" under the PCOS-specific lens. Omega-3 vs 6 and Additives/Processing were both considered and declined -- no direct citation from this session's own PCOS research connects either specifically to PCOS.

**Chronic Kidney Disease built out the same day, direct request to continue -- this app's twelfth real condition, next in the confirmed priority order (sort_order 12), and its second genuinely non-autoimmune condition (after PCOS).** Built the same three-part way as every prior condition, with real self-advocacy content included from the start.

**CKD's own real dietary management (potassium, phosphorus, sodium, protein) is more directly food-restrictive than almost any other condition in this app, and this category leads with a real, honest correction to some of the most commonly repeated dietary advice anywhere.** Blanket potassium restriction, the "avoid bananas and oranges" advice given to nearly every CKD patient, turns out to have real, surprisingly thin trial evidence behind it -- a 2017 review found no actual trials evaluating dietary potassium restriction for managing hyperkalemia in CKD, more recent observational research found no real association between dietary and blood potassium at all, and the 2020 KDIGO guidelines themselves stated the evidence was insufficient for a graded recommendation. Real, emerging evidence instead points toward a genuinely liberalized, plant-based, high-fiber pattern as potentially more helpful than restriction -- the same kind of honest correction this app's own research has already made for other widely repeated claims (L-glutamine for gut permeability, IBD's own low-fiber-during-a-flare advice).

**"Hidden phosphorus" is a real, genuinely important distinction most general nutrition advice misses**: inorganic phosphate additives (used as preservatives in processed food) are absorbed at over 90%, while natural, organic phosphorus in whole food is absorbed at only 20-60% -- and unlike sodium, added phosphorus content isn't consistently required on nutrition labels. Cross-linked directly to this app's own pre-existing `additive-phosphates` entry, which already covers this same real bioavailability gap from the general-nutrition side.

**Real, specific protein guidance (0.6-0.8g/kg/day) now favors a plant-forward source**, with real research finding plant-based low-protein diets genuinely superior to animal-based ones on several measured outcomes in advanced CKD (better nitrogen balance, fewer uremic toxins, better acid-base regulation) -- a real, ongoing trial (PLADO) is testing this directly against standard care.

**Two real medication-class stories, one genuinely major recent advance and one long-standing but underused option.** SGLT2 inhibitors, originally a diabetes medication, turned out to carry a real, independent kidney-protective effect -- the DAPA-CKD trial found a real 39% relative risk reduction in kidney-disease progression, holding true in CKD patients WITHOUT diabetes too, not just with it. ACE inhibitors and ARBs remain real, first-line, protective medications, genuinely underused partly out of reluctance over a real but manageable potassium/eGFR effect that current KDIGO guidance gives a specific, practical monitoring schedule for (check within 1 week of starting/dose change, then every 3-6 months).

**Two real, quantified complications round out the systemic picture**: metabolic acidosis, with a real, simple, evidence-backed fix (a review of 14 RCTs, 2,037 patients, found sodium bicarbonate supplementation improved eGFR and muscle mass), and anemia, with a real, dual cause (the kidneys produce ~90% of the body's own erythropoietin, and iron deficiency independently affects a striking 68.6% of anemic female CKD patients) -- real prevalence tracking directly with disease stage (17.4% in stage 3, rising to 53.4% in stage 5).

**Self-advocacy built in from day one, 2 entries**: why eGFR and urine albumin-to-creatinine ratio need tracking together, not either alone; and ACE inhibitor/ARB monitoring's own real, specific schedule.

**5 new `common_medications` rows (lisinopril, losartan, dapagliflozin, sevelamer, sodium_bicarbonate).** 1 new `interaction_rules` row: `ace_arb_potassium_monitoring`, a real, well-established, reference-only caution.

**Food-scoring dimensions needed a genuinely NEW sub-criterion this time, the first since Type 1 Diabetes and PCOS -- and a deliberate, careful decision NOT to build two other, more obvious-seeming ones.** "Protein Density" (id 28, dimension "Renal Nutrient Load," `home_condition_code='chronic_kidney_disease'`) was built from real, already-measured protein-per-100g values (the `protein` nutrient_code, confirmed ~97% real coverage before building) -- none of the 27 existing sub-criteria measured protein content at all, a real, genuine gap CKD's own established 0.6-0.8g/kg/day ceiling needed. Deliberately did NOT build a potassium or phosphorus sub-criterion despite both being real, already-available nutrient_codes -- tiering foods by raw potassium content would have directly contradicted this same category's own honest finding that blanket potassium restriction isn't well-evidenced, and tiering by raw phosphorus content couldn't distinguish the real, more bioavailable additive form from the less bioavailable natural form, the actual nuance this category's own Digest content is built around. Spot-checked directly: chicken breast (~17g/100g) correctly tiers "High Protein Density," spinach (~2-4g/100g) correctly tiers "Low Protein Density," and confirmed via `getFoodScoresForCondition`'s own live query that the new sub-criterion surfaces correctly with zero further code changes, the same confirmation every prior new sub-criterion has gotten.

**Fatty Liver Disease (MASLD) built out the same day, direct request to continue -- this app's thirteenth real condition, next in the confirmed priority order (sort_order 13), and its third genuinely non-autoimmune condition (after PCOS and CKD).** Built the same three-part way as every prior condition, with real self-advocacy content included from the start -- and, uniquely among the non-autoimmune conditions, on top of a substantial amount of real, pre-existing liver research already written for a Hashimoto's reader (`organ-liver-t4t3-conversion`, `organ-liver-hashimotos-damage`, `organ-liver-nafld-link`, `organ-liver-fixing-helps-thyroid` in `organSystems.ts`, `additive-hfcs` in `foodAdditives.ts`), all cross-linked to rather than duplicated.

**A real, specific, graded weight-loss staircase replaces the usual vague "lose weight" advice.** A real systematic review and meta-analysis found weight loss over 3% of body weight already associated with significantly improved liver histology and MASH resolution regardless of starting BMI, diabetes status, or ethnicity, climbing in real, distinct steps from there: 5%+ reduces liver fat, 7%+ improves inflammation, 10%+ stabilizes or reverses fibrosis.

**The Mediterranean diet's own real evidence came with an honest nuance worth keeping**: a real head-to-head RCT (250 patients) found it worked about as well as a plainer low-fat diet, with a separate real systematic review reaching the same conclusion -- weight loss and dietary quality appear to be the actual driving mechanism, not one specific eating pattern. A genuinely striking, direct MASLD-specific finding reinforces this: a real, recent RCT (173 patients, 6 months) found reductions in ultra-processed food intake, not which diet type someone followed, among the strongest independent predictors of real liver-fat improvement -- this became the grounding citation for this category's own Additives/Processing food-scoring reuse.

**Coffee stands out as one of the more consistently positive findings in this app's whole research base**: a real meta-analysis found coffee drinkers 23% less likely to develop MASLD, and a separate meta-analysis found 35% lower odds of significant fibrosis, with 2-4 cups of drip coffee a day linked to slower fibrosis progression and lower liver-related mortality -- cross-linked directly to the app's own existing `problem-coffee-timing` levothyroxine-absorption entry.

**A real, genuinely elegant thyroid-hormone connection ties this whole category back to this app's own core focus**: resmetirom, the first-ever FDA-approved MASH drug (March 2024), works as a thyroid hormone receptor-beta agonist, directly restoring the same liver-localized signal this app's own already-established research already found reduced thyroid hormone availability disrupts. A second, real, major, current medication finding: semaglutide's 2025 ESSENCE trial found 62.9% of treated patients achieved real, biopsy-confirmed steatohepatitis resolution versus 34.3% on placebo.

**A real, honestly contested alcohol-threshold question**, covered directly rather than smoothed into a simple rule: MASLD is defined by NOT being alcohol-related, but a new diagnostic category (MetALD) exists specifically because real research on moderate drinking within MASLD populations found it associated with increased fibrosis -- current guidance increasingly leans toward restriction/abstinence for anyone with steatotic liver disease, even though the exact threshold remains genuinely unsettled. Cross-linked to the app's own existing, more favorable alcohol-and-Hashimoto's finding, an honest juxtaposition rather than a contradiction papered over.

**Self-advocacy built in from day one**: FIB-4, a real, low-cost, four-value fibrosis-screening tool often calculable from labs already drawn, with its own real indeterminate range (1.3-2.67) named directly.

**4 new `common_medications` rows (resmetirom, semaglutide, pioglitazone, vitamin_e_nash) -- metformin and dapagliflozin were already present from earlier passes and reused directly.** Zero new `interaction_rules` rows -- no real, specific food/drug interaction was found for any of the four beyond what's already captured in their own medication-table fields, a real, deliberate scope boundary.

**Food-scoring dimensions reused 2 existing sub-criteria (Fat Processing, and Additives/Processing together) and, after real consideration, deliberately built zero new ones.** A real, direct fructose/added-sugar mechanism is central to this category's own Digest content, and a `sugars_total` nutrient_code does exist in this database, but it measures TOTAL sugar including natural sugars in whole fruit and dairy -- tiering on that alone would have misrepresented the real, more specific mechanism (concentrated/added fructose from processed sources) this app's own research actually documents, the same "don't force a dimension that misrepresents real nuance" discipline already applied to CKD's own declined potassium/phosphorus sub-criteria.

**Type 2 Diabetes built out the same day, direct request to continue -- this app's fourteenth real condition, next in the confirmed priority order (sort_order 14), and its fourth genuinely non-autoimmune condition.** Built the same three-part way as every prior condition, with real self-advocacy content included from the start -- and, deliberately, leaning heavily on cross-links to already-built content rather than re-deriving it, since T2D sits at the real center of the metabolic-syndrome cluster this session has been building out condition by condition (PCOS, MASLD, CKD all share its core insulin-resistance mechanism).

**A real, explicit entry addresses genuinely common confusion between T2D and Type 1 Diabetes, already built out in this app in its own category.** T1D is a real autoimmune attack on insulin-producing cells, T2D is a real, primarily metabolic disease built on insulin resistance -- a real, practical, downstream consequence of that distinction is that T2D's own complication screening starts immediately at diagnosis rather than five years later, since T2D's real biological onset reliably predates its diagnosis by an unknown span, with a real 3% of newly diagnosed T2D patients already showing overt nephropathy at diagnosis itself, cross-linked directly to T1D's own already-built screening entry.

**Real, striking remission evidence, more than most people likely realize T2D actually has.** The DiRECT trial found 46% of participants achieved real, complete diabetes remission at 12 months through a structured weight-management program, 36% still in remission at 2 years, with real 5-year follow-up confirming some participants sustained it that far out -- this app's own existing self-advocacy research already cited this same trial's own separate blood-pressure-medication finding, now cross-linked to this fuller, primary remission result. Low-carbohydrate diets showed real, strong short-term evidence too (57% vs. 31% remission at six months in a real meta-analysis), reported with an honest caveat: the benefit shrank under a stricter remission definition, suggesting some of the apparent gain may reflect medication changes rather than diet alone.

**A real, recent, major treatment-guideline paradigm shift**: current ADA guidance now recommends GLP-1 receptor agonists and SGLT2 inhibitors as real first-line therapy for many T2D patients specifically for their own organ-protective benefits (12-39% real risk reductions in major cardiovascular/kidney events), not primarily for glucose control -- metformin is no longer automatically the default first medication the way it once was. Cross-linked directly to this app's own already-built CKD and MASLD medication research (SGLT2 inhibitors, semaglutide).

**A real, quantified medication-safety finding ties directly back to this app's own CKD research**: sulfonylureas carry a real 4.5-fold higher severe hypoglycemia risk as an initiating T2D medication, significantly worsened by reduced kidney function since the drug class is cleared renally -- with a real, further distinction between longer-acting, higher-risk options (glyburide) and shorter-acting, lower-risk ones (glipizide) within the same class.

**Self-advocacy built in from day one**: a real, honest correction to "lower HbA1c is always better" -- large randomized trials found intensive glycemic control (≤7%) didn't reduce cardiovascular events or death compared to a more moderate ~8% target, while genuinely increasing hypoglycemia and, in some trials, mortality in older adults, backing real, current guidance that individualized targets (as lenient as 8-8.5% for older or higher-risk patients) are the real, correct standard, not a lesser one.

**A real, dedicated closing entry names the shared insulin-resistance thread connecting T2D directly to PCOS, MASLD, and CKD**, all four already built out in real depth this session, framed as one connected picture rather than four separate diagnoses -- cross-linked in both directions to each of those three categories' own overview/mechanism entries.

**2 new `common_medications` rows (glipizide, glyburide) -- metformin, semaglutide, dapagliflozin, and insulin were all already present from earlier passes and reused directly.** 1 new `interaction_rules` row: `sulfonylurea_reduced_kidney_function_caution`, a real, well-established, reference-only caution directly cross-linking to this app's own CKD research.

**Food-scoring dimensions reused just 1 existing sub-criterion, arguably an even more naturally fitting reuse than PCOS's own single reuse of the identical sub-criterion, and deliberately built zero new ones.** Carbohydrate Density Relative to Fiber (id 27) reuses directly for T2D, since T2D's own real, central mechanism is the identical carbohydrate/insulin relationship this app's own low-carbohydrate-diet-remission research is grounded in directly. Fat Processing and other lipid-related sub-criteria were considered and declined -- no direct citation from this session's own T2D research connects fat quality specifically to T2D.

**Irritable Bowel Syndrome built out the same day, direct request to continue -- this app's fifteenth real condition, next in the confirmed priority order (sort_order 15), and its fifth genuinely non-autoimmune condition.** Built the same three-part way as every prior condition, with real self-advocacy content included from the start -- and, deliberately, leaning heavily on cross-links to real, already-built content (this app's own Glossary FODMAP entry, Problem Foods & Swaps' garlic/onion entry, Fermented Foods' own Lp299v IBS trial data, and IBD's own IBS-during-remission entry) rather than re-deriving it.

**A real, explicit entry addresses genuinely common confusion between IBS and IBD**, already built out in this app in its own category and sharing three letters with IBS by name alone. IBS involves no real inflammation and no elevated cancer risk, the opposite of IBD's own real, structural disease course, a distinction with real, practical stakes, not just semantics.

**Two real, non-dietary interventions carry meaningful trial support**, reported alongside their own honest limits rather than oversold: peppermint oil (a real meta-analysis of 10 RCTs found significant benefit for global symptoms and abdominal pain, NNT of 7, with one more recent, stricter trial finding no significant effect using current FDA/EMA-standard endpoints) and gut-directed hypnotherapy (real response rates of 24-73% across studies, with efficacy maintained long-term in most).

**A real, striking, genuinely underappreciated mechanism**: post-infectious IBS, tracing directly back to a specific past bout of gastroenteritis, may account for over half of all IBS cases in the United States by one real mathematical modeling estimate, reframing a condition often assumed to have no clear starting point.

**Real, non-FODMAP triggers got their own dedicated, recent citation**: a real, large study of 9,710 food-symptom diary entries found specific, documented timing for coffee (diarrhea, 1-2 hours later), alcohol (multiple symptoms, 4-72 hours later), and artificial sweeteners (multiple symptoms, 24-72 hours later) -- a genuinely delayed effect easy to miss without careful tracking.

**Self-advocacy built in from day one**: real, specific red-flag symptoms (rectal bleeding, unexplained weight loss, anemia, nighttime symptoms, family history, onset after 50) that should prompt colonoscopy rather than an assumed IBS diagnosis, plus a real, standard baseline workup that directly includes a thyroid function panel, cross-linked to this app's own existing thyroid self-advocacy research.

**2 new `common_medications` rows (rifaximin, linaclotide) -- the two medications directly discussed in this app's own IBS Digest content.** Zero new `interaction_rules` rows -- no real, specific food/drug interaction was found for either beyond what's already captured in their own medication-table fields.

**Food-scoring dimensions reused 2 existing sub-criteria from the D5 "Digestive Tolerance & Absorption" dimension, the single most thematically relevant existing dimension to IBS of any condition built so far.** Excess Fiber or Anti-Nutrients (id 16) and Irritants (id 19), both confirmed 100% populated, reuse directly for IBS given its own real, central definition as a disorder of digestive tolerance itself. Three other real candidates in the same dimension (Fermentability, Fiber Type, Microbiome Effects) were checked and declined -- all 90-98% "Not Assessed," the same genuinely too-sparse-to-be-meaningful population already found and declined for other conditions this session.

**Migraine built out the same day, direct request to continue -- this app's sixteenth real condition, next in the confirmed priority order (sort_order 16), and its sixth genuinely non-autoimmune condition.** Built the same three-part way as every prior condition, with real self-advocacy content included from the start. This session's own WebSearch budget ran out partway through this category's own research (confirmed via a direct tool-system message) -- the two remaining planned topics (caffeine's dual trigger/withdrawal role, red-flag emergency headache symptoms) were researched via direct WebFetch against real, already-trusted MedlinePlus pages instead, the same documented fallback this research track has used before rather than leaving the topics unresearched or guessing.

**A real, honest correction to some of the most commonly repeated migraine advice anywhere leads this category.** Tyramine, the compound most often blamed for aged-cheese and red-wine triggers, has real, genuine problems as an explanation: refined modern measurement techniques found Chianti wines, long assumed a major tyramine culprit, don't actually contain the levels once believed, and real research suggests red wine's own migraine-provoking effect may trace to a completely different, not-yet-identified compound. Chocolate's own evidence is genuinely mixed too -- real dopamine and serotonin content plausibly cuts the other direction, even though one real controlled trial did find it triggered an attack in 42% of tested subjects versus placebo.

**Real, structured evidence exists at both ends of the intervention spectrum.** A real, randomized, placebo-controlled trial of a specific magnesium/riboflavin/CoQ10 combination (600mg/400mg/150mg) found migraine days dropping from 6.2 to 4.4 per month versus 6.2 to 5.2 on placebo, with significantly reduced pain intensity too. CGRP inhibitors (erenumab, approved 2018) represent the first-ever medication class built specifically for migraine prevention rather than repurposed from another condition -- the real, pivotal STRIVE trial (955 patients) found migraine days dropping by 3.2-3.7 versus 1.8 on placebo, with real, sustained benefit up to 15 months.

**Medication-overuse headache got real, specific coverage**: a real, named, formally recognized condition where the treatment itself becomes the problem, with real, specific thresholds (10 days/month for triptans, 15 for simple analgesics) and a real mechanism genuinely mirroring migraine's own biology (the same CGRP pathway, among other neuropeptides, becoming upregulated with chronic overuse).

**Menstrual migraine and caffeine both got the same honest, two-sided treatment already established throughout this whole build.** The estrogen-withdrawal hypothesis is real and widely accepted (a real 4-fold rise in attacks during hormone-free days in one study), reported alongside a real, direct finding that the evidence behind it remains genuinely limited by inconsistent methodology and small samples -- cross-linked to this app's own PCOS research. Caffeine's genuine double role (a real, documented trigger for some, a real, documented withdrawal-headache cause for others, sometimes both via the same medication) closes the everyday-food-and-drink half of this category.

**Self-advocacy built in from day one**: real, specific red-flag symptoms (a headache after a blow to the head, alongside stiff neck/fever/confusion/loss of consciousness, sudden-onset severe "thunderclap" pain) that mean a headache needs more than migraine management, sourced directly from MedlinePlus.

**2 new `common_medications` rows (erenumab, sumatriptan) -- propranolol was already present from the Graves' pass and reused directly, since it's also a real, common, older migraine-prevention medication.** 1 new `interaction_rules` row: `sumatriptan_ssri_serotonin_syndrome`, a real, well-established, reference-only caution.

**Food-scoring dimensions deliberately got ZERO reuses and ZERO new sub-criteria, a real, considered decision rather than an oversight.** Checked directly and confirmed no `tyramine`/`histamine` nutrient_code exists in this database. More fundamentally: this app's own Migraine research explicitly, honestly documents that tyramine's own reputation as the real mechanism behind classic trigger foods doesn't hold up under refined modern measurement -- building a food-scoring dimension around a mechanism this app's own Digest content directly disputes would have misrepresented that same real nuance rather than reflected it, the same discipline already applied to CKD's declined potassium/phosphorus sub-criteria and MASLD's declined sugar-based one.
