**Cardiovascular Disease built out the same day, direct request to continue -- this app's seventeenth real condition, next in the confirmed priority order (sort_order 17), and its seventh genuinely non-autoimmune one.** Built the same three-part way as every prior condition, with a real, distinctive fourth ingredient: this category was already touched from five separate angles across this app's own existing content before it ever existed as its own category -- lupus's own real 50-fold heart-attack risk in young women, Hashimoto's own organ-systems entry on hypothyroid heart effects, PCOS's own lipid-panel/cardiometabolic entry, and both psoriasis's and rheumatoid arthritis's own self-advocacy entries on elevated cardiovascular risk. All five of those existing entries were cross-linked back to the new category once it existed, the same "heavy cross-linking rather than re-derivation" discipline already established for every prior condition.

**This session's own WebSearch budget was already exhausted before CVD research began** (confirmed via a direct tool-system message, the same exhaustion state already documented for Migraine), so every citation here came via the same established `WebFetch`-against-real-pages fallback -- MedlinePlus (proven reliable throughout this whole build) plus direct PubMed fetches against real PMIDs.

**Two real, quantified findings from the same trial, reported honestly in opposite directions, anchor this category's own central lesson about how medical guidance actually changes.** The real, large ASCEND trial (15,480 adults with diabetes, no diagnosed cardiovascular disease, 7.4-year follow-up) tested both daily low-dose aspirin and omega-3 supplementation for primary prevention, in the same population, at the same time. Aspirin showed a real, genuine benefit (9.6% to 8.5% serious vascular events) fully counterbalanced by a real, quantified bleeding cost (3.2% to 4.1% major bleeding events) -- converted to concrete numbers, roughly 85 additional vascular events prevented against roughly 90 additional major bleeds caused per 10,000 people treated, the trial's own authors stated plainly that "the absolute benefits were largely counterbalanced by the bleeding hazard." Omega-3 supplementation, tested in the very same trial, produced a real, honest null result (8.9% vs. 9.2% serious vascular events, not statistically significant) -- reported exactly as directly as a positive finding would be, a genuinely more sobering result than omega-3's popular reputation for heart health. Both entries are cross-linked to each other directly, since together they're a real, direct example of why guidance shifts when a large trial's own honest numbers say so, not a reason for distrust.

**The Mediterranean diet's own PREDIMED trial got a genuinely honest treatment of its real 2018 retraction-and-republication story, without inventing an unverified statistic to fill the gap.** The trial's own real design and real correction (randomization irregularities at some study sites led to a formal 2018 retraction of the original 2013 publication, followed by a corrected re-analysis published the same year reaching the same real substantive conclusion) are both confirmed and cited directly -- but the corrected paper's own exact reported hazard ratio could not be independently re-confirmed this session (the abstract fetch cut off before the results section, and two follow-up attempts at a direct primary source both failed), so the entry describes the trial's real design and real retraction story honestly without stating an unverified specific number, consistent with this whole build's standing rule to never fabricate or guess at a citation's exact figures.

**Statins got a real, large, consistent evidence review, directly addressing their commonly debated online reputation**: the Cholesterol Treatment Trialists' Collaboration meta-analysis (14 trials, 90,056 participants) found a real 12% all-cause mortality reduction and 21% major vascular event reduction per 1 mmol/L LDL reduction, with no real increased cancer risk found in the same pooled analysis -- a direct, evidence-based answer to one of the most commonly repeated statin concerns.

**Self-advocacy built in from day one, 2 entries**: real, specific lipid-panel testing intervals by age and sex, with a brief, honestly-scoped mention of apolipoprotein B (apoB) as a real, increasingly discussed concept in cardiology, deliberately without a dedicated citation since a primary apoB source could not be independently re-verified this session -- the concept is presented as real and worth raising by name, not backed by an unverified statistic; and heart attack red flags, including the real, documented pattern that women more often experience atypical symptoms (especially unusual fatigue) rather than classic chest pain, a real, documented reason heart attacks in women are more often missed or delayed.

**2 new `common_medications` rows (atorvastatin, a representative statin; low-dose aspirin) -- lisinopril, losartan, and propranolol were all already present from earlier CKD/Graves' passes and reused directly, since all three are also real, common cardiovascular medications.** 2 new `interaction_rules` rows: `aspirin_ibuprofen_timing` (a real, checkable `timing_separation` rule -- NSAIDs can competitively block aspirin's own irreversible antiplatelet binding if taken too close together, real standard guidance separates the two by several hours) and `atorvastatin_grapefruit_caution` (a real, well-documented, reference-only CYP3A4 interaction, the same pattern already established for `cyclosporine_grapefruit_avoidance`).

**Food-scoring dimensions reused 5 existing sub-criteria under two real dimension labels, plus one genuinely new sub-criterion, after checking real tier population for every candidate before reusing.** Additives (id 8) and Processing (id 9), both over 99% populated with a real Neutral/High Risk/Mild Risk split, reused under "Whole-Food, Mediterranean-Pattern Fit," tying directly to the real Mediterranean-diet and DASH evidence already cited. Omega-3 vs 6 (id 11, ~47% real coverage -- the same real, partial-but-meaningful population level RA and Psoriasis had already reused this same sub-criterion at), Oxidation Risk (id 12, fully populated, a real, direct tie to oxidized LDL as a documented atherogenic mechanism), and Fat Processing (id 13, fully populated, directly matches saturated/trans fat guidance) reused together under "Lipid & Oxidative Stress Profile," tying directly to the statin and omega-3 entries. A genuinely new sub-criterion (id 29, "Sodium Density (DASH-Aligned)") was built directly from real, already-measured sodium content already sitting in `food_nutrients` (98% real coverage across all 22,022 foods, confirmed before building this) -- tiered using the UK Food Standards Agency's own real, official per-100g traffic-light sodium thresholds (Low <=120mg/100g, High >600mg/100g, Moderate in between), a real, standard, citable convention chosen specifically because it's already expressed per 100g, unlike the FDA's own regulatory sodium-claim thresholds, which are defined per reference serving amount and would have misrepresented that rule if applied here directly. Spot-checked directly against real foods: cured/smoked bacon (2,200-2,800mg sodium/100g across three sources) correctly tiers "High Sodium (DASH Caution)." Verified directly against a real food (a real Cheddar Cheese row) via `getFoodScoresForCondition`'s own live query -- confirming the reuse-first architecture generalizes to a seventh condition, including its own brand-new sub-criterion, with, once again, zero code changes.

**Gout built out the same day, direct request to continue -- this app's eighteenth real condition, and, per the `conditions` table's own sort_order, the last one currently planned. Its eighth genuinely non-autoimmune one.** Built the same three-part way as every prior condition. Gout is a real, structurally different kind of condition from most already covered: its own strongest evidence is a specific, individually well-studied list of foods and drinks (not one broad dietary pattern), each with its own dedicated large study, giving this category an unusually concrete, food-first character throughout.

**This session's own WebSearch budget was already exhausted before Gout research began** (confirmed via a direct tool-system message, the same exhaustion state already documented for Migraine and Cardiovascular Disease), so every citation here came via the same established `WebFetch`-against-real-pages fallback -- MedlinePlus and StatPearls (both proven reliable throughout this whole build) plus direct PubMed/PMC fetches against real PMIDs. Several citations needed a corrected second search after an initial guessed PMID returned an unrelated paper (a bisphosphonate osteoarthritis trial instead of the coffee/gout study; a lateral-flow-assay methods paper instead of the cherry/gout study) -- both caught immediately by checking the fetched content actually matched the claimed topic before trusting it, rather than assuming a plausible-looking PMID was correct.

**Two real findings from the same landmark 12-year, 47,150-man prospective study anchor this category's own central lesson: not all protein or purine sources carry the same real risk.** Meat carried a real 41% higher gout risk and seafood a real 51% higher risk (highest vs. lowest intake quintile), while dairy carried a real 44% LOWER risk, a genuine protective association from the very same study. The same study's own real, honestly surprising finding: purine-rich vegetables and total protein intake overall were NOT associated with increased risk at all, directly countering the common "all purines are equally risky" assumption. A companion real, large study found sugar-sweetened soft drinks and fructose carrying a real, dose-dependent risk (up to 85% higher at 2+ daily servings) while diet soda showed none, and a third real study found beer carrying the strongest real risk of any alcohol type (49% higher per daily serving) while wine showed no significant association at all -- three separate real "it's not the category, it's the specific thing" findings, each with its own dedicated citation.

**Three individually-tested protective foods each got their own real, dedicated entry.** Cherries: a real case-crossover study found a 35% lower attack risk from cherry intake alone, and a real, striking 75% lower risk when combined with allopurinol, stronger than either alone -- cross-linked directly. Vitamin C: a real, double-blind RCT (184 nonsmokers, 500mg/day for 2 months) found a real, significant uric acid reduction (-0.5 mg/dL vs. +0.09 mg/dL placebo, P<0.0001). Coffee: a real, large study found 6+ daily cups of caffeinated coffee carrying a real 59% lower risk, with decaf also protective (27% lower at 4+ cups) and total caffeine/tea intake showing no effect at all -- real evidence the protective mechanism isn't caffeine itself, cross-linked to this app's own MASLD research, which independently found the same real coffee-protective pattern for a different condition.

**A real, serious medication safety distinction anchors the medications entry**: the real, dedicated CARES safety trial (6,190 patients) comparing the two most common urate-lowering therapies found febuxostat carrying a real 34% higher cardiovascular death risk and 22% higher all-cause mortality risk than allopurinol, despite similar performance on the trial's own broader composite safety measure -- a real, direct reason allopurinol remains the standard first choice, cross-linked to this app's own Cardiovascular Disease research.

**Self-advocacy built in from day one, 2 entries**: HLA-B*58:01 genetic testing before starting allopurinol, with real, specific, named population allele frequencies from the actual 2012 ACR gout guideline's own full text (Han Chinese and Thai individuals ~6-8% regardless of kidney function, Koreans with stage 3+ CKD ~12%, versus ~2% in people of European descent) -- also surfacing the real ACR-recommended serum urate target (<6 mg/dL, or <5 mg/dL for more severe disease); and distinguishing a real gout flare from real septic (infected) arthritis, which can look nearly identical and can genuinely coexist in the same joint, with real guidance to aspirate the joint before a steroid injection when the diagnosis isn't already clear.

**3 new `common_medications` rows (allopurinol, febuxostat, colchicine) -- prednisone and ibuprofen were already present from earlier passes and reused directly, since both are also real, common gout-flare treatments.** 2 new `interaction_rules` rows, both real, checkable `concurrent_use_caution` rules: `allopurinol_azathioprine_toxicity` (allopurinol blocks the same enzyme that normally clears azathioprine, a real, serious toxicity risk without a deliberate dose reduction -- azathioprine already existing in this database from the RA/Lupus/IBD passes makes this a genuinely relevant, not hypothetical, overlap) and `colchicine_atorvastatin_myopathy` (a real, well-documented muscle-related interaction, reusing atorvastatin from the CVD pass).

**Food-scoring dimensions deliberately declined a new sub-criterion, after checking two real candidates and finding both unbuildable or misleading.** Confirmed directly via query that no purine `nutrient_code` exists anywhere in this database's own `food_nutrients` table (none of the 7 national sources measure purine content), so a real, data-backed "Purine Density" dimension isn't buildable without inventing data. A fructose/sugar-specific dimension was considered next and declined too: the only real sugar-related `nutrient_code` (`sugars_total`) measures ALL sugars, including the natural sugar in dairy and whole fruit -- and this app's own Gout Digest content explicitly, honestly documents dairy as PROTECTIVE against gout (from the very same study that found meat/seafood raise risk), so a sugar-based dimension would have directly contradicted that finding, the same "don't force a dimension that misrepresents real nuance" discipline already applied to MASLD's and Migraine's own declined sub-criteria. Reused 2 existing sub-criteria instead (Additives id 8, Processing id 9, both >99% populated) under a real "Processed & Sugar-Sweetened Food Pattern" dimension label, tied directly to the real, cited sugar-sweetened-beverage/gout finding -- a directional, honest proxy for processed/sweetened food, not a literal fructose measurement. Verified directly against a real food (a real Cheddar Cheese row) via `getFoodScoresForCondition`'s own live query -- confirming the reuse-first architecture generalizes to an eighth condition with, once again, zero code changes.

**A real, substantial Basic Health expansion followed immediately, same day, direct request: "I think there are many more things that we can put into the basic health information, such as the real cause of high cholesterol based on actual evidence. And the real truth about eggs... along with the use of butter as a fat due to how well we metabolize it... I really want this to be extremely inclusive of tons of true and factual... peer reviewed sources. I think this same train of thought should be applied to all of the 18 Digest conditions."** The three specifically named topics were built out immediately as 4 new, genuinely deep `basicHealth` entries in `lib/digest/foodIndustryHistory.ts` (the file that already carried the real eggs/margarine *history* -- these go one layer deeper, into the actual physiology, not a duplicate of what was already there). The broader "apply this to all 18 conditions" direction is treated as a real, large, standing initiative to keep working through incrementally in future passes, not something completed in one turn -- stated here plainly rather than implied as finished.

This session's own WebSearch budget was already exhausted before this pass began (the same exhaustion state documented for every condition since Migraine), so every citation came via the established WebFetch-against-real-pages fallback, with two guessed PMIDs along the way returning unrelated papers (a cardiac MRI study instead of an insulin-resistance/small-dense-LDL review; a lymphatic apolipoprotein-transport study instead of a milk-fat-composition one) caught immediately by checking the fetched content actually matched the claimed topic, then corrected with a proper search.

**`foodhistory-cholesterol-real-drivers`** directly answers "the real cause of high cholesterol" with genuinely more nuance than the popular "saturated fat causes it" framing: familial hypercholesterolemia as a real, genetic driver affecting 1 in 200-250 people regardless of diet; two real, large studies (Siri-Tarino et al. 2010, 21 studies/347,747 people, found no significant saturated-fat/CVD association; the PURE study, 2017, 18 countries, found saturated fat linked to LOWER mortality and stroke risk, with higher carbohydrate intake linked to higher mortality) showing the dietary-saturated-fat-to-CVD-events link is genuinely more contested than assumed; and insulin resistance driving real atherogenic dyslipidemia (small, dense LDL particles, an independent real risk factor) as a better-supported explanation for many people. Explicitly doesn't contradict this app's own existing, real statin evidence (LDL-lowering itself still shows real mortality benefit, a separate question from what drives LDL up dietarily) -- both stated honestly rather than picking one and ignoring the other.

**`foodhistory-apob-particle-count`** resolves a real gap this document has carried since the Cardiovascular Disease build: the CVD lipid-panel entry had deliberately left apoB uncited, since a primary source couldn't be verified in that earlier pass. Found and verified this time: the real INTERHEART study (52 countries, 12,461 cases, 14,637 controls) found the apoB/apoA1 ratio carrying a real, significantly higher population-attributable risk (54%) for heart attack than LDL/HDL (37%) or total cholesterol/HDL (32%), p<0.0001, consistent across ethnicities/sexes/ages. The existing `cvd-lipid-panel-self-advocacy` entry was updated in place to cite this real finding directly and its tier raised from moderate to strong, rather than left with its old "not citing a specific study" caveat now that a real one exists.

**`foodhistory-eggs-nutrient-density`** is a deliberate complement to, not a duplicate of, the existing `foodhistory-scapegoat-eggs` entry -- that one is a defense (dietary cholesterol doesn't move blood cholesterol much for most people), this one is the separate, positive case: a real, current (2025) nutrition review naming eggs' real choline, vitamin D/E, lutein, and zeaxanthin content, with choline specifically flagged as genuinely hard to get elsewhere and a real, already-tracked nutrient in this app's own reference database.

**`foodhistory-butter-short-chain-fat`** answers "how well we metabolize butter" directly: butter is a real, natural dietary source of butyrate, the same short-chain fatty acid this app's own Gut & Microbiome research already covers in depth (Treg induction, anti-inflammatory signaling) -- plus a real, classic physiology finding (Nishimura et al. 1996) that short/medium-chain fats are absorbed directly into the portal vein for immediate liver energy use, a genuinely different real pathway from the lymphatic, storage-oriented route most long-chain fats take. Deliberately honest about scope: eating butter isn't a significant therapeutic butyrate source compared to fiber fermentation (still the real, primary source), and this doesn't make butter calorie-free -- the real point is that "all saturated fat behaves identically" was always a chemical oversimplification, not that decades-old guidance was reversed on this specific point.

Real, reciprocal cross-links added in both directions: `cvd-lipid-panel-self-advocacy`, `cvd-statin-evidence`, `type2-metabolic-syndrome-cluster`, `gout-metabolic-cluster-connection`, `foodhistory-scapegoat-eggs`, `foodhistory-scapegoat-margarine`, and `gut-scfa-treg` (gutMicrobiome.ts) all updated to link back to their new companion entries, not just one-directional. **A second, more thorough Basic Health audit, same day, direct request: "go through the Basic Health area and move everything that is about autoimmune diseases and Hashimoto's references and hypothyroidism and all of the rest of the autoimmune disease references specifically throughout the information provided into the specific to those autoimmune disease's areas. I think the Basic Health area has really missed the mark by not treating the autoimmunity things and all 18 other tracked conditions as the things a healthy person is trying to avoid."** The first Basic Health scope correction (2026-08-08, earlier in this same document) caught whole TOPIC FILES that were substantively autoimmune-mechanism content (Gut & Microbiome, Complementary Therapies) -- this pass went a level deeper, scanning every individual `basicHealth`-tagged entry's own title/teaser/summary text for real, substantive references to autoimmune disease, Hashimoto's, hypothyroidism, or any of the other 17 tracked conditions, via a real Node.js script (not a guess) checking against a real keyword list, then reading every flagged entry's full text by hand to make an individual call, the same discipline this whole Digest's build has used throughout, rather than a blind keyword-triggered move.

**Glossary turned out to be the overwhelming majority of the real problem: 30 of the 44 entries flagged.** Investigated why directly: the Glossary was originally commissioned (2026-08-07) explicitly as "words and phrases... what they do for the body and how they tie in to Hashimoto's" -- built with Hashimoto's-flavored examples and citations baked into its own design brief from day one, before this session's own later restructure (and now this second correction) redefined what Basic Health is supposed to be. Read all 57 terms in full and sorted each into one of three real buckets: **18 moved outright** (16 to `hashimotos`: AIP, ALT/AST framed via hypothyroidism, APS-2, the autoimmune-disease definition itself, autophagy/mitophagy framed via Hashimoto's IL-23 research, 6-DFF, euthyroid/subclinical-hypothyroid states, Healing Stages, IL-6/IL-23/TNF framed via Hashimoto's, levothyroxine, molecular mimicry, MOTS-c, Tg/anti-Tg antibody, Th17, TPO, Wolff-Chaikoff; 1 to `graves`: the Graves' disease definition itself, which reads exactly like every other condition's own -overview entry; 1 to `fattyLiverDisease`: NAFLD/MASLD, now a real, separately-tracked condition with its own category to define itself in) -- moved by reassigning each entry's own `category` field only, keeping every entry physically in `glossary.ts` (the same "reassign the field, don't fragment the file" precedent the original 2026-08-08 restructure already set for `ProblemFoodEntry`), since a single A-Z file stays the simplest way to author and browse the full term list regardless of which category tab a given term now displays under, and the existing Search All lens already covers the "find a term without knowing which category it's filed under" problem the Glossary's own original front-placement was partly designed to solve.

**15 more terms were genuinely universal, cross-cutting biology vocabulary (antibody & antigen, cytokine, eGFR, gliadin, insulin resistance, leaky gut, mitochondria, mTOR, ROS, Treg, visceral fat, zonulin, T4, tight junction, TSH) that only read as Hashimoto's-specific because of how they were originally worded or which citation backed them, not because the underlying concept actually is.** Each was individually reworded to describe the real, general, healthy-body concept first (a cell's own energy-production structures; the immune system's own normal recognition system; a real, shared metabolic mechanism connecting T2D/PCOS/MASLD/CKD/gout, not Hashimoto's alone), with any Hashimoto's-specific study either dropped from `citations` entirely (several of these had cited a Hashimoto's-only paper as their SOLE source -- keeping that citation while claiming the term is now "general" would have been a real, honest mismatch between claim and evidence, so those became `citations: []`, matching this file's own existing precedent for pure definitional terms) or kept only where the citation itself was already general, disease-agnostic research (Fasano's zonulin review, Furusawa's SCFA/Treg paper). Every one of these 15 stayed in `basicHealth`.

**Every other file's own flagged entries got the same individual read rather than a blind sweep, and most needed no change at all.** A real, working distinction emerged and was applied consistently: content substantively about ONE disease's own diagnosis, management, or this app's own disease-specific feature (TPO monitoring, the AIP elimination diet, this app's own Healing Stages framework) moves; content about a real, general, POPULATION-LEVEL finding or normal-body mechanism that merely uses one disease as an illustrative example, or that traces a historical/food-industry trend across many diseases at once (the whole Food Industry & History category, including its own first-person opinion synthesis) stays, since understanding population-level risk and historical trends is itself real preventive knowledge for a healthy reader, matching the user's own "things a healthy person is trying to avoid" framing directly. Trans fat's and HFCS's own real cardiovascular/liver mechanisms stayed (universal food-safety physiology, not CVD/MASLD management); a fermented-strain entry's passing "autoimmune-disease research" phrase and an exercise-inflammation entry's "for an autoimmune condition" phrase were both softened to general language; a straight-fruit-juice entry's own title and framing were rebalanced to lead with its real, general population-level T2D-risk finding rather than center the entry's own identity on its secondary, Hashimoto's-specific cortisol-to-thyroid-conversion mechanism; a CBC self-advocacy entry's one incidental "autoimmune gastritis" example was genericized. `foodhistory-timeline-glyphosate-hfcs`, `foodhistory-mechanism-disappearing-microbiota`, `foodhistory-opinion-synthesis`, `additive-trans-fats`, `additive-hfcs`, `foodhistory-scapegoat-eggs`, `foodhistory-cholesterol-real-drivers`, `interaction-tying-together`, and `advocacy-fasting-insulin` were all read and confirmed to need no change -- each mentions a specific condition only as one illustrative example within otherwise genuinely general content, not as its own real subject.

**A real, valuable discovery before writing anything new: substantial, already-verified magnesium data was already sitting unused in this app's own reference tables**, built during an earlier session's My Meds work and never surfaced in any real Digest entry until now -- real bioavailability percentages for glycinate/citrate/oxide/threonate in `supplement_forms` (a real, direct 1994 clinical trial: glycinate 23.5% vs. oxide 11.8%, same patients), the Na+/K+-ATPase magnesium-cofactor mechanism and the calcium/PTH regulatory loop in `nutrient_interactions`, and real cardiovascular/mood/sleep/bowel-function findings in `nutrient_system_effects`. These real, precise percentages replaced the spreadsheet's own unsourced 1-10 ratings directly -- a genuine improvement, not just a reformat, and the clearest example of why the spreadsheet was treated as a structural template rather than a citable source.

**Explicitly did NOT copy the spreadsheet's own 0-10 system-dependency or 1-10 absorption-form scores**, since neither has a visible citation trail this app could independently verify -- rebuilt the same topics around real, primary research instead. Real, newly-verified findings: a global deficiency review (2025) estimating 31% of the world's population, 2.4 billion people, falls short, with real named causes including soil depletion (cross-linked directly to this app's own already-built Food Industry & History soil research); real staged deficiency/toxicity thresholds with actual measured blood-magnesium levels (StatPearls -- severe deficiency below 1.25 mg/dL can cause seizures; toxicity staged at 2.6-6, above 6, and above 15 mg/dL, the last one real cardiac-arrest territory, concentrated almost entirely in kidney failure); the real TRPM6/TRPM7 intestinal transport channels; a real, modest, trial-confirmed blood pressure effect (2.00/1.78 mmHg systolic/diastolic, 34 RCTs, 2,028 participants); real glucose/insulin-resistance improvement across 18 RCTs; and a real, honest correction matching this whole Digest's own standing discipline -- a 2020 Cochrane review found no real benefit for muscle cramps, magnesium's own most popular real-world use.

**The single most notable find: a real, brand-new (November 2025) randomized crossover trial, the first of its kind, directly testing magnesium's effect on levothyroxine absorption for the first time ever** -- magnesium aspartate reduced levothyroxine exposure by a real, statistically significant 12%; magnesium citrate by a smaller, non-significant 7% in the same trial. More current than almost anything else in this whole Digest. Immediately used to add a real, new, checkable `levothyroxine_magnesium_timing` interaction rule to the reference database (4-hour separation, mirroring the existing `levothyroxine_calcium_timing`/`levothyroxine_iron_timing` rows exactly), not just written up as prose -- a real functional addition to the app's own interaction-checking engine, not only Digest content.

**A modest, honestly-tiered thyroid-magnesium connection entry, deliberately kept at 'moderate' rather than oversold**: a real, current (2024) nutrition-and-thyroid review lists magnesium among the micronutrients influencing thyroid hormone regulation without yet mapping its own specific mechanism, and real, older (1970s-80s, mostly animal-model) research found the relationship runs the other direction too -- thyroid status itself measurably affects renal magnesium handling. Both stated honestly as real but preliminary, with the new levothyroxine-timing finding named directly as the actually best-evidenced, most actionable magnesium-thyroid connection currently available.

**Real food-source data pulled directly from this app's own 22,022-food reference database** rather than an external, unsourced list -- pumpkin seed kernels (592mg/100g, the real leader by a wide margin), unsweetened cocoa powder (499mg), chia seeds (335mg), cashews (~290mg), almonds (~280mg), black beans (171mg), and boiled spinach (~85mg), visualized as a real `DigestChart`. Built as a real, new browsing mode in `app/(tabs)/purple-digest.tsx`, scoped to Basic Health specifically per the request -- every other category's own entry count (9-18) already reads fine as a flat list; only Basic Health had grown large and heterogeneous enough to need this. Real, independently-verified findings: a real, current (2023) pooled analysis of 7.9 million participants finding 76.6% of people worldwide fall short of full sufficiency; real staged deficiency symptoms (StatPearls -- most deficiency is genuinely asymptomatic until it progresses to secondary hyperparathyroidism, rickets in children, osteomalacia in adults) and real toxicity thresholds (88 ng/mL for hypervitaminosis, 150 ng/mL for definite toxicity, essentially always from supplement over-dosing, never sun or food); the real D2-vs-D3 bioavailability difference already sitting in this app's own `supplement_forms` table, now surfaced in a real Digest entry for the first time; a real, specific, mechanistic reason obesity tracks with lower vitamin D (fat-tissue sequestration, with a real, already-established clinical response -- bariatric-surgery guidelines recommending several times the standard dose); and two real, named anticonvulsant medications (phenobarbital, valproic acid) with two genuinely different, specific, documented mechanisms interfering with vitamin D activation.

**The single most notable find: a real, current (2024) 18-expert Endocrine Society clinical practice guideline recommending something genuinely more conservative than the "everyone should test and take vitamin D" reflex most popular advice repeats** -- it suggests against routine population-wide testing and against supplementing most healthy adults under 75 above the standard target, reserving real, targeted supplementation for specific, named groups instead (children, adults 75+, pregnant people, high-risk prediabetes). Paired directly with the real, large (25,871-participant), rigorous VITAL trial's own honest null result for cancer and cardiovascular disease prevention -- the same "report a null finding exactly as directly as a positive one" discipline this whole Digest has held to throughout, now applied to one of the most commonly recommended supplements in the whole build.

Real food-source data pulled directly from this app's own 22,022-food reference database (cod liver oil at a real, wide-margin-leading 250mcg/100g; UV-treated "vitamin D enhanced" mushrooms specifically, not ordinary mushrooms, at ~24mcg/100g; canned salmon and cooked trout both around 19-22mcg/100g), visualized as a real `DigestChart`, the same "improve on the spreadsheet with real, sourced numbers" discipline established for Magnesium.

**Iron built as the third Essential Nutrients deep-dive, 2026-08-08, same day, direct request: "I like the Magnesium information, move to the next one after Magnesium, and continue through all of the rest of the macro and micronutrients, and acids, and all things within reason... in the sections about specific 18 conditions there should be things just like this but specific to them and their disease and how it affects them."** Confirms this is meant as a real, ongoing series (matching `essentialNutrients.ts`'s own header comment, already written to say so) -- the second half of the request, condition-specific deep-dive nutrient content, is a real, separate, standing direction to apply going forward as each condition's own nutrient story warrants it, not something retrofitted onto all 18 conditions in one pass.

Checked this app's own reference tables first, the same discipline as Magnesium/Vitamin D: `supplement_forms` already carried real, cited ferrous sulfate vs. iron bisglycinate GI-tolerance data (Tolkien et al. 2015, PLoS One, PMID 25700159, a 43-trial/6,800-adult meta-analysis, OR 2.32 for GI side effects vs. placebo) from the earlier My Meds work, never surfaced in a Digest entry until now. This session's own WebSearch budget was already exhausted before this research began (confirmed via a direct tool-system message), so every new citation came via the established WebFetch-against-real-pages fallback -- four StatPearls/NCBI Bookshelf articles (Iron Deficiency Anemia, Hemochromatosis, Iron Toxicity, Dietary Iron) plus one more PubMed-verified systematic review.

10 new entries in `lib/digest/essentialNutrients.ts`: overview, deficiency prevalence/causes (a real 14% US-adult prevalence by NHANES data, with the exact number swinging based on which ferritin cutoff is used), staged deficiency symptoms (a real three-phase progression -- stores depleted, then circulating iron falls, then hemoglobin synthesis itself fails -- with real symptoms like fatigue and restless leg syndrome documented to appear before anemia itself shows up on a standard blood count), acute overdose (a real, named five-stage clinical course, including the genuinely dangerous Stage 2 "apparent recovery" window where organ damage is still silently progressing), hereditary hemochromatosis (a real 1-in-300-to-500 prevalence in white populations, real organ-by-organ damage data, and a striking, directly relevant finding: an 80-fold increased hypothyroidism risk in affected men), the heme-vs-non-heme absorption mechanism (25% vs. 17% or less, hepcidin as the real master regulator, the real "MFP factor" enhancing non-heme absorption 2-3 fold), the supplement-forms comparison (pulled directly from the app's own already-cited reference data), a real, honestly-mixed finding on cooking in cast iron cookware (a 2021 systematic review of 13 studies found consistent improvement in food iron content, but only 4 of 13 studies found a significant resulting hemoglobin increase), a real food-sources chart pulled from this app's own 22,022-food database (pork liver leading at 23.3mg/100g, with spinach's own popular reputation as an iron powerhouse honestly corrected against its real, more modest 3.6mg/100g non-heme value), and a closing tying-together entry.

Deliberately did NOT duplicate `advocacy-iron-ferritin` (the existing hashimotos-category entry covering iron's own measured TSH/FT4/FT3 correlation and testing recommendation) -- that stays exactly where it is, cross-linked from the new `iron-tying-together` and `iron-deficiency-symptoms-staged` entries, per this session's own Basic Health scope-correction rule (universal biology belongs in Basic Health; a specific disease's own testing guidance belongs in that disease's own category). Reciprocal cross-links added both directions: `advocacy-iron-ferritin` (selfAdvocacy.ts) and `ckd-anemia-erythropoietin` (chronicKidneyDisease.ts) both gained real relatedIds pointing back to the new content; all four of the pre-existing Nutrient Interactions entries touching iron (calcium/iron, vitamin C/iron, iron/zinc/manganese, tannins/iron) gained a reciprocal link to `iron-absorption-mechanism`, the new entry covering the actual regulatory mechanism (hepcidin) underneath all four of those individual pairwise interactions. `BASIC_HEALTH_GROUPS` (`app/(tabs)/purple-digest.tsx`) gained a new "Iron" shelf-row entry, placed right after Vitamin D.

**Zinc built as the fourth Essential Nutrients deep-dive, 2026-08-08, same day, direct continuation: "Continue with zinc next."** Unlike Magnesium/Vitamin D/Iron, zinc already had a fair amount of real content in this Digest before this pass -- the zinc/copper antagonism (`interaction-zinc-copper`, `advocacy-zinc-copper`), the vitamin A/zinc mechanistic link (`interaction-vitamina-zinc`), the zinc-carnosine gut-barrier entry (`gut-zinc-carnosine`), and a Hashimoto's-specific zinc/iron/B12 symptom-overlap entry (`nutrient-zinc-iron-b12`) -- so this pass was scoped specifically to fill the real remaining gap (a genuine deep dive: staged deficiency, toxicity, absorption mechanism, food sources, tying together) rather than duplicate any of it, cross-linking to the existing entries both directions instead.

WebSearch remained exhausted; every new citation came via the established WebFetch-against-StatPearls/PubMed fallback -- two StatPearls articles (Zinc Deficiency, Zinc Toxicity) plus a real, individual-patient-data meta-analysis on zinc and the common cold.

9 new entries: overview (over 300 real zinc-dependent enzymes), deficiency prevalence (a real, estimated 2 billion people worldwide, with real regional variation from 7.5% in high-income countries up to a third of the population in parts of Sub-Saharan Africa/Southeast Asia/the Middle East), staged deficiency symptoms (a real, distinctive picture -- taste/smell loss, spearhead-shaped brittle hair, eczema-like skin lesions in friction zones -- genuinely more specific than most other nutrient deficiencies), toxicity (both acute high-dose GI illness at roughly 1-2g and, more insidiously, the real "swayback" neurological picture from sustained over-supplementation via the same copper-depletion mechanism already covered in this app's existing zinc/copper entries, cross-linked rather than re-derived), the absorption mechanism (real, named ZnT/ZIP transporter gene families, phytates as the dominant real blocker, the acrodermatitis enteropathica genetic case as a real demonstration of how essential the Zip4 transporter is), the supplement-forms comparison (reusing the app's own already-cited data), a real, moderately-tiered entry on zinc lozenges and the common cold (2.73-2.94 days shorter colds in a real individual-patient-data meta-analysis, honestly caveated at just 199 total patients), a real food-sources chart (oysters at a real, enormous ~91mg/100g lead, dwarfing every other source), and a closing tying-together entry.

**Seven more Essential Nutrients deep-dives built in one continuous pass, 2026-08-08, same day, direct request: "Continue with B12 next, and then continue automatically through the rest and report here which ones were completed."** B12, Folate, Calcium, Potassium, a deeper Iodine pass, Vitamin C, and Vitamin A, in that order, each following the same established pattern (check `supplement_forms`/`nutrient_interactions`/`nutrient_timing` first for real, already-cited data from the earlier My Meds work; verify every new claim via the established WebFetch-against-StatPearls/PubMed fallback, WebSearch remaining exhausted all session; reciprocal cross-links added both directions rather than duplicating any existing content; a real `tsc`/`eslint`/dangling-id verification and a `BASIC_HEALTH_GROUPS` shelf-row entry added after each one). 45 new entries total (588 unique ids overall, up from 543, zero duplicates, zero dangling `relatedIds`).

**B12** (8 entries) covered a genuinely different shape of nutrient story from Magnesium/Vitamin D/Iron/Zinc before it: its real absorption relay (salivary R-factor, gastric intrinsic factor, terminal ileum receptors, explaining why so many unrelated conditions -- gastric bypass, Crohn's, celiac, pernicious anemia -- each independently cause deficiency), and a real, honest toxicity non-issue (the literature states directly "no overdosage occurs," a genuine exception worth naming plainly rather than forcing a generic caution that doesn't apply). Cross-linked to, not duplicated with, the existing Hashimoto's-specific `advocacy-b12-folate` (the real 28%-of-AITD-patients/pernicious-anemia finding).

**Folate** (7 entries) led with the single most clinically important cross-nutrient fact in this whole session: folic acid can correct B12-deficiency anemia (the visible lab sign) while B12's own separate neurological damage keeps progressing completely unrecognized, straight from the same StatPearls source, real, direct, and worth its own dedicated entry rather than a footnote. Also covered the real, settled neural-tube-defect public-health win (600mcg pregnancy / 4-5mg high-risk) and reused the app's own already-cited L-methylfolate/MTHFR-vs-folic-acid data.

**Calcium** (7 entries) surfaced a real, direct thyroid-surgery connection worth naming: postsurgical parathyroid injury (most often during thyroidectomy) accounts for roughly 75% of real hypoparathyroidism cases, with transient post-thyroidectomy hypocalcemia affecting a real 6.9-49% of patients -- cross-linked to Graves' `graves-overview`, since thyroidectomy is a real treatment path there. Hyperthyroidism itself is also a real, documented hypercalcemia cause, the same cross-link working in both directions. Reused the app's own rich, already-cited `supplement_forms`/`nutrient_timing` data (citrate vs. carbonate, the real 500mg single-dose absorption ceiling).

**Potassium** (7 entries) covered both real, dangerous extremes (hypokalemia and hyperkalemia) with their own real, staged ECG progressions, and a real, honest reframing of "just eat a banana": in this app's own database, white beans, avocado, salmon, and sweet potato all carry more potassium per 100g than banana's own real, more modest 358mg/100g. Cross-linked to the existing CKD potassium-restriction and ACE/ARB-monitoring entries, and PCOS's spironolactone caution, rather than re-deriving any of them.

**Iodine** (5 entries, deliberately the tightest pass of the seven) filled the real remaining gap in an already iodine-rich Digest (the NIS transporter and Wolff-Chaikoff effect already in Glossary, the 1924 Michigan history entry, Hashimoto's own two-edged-nutrient framing, a full Graves'-specific entry, pregnancy needs, kelp caution): the actual universal thyroid-hormone-synthesis mechanism (transport -> organification -> coupling) and real global deficiency/toxicity numbers (a leading, preventable cause of fetal brain damage; real safe-upper/harmful thresholds at ~1mg/1.1mg per day).

**Vitamin C** (6 entries) covered scurvy as a genuinely still-modern condition (real prevalence from 7.1% in the US to 73.9% in parts of northern India, not a historical curiosity), a real, narrow toxicity risk concentrated almost entirely in kidney-stone-prone men, and a real, distinctive dose-dependent absorption mechanism (efficient to ~100mg/day, dropping to 50% or less above 1,500mg in one dose) -- the identical single-dose-ceiling pattern already established for calcium, named directly as a recurring theme across this whole series rather than a coincidence. Cross-linked to the existing gout vitamin-C entry and the iron/vitamin-C interaction entry.

**Vitamin A** (5 entries) is built around what turned out to be the real, single most important teaching point in the whole entry: preformed retinol (liver, cod liver oil) and provitamin A carotenoids (carrots, sweet potato) carry genuinely different real safety profiles, not a symmetric "vitamin A in excess is dangerous" rule. Real, specific, sobering numbers: adverse fetal effects documented at doses as low as 25,000 IU daily during pregnancy, with the literature stating directly there is no established safe threshold for oral retinoid exposure in pregnancy -- cross-linked to the Pregnancy & Family Planning category's own tying-together entry. Real food-source data independently confirms the same split: liver and cod liver oil run 10,000-30,000mcg/100g, concentrated enough that a large real serving can meaningfully approach the acute toxicity threshold covered in the same entry.

**Vitamin E** (5 entries) found a real, direct, worth-remembering connection sitting unused in this app's own reference database: `interaction_rules` already carried a real, cited vitamin K2/warfarin dose-consistency rule from the earlier My Meds work, and vitamin E's own real bleeding-risk mechanism (three real, documented pathways -- vitamin K antagonism, reduced glutamate/factor IX production, platelet inhibition) works through the exact same warfarin pathway, from the opposite direction. Real, specific numbers: drug interactions reported at doses as low as 300mg/day, symptoms typically starting around 1,000mg/day.

**Vitamin K** (6 entries) reused rich, already-cited `supplement_forms` data (K1 vs. MK-4 vs. MK-7, the real 45mg/day Japanese osteoporosis-trial dose) and filled the real remaining gap: the genuine K1 (liver/clotting) vs. K2 (bone/soft-tissue calcium-placement) functional split, and the real, physiological reason newborns get a routine vitamin K shot (a real, three-stage VKDB timing classification, plus a real, current, worth-knowing finding that VKDB incidence is rising, tied directly to parental refusal of the shot). Cross-linked directly to Vitamin E's own bleeding-risk entry -- two real, different vitamins, interacting with the same medication through genuinely opposite mechanisms.

**Omega-3 & Omega-6 Fatty Acids** (6 entries) hit a genuinely different research situation from every prior nutrient: this app's own reference database tracks only aggregate fat categories, not EPA/DHA/ALA/omega-6 specifically, so no real, DB-verified food-sources chart was possible -- deliberately left out rather than faked, the first entry in this whole series to skip a chart for that reason. Real, specific numbers found instead: whole-body ALA-to-DHA conversion runs below 5% (Brenna 2002, the classic, still-cited reference on this exact figure), and the real omega-6:omega-3 ratio mechanism (each family is the literal biochemical raw material for opposing inflammatory signaling molecules) was built as a genuine biochemistry deep-dive complementing an already-existing, food-swap-focused Problem Foods entry on the same ratio, rather than duplicating it. The real standout of this whole nutrient: 5 already-built, condition-specific omega-3 entries (RA strong, Sjögren's positive, Psoriasis/Lupus mixed, CVD honest null) all cross-linked into one closing entry, the clearest real demonstration anywhere in this Digest of the same nutrient landing in genuinely different evidence tiers depending on which condition and which outcome is actually being asked about.

**Protein & Essential Amino Acids** (6 entries, the fourth and final nutrient of this batch) covered the real 9 essential amino acids, the real marasmus-vs-kwashiorkor distinction (two genuinely different diseases, not two names for the same thing -- kwashiorkor's own real defining sign, body-wide edema with minimal visible wasting, is the literal opposite of what most people picture), and a real, named, historically-documented toxicity syndrome most people don't expect protein to have at all: "rabbit starvation," with a real, specific research threshold (protein above 35% of total energy intake) and real safe-maximum guidance (roughly 2-2.5g/kg/day, well below the real theoretical maximum the liver's own nitrogen-processing capacity could handle). Cross-linked directly to CKD's own existing low-protein-diet entry -- the one real, direct exception in this whole app to protein's otherwise-wide safety margin, since damaged kidneys genuinely can't clear the same nitrogen load a healthy person's own safety margin assumes.

**A real, standing direction change, 2026-08-08, same day, stated directly: "we also need to stop saying things in the app about comparisons to Hashimoto's for any reason just because I started out trying to build this app just for that one disease... all of them need to be just as in depth and well thought out and inclusive as the Hashimoto's information."** Two things landed from this. First, a real, bounded reframing audit across the non-Hashimoto's condition files: grepped every one of the 18 condition-specific Digest files for "Hashimoto" mentions and hand-reviewed each. Most turned out to be legitimate, real, quantified comorbidity findings (RA-Hashimoto's polyautoimmunity, MS's 20-25% thyroid-autoimmunity overlap, celiac's 15.4%-vs-7.5% case-control finding) worth keeping exactly as real epidemiology -- those stayed. A real, different pattern was fixed where it existed: Graves' own overview/smoking/tying-together text (built explicitly around "the mirror image of Hashimoto's" as its organizing frame), Multiple Sclerosis's smoking entry ("unlike this app's own Hashimoto's research, there is no paradox here"), and Rheumatoid Arthritis's overview/comorbidity/tying-together text (explicitly framed as "why this app is building RA first" for "the people this app already exists for") were all reworded to stand on their own, describing each disease's real mechanism and evidence directly rather than measuring it against Hashimoto's as the reference point. A minor tweak to Lupus's own teaser (which used Hashimoto's as an illustrative comparison point) rounded this out. This is a real, permanent standard now, not a one-time cleanup -- future condition entries should never be framed relative to Hashimoto's, even when a real cross-condition finding legitimately involves it.

Second, and much larger: **"full depth parity" for all 18 non-Hashimoto's conditions is now a real, explicit, standing goal**, not yet complete. Checked directly rather than assumed: each condition-specific file currently holds 9-15 entries (RA 13, Psoriasis 15, Graves' 10, T1D 11, Celiac 10, IBD 11, MS 15, Lupus 11, Sjögren's 11, PCOS 11, CKD 10, MASLD 10, T2D 10, IBS 10, Migraine 9, CVD 9, Gout 12, now Prostate Health 12) against Hashimoto's own 176 entries spread across a dozen real sub-categories (Healing Stages, Organs & Body Systems, History & Milestones, Pregnancy & Family Planning, its own Self-Advocacy split, Big Picture's narrative device, and the Hashimoto's-tagged portions of Gut & Microbiome/Lifestyle & Environment/Mitochondria & Metabolism/Nutrients/Labs & Medication Timing/Problem Foods, plus the dedicated "Why This App Exists" section). That's a real, roughly 12-to-1 depth gap, not a close call. The `conditions` reference table's own `status` column already tracked this honestly -- every condition but Hashimoto's reads `in_progress`, not `built` -- so the real target for each of the 18 is the same subcategory checklist Hashimoto's already has: its own real Healing/Management-Stages-equivalent content (what changes at diagnosis, through active management, into remission/control, where that framework exists for the condition), its own Organs & Body Systems entries (not just the primary organ, every real documented system effect), its own History & Milestones (real diagnostic/treatment turning points), Pregnancy & Family Planning where medically relevant, and self-advocacy content already mostly present per-condition since the Graves'-onward build. This is real, large, multi-session work, comparable in scope to the reference-database build itself -- not attempted in full this pass. **Deliberately not started in this same pass beyond the reframing fix and the new condition below** -- the next session picking this up should work through the 17 non-Hashimoto's conditions in the same priority order already established (`conditions.sort_order`), building out whichever subcategories a given condition is thinnest in first, the same "continue automatically, report what's done" pattern already proven across the nutrient series.

**Prostate Health added as this app's nineteenth real condition, 2026-08-08, same day, direct question: "Is there any solid reason for prostate health to be included related to food health and gut microbiome and whole foods?"** Researched directly before deciding, not assumed from the premise alone -- and the real evidence answered yes, decisively. Benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPH) is genuinely one of the most common conditions in aging men, real data confirming 29% prevalence in men in their 50s, climbing to 44.7% in their 60s, 58.1% in their 70s, and 69.2% at 80+; prostate cancer is the most commonly diagnosed cancer in American men (~27% of new male cancer diagnoses). The gut-microbiome connection is real and specific, not a stretch to include here: a real systematic review found gut dysbiosis (an elevated Firmicutes/Bacteroidetes ratio, real shifts in Prevotella/Ruminococcus/Lactobacillus abundance) directly linked to BPH through the same SCFA-production/systemic-inflammation mechanism this app's own Gut & Microbiome research already documents for autoimmune disease; specific Ruminococcus strains can directly convert hormone precursors into androgens inside the gut, with real, documented worse prognosis in castrate-resistant prostate cancer patients carrying more of it; and a real, large, 22-year study (47,896 men) found the highest quintile of dietary choline intake carried a 70% higher risk of lethal prostate cancer, mediated by gut bacteria converting choline into TMAO.

Added to the `conditions` reference table (`prostate_health`, non_autoimmune, sort_order 19, status `in_progress`) -- Profile's own condition picker already reads this table dynamically, so no app-code change was needed for it to appear there. New `lib/digest/prostateHealth.ts`, 12 entries: overview; the real gut-dysbiosis-to-BPH mechanism (cross-linked to `gut-scfa-treg`); the real Ruminococcus-to-androgen mechanism; a real whole-food/plant-forward diet-pattern entry; two real, individually strong protective foods (lycopene/tomatoes, a real dose-response meta-analysis; cruciferous vegetables/sulforaphane, explicitly cross-referencing the same glucosinolate compound family this app's own Hashimoto's research already flags for its raw goitrogenic effect -- a genuine "same compound family, different real effect in a different context" teaching moment); the real choline/TMAO finding; zinc's real, distinctive prostate-tissue concentration (reusing this app's own existing Zinc sub-criterion and Essential Nutrients deep-dive directly, cross-linked both directions); a real, honest correction (the landmark SELECT trial found selenium doesn't prevent prostate cancer, and vitamin E, its usual supplement partner, may raise risk by 17% -- a genuine humbling contrast to selenium's own strong Hashimoto's evidence, both stated honestly); saw palmetto's genuinely mixed evidence (a real null trial and real positive meta-analyses side by side, likely explained by non-standardized commercial extracts); a real self-advocacy lab-interpretation trap (5-alpha-reductase inhibitors cut PSA roughly in half, the same "check the medication before trusting the number" discipline already established for biotin's own lab-interference entry); PSA screening's own honest, quantified benefit-versus-harm tradeoff (real USPSTF numbers: at most a very small mortality benefit even in the optimal 55-69 age range, against a real ~1-in-5 long-term incontinence rate and ~2-in-3 long-term erectile-dysfunction rate after prostatectomy); and a closing tying-together entry.

3 new `common_medications` rows (finasteride, dutasteride, tamsulosin) -- no new `interaction_rules` row, since no real, verifiable food/drug interaction was found for any of the three beyond the PSA-monitoring point already covered as Digest content, a real, deliberate scope boundary rather than a gap. Food-scoring dimensions reused 3 existing sub-criteria, zero new ones needed: Zinc (id 3, under a new "Prostate Tissue Mineral Density" dimension label, grounded in the real tissue-concentration finding) and Additives/Processing (id 8/9, under "Whole-Food, Anti-Dysbiosis Pattern," grounded in the real Western-diet-to-dysbiosis mechanism). Verified directly via a live SQL join (the same `getFoodScoresForCondition` query shape used to confirm every prior condition) that real foods resolve correctly under `condition_code='prostate_health'` with zero code changes needed -- the reuse-first architecture built for RA now confirmed generalizing to a ninth real non-autoimmune condition.

**The Essential Nutrients series finished the same day, direct request: "Finish the rest of the macronutrients, micronutrients, acids, and hormones first."** 50 new entries: the full B-vitamin family (Thiamine/B1, Riboflavin/B2, Niacin/B3, Pantothenic Acid/B5, Biotin/B7, and a fuller Vitamin B6 deep-dive), four remaining trace-mineral/nutrient candidates named at the end of the original 15-topic build (Chromium, Manganese, Copper, Choline), the two remaining macronutrients (Carbohydrates & Fiber, Water & Hydration), and a genuinely new topic area, Hormones (13 entries in a new `lib/digest/hormones.ts` -- insulin, cortisol/HPA axis, a compact thyroid-hormones overview, leptin & ghrelin, estrogen & progesterone, testosterone). "Acids" was confirmed already covered rather than rebuilt -- Omega-3/6 Fatty Acids and Protein & Essential Amino Acids, both already real, full deep-dives from the original 15-topic series.

Every citation independently verified via WebSearch (this session's own budget was fresh, not exhausted, so no WebFetch fallback was needed this pass). Real, distinctive findings per topic, not filler: thiamine's own real history ties directly to this app's own food-industry-history milling entry (industrial rice/grain milling is the actual documented cause of historic beriberi epidemics); riboflavin's real, strong migraine-prevention trial evidence (50% frequency reduction, formal AAN/AHS endorsement) cross-links to the existing `migraine-magnesium-riboflavin-coq10` entry; niacin's real two-sided deficiency (pellagra, tied to un-nixtamalized corn as a dominant staple) and toxicity (flushing's own GPR109A mechanism, real hepatotoxicity at high supplemental doses); biotin's real deficiency mechanism (raw egg-white avidin binding) explicitly distinguished from its much more commonly relevant story, already covered in this app's own Labs & Medication Timing category, that high-dose supplementation fakes thyroid lab results; B6's genuine two-sided neuropathy story (deficiency and supplement-dose toxicity damage nerves through two different, real mechanisms); chromium's honestly mixed, moderate-tier evidence (a real 28-study meta-analysis found significant HOMA-IR/fasting-glucose reduction but no significant HbA1c change, explicitly not oversold); manganese's real, distinctive lopsided risk profile (deficiency barely documented in humans; toxicity a real, serious occupational-exposure risk, with a genuine, useful tie to iron status -- iron deficiency increases manganese uptake and brain deposition); copper's two real, named, opposite genetic diseases (Menkes, Wilson) plus the far more practically relevant acquired deficiency from high-dose zinc supplementation, cross-linked to the app's own existing zinc-copper interaction entry; choline's genuine two-extremes arc (deficiency directly impairs the liver's own fat-export pathway, a real mechanistic MASLD link; excess ties directly back to this app's own already-built `prostate-choline-tmao` finding, completing both sides of the same nutrient's story); fiber's real, striking intake gap (94% of Americans don't meet the RDA) cross-linked to the reuse-first "Carbohydrate Density Relative to Fiber" sub-criterion already built for Type 1 Diabetes/PCOS/Type 2 Diabetes; and water's own genuinely unusual status as the one nutrient in this whole series with no official government DRI at all, with real, quantified water-intoxication risk shown to concentrate almost entirely in two specific situations (drinking contests, endurance exercise outpacing real sodium loss), not ordinary daily drinking.

**Hormones is a genuinely new content area for this Digest, built the same evidence-tiered, cited way as every nutrient entry, and deliberately scoped to the hormones this app's own conditions already touch most directly rather than an exhaustive endocrinology textbook.** Its own real throughline, stated directly in a closing tying-together entry: insulin resistance is the same shared real mechanism this app's own Type 2 Diabetes research already names as connecting T2D, PCOS, MASLD, and CKD; cortisol/HPA-axis dysregulation is the same shared real pathway this app's own Lifestyle & Environment research already traces through alcohol, sleep, high-intensity exercise, and glucose crashes -- both cross-linked directly rather than re-derived. A real, new, independently-verified finding not previously in this app: chronic stress genuinely breaks the HPA axis's own negative-feedback loop rather than just "raising cortisol," producing glucocorticoid-receptor resistance and a real, documented shift toward autoimmunity specifically, not generic inflammation (PMC12563903). Leptin's own real, counterintuitive finding (obesity involves too MUCH leptin, not too little, with resistance -- not deficiency -- as the actual mechanism) is presented as a direct parallel to insulin resistance's own already-established shape. Thyroid hormones got a deliberately compact entry, since this app's own real depth on that specific system already lives in Hashimoto's and Graves' -- duplicating it here would have been redundant, not additive. Testosterone's own real, quantified age-decline data (roughly 20%/30%/50% of men over 60/70/80 testosterone-deficient) closes with a direct, practical cross-link to this app's own Prostate Health research, since testosterone-replacement decisions in men with BPH or prostate-cancer risk genuinely require real coordination with a urologist.

**A real, latent bug in the Basic Health shelf-browsing UI was caught and fixed while wiring this batch in, before it could ship broken.** `BASIC_HEALTH_GROUPS` (`app/(tabs)/purple-digest.tsx`) needed several DIFFERENT id-prefixes to share ONE shelf label for the first time (six B-vitamin prefixes all under "B-Vitamins," three mineral prefixes under "Chromium, Manganese & Copper") -- but `groupBasicHealthEntries`'s own `order` array was built directly from `BASIC_HEALTH_GROUPS.map(g => g.label)` with no deduplication, meaning a shared label would have produced one fully-duplicated shelf row per prefix that mapped to it (six identical "B-Vitamins" rows, not one merged row) rather than the single real, correctly-merged shelf intended. Caught by tracing the actual rendering logic before trusting the new data, not after a report -- fixed with a one-line `[...new Set(...)]` dedup, hoisted to module scope since it no longer needs to be recomputed inside the function.

Whole-directory verification: 674 unique ids (624 + 50 new), zero duplicates, zero dangling `relatedIds`. `PURPLE_DIGEST_VERSION` bumped again the same day (double-checked as numerically later than the prior value) -- no `REFERENCE_DB_VERSION` change needed, pure TypeScript content, no SQL/reference-database changes this pass. Verified via `npx tsc --noEmit` (clean across the whole project) and `npx eslint` on every touched file (`lib/digest/essentialNutrients.ts`, `lib/digest/hormones.ts`, `lib/digest/index.ts`, `lib/digest/labsMedication.ts`, `lib/digest/foodIndustryHistory.ts`, `lib/digest/type2Diabetes.ts`, `lib/digest/pcos.ts`, `lib/digest/fattyLiverDisease.ts`, `lib/digest/type1Diabetes.ts`, `app/(tabs)/purple-digest.tsx`) -- all clean. Metro was already running fresh (relaunched earlier the same day) and left running, since this pass needed no reference-database reimport. **Not yet exercised on-device** -- the new "B-Vitamins"/"Chromium, Manganese & Copper"/"Hormones" shelf rows in particular are worth a real on-device check given the shelf-merging bug just fixed above.

**The last remaining macronutrient, Dietary Fat, built as its own real topic** (distinct from the existing Omega-3/6 balance entry, which covers a specific fat-quality ratio, not fat as a macronutrient category itself) -- 4 new entries in `essentialNutrients.ts`: real function (9 kcal/g energy density, cell-membrane structure, the real, practical fact that vitamins A/D/E/K genuinely need dietary fat present to absorb well), a real, honest complication to popular olive-oil advice (a major meta-analysis found cutting saturated fat lowers cardiovascular risk by a real 21%, but found clear replacement evidence only for polyunsaturated fat and carbohydrate, with monounsaturated-fat-specific replacement data genuinely too limited to draw a firm conclusion from, distinct from the separately well-evidenced Mediterranean-diet-as-a-whole-pattern research already in this app), a real food-sources chart pulled directly from this app's own database (`fat_monounsaturated` nutrient code -- olive oil 70.2g/100g, almonds 35.1g, avocado 9.8g), and a tying-together entry. All macronutrients (Protein, Carbohydrates & Fiber, Fat, Water) are now real, complete Digest topics.

**Full-depth parity work started, and completed for 10 of 18 non-Hashimoto's conditions this same pass: Rheumatoid Arthritis, Psoriasis, Graves' Disease, Type 1 Diabetes, Celiac Disease, IBD, Multiple Sclerosis, Lupus, Sjögren's Syndrome, and PCOS.** Each condition got the same real, consistent 4-entry addition, directly answering the "true drivers" framing from the user's own context above -- a real formal disease-staging/classification system where one genuinely exists (RA's ACR/EULAR remission criteria and Treat-to-Target strategy; Psoriasis's PASI/BSA severity classification; Graves' real antithyroid-drug remission-rate data by treatment length; T1D's JDRF 3-stage presymptomatic framework; Celiac's Marsh histological staging; IBD's Montreal classification; MS's four real disease-course types; Lupus's SLEDAI disease-activity scoring; Sjögren's real primary-vs-secondary distinction; PCOS's four Rotterdam phenotypes A-D); a real, additional Organ & Body Systems entry naming systemic effects beyond what self-advocacy content already covered (RA's real extra-articular manifestations -- nodules, lung, eye; Psoriasis's real metabolic-syndrome/NAFLD/depression comorbidity web; Graves' real cardiac risk up to thyroid storm; T1D's real long-term complication burden; Celiac's real neurological/dermatologic/reproductive reach; IBD's real quantified joint/skin/eye split by disease; MS's real bladder/bowel/fatigue/cognitive burden, often more universal than mobility symptoms; Lupus's real skin/neuropsychiatric/clotting reach; Sjögren's real lung/vasculitis/neuropathy involvement; PCOS's real sleep-apnea/depression/NAFLD web); a real History & Milestones entry (RA: Garrod father-and-son naming, cortisone 1948, methotrexate 1988, etanercept 1998; Psoriasis: Willan separating it from leprosy in 1808, autoimmune recognition only in 1963; Graves': the real Parry-vs-Graves naming irony, radioactive iodine's 1941 wartime-era origin; T1D: Banting and Best's 1921 discovery, a genuinely fast ~2-year path to treatment; Celiac: Dicke's real accidental WWII bread-shortage discovery; IBD: the two diseases' real 57-year discovery gap; MS: Charcot's 1868 description, a real 130-year wait for the first disease-modifying therapy; Lupus: four real sequential 19th/20th-century discoveries; Sjögren's: Henrik Sjögren's real 19-patient 1933 thesis; PCOS: Stein-Leventhal's 1935 case series, three real subsequent redefinitions); and, where clinically relevant, a real Pregnancy & Family Planning entry with genuinely specific, quantified numbers (RA's real pregnancy-improves/postpartum-flares pattern and methotrexate's real 3-month-before-conception rule; Psoriasis's real, genuinely mixed 40-60%-improve/10-20%-worsen pregnancy split plus real biologic-safety data; Graves' real, counterintuitive finding that a mother's own TRAb can cause fetal thyrotoxicosis even after SHE has become hypothyroid from past treatment; T1D's real, tighter pregnancy glucose targets and the real, quantified stakes of falling short of them; Celiac's real 8.9-fold miscarriage risk that fully reverses on a gluten-free diet; IBD's real finding that remission AT CONCEPTION, not just during pregnancy, is the single biggest predictor of a healthy outcome; MS's real, dramatic 70%-relapse-reduction-then-postpartum-doubling swing; Lupus's real anti-Ro/anti-La congenital-heart-block risk; Sjögren's own real, quantified version of that same antibody risk, 1-2% baseline jumping to 17-18% after one affected pregnancy; PCOS's real letrozole-over-clomiphene evidence for ovulation induction).

**One real, self-caught id collision, worth remembering for the remaining conditions**: IBD's own new "extra-articular manifestations" entry was drafted with an id (`ibd-extraintestinal-manifestations`) that turned out to already exist from the original build (a real, different entry covering PSC specifically). Caught by the same whole-directory dangling/duplicate-id verification script this Digest's entire build has used throughout, not by inspection -- renamed to `ibd-extraintestinal-real-prevalence-split`, reframed as a direct, quantified companion to the original entry rather than a duplicate, and cross-linked both directions. Worth checking existing ids in a condition's own file before writing predictably-named new ones (`condition-organ-systems`-style names are the real collision risk) for whichever conditions come next.

**"Keep going," direct instruction the same day, immediately after the 10-of-18 checkpoint above -- the remaining 8 conditions (Chronic Kidney Disease, Fatty Liver Disease, Type 2 Diabetes, IBS, Migraine, Cardiovascular Disease, Gout, Prostate Health) all completed the same real 4-entry treatment in this same continuous pass.** Real staging systems this batch surfaced: CKD's KDIGO "heat map" (G1-G5 combined with A1-A3 into a real color-coded risk zone, more predictive than either number alone); MASLD's real F0-F4 fibrosis staging (94% of MASLD stays in the still-reversible F0-F3 range, only 5-6% reaches cirrhosis); T2D's real, formal prediabetes stage (detectable up to 20 years before symptoms, 60% progressing to full T2D within a decade untreated); IBS's real Rome IV subtypes (IBS-C/D/M/unclassified via a real 14-day Bristol Stool Scale diary); migraine's real, still-unsettled episodic-vs-chronic 15-day line (real research finding people at 8-14 days genuinely just as disabled as the formal "chronic" threshold); CVD's real ACC/AHA heart-failure Stages A-D (Stage A applies to real, common risk factors like hypertension before any actual heart damage exists); gout's real four-stage natural history (asymptomatic hyperuricemia through chronic tophaceous, with the deceptively quiet intercritical stage still silently progressing underneath); and prostate health's two genuinely different real systems (the AUA Symptom Score for BPH, Gleason/Grade Groups plus TNM for cancer).

Real, additional organ-system reach surfaced per condition: CKD's real CKD-MBD syndrome (3-fold fracture risk, 10-fold cardiovascular risk, one shared mineral-dysregulation mechanism driving both); MASLD's real, independent cardiovascular/kidney risk (fibrosis stage, not raw liver fat, is the strongest predictor); T2D's real, often-underdiagnosed cognitive decline (53.88% of one over-60 T2D cohort already showing mild cognitive impairment, tied directly to liver fibrosis via the shared FIB-4 marker); IBS's real bidirectional fibromyalgia link (each condition roughly doubling the other's risk over 5 years, part of a real, named "central sensitivity disorder" cluster with migraine); migraine-with-aura's real, independent stroke/cardiovascular risk (nearly double, holding up even after adjusting for other risk factors); CVD's real reach into kidney, brain, and legs (200+ million people with peripheral artery disease worldwide, one shared atherosclerotic process); gout's real 71%-CKD/74%-hypertension comorbidity burden; and BPH's real, documented capacity to damage the bladder and, in severe cases, the kidneys if left untreated.

Real history entries: CKD's own genuinely improvised 1943 wartime dialysis machine (sausage casings, orange juice cans, a washing machine); MASLD's unusually fast real three-name history (1980 to 1986 to a 2023 supermajority-vote rename); T2D's metformin traveling from a traditional herbal remedy through 1940s malaria research before finally being tested for diabetes in 1957; IBS's real diagnostic framework being barely a decade old in its current (2016) form despite the condition itself being ancient; migraine's nearly-2,000-year gap between Aretaeus's A.D. 81 description and 1991's first real breakthrough treatment; CVD's real, single ongoing Framingham study (launched 1948) that established the risk-factor framework this whole app's own CVD research still runs on; gout's real 4,600-year documented history (ancient Egypt, Hippocrates) against uric acid only being identified as the actual cause in 1848; and prostate health's real PSA test not existing at all until 1986.

Pregnancy/family-planning was genuinely adapted, not forced, where the real underlying condition made a standard pregnancy-risk entry the wrong shape: CKD's real bidirectional preeclampsia relationship (CKD raises preeclampsia risk up to 79% in some studies; preeclampsia itself then measurably accelerates real, long-term kidney decline afterward); MASLD's real, quantified elevated risk (23% gestational-diabetes rate vs. 7-8%) paired with a real, explicit reassurance that MASLD does NOT raise risk of the separate, rare emergency (AFLP) it's sometimes confused with; T2D's real finding that pre-existing T2D nearly doubles congenital-anomaly risk compared to gestational diabetes, with A1C in the first 14 weeks the one real sustained predictor; IBS's own entry honestly tiered weaker than this app's other pregnancy research, since the real underlying evidence here is genuinely thinner (mostly clinical experience, not large quantified cohorts); migraine's real, striking 87%-improved-by-third-trimester finding, the same estrogen-stability mechanism already named in this app's own menstrual-migraine research working in reverse; CVD's real mWHO pregnancy risk-classification system plus peripartum cardiomyopathy, a real, distinct heart failure that can strike with no prior heart disease at all; and gout's real, honest finding that pregnancy-related gout content is genuinely thin because gout in pregnancy is remarkably rare, estrogen's own real uricosuric effect actively protecting against it. Prostate Health, the one condition with no real pregnancy angle at all, got a real, directly relevant male-line substitute instead: family history and inherited genetic risk, including a real, striking figure (BRCA2 carriers facing up to 60% lifetime prostate cancer risk by age 85) that functions as the same kind of concrete, actionable risk signal pregnancy content provides elsewhere.

**One more real, self-caught issue this batch, the same class already documented for IBD**: none this time -- the "check existing ids before writing predictably-named new ones" lesson from the IBD collision held, zero further duplicate-id incidents across all 8 remaining conditions.

**An honest, important distinction worth stating plainly, not overstated**: all 18 non-Hashimoto's conditions now have a real, genuine presence in every one of the four subcategory types Hashimoto's own depth is built from (staging/classification, organ systems, history, pregnancy/family planning). This is real, structural parity, not volumetric parity -- each condition gained roughly 4 new entries this pass (now totaling 13-19 entries per condition), a real fraction of Hashimoto's own 176. The `conditions` reference table's own `status` column is deliberately left at `in_progress` for all 18, not flipped to `built`, since that would overstate what's actually been achieved -- reaching Hashimoto's own true depth (the dozens of entries per subcategory, not just a first real representative of each) remains real, further, multi-session work, not completed by this pass.
